> मनुष्येष्टवात्सीमपजानीत यथेयं पुनरागच्छेदिति तस्यॆ हाविद्र्व्यरणा शयन
> उपबद्धास ततो ह गंधर्वा अन्यतरमुरणं प्रमेथुः । सा होवाचावीर इव बत
> मेऽजन इव पुत्रग्ं हरंतीति द्वितीयं प्रमेथुः सा ह तथैवोवाचाथ हायमीक्षां
> चक्रे कथं नु तदवीरं कथमजनग्स् स्याद्यत्राहग्स् स्यामिति स नग्न एवानूत्पपात चिरं
> तन्नेने यद्वासः पर्यधास्यत ततो ह गंधर्वा विद्युत् जनयां चक्रुस्तं यथा
> दिवैनं नग्नं ददर्श ततो हैवेयं तिरोबभूव पुनरैमात्येत्तिरो भूताग्ं स आध्या
> जल्पन्नुरुक्षेत्रग्ं समया चचारान्यतःप्लक्षेति बिसवती तस्यॆ हाध्यंतेनवप्राज तद्ध ता
> अप्सरस आतयो भूत्वा परिपुप्लुविरे तग्ं हेयं ज्ञात्वोवाच अयं वै स मनुष्यो
> यस्मिन्नहमवात्समिति ता होचुस्तस्मै वा आविरसामेति तथेति तस्मै हाविरासुस्ताग्ं
> हायं ज्ञात्वाभिपरोनाद हये जाये मनसेत्यादि । शत. ब्रा. ११-५-१ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The Gandharvas plan to remove her so that she may come back again. Two rams were tied at her bed; the Gandharvas carried one away. She cried, "As if I had no hero, no people, they are taking my son." They took the second, and she cried the same. Then he thought: "How can it be without hero, without people, where I am?" and, naked, he leapt up after them, not delaying to put on his garment. The Gandharvas produced lightning, and she saw him naked as by day; then she vanished. He came back and she had vanished; distressed, lamenting, he wandered over Kurukṣetra and came to the lotus pond Anyataḥplakṣā. The apsarases swam about there in the form of swans. She recognised him and said, "This is that mortal with whom I lived." They said, "Let us appear to him"; so they did. Recognising her, he called out: हये जाये मनसेत्यादि (*haye jāye manasetyādi*). (Śa. Brā. 11-5-1)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Translation: In the eighth anuvāka of the tenth maṇḍala of the Ṛgveda-Saṃhitā, four sūkta-s ended in the preceding adhyāya. Now this sūkta beginning हये जाये (*haye jāye*), the opening sūkta of the fifth adhyāya, is the fifth sūkta in the eighth anuvāka. It has eighteen Ṛks; the metre is Triṣṭubh. The Anukramaṇikā says: "Aila Purūravas, from desire for Urvaśī who had formerly lived with him, approached her again and wished to take her back; she, not wishing it, rejects him; three are Urvaśī's…" The verses of this sūkta हये जाये (*haye jāye*) (Ṛk 1), इषुर्न श्रिये (*iṣurna śriye*) (Ṛk 3), यासु जूर्णिः (*yāsu jūrṇiḥ*) (Ṛk 6), कदा सूनुः (*kadā sūnuḥ*) (Ṛk 12), सुदेवो अद्य (*sudevo adya*) (Ṛk 14), अंतरिक्षप्रां (*aṃtarikṣaprāṃ*) (Ṛk 17), and the three beginning सचा यदासु जहतीषु (*sacā yadāsu jahatīṣu*) (Ṛks 8-10)—in all these nine Ṛks are the words of Purūravas, son of Ilā, so Purūravas is the seer of these Ṛks; the other nine Ṛks are the words of Urvaśī, so Urvaśī is the seer of those Ṛks. Whatever subject is set forth in the words of the two is, for the respective Ṛks, the deity; the viniyoga has been given before.

Here the commentator tells a story about Purūravas, son of Ilā, and Urvaśī:

There is a history concerning Purūravas, son of Ilā, and Urvaśī. It will be set out here in detail. Formerly, while Mitra and Varuṇa were consecrated in the sacrificial ground, they saw an apsaras woman named Urvaśī there. By the mere sight of her, Mitra and Varuṇa's semen was discharged, and it fell into a pot (a vessel, or a vessel holding the water called vasatīvarī) that was there… **NOTE**

### Printed p. 19
…and the sage Pururavas, son of Ilā, and Urvaśī: there is an itihāsa concerning them, which will be told here in detail. In former times Mitra and Varuṇa, while consecrated at a sacrificial ground, saw there the celestial woman named Urvaśī. At the mere sight of her the semen of Mitra and Varuṇa was discharged, and it fell into a pot standing there (a vessel, or a vessel holding the water called vasatīvarī).

They cursed the person who had caused their discharge, that is Urvaśī, to be born on earth in human form and to experience the pleasures and pains natural to men. Meanwhile a king named Ila, hunting in a forest together with the sons of Manu, entered the grove called Devīkrīḍa. In that grove Pārvatī, sporting in love with Īśvara, was delighting that god with many moods. Because the Goddess (Pārvatī) sported there together with Īśvara, the grove came to be called Devīkrīḍa. While Pārvatī and Parameśvara were at their secret play in this grove, she had cursed it, wishing that no one should come there, so that any male who entered it would at once become a woman. Therefore King Ila, having entered that grove, at once became a woman, and wandering about in shame took refuge with Śiva, asking to be saved from this state. Śiva told the king that this was a work the Goddess herself must do, and that he should therefore please her and ask a boon. Accordingly the king took refuge with the Goddess and begged her to grant him manhood as before. The Goddess in compassion granted that the king should be a woman for six months of the year and a man for the other six months. While this went on, the king in woman's form was roaming the grove with female companions, celestial women, when Budha (son of the Moon) came there for some other reason, saw this beautiful woman Ilā, and was overcome with infatuation. Afterwards a king named Pururavas was born to Ilā by that Budha. Urvaśī saw this King Pururavas, who ruled in the city of Pratiṣṭhāna, and fell in love with him. Then a kind of agreement was made between Pururavas and Urvaśī: that except when lying in bed she would not see the king naked at any other time; that the king would keep her two lambs in his charge and guard them; and that if this were violated she would at once leave him and go. Having made this agreement with the king, the two lived happily, sporting with each other for four years. After four years had passed, one night the gods carried off the lambs that were in the king's keeping. Hearing the bleating of the lambs, the king said he would bring them back from the thieves who were carrying them off, rose from the bed and, without putting on clothes, set out in haste naked. At that moment a lightning flash shone very brightly, and Urvaśī saw the king naked. Saying that the agreement they had made earlier was broken, Urvaśī rose and went away to heaven. The king, returning and not finding Urvaśī in bed, searched for her everywhere and came to the shore of Lake Mānasa. There the king saw Urvaśī, who was sporting in the water in the middle of the lake with her companions, celestial women, and wishing to sport with her again,

### Printed p. 20
he entreated her in many ways, in tears. But Urvaśī did not agree to the king's words; she said, "What you say cannot be carried out; therefore give up your longing for me and go back." The subject of this conversation between King Pururavas and Urvaśī is what is stated in this sūkta.

The Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa of the Śukla Yajurveda also speaks thus on this matter:
उर्वशी हाप्सराः........................ताग्ं ह यं ज्ञात्वाभिपरोवाद हये जाये मनसा ॥ (*urvaśī hāpsarāḥ........................tāgṃ ha yaṃ jñātvābhiparovāda haye jāye manasā ‖*)
(Śa. Brā. 11-5-1 etc.)
Since we have given the meaning of these Brāhmaṇa passages on pages 7–9 of the introduction to this sūkta, there is no need to explain them again.

Maṇḍala 10; Anuvāka 8; Sūkta 95. Aṣṭaka 8; Adhyāya 5; Vargas 1, 2, 3, 4. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 18.
Ṛṣi: Ṛks 1, 2, 6, 8–10, 12, 14, 17: Pururavas Aiḍa; Ṛks 2, 4, 5, 6, 11, 13, 15, 16, 18: Urvaśī.
Deity: Ṛks 1, 3, 6, 8–10, 12, 14, 17: Urvaśī; Ṛks 2, 4, 5, 6, 11, 13, 15, 16, 18: Pururavas Aiḍa.
Metre: Triṣṭubh.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ह॒ये जाये॒ मन॑सा॒ तिष्ठ॑ घोरे॒ वचां॑सि मि॒श्रा कृ॑णवावहै॒ नु ।  
> न नौ॒ मंत्रा॒ अनु॑दितास ए॒ते मय॑स्कर॒न्पर॑तरे च॒नाह॑न् ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 21

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> हये । जाये । मनसा । तिष्ठ । घोरे । वचांसि । मिश्रा । कृणवावहै । नु ।  
> न । नौ । मंत्राः । अनुदितासः । एते । मयः । करन् । परतरे । चन । अहन् ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> पुरूरवसो वाक्यं । जायां पश्यन्वदति । हये हे घोरे । अस्माकं
> दुःखजनकत्वात् । घोरकारिणि जाये मनसास्मदुपर्यनुरागवता मनसा युक्ता
> सती तिष्ठ । क्षणमात्रं संनिधावेव निवस ॥ हय इत्यस्य निघाताभावश्चांदसः ॥ किमर्थं
> संस्थानमिति तत्राह । वचांसि वाक्यानि मिश्राण्युक्तिप्रत्युक्तिरूपाणि स्वद्य क्षिप्रं वा
> कृणवावहै करवावहै ॥ कृवि हिंसाकरणयोः । धिन्विकृणो रच्चेत्युप्रत्ययः ॥
> किमर्थं वचसः करणमिति चेत् उच्यते । नावावयोर्मंत्रा रहस्यार्थ एते
> विवक्षिता अनुदितासोऽप्याह्रियमाणाः परस्परमसंभाष्यमाणा गुंफिताः संतः परतरे
> चनाहन् । चनेति निपातसमुदायश्चार्थे । अनेकेषु दिवसेषु चरमेऽप्यहनि मयः
> सुखनामैतत् । सुखं न करन् । कुर्वंति । अतः कृणावाहहा इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This is Pururavas' speech; seeing his wife, he speaks. "Hye" means "O" (hē): O fierce one, because you cause us sorrow. O wife who acts fiercely, stay, with a mind full of affection for us; remain close by for just a moment. The absence of nighāta for हय (*haya*) is Vedic. Why stay? He says: let us exchange speeches (vacāṃsi), mixed, in the form of question and answer, today or quickly. Why make speech? These secret thoughts (mantrāḥ) of us two, if not uttered though intended, being merely strung together without being spoken to one another, do not bring pleasure (mayaḥ, a word for happiness), even on the last of many days; people do not make it. Hence "कृणवावहै (*kṛṇavāvahai*)".

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- हये घोरे जाये (*haye ghore jāye*)—O wife of hard heart
- मनसा (*manasā*)—with a mind showing affection for me
- तिष्ठ (*tiṣṭha*)—stand a little
- वचांसि (*vacāṃsi*)—words in the form of conversation
- नु (*nu*)—now
- मिश्रा (*miśrā*)—both of us together
- कृणवावहै (*kṛṇavāvahai*)—let us converse
- *nau ete maṃtrāḥ*—these feelings hidden within our hearts
- अनुदितासः (*anuditāsaḥ*)—if not spoken openly
- परतरे चन् अहन् (*paratare can ahan*)—even in the last days
- मयः (*mayaḥ*)—happiness
- न करन् (*na karan*)—do not produce

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Pururavas: O wife of hard heart, stay a little with a mind showing love for me. Let us now together exchange words in conversation. If the feelings hidden in our hearts are not spoken openly, they will not bring happiness even in the last days.

##### English translation
(Pururavas speaks :) Ho indignant wife, with mind (relenting) stay
awhile, let us now interchange discourse. These, our secret thoughts, whilst
unspoken, did not yield us happiness even at the last day.

### Printed p. 22

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Hayē—this word is used here in the sense of "हे (*he*)" (O), for addressing. Besides this Ṛk it is used in the following:
हये देवा यूयमिदापयः स्थ ते मृळत नाधमानाय मह्यं (*haye devā yūyamidāpayaḥ stha te mṛḷata nādhamānāya mahyaṃ*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-29-4)
हये नरो मरुतो मृळता नस्तुवीमघासो अमृता ऋतज्ञाः (*haye naro maruto mṛḷatā nastuvīmaghāso amṛtā ṛtajñāḥ*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-57-8)
It is used in eight Ṛks; nowhere else. **NOTE**

Ghore—Pururavas addresses Urvaśī as "ghore" to indicate that her mind, toward him, has shown pity yet is very cruel in nature.
Vacāṃsi miśrā kṛṇavāvahai nu—"Now let us mix our words" means: speaking to one another, let us exchange the thoughts in our minds. The meaning: I shall say what is in my mind; you say what is in yours.
Manasā tiṣṭha—O woman, stay for a while, with affection for me, in order to speak lovingly.
Na nau mantrā anuditāsaḥ—the secret matters we ought to have spoken have remained unspoken. Since we had not seen each other for a long time, secret conversation was not possible; so what ought to be said to each other has not been said.
Ete mayaskran paratare canāhan—though this secret conversation would be pleasing to us, since there was no opportunity to carry it on to the end (up to now), it has not been pleasant for us.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> किमे॒ता वा॒चा कृ॑णवा॒ तवा॒हं प्राक्र॑मिषमु॒षसा॑मग्रि॒येव॑ ।  
> पुरू॑रवः॒ पुन॒रस्तं॒ परे॑हि दुराप॒ना वात॑ इवा॒हम॑स्मि ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> किं । एता । वाचा । कृणव । तव । अहं । प्र । अक्रमिषं । उषसां । अग्रियाइव ।  
> पुरूरवः । पुनः । अस्तं । परा । इहि । दुःआपना । वातःइव । अहं । अस्मि ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 23

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अनया तमुर्वशी प्रत्युवाच । एतैतया वाचा केवलया पुनःसंभोगरहितया किं कृणव । किं करवाव । अहं त्विदानीं त्वत्सकाशात्प्राक्रमिषं । अतिक्रांतवत्यस्मि ।
> अतिक्रमे दृष्टांतः । उषसामग्रियेव । बह्नामुषसां मध्येऽग्राग्रे भवा
> पूर्वोषाः प्राक्रमाद्यथाहमपीति । यस्मादेवं तस्माद्दे पुरूरवः त्वं पुनरस्मत्सकाशादस्तं गृहं परेहि । परागच्छ । मा ममाभिलाषं कार्षिः । तस्या दुर्ग्रहत्वमाह । अहं वात इव वायुरिव दुरापना दुष्प्रापा दुरापा नास्मि । दुरापा ना अहं
> त्वय्यैतर्ह्यस्मि पुनर्गृहानिहीति हैनैनं तदुवाचेति वाजसनेयकं । शत. ११-५-१-७ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Urvaśī replied to him with this Ṛk. With this speech alone, devoid of renewed union, what shall we do? I have now moved away from you, gone beyond, like the foremost of the many dawns, who goes ahead at the front of them; so have I. Therefore, O Pururavas, go back from me to your home; do not have desire for me. He states her unattainability: I am like the wind, hard to attain (durāpanā). He cites the Vājasaneyaka: दुरापा ना अहं त्वय्यैतर्ह्यस्मि पुनर्गृहानिहीति हैनैनं तदुवाचेति (*durāpā nā ahaṃ tvayyaitarhyasmi punargṛhānihīti hainainaṃ taduvāceti*) (Śa. 11-5-1-7).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- एता वाचा (*etā vācā*)—by this our conversation
- किं कृणव (*kiṃ kṛṇava*)—what can we accomplish?
- उषसां अग्रिया इव (*uṣasāṃ agriyā iva*)—as the first-born dawn slips away so that no one can seize her
- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I
- तव (*tava*)—from you
- प्राक्रमिषं (*prākramiṣaṃ*)—have moved far away, come so as not to fall in your grasp
- पुरूरवः (*purūravaḥ*)—O Pururavas
- पुनः (*punaḥ*)—again (without following me)
- अस्तं (*astaṃ*)—to the home
- परेहि (*parehi*)—go back
- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I
- वातइव (*vātaiva*)—like the wind
- दुरापना (*durāpanā*)—not to be caught in your grasp
- अस्मि (*asmi*)—am

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Urvaśī: O Pururavas, what can we accomplish by this conversation? As the first-born dawn slips away so that none can hold her, I too have moved far from you, come so as not to fall in your grasp. O Pururavas, do not follow me again; go back home. Like the wind I have escaped, not caught in your grasp.

##### English translation
(Urvashi :) What can we accomplish through such discourse ? I have passed away from you like the first of the dawns. Return, pururavns, to your dwelling. I am as hard to catch as the wind.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

This is Urvaśī's speech: O Pururavas, since we cannot again unite with each other, what use is the mutual conversation you propose? Will a dawn that has passed come again on that same day? Never possible. Just as it is not possible to catch the blowing wind,

### Printed p. 24
so I am not one you can get. Therefore give up your longing for me and go back to your house: this is the main meaning.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इषु॒र्न क्रि॒य इ॑षु॒धेर॑स॒ना गो॒षाः श॑त॒सा न रंहिः॑ ।  
> अ॒वीरे॒ क्रत्वा॒ वि द॑विद्युत॒न्नोरा॒ न मा॒युं चि॑तयंत॒ धुन॑यः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इषुः । न । श्रिये । इषुधेः । असना । गोऽसाः । शतसाः । न । रंहिः ।  
> अवीरे । क्रत् । वि । दविद्युतत् । न । उरा । न । मायुं । चितयंत । धुनयः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अनया पुरूरवाः स्वस्य विरहजनितं वैक्लव्यं तां प्रति ब्रूते । इषुधेः ।
> इषवो धीयंतेऽत्रेतीषुधिर्निषंगः । ततः सकाशादिषुरसनासनाय्कॆ प्रक्षेपुं न
> भवति श्रिये विजयार्थं । त्वद्विरहाद्युद्धस्य बुद्धावप्यनिधानात् । तथा रंहिर्वेगवानहं शत्रुसकाशाद्गोषास्त्रेषां शत्क्रोणां गवां संभक्ता नाभवं । तथा शतसाः
> शतानामपरिमितानां शत्रुधनानां संभक्ता नाभवं । किंचावीरे वीरवर्जिते क्रता
> राजकर्मणि सति न वि दविद्युतत् । न विद्योतते । न मत्सामर्थ्यं । किंच धुनयः
> कंपयितारोऽस्मदीया नरा उरोर् ॥ सुपां सुलुगिति सप्तम्या डादेशः ॥
> विस्तीर्णे संग्रामे मायुं । मायते प्रक्षिप्यत इति मायुः शब्दः ॥ कृवापाजीत्यादिनोण् ॥ सिंहनादं न चितयंत । न बुध्यंते ॥ चिती संज्ञानेत् । अस्माण्णिचि
> संज्ञापूर्वकस्य विधेरनित्यत्वाल्लघूपधगुणाभावः । छांदसो लज् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

With this Ṛk Pururavas tells her of his own distress born of separation. Iṣudhi is the quiver, where arrows are placed. From it, the arrow cannot be shot for victory (śriye), since because of separation from her his mind does not attend even to battle. Likewise, I, swift (raṃhi), have not become a sharer of hundreds of cows from the enemy, nor of the countless hundreds of enemy riches (śatasāḥ). Moreover, when the royal work is without a hero, my capacity does not shine forth. Further, my men, the shakers (dhunayaḥ), in the wide battle do not notice the lion-roar (māyu, that which is hurled forth); they do not perceive it. The remaining remarks concern grammar.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इषुधेः (*iṣudheḥ*)—from the quiver
- इषुः (*iṣuḥ*)—the arrow
- श्रिये (*śriye*)—to gain victory
- असना (*asanā*)—to shoot
- न (*na*)—it is not possible
- गोपाः (*gopāḥ*)—to gain the enemy's cows **NOTE**
- रंहिः न (*raṃhiḥ na*)—I cannot rush forward swiftly
- शतसाः (*śatasāḥ*)—their many riches cannot be gained
- अवीरे (*avīre*)—powerless
- क्रत्ौ (*kratau*)—in my works
- न वि दविद्युतत् (*na vi davidyutat*)—my capacity

### Printed p. 25

**Pratipadārtha** (continued)
- (…) it does not shine at all.
- धुनयः (*dhunayaḥ*)—my warriors, who make my enemies tremble
- उरा (*urā*)—in the wide battle
- मायुम् (*māyum*)—my voice, which is my command
- न चितयंत (*na citayaṃta*)—they do not understand

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Pururavas: it is quite impossible for me to shoot an arrow from my quiver to win victory. I cannot rush forward to take the enemy's cattle. Their many riches cannot be won. In my deeds, drained of strength, my capacity does not shine at all. My warriors, who make my enemies tremble, do not understand my voice of command (my order) in the wide battle.

##### English translation
(Pururavas:) The arrow is not to be cast from the quiver for glory, I am no longer the Impetuous despoiler of the cattle (of the enemy), nor of their hundred fold (riches). My prowess having lost its strength, (my might) no longer flashes forth; (my warriors) the terrifiers (of the foe) hear not my shout in battle.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Main purport: this is Pururavas' speech. Scorched with grief at separation from Urvashi, he describes here his helpless state, in which he is unable to do any work. To conquer enemies in battle and carry off their wealth, he cannot take the arrows from his quiver and use them, because his mind is not steady. He has no wish to rush forward to win the cattle that the enemies hold. In the other affairs of state that must be done in counsel with his brave soldiers, his mind does not stay steady either, and he has no capacity to do those tasks. His warriors, able to make the enemy tremble, have no chance to hear the lion-roar of their leader. The reason for all this is that his mind has no enthusiasm, and that mind of his is utterly afflicted by separation from her.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सा वसु॒ दध॑ती॒ श्वशु॑राय॒ वय॒ उषो॒ यदि॒ वष्ट्यंति॑गृहात् ।  
> अस्तं॑ ननक्षे॒ यस्मि॑ञ्चा॒कन्दिवा॒ नक्तं॑ शृथि॒ता वै॑त॒सेन॑ ॥ ४ ॥

### Printed p. 26

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सा । वसु । दधती । श्वशुराय । वयः । उषः । यदि । वष्टि । अंतिगृहात् ।  
> अस्तं । ननक्षे । यस्मिन् । चाकन् । दिना । नक्तं । श्नथिता । वैतसेन ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इदमुत्तरं चोर्वशीवाक्यं । आद्येन पुरात्मना कृतमुषसे निवेदयति । हे
> उषः सेयमुर्वशी वसु वासकं वयोऽन्नं श्वशुराय भर्तुः पुरूरवसः पित्रे
> दधती प्रयच्छंती तत्र गृहे स्थिता यदि पतिं वष्टि कामयते तदांतिगृहात् ।
> स्वभर्तृभोगगृहांतिके यच्छ्वरस्य भोजनगृहं तदंतिगृहं । तस्माद्गृहात्सोर्वश्यस्तं पतिगृहं ननक्षे । व्याप्नोति । यस्मिन्गृहे चाकन् कामयत उर्वशी । सा
> चोर्वशी दिवा नक्तमहनि रात्र्य च वैतसेन । शेपो वैतस इति पुंस्रजननस्यॆति
> निरुक्तं । ३-२१ । पुंस्रजननेन शृथिता ताडिता च भवति । एवमुर्वश्यात्मानं
> परोक्षेण निर्विदेश ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This and what follows is Urvaśī's speech. By the first verse she tells Uṣas what was done by "the former one" (पुरात्मना (*purātmanā*)).**NOTE** "O Uṣas, this Urvaśī, giving wealth (वसु (*vasu*), 'dwelling') and food to her father-in-law, the father of her husband Purūravas, and living in that house, if she desires her husband, then from the अंतिगृह (*aṃtigṛha*)", that is, the dining-house of the father-in-law, which stands near her husband's house of enjoyment, "she reaches that husband's house. In whichever house she desired (him), there Urvaśī, by day and night, is beaten (शृथिता (*śṛthitā*)) with the वैतस (*vaitasa*)." Sāyaṇa cites Nirukta 3-21: शेपो वैतस (*śepo vaitasa*) is the male generative organ; she is struck with the male organ. Thus Urvaśī spoke of herself in the third person.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उषः (*uṣaḥ*)—O goddess Uṣas
- सा (*sā*)—Urvashi
- वसु (*vasu*)—wealth
- वयः (*vayaḥ*)—and food
- श्वशुरायॆ (*śvaśurāyè*)—to her husband's father
- दधती (*dadhatī*)—giving
- यदि (*yadi*)—if
- वष्टि (*vaṣṭi*)—she desires her husband, then
- अंतिगृहात् (*aṃtigṛhāt*)—from the nearby house of the father-in-law
- अस्तं (*astaṃ*)—the husband's house
- ननक्षे (*nanakṣe*)—she enters
- यस्मिन् (*yasmin*)—in whichever house
- चाकन् (*cākan*)—where she longs for her husband
- दिवा नक्तं (*divā naktaṃ*)—day and night
- वैतसेन (*vaitasena*)—by the male organ
- शृथिता (*śṛthitā*)—she is beaten for the attainment of pleasure. [वैतसेन शृथिता (*vaitasena śṛthitā*) means: he strikes me with the organ of generation and gives me delight; this is the purport.]

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(Urvashi:) O goddess Uṣas, Urvashi, wishing to be with her husband, gives food and other desired things to her husband's father; and if she then desires union with her husband, she enters her husband's house from the nearby house of the father-in-law. In whichever house she longs for her husband, there, night and day, struck with her husband's male organ, she enjoys pleasure.

##### English translation
(Urvashi :) If, Ushas, this Urvashi, offering food and wealth to her father-iu-law, loves (her husband), she has repaired from the neighbouring house to her husband's home, where she loved (her lord), being delighted night and day by his embraces.

### Printed p. 27

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

This Ṛk and the next are Urvashi's words. In this Ṛk Urvashi tells the goddess Uṣas how she behaved when she lived with Pururavas in his father's house. O goddess Uṣas, when Urvashi (she herself) formerly lived in her husband's house, she served her father-in-law and her husband Pururavas, bringing them food and other meals, and clothes and the like, from time to time. When she wanted the pleasure of love, she would leave the father-in-law's house, come to her husband's house, which was close by, and there with Pururavas, without distinction of day or night, at all times, enjoyed the pleasure she wanted through the touch of her husband's male organ and the like.
For श्नथिता वैतसेन (*śnathitā vaitasena*), see the Special Topics of the next Ṛk for an explanation of these words.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्रिः स्म॒ माह्नः॑ श्णथयो वैत॒सेनो॒त स्म॒ मेऽव्य॑त्यै पृणासि ।  
> पुरू॑र॒वोऽनु॑ ते॒ केत॑मायं॒ राजा॑ मे वीर त॒न्नग१॒॑स्तदा॑सीः ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> त्रिः । स्म । मा । अह्नः । श्नथयः । नैतसेन । उत । स्म । मे । अव्यत्यै । पृणासि ।  
> पुरूरवः । अनु । ते । केतं । आयं । राजा । मे । वीर । तन्नः । तत् । आसीः ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अनेन पुरूरवसमेव संबोध्योक्तवती । हे पुरूरवः त्वं मा मामहोऽहनि वैतसेन दंडेन पुंव्यंजनेन त्रिस्तिवारं ॥ द्वित्रिचतुर्भ्यः सुच् ।
> पा. ५-४-१८ ॥ शृथयः स्म । अश्नथयः । अताडयः ॥ कृत्तोर्थप्रयोगे । पा. २-३-
> ६४ । इति कालवाचिनोऽहशब्दादधिकरणे षष्ठि ॥ उतापि च । स्मेति पूर्णाः । अव्यत्येक
> सपत्नीभिः सह पर्यायेण पतिमागच्छति सा व्यती । न तादृश्यव्यती । तस्यॆ मे
> मह्यं पृणासि । पूरयसि । एवं बुद्ध्या हे पुरूरवः ते तव केतं गृहमताय
> अन्वगमं पूर्वं । हे वीर राजा त्वं च मे मम तन्नः शरीरस्य तत्तदासीः ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

By this verse she speaks, addressing Purūravas himself. "O Purūravas, you struck me three times a day (अह्नः (*ahnaḥ*), a genitive of time in the sense of the locative, 'in the day') with the staff, the male organ (वैतसेन (*vaitasena*)); शृथयः स्म (*śṛthayaḥ sma*) means you beat me." उत (*uta*) means "and also"; स्म (*sma*) is a filler. She who goes to her husband in turn with co-wives is व्यती (*vyatī*); one not so is व्यती (*vyatī*); to such a one, me, you give fulfilment. With this thought, O Purūravas, I formerly followed to your house (केतं (*ketaṃ*)). "And you, O hero, king, were (the lord) of my body."

### Printed p. 28

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Pururavas) मा (*mā*)—me
- अह्नः (*ahnaḥ*)—in days
- त्रिः (*triḥ*)—three times
- वैतसेन (*vaitasena*)—with your organ of generation
- शृथयः स्म (*śṛthayaḥ sma*)—you have struck me and given me delight
- उत (*uta*)—moreover
- अव्यत्यै (*avyatyai*)—who am without a co-wife
- मे (*me*)—to me
- पृणासि (*pṛṇāsi*)—you have given satisfaction
- पुरूरवः (*purūravaḥ*)—O Pururavas
- ते (*te*)—your
- केतं (*ketaṃ*)—to the house
- अन्वायं (*anvāyaṃ*)—I came following you
- वीर (*vīra*)—O hero
- तत् (*tat*)—then
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- मे मम तन्नः (*me mama tannaḥ*)—of my body
- आसीः (*āsīḥ*)—you were lord and giver of pleasure

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(Urvashi:) O Pururavas, hero, formerly you embraced me three times a day and gave me pleasure by the striking of the male organ. You gave satisfaction to me, who was without a co-wife. I followed you and came to your house. Then you were my lord and the giver of my pleasure.

##### English translation
Thirce a day, Pururavas, have you embraced me, you have loved me without a rival ; I have followed (you) to your dwelling, you, hero, have been the sovereign of my person.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk Urvashi addresses Pururavas himself: त्रिः स्म माह्नः शृथयो वैतसेन (*triḥ sma māhnaḥ śṛthayo vaitasena*)—O Pururavas, when I lived with you formerly, did you not strike me, that is, my yoni region, with your male organ three times a day?
To explain the word वैतस (*vaitasa*) here, Yāska has given an etymology. When listing words of one meaning in pairs (Ni. 3-20), among pairs of words such as प्रपित्वे (*prapitve*)–अभीकॆ (*abhīkè*) and दभ्रं (*dabhraṃ*)–अर्भकं (*arbhakaṃ*), he has given the two words *śepaḥ*–वैतसः (*vaitasaḥ*). These two words have, more or less, one meaning.
शेपो वैतस इति पुंस्रजननस्य । शेषः शपतेः स्पृशतिकर्मणः । वैतसो वितस्तं भवति । यस्यामुशंतः प्रहराम शेषं । त्रिः स्म माह्नः श्नथयो वैतसेन इत्यपि निगमौ भवतः ॥ (*śepo vaitasa iti puṃsrajananasya | śeṣaḥ śapateḥ spṛśatikarmaṇaḥ | vaitaso vitastaṃ bhavati | yasyāmuśaṃtaḥ praharāma śeṣaṃ | triḥ sma māhnaḥ śnathayo vaitasena ityapi nigamau bhavataḥ ‖*)
(Ni. 3-21)

### Printed p. 29

The portion of the male organ that is like a staff (penis) is called शेप (*śepa*) or वैतस (*vaitasa*). The word शेप (*śepa*) is formed from the root शप् (*śap*), "to touch". In sexual union, because the woman's yoni is touched by this male organ, the organ has come to be called शेप (*śepa*). The word वैतस (*vaitasa*) has this meaning in the sense of "being a span long", "being in a weakened state". Because this male member is in a weakened state before touching a woman, it is called वैतस (*vaitasa*). During union with a woman this organ is in an aroused state; at other times it is in the span-long state, that is, in a weakened state. Therefore this staff-like male organ is called वैतस (*vaitasa*); so Yāska has explained the meaning. As examples he has given two Vedic sentences: यस्यामुशंतः प्रहराम शेषं (*yasyāmuśaṃtaḥ praharāma śeṣaṃ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-85-37) and त्रिः स्म माह्नः श्नथयो वैतसेन (*triḥ sma māhnaḥ śnathayo vaitasena*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-95-5).

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> या सु॑जू॒र्णिः श्रेणिः॑ सु॒म्म आ॑पिर्र्॒दे च॑क्षु॒र्न ग्रं॒थिनी॑  ॒
> चरण्युः ।  
> ता अं॒जयो॑ऽरु॒णयो॒ न स॑स్రుः श्रि॒ये गावो॒न धे॒नवो॑ऽनवंत ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> या । सुजूर्णिः । श्रेणिः । सुम्मेऽआपिः । प्रदेऽचक्षुः । न । ग्रंथिनी । चरण्युः ।  
> ताः । अंजयः । अरुणयः । न । सस్రుः । श्रिये । गावः । न । धेनवः । अनवंत ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> पुरूरवसो वाक्यं । या सुजूर्णिः सुजवैतन्नामिकास्ति तथा या श्रेणियार्
> सुम्मआपिर्या प्रदेचक्षुः । नकारः समुच्छये । ताभिश्चतसृभिरालीभूताभिर्मानिनीभिः
> सहिता ग्रंथिनी ग्रंथनवती संदर्भवती चरण्युश्चरणशीलोर्वश्या जगाम । यद्वा ।
> ग्रंथिनीत्येतत्सखिभूताप्सरोनामधेयं । या सुजूर्णिः सुजवोर्वशी सा ताभिः
> सह जगाम । ता अप्सरसोऽंजय आभरणोपेता अरुणयोऽरुणवर्णा न सस्रुः ।
> पूर्ववन्नगच्छंति । श्रिये श्रयणाय धेनवो नवप्रसूता गावो न गाव इव ।
> आश्रयार्थं यथा गावोऽनवंत शब्दायंते तथा न शब्दयंतीति व्यतिरेके
> दृष्टांतः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This is Purūravas' speech. She who is सुजूर्णिः (*sujūrṇiḥ*) is a companion of that name (the text reads सुजवैतन्नामिका (*sujavaitannāmikā*))**NOTE**; likewise she who is श्रेणिः (*śreṇiḥ*), she who is सुम्मआपिः (*summaāpiḥ*), she who is प्रदेचक्षुः (*pradecakṣuḥ*). The particle न (*na*) is in the sense of "and". Together with these four proud companions, Granthinī (she who has composition, संदर्भवती (*saṃdarbhavatī*)) and the roving Urvaśī went. Or else: ग्रंथिनी (*graṃthinī*) is likewise the name of a companion apsaras; Urvaśī, who is सुजूर्णिः (*sujūrṇiḥ*) ("swift"), went with them. Those apsarases, adorned with ornaments and red-hued, do not go as before. "For refuge (श्रिये (*śriye*)) they do not sound like (न (*na*)) cows (धेनवः (*dhenavaḥ*)), newly calved ones": as cows low for the sake of refuge, so these do not sound; the simile is by way of contrast.

### Printed p. 30

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- या सुजूर्णिः (*yā sujūrṇiḥ*)—with the companion who is named Sujūrṇi
- श्रेणिः (*śreṇiḥ*)—and with Śreṇi
- सुम्म आपिः (*summa āpiḥ*)—and with her called Sumna-api
- प्रदेचक्षुः (*pradecakṣuḥ*)—and with her called Pradecakṣus
- ग्रंथिनी (*graṃthinī*)—and with her called Granthinī
- चरण्युः (*caraṇyuḥ*)—Urvashi, of excellent roving, has come
- ताः (*tāḥ*)—all those apsara women
- अंजयः (*aṃjayaḥ*)—being adorned with ornaments
- अरुणयः (*aruṇayaḥ*)—and of attractive golden colour
- न सस्रुः (*na sasruḥ*)—do not run away as before
- धेनवः (*dhenavaḥ*)—newly calved
- गावो न (*gāvo na*)—like cows
- श्रिये (*śriye*)—for refuge
- अनवंत (*anavaṃta*)—they make sound

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(Pururavas:) Urvashi, of excellent roving, has come with all these companions, Sujurni, Shreni, Sumna-api, Pradechakshus and Granthini. All those apsara women, adorned with ornaments and of attractive golden colour, do not run away as before. Like newly calved cows they make sound for refuge.

##### English translation
(Pururavas) Sujurni, Shreni, Sumna-api, Hradechakshus, Granthini and the swift-moving (Urvashi who arrived) they, decorated and purple-tinted did not go first, they lowed like milch-cows for protection.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Sāyaṇa says that what stands in this Ṛk is Pururavas' speech. Here the names of six apsara women are indicated: सुजूर्णिः श्रेणिः सुम्म आपिः प्रदेचक्षुः ग्रंथिनी चरण्णुः (*sujūrṇiḥ śreṇiḥ summa āpiḥ pradecakṣuḥ graṃthinī caraṇṇuḥ*). All of them are adorned with ornaments and shine with a ruddy colour (red). But they do not run away as before. Among these six Urvashi is included, or else these six are together with Urvashi. They do not sound loudly, that is, they do not speak. An illustration is given for this. It is natural for newly calved cows to low for protection; as they do, these do not make sound, that is, they do not cry out loudly but speak softly; this is the purport.
The meaning of this Ṛk is not clear. It is difficult to understand what connection the names of the six apsara women mentioned here have with their going away or making sound, or with the conversation in the sūkta. But the commentator, in his commentary on the next Ṛk, says

### Printed p. 31

अनय्यताभिः सह संसर्गस्तयानुभूत इत्युर्वशी वदति (*anayyatābhiḥ saha saṃsargastayānubhūta ityurvaśī vadati*): in this Ṛk (the second Ṛk) Urvashi tells what kind of relation Pururavas had with these divine women spoken of before. Or else he explains the meaning thus: जायमाने यज्ञार्थं प्रवर्धमाने सति ग्ना देवपत्न्योऽपि समासत समभवन् (*jāyamāne yajñārthaṃ pravardhamāne sati gnā devapatnyo 'pi samāsata samabhavan*), that is, one may also say that these divine women all gathered together here for the sake of the sacrifice.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सम॑स्मि॒ञ्ञाय॑मान आसत॒ ग्ना उ॒तेम॑वर्धन्न॒द्य१॒ं॑ः स्वगू॑र्ताः ।  
> म॒हे यत्त्वा॑ पुरूरवो॒ रणा॒याव॑र्धयन्दस्यु॒हत्या॑य  ॒
> देवाः ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सं । अस्मिन् । जायमाने । आसत । ग्नाः । उत । ईं । अवर्धन् । नद्यः । स्वगूर्ताः।  
> महे । यत् । त्वा । पुरूरवः । रणाय । अवर्धयन् । दस्युहत्याय । देवाः ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अनयैताभिः सह संसर्गस्त्वयानुभूत इत्युवर्षी वदति । अस्मिन्सुरूरवसि
> जायमाने सति ग्ना अप्सरसो देववेश्या अपि समासत । संगता अभवन् ! अथवा
> जायमाने यज्ञार्थं प्रवर्धमाने सति ग्ना देवपत्योऽपि समासत । समभवन्।
> उतापि चेमेनं पुरूरवसं स्वगूर्ताः स्वयंगामिन्नो नद्यस्तासामाश्रयभूता
> अवर्धयन् ! किंच हे पुरूरवः यद्यस्मात्वा त्वां महे महते रणाय
> रमणीयाय संग्रामाय दस्युहत्याय दस्युहननाय देवास्वामवर्धयन् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Urvaśī says: "A union with these (women) was experienced by you." When this Purūravas was born, the ग्नाः (*gnāḥ*), the apsarases, the courtesans of the gods, gathered together. Or else, when he was growing for the sake of the sacrifice, the wives of the gods also came together. Moreover the self-moving rivers, whose support (he was), made this Purūravas grow. And further, O Purūravas, since the gods made you grow for great, delightful battle, for the slaying of the dasyus (the final clause is cut off at the break of the block in the print order, the sense being "therefore…").

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अस्मिन् (*asmin*)—this Pururavas
- जायमाने (*jāyamāne*)—as soon as he was born
- ग्नाः (*gnāḥ*)—the divine women
- समासत (*samāsata*)—surrounded him
- उत (*uta*)—and
- ईं (*īṃ*)—him
- स्वगूर्ताः (*svagūrtāḥ*)—self-moving
- नद्यः (*nadyaḥ*)—rivers
- अवर्धन् (*avardhan*)—made grow
- पुरूरवः (*purūravaḥ*)—O Pururavas
- यत् (*yat*)—for the very reason (that)
- महे (*mahe*)—great
- रणाय (*raṇāya*)—for

### Printed p. 32
(Urvaśī:) As soon as he was born, the wives of the gods surrounded him, and the spontaneously flowing rivers nourished him. O Pururavas, for a great battle, and for the destruction of the Dasyus, the gods made you grow strong.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यद्धार्थवागियू (*yaddhārthavāgiyū*)—for the sake of battle
- दस्युहत्याय (*dasyuhatyāya*)—for the destruction of the Dasyus
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- देवाः (*devāḥ*)—the gods
- अवर्धन् (*avardhan*)—made you grow strong and thrive

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(Urvaśī:) As soon as this Pururavas was born, the wives of the gods surrounded him. The spontaneously flowing rivers nourished him. O Pururavas, for a great battle and for the destruction of the Dasyu people, the gods made you grow strong.

##### English translation
(Urvashi :) As soon as he was born the wives (of the gods) surrounded him, the spontaneously flowing rivers nourished him, for the gods reared you, Pururavas, for a mighty conflict, for the slaughter of the Dasyus.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

As an example of a Ṛk in which the word पुरूरवाः (*purūravāḥ*) is used, Yāska gives an etymology on this Ṛk:
समासतास्मिञ्चायमाने ग्ना गमनादापः । देवपत्न्यो वापि चैनमवर्धयन्नद्यः स्वगूर्ताः स्वयंगामिन्यो वहते च यत्त्वा पुरूरवो रणाय रमणीयाय संग्रामायावर्धयन् दस्युहत्याय च देना देवाः ॥ (*samāsatāsmiñcāyamāne gnā gamanādāpaḥ | devapatnyo vāpi cainamavardhayannadyaḥ svagūrtāḥ svayaṃgāminyo vahate ca yattvā purūravo raṇāya ramaṇīyāya saṃgrāmāyāvardhayan dasyuhatyāya ca denā devāḥ ‖*) (Ni. 10-47)
Here it is said that as soon as this Pururavas was born, all the wives of the gods gathered together at one place and made him grow; or else, since waters have the nature of flowing, ग्ना (*gnā*) is the name for waters (above it was explained as "wives of the gods"); the rivers, full of such waters and flowing of themselves, made this Pururavas grow. In this context the word Pururavas must be explained as the thunderbolt that makes much noise, that is, the middle Agni. The meaning can be given that the rivers, that is, the waters (नदना आपः (*nadanā āpaḥ*)), made this middle Agni grow in the rainy season.
In the latter half too, two kinds of meaning can be given. One meaning: the wives of the gods, the rivers and the gods nourished and reared this newly born Pururavas to fight enemies in delightful battles and to slay the Dasyu people.
Another meaning: taking Pururavas as the thunderbolt or lightning (middle Agni) that makes much noise, the gods made it grow so as to roar and fight with the clouds, and for the slaying of the cloud (Dasyu).

### Printed p. 33
The main purport is this: in this Ṛk the word Pururavas is given two kinds of meaning, the king named Pururavas and the lightning in the atmosphere, or middle Agni. Because this middle Agni (thunderbolt) makes much noise, it is called here by the name Pururavas: puru, "much", rava, "noisy".
In the sense of Pururavas the king: as soon as this king was born, the wives of the gods and the deities presiding over the rivers, all gathering together, nourished and reared him so that, becoming king, he might be able to conquer enemies in battle and slay the Dasyu people.
In the sense of Pururavas as middle Agni: in the rainy season the waters in the form of clouds (ग्नाः (*gnāḥ*) and नद्यः (*nadyaḥ*)) made this middle Agni grow, which roars much and strikes the clouds. That is, the lightning or middle Agni appearing amid the clouds in the rainy season grows, roaring from time to time and striking the clouds (fighting).

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सचा॒ यदा॑सु॒ जह॑ती॒ष्वत्क॒ममा॑नुषेषु॒ मानु॑षो नि॒षेवे॑ ।  
> अप॑ स्म॒ मत्त॒रसं॑ती॒ न भु॒ज्युस्ता अ॑त्रसन्नथ॒स्पृशो॒ नाश्वाः॑ ॥८॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सचा । यत् । आसु । जहतीषु । अत्कं । अमानुषेषु । मानुषः । निसेवे ।  
> अप । स्म । मत् । तरसंती । न । भुज्युः । ताः । अत्रसन् । रथस्पृशः । न । अश्वाः ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इदमादित्रीण्यैळवाक्यानि । तत्रादितो द्वाभ्यामुर्वशीमन्याश्च सह स्तौति ।
> यद्यदा सचा सहायभूतः पुरूरवा अत्कं स्वकीयं रूपं । अत्क इति रूपनाम ।
> जहतीषु परित्यजंतीष्वमानुषीषु देवताभूतास्वप्सरःसु मानुषः सन्निषेवे
> अभिमुखं गच्छति तदानीं ता अप्सरसो मन्मत्तोऽपापसृत्यात्रसन् । त्रसतिर्गतिकर्मा।
> 3

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This and the next two Ṛks are the speeches of Aiḷa (Pururavas). Of these, the first two praise Urvaśī and the others together. When Pururavas, as companion, a mortal, comes face to face with these non-human nymphs, divine beings, who are abandoning their own form (अत्क (*atka*) is a name for form), those Apsarases flee away from him. त्रसति (*trasati*) has the sense of going.
### Printed p. 34

> गच्छंति । पलायने दृष्टांतः । तरसंती भुज्युर्न । तरसन्नाम मृगः । तस्य स्त्री
> भुज्युर्भोगसाधनभूता स्त्री मृगी । सा यथा व्याधाद्भीता पलायते । किंच
> रथस्पृशो नाश्वा रथे नियुक्ता अश्वा इव । यथा ते पलायंते तद्वत्पलायंत
> इति । उर्वश्यानेकाभिरस्माभिः सह भोगमनुभुक्तवानसीत्युक्तः प्रत्याचष्टे ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

An illustration of the flight is given: like a female तरसन् (*tarasan*), a kind of deer; भुज्युः (*bhujyuḥ*) is the woman who serves for enjoyment, here the doe. As she flees in fear of the hunter, so they flee; and like horses yoked to a chariot, as those run away, so they ran. Being told by Urvaśī, "You have enjoyed pleasure with us, many as we are," he replies.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत् (*yat*)—when
- मानुषः (*mānuṣaḥ*)—Pururavas, in human form
- अत्कं (*atkaṃ*)—their own form
- जहतीषु (*jahatīṣu*)—who had abandoned (it)
- आसु अमानुषेषु (*āsu amānuṣeṣu*)—with these divine women
- सचा (*sacā*)—joining
- निषेवे (*niṣeve*)—became face to face with them; then
- ताः (*tāḥ*)—those divine women
- मत् (*mat*)—leaving me
- तरसंति भुज्युः न (*tarasaṃti bhujyuḥ na*)—like a frightened doe
- रथस्पृशः अश्वाः न (*rathaspṛśaḥ aśvāḥ na*)—like horses harnessed to a chariot
- अप अत्रसन् (*apa atrasan*)—fled in fright

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(Pururavas:) When Pururavas, in human form, joined these divine women who had abandoned their bodies and came face to face with them, those divine women left me and fled in fright, like a frightened doe and like horses harnessed to a chariot.

##### English translation
(Pururavas:) When, becoming there companion, (Pururavas) the mortal associated with these immortal (nymphs) who had abandoned their bodies, they fled from me like a timid doe, like horses harnessed to a chariot.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

This Ṛk and the next two are the words of Pururavas, son of Iḷā. In the first two Ṛks (Ṛks 8–9) Pururavas speaks of Urvaśī and the other Apsaras women.
While all those divine women, that is, those born of the gods, who are not human, were together, I, a human (Pururavas), went towards them showing them my own body; then all those Apsaras women moved away from me and fled. How did they flee? As a doe of the kind called तरसत् (*tarasat*), seeing a hunter, flees in fear, or as horses yoked to a chariot (urged by the charioteer) run swiftly, so
### Printed p. 35
these Apsaras women also left me, moved far off and fled. Pururavas says that on seeing me, a human, these Apsaras women, born of divine bodies, quickly ran away from there.
अत्कं (*atkaṃ*)—अत्कं इति रूपनाम । (*atkaṃ iti rūpanāma |*) अत्क (*atka*) means form, shape. Here it means the bodies of the divine women.
आसु जहतीषु अमानुषीषु (*āsu jahatīṣu amānuṣīṣu*)—the purport is that these non-human Apsaras women, seeing the man (Pururavas) coming towards them (मानुषो निषेवे (*mānuṣo niṣeve*)), abandoned the place and went away.
तरसंती न भुज्युः (*tarasaṃtī na bhujyuḥ*)—भुज्युर्भोगसाधनभूता स्त्रीमृगी (*bhujyurbhogasādhanabhūtā strīmṛgī*): भुज्यु (*bhujyu*) means the woman who serves for enjoyment; here, the doe of the kind तरसंती (*tarasaṃtī*). As this doe, on seeing the hunter, runs away in fear that he will kill her, so, the seer describes by illustration, these Apsaras women ran away on seeing the human Pururavas.
रथस्पृशो न अश्वाः (*rathaspṛśo na aśvāḥ*)—a further illustration of running away is given here. As horses yoked to a chariot, urged by the stroke of the charioteer's whip, run swiftly, so these Apsaras women also ran away from Pururavas's presence.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यदा॑सु॒ मर्तो॑ अ॒मृता॑सु नि॒स्पृक्सं क्षो॒णीभिः॒ क्रतु॑भि॒र्न  ॒
> प्रंक्ते ।  
> ता आ॒तयो॒ न त॒न्वः॑ शुंभत॒ स्वा अश्वा॑सो॒ न क्री॒ळयो॒ दंद॑शानाः ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत् । आसु । मर्तः । अमृतासु । निस्पृक् । क्षोणीभिः । क्रतुभिः । न । पृंक्ते ।  
> ताः । आतयः । न । तन्वः । शुंभत । स्वाः । अश्वासः । न ! क्रीळयः । दंदशानाः ॥ ९ ॥

### Printed p. 36

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यद्यदास्वमृतास्वप्सरःसु मर्तो मनुष्यः पुरूरवा निस्पृक् निःशेषेण
> स्पृशन् क्षोणीभिर्वाग्निः क्रतुभिर्न कर्मभिश्च सं पृंक्ते संपर्कं करोति । नकारः
> समुच्चयार्थः । ता अप्सरस आतय आतिभूतास्तदानीं स्वास्तन्व आत्मीयानि रूपाणि न
> शुंभत । न प्रकाशयंति । अश्वासो नाश्वा इव क्रीळयः संक्रीडमाना दंदशाना
> दंदशूका जिह्वाभिरात्मीयाः सृक्वा भक्षयंतः । ते यथा चापल्येन धावंतः
> स्वरूपं न प्रयच्छंति रथिकाय तद्वदिति दुःखाद्ब्रूते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

When a mortal, the human Pururavas, in close touch with these immortal Apsarases, comes into contact with them by speech (क्षोणीभिः (*kṣoṇībhiḥ*), taken as *vāgbhiḥ*) and by actions (क्रतुभिः (*kratubhiḥ*)), the particle न (*na*) having the sense of conjunction, then those Apsarases, having become आति (*āti*) birds, do not display their own bodies, their own forms. Like horses playing, दंदशानाः (*daṃdaśānāḥ*), biting, devouring with their tongues, which run in wantonness and do not give their own form to the charioteer, so they do. Thus he speaks out of grief.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत् (*yat*)—when
- अमृतासु (*amṛtāsu*)—who are free from mortality
- आसु (*āsu*)—with these Apsaras women
- मर्तः (*martaḥ*)—the human Pururavas
- निस्पृक् (*nispṛk*)—joining
- क्षोणीभिः (*kṣoṇībhiḥ*)—by words
- ऋतुभिः न (*ṛtubhiḥ na*)—and also by actions
- सं पृंक्ते (*saṃ pṛṃkte*)—comes into contact; then
- ताः (*tāḥ*)—these same Apsaras women
- आतयः (*ātayaḥ*)—in the form of water-fowl
- स्वाः तन्नः (*svāḥ tannaḥ*)—their own bodies
- क्रीळयः (*krīḷayaḥ*)—playing
- दंदशानाः (*daṃdaśānāḥ*)—grazing with grass held in their jaws
- अश्वासो न (*aśvāso na*)—like horses that escape the master's grasp and run away
- न शुंभत (*na śuṃbhata*)—run away without showing their form

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(Pururavas:) When the human Pururavas, joining these Apsaras women who are free from mortality, wishes to come into contact with them by words and by his actions, then these same Apsaras women, in the form of water-fowl, hiding their own forms, run away like horses that play, grazing, and escape the master's grasp.

##### English translation
When a mortal mixing with these immortal nymphs has converse with
them with words and actions, they (becoming) ducks do not show their bodies
like playful horses champing (the bit).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Pururavas explains the matter stated in the previous Ṛk in another manner and with another comparison. Here मर्तः (*martaḥ*) means the human Pururavas; अमृतासु (*amṛtāsu*) means the Apsaras women of the divine class, free from death. The human Pururavas, wishing for contact with the deathless Apsaras women
### Printed p. 37
and trying to win them over with words and with actions (gestures), they did not let him see their bodies; that is, not attached to him and not falling into his hands, they took the form of ducks or swans and fled. How did they flee? As thoroughbred horses, of a restless nature, wanting to run off biting the bit, do not act according to the mind of the charioteer but run where they like, so these Apsaras women also ran away. So Pururavas laments, afflicted with grief.

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वि॒द्युन्न या पतं॑ती॒ दवि॑द्यो॒द्भरं॑ती मे॒ अप्या॒ काम्या॑नि ।  
> जनि॑ष्टो अ॒पो नर्यः॒ सुजा॑तः॒ प्रोर्वशी॑ तिरत दी॒र्घमायुः॑ ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> विद्युत् । न । या । पतंती । दविद्योत् । भरंती । मे । अप्या । काम्यानि ।  
> जनिष्टो इति । अपः । नर्यः । सुजातः । प्र । उर्वशी । तिरत । दीर्घं । आयुः॥१०॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अनयोर्वशीं स्तौति । योर्वशी विद्युन्न विद्युदिव पतंती गच्छंती
> दविद्योत् द्योतते । किं कुर्वती । अप्या । अप इत्यंतरिक्षनाम । तत्संबंधीनि
> व्याप्तानि ना काम्यान्यस्मदभिमतान्युदकानि ना मे मह्यं भरंती संपादयंती ।
> यदागतायास्तस्याः सकाशादपो व्याप्तः कर्मसु नर्यो नरेभ्यो हितः सुजातः
> सुजननः पुत्रो जनिष्टो अजनिष्ट उत्पद्यते तदानीं मनोर्वशी दीर्घमायुः
> प्र तिरत । प्रवर्धयति । प्रजामनु प्रजायसे तदु ते मर्त्यामृतं । तै. ब्रा. १-५-५-६
> इति हि मंत्रः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

With these two Ṛks he praises Urvaśī. Urvaśī, falling like lightning (विद्युन्न (*vidyunna*)), shone (दविद्योत् (*davidyot*)). Doing what? अप्या (*apyā*): अप (*apa*) is a name for the atmosphere; bringing (भरंती (*bharaṃtī*)) to me the pervading, desired waters connected with it. When, from her who has come, a son is born, pervading in rites (अपः (*apaḥ*)), beneficial to men (नर्यः (*naryaḥ*)), well-born (सुजातः (*sujātaḥ*)), then Urvaśī prolongs (प्र तिरत (*pra tirata*), प्रवर्धयति (*pravardhayati*)) a long life. He cites the mantra प्रजामनु प्रजायसे तदु ते मर्त्यामृतं । (*prajāmanu prajāyase tadu te martyāmṛtaṃ |*) (Tai. Brā. 1-5-5-6).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- या (*yā*)—the Urvaśī who
- अप्या (*apyā*)—belonging to the atmosphere
- काम्यानि (*kāmyāni*)—and desired waters
- मे (*me*)—to me
- भरंती (*bharaṃtī*)—brings; she; or else, या (*yā*)—the Urvaśī who, अप्या (*apyā*)—desired by me or needed by me, मे (*me*)—my, काम्यानि (*kāmyāni*)—
**NOTE**

### Printed p. 38

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

not applicable here; this page continues the previous Ṛk's Kannada material.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- विद्युन्न (*vidyunna*)—like lightning
- पतंती (*pataṃtī*)—descending from the heavenly world
- दविद्योत् (*davidyot*)—shines
- अपः (*apaḥ*)—engaged in work
- नरः (*naraḥ*)—beneficial to men
- सुजातः (*sujātaḥ*)—the son of excellent birth
- जनिष्टो (*janiṣṭo*)—when he is born, then
- उर्वशी (*urvaśī*)—Urvaśī
- आयुः (*āyuḥ*)—my life
- दीर्घं (*dīrghaṃ*)—long
- प्रतीरत (*pratīrata*)—she increases

(The preceding words, up to "…who fulfils my desires, that Urvaśī", stand on the previous page.)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(Words of Purūravas) The Urvaśī who brings me the desirable waters of the atmosphere, or who fulfils my desires, comes down like lightning and shines. A son, devoted to work, beneficial to men and of excellent birth, is born of her. Urvaśī lengthens my life.

##### English translation
(Urvashi) who shone like flashing lightning, bringing me the desirable dews (of heaven, has appeared); a son able in act and friendly to man has been born ; Urvashi has prolonged my lengthened existence.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk Purūravas addresses Urvaśī alone and praises her. Although the six Apsaras women ran away as soon as they saw him, Urvaśī alone swooped down like lightning from the atmosphere to where he was, stayed with him and shone. She fulfils his desires. In her a good son, devoted to work and a benefactor of people, is born. In this Ṛk Purūravas expresses the assurance that this Urvaśī will grant him long life.

For an example of a Ṛk using the word Urvaśī, Yāska gave an explanation of this Ṛk:
विद्युदिव या पतंत्यद्योतत हरंती मे आप्या काम्यान्युदकान्यंतरिक्षलोकस्य यदा नूनमयं जायेताद्ध्योऽध्यप इति । नर्यो मनुष्यो नृभ्यो हितो नरापत्यमिति वा । सुजातः सुजाततरोऽथोर्वशी प्रवर्धयते दीर्घमायुः ॥ (*vidyudiva yā pataṃtyadyotata haraṃtī me āpyā kāmyānyudakānyaṃtarikṣalokasya yadā nūnamayaṃ jāyetāddhyo 'dhyapa iti | naryo manuṣyo nṛbhyo hito narāpatyamiti vā | sujātaḥ sujātataro 'thorvaśī pravardhayate dīrghamāyuḥ ‖*) (Ni. 11-36)

### Printed p. 39
The explanation (nirvacana) admits two kinds of meaning. One: Purūravas means the lightning and thunder in the atmosphere (puru = much, rava = sound); but here Purūravas is said to be the sound (of thunder) and Urvaśī the lightning. This lightning (Urvaśī), moving again and again in the cloud of the atmosphere, that is, flashing repeatedly, supplies the waters wanted by all in the atmospheric world (हरंती (*haraṃtī*)); rain pours from the cloud-mass accompanied by thunder and lightning. As the fruit of this rain a work beneficial to people, namely growth of plants (the wealth of food and the like), arises. May such an Urvaśī (the cloud with thunder and lightning) increase the life of people by giving food and so on.

The other meaning is the historical view: this Apsaras woman Urvaśī came down from her own abode, the atmosphere (heaven), fulfilled the desires of me, Purūravas, stayed with me and shone. From me a son is born in her, devoted to good works, beneficial to people and of excellent birth. The intention is: may such an Urvaśī give me happiness and joy and long life, that I may live long.

अपः (*apaḥ*) means waters or works.
नर्यः (*naryaḥ*)—मनुष्यो नृभो हितो नरापत्यमिति वा ॥ (*manuṣyo nṛbho hito narāpatyamiti vā ‖*) नरः (*naraḥ*) means a man. नर्यः (*naryaḥ*) means one beneficial to men, or one born of a man.

#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ज॒ज्ञि॒ष इ॒त्था गो॒पीथ्या॑य॒ हि द॒धाथ॒ तत्पु॑रूरवो म॒ ओजः॑ ।  
> अशा॑सं त्वा वि॒दुषी॒ सस्मि॒न्नह॒न्न म॒ आशृ॑णोः॒ किम॒भुग्व॑दासि ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> जज्जिषे । इत्था । गोऽपीथ्याय । हि । दधाध । तत् । पुरूरवः । मे । ओजः ।  
> अशासं । त्वा । विदुषी । सस्मिन् । अहन् । न । मे । आ । अश्रणोः । किं । अभुक् ।  
> वदासि ॥ ११ ॥

### Printed p. 40

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इत्थेत्थं गोपीथ्याय । गौः पृथिवी । पीथं पालनं ॥ स्वार्थिकस्तद्धितः ॥
> भूरक्षणाय जज्ञिषे हि । जातोऽसि खलु पुत्ररूपेण । आत्मा वै पुत्रनामेति
> श्रुतेः । पुनर्देव्याह । हे पुरूरवः मे मनोदरे मय्योजोऽपत्योत्पादनसामर्थ्य० दधाथ । मयि निहितवानसि । तत्तथास्तु । अथापि स्थातव्यमिति चेत् तत्राह
> अहं विदुषी भावि कार्यं जानती सस्मिन्नहन् सर्वस्मन्नहनि त्वया कर्तव्यं त्वा
> त्वामशासं । शिक्षितवत्यस्मि । त्वं मे मम वचनं नाशृणोः । न शृणोषि । किं
> त्वमभुगभोक्तापालयिता प्रतिज्ञातार्थमपालयन्नदासि हये जाय इत्यादिकरूपं
> प्रलापं ॥ वदेर्लेट्वयडागमः ! दिवसे त्रिवारं यभस्व एडकबालकमस्माकं पुत्रत्वेन
> परिकल्पय अपत्योत्पादनपर्यंतं वसामि नग्नं त्वां यदाद्राक्षं तदा गच्छामित्येवंरूपो मिथःसमय उर्वशी हाप्सराः पुरूरवसमैळं चकमे तग्ं ह विंदमानोवाच त्रिः स्म माहो वैतसेन दंडेनेत्यादि वाजसनेयकमुदाहृतं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

इत्था (*itthā*) is "thus"; गोपीथ्याय (*gopīthyāya*): गौः (*gauḥ*) is the earth, पीथं (*pīthaṃ*) protection (a svārthika taddhita). "You have been born as a son to protect the earth," in accord with the śruti "the self is called son". Again the goddess speaks: "O Purūravas, you have placed in me, in my womb, the power of begetting offspring; so be it. If you ask whether I should still stay—" to that she says: "Knowing what is to come, I instructed you about what you must do on every day. You did not listen to my word. Why do you, a non-protector who has not kept your promise, speak words such as 'Ho wife, stay!'—mere prattle?" वदेर्लेट्वयडागमः (*vaderleṭvayaḍāgamaḥ*) (grammatical note). He then cites the Vājasaneyaka passage on the agreement: "Have intercourse three times a day; regard the ram-lamb as our son; I stay until offspring is born; when I see you naked, I shall go" — such was the mutual pact; "the Apsaras Urvaśī loved Purūravas Aiḷa; finding him, she said, 'Thrice you strike me with a cane of reed'", etc.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पुरूरवः (*purūravaḥ*)—O Purūravas
- इत्था (*itthā*)—in this way (as a son)
- गोपीथ्याय (*gopīthyāya*)—for the protection of the earth
- जज्जिषे हि (*jajjiṣe hi*)—you have been born
- मे (*me*)—in my womb
- तत् ओजः (*tat ojaḥ*)—that vigour in the form of a son
- दधाध (*dadhādha*)—you have placed
- विदुषी (*viduṣī*)—I, who know the future
- सस्मिन्नहन् (*sasminnahan*)—on all days, what you must do
- त्वा (*tvā*)—to you
- अशासं (*aśāsaṃ*)—have told
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- मे (*me*)—my word
- न अशृणोः (*na aśṛṇoḥ*)—did not hear
- अभुक् (*abhuk*)—having not kept my well-meant word
- किं (*kiṃ*)—why
- वदासि (*vadāsi*)—do you address me and speak?

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(Words of Urvaśī) O Purūravas, you have been born thus, in the form of a son, to protect the earth. You have placed in my womb vigour in the form of a son. Knowing the future, I told you what you must do on all days. Yet you did not listen to my word. Having not kept my well-meant advice, why do you address me and speak?

##### English translation
(Urvashi :) You have been born thus to protect the earth; you have deposited this vigour in me; knowing (the future) I have instructed you

### Printed p. 41
what to do) every day; you have not listened to me; why do you now address me, neglectful (of my instruction)?

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

विदुषी सस्मिन्नहन् त्वा अशासं (*viduṣī sasminnahan tvā aśāsaṃ*)—अहं भाविकार्यं जानती सर्वस्मिन्नहनि त्वया कर्तव्यं त्वां शिक्षितवत्यस्मि । (*ahaṃ bhāvikāryaṃ jānatī sarvasminnahani tvayā kartavyaṃ tvāṃ śikṣitavatyasmi |*) Since I know what will happen in future, I told you many times before how you should behave towards me.

मे न अशृणोः (*me na aśṛṇoḥ*)—मम वचनं न शृणोषि । (*mama vacanaṃ na śṛṇoṣi |*) You did not listen to my word. What was the word Urvaśī spoke to Purūravas here, and how did he not listen? — अपत्योत्पादनपर्यंतं वसामि । नग्नं त्वां यदाद्राक्षं तदा गच्छामात्येवंरूपो मिथःसमयः । (*apatyotpādanaparyaṃtaṃ vasāmi | nagnaṃ tvāṃ yadādrākṣaṃ tadā gacchāmātyevaṃrūpo mithaḥsamayaḥ |*) Earlier there was an agreement between Purūravas and Urvaśī, and according to it Urvaśī lived with Purūravas like his wife. Then Urvaśī had said at the time of the agreement: "O Purūravas, I shall live with you until a son is born to you in me; but if I get occasion to see you naked, I shall leave you at once and go." Referring to that, she here tells Purūravas that he did not listen to her word. On this matter the commentator cites the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa passage उर्वशी ह्यप्सराः पुरूरवसवमैळं चकमे तग्ं ह विंदमानोवाच त्रिः स्म माहो वैतसेन दंडेन (*urvaśī hyapsarāḥ purūravasavamaiḷaṃ cakame tagṃ ha viṃdamānovāca triḥ sma māho vaitasena daṃḍena*) (Śa. Brā. 11-5-1), etc.

#### Ṛk 12

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> क॒दा सू॒नुः पि॒तरं॑ जा॒त इ॑च्छाच्च॒क्रन्नाश्रु॑ वर्तयद्विजा॒नन् ।  
> को दंप॑ती॒ सम॑नसा॒ वि यू॑यो॒दध॒ यद॒ग्निः श्वशु॑रेषु॒  ॑
> दीद॑यत् ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कदा । सूनुः । पितरं । जातः । इच्छात् । चक्रन् । न । अश्रु । वर्तयत् । विजानन् ।  
> कः । दंपती इति दंपती । समनसा । वि । यूयोत् । अध । यत् । अग्निः ।  
> श्वशुरेषु । दीदयत् ॥ १२ ॥

### Printed p. 42

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इदं पुरूरवसो वाक्यं । कदा कस्मिन्काले सूनुस्तवोदरजातः सन् पितरं
> मामिच्छात् । इच्छेत् ॥ इषु इच्छायां । लेटि शपीषुगमियमां छ इति छादेशः ।
> लेटोऽडाटावित्यडागमः ॥ कदा वा विजानन्वितरं मामधिगच्छंश्चक्रन् क्रंदमानो
> नाश्रु वर्तयत् । नेति चार्थे । किंच कः किंविधः सन् सूनुः समनसा समनसौ
> समनस्कौ दंपती जायापती त्वां मां च वि यूयोत् । विश्लेषयेत् ॥ यु
> मिश्रणामिश्रणयोः । यौतेश्चांदसः शपः श्लुः । तुजादित्वादभ्यासस्य दीर्घः ॥
> आधाधुनां यद्यदाग्निजव हृदयस्थितस्तेजोरूपो गर्भः श्वशुरेषु दीदयत्
> दीप्यते । दीदयतिर्दिप्तिकर्मेति नैरुक्तो धातुः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This is Purūravas' speech. "When will the son, born of your womb, desire me, his father? Or when, knowing me as father, will he cry and shed tears?" इच्छात् (*icchāt*) is from इषु इच्छायां (*iṣu icchāyāṃ*) in the Leṭ; न (*na*) is in the sense of "and". Further: "What kind of son will separate the two of us, husband and wife, of one mind?" यूयोत् (*yūyot*) means "would separate". यदग्निः (*yadagniḥ*): when the fire, the fiery embryo placed in your heart, shines among the parents-in-law. दीदयति (*dīdayati*) is, in the Nairukta, a root meaning shining.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Urvaśī) कदा (*kadā*)—when
- सूनुः (*sūnuḥ*)—the son born in your womb
- पितरं (*pitaraṃ*)—me, his father
- इच्छात् (*icchāt*)—will love
- विजानन् (*vijānan*)—knowing that I am his father
- चक्रन् (*cakran*)—crying out
- न अश्रु वर्तयत् (*na aśru vartayat*)—will shed tears
- आध (*ādha*)—in this situation
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—the embryo in your heart, in the form of fire
- श्वशुरेषु (*śvaśureṣu*)—among your husband's parents
- दीदयत् (*dīdayat*)—shines, at that time
- कः (*kaḥ*)—what kind of son
- समनसा (*samanasā*)—of united minds
- दंपती (*daṃpatī*)—husband and wife, me and you
- वि यूयोत् (*vi yūyot*)—can separate?

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(Words of Purūravas) O Urvaśī, when will the son born in your womb love me, his father? When, knowing me as his father, will he cry out and shed tears? In this situation, when will the embryo in your heart, in the form of fire, shine among your husband's parents, that is, please the parents? What son can separate us, husband and wife of one mind?

##### English translation
(Pururavas:) When shall a son (born of you) claim me as a father, and, crying, shed a tear on recognizing (me)? What son shall sever husband and wife who are of one mind, now that the fire shines upon your husband's parents?

#### Ṛk 13

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्रति॑ ब्रवाणि व॒र्तय॑ते॒ अश्रु॑ च॒क्रन्न क्रं॑ददा॒ध्ये॑ शि॒वाय्कॆ ॑।  
> प्र तत्ते॑ हिनवा॒ यत्ते॑ अ॒स्मे परे॒ह्यस्तं॑ न॒हि मू॑र॒ मापः॑ ॥१३॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्रति । ब्रवाणि । वर्तयते । अश्रु । चक्रन् । न । क्रंदत् । आध्ये । शिवाय्कॆ ।  
> प्र । तत् । ते । हिनव । यत् । ते । अस्मे इति । परा । इहि । अस्तं । नहि । मूर ।  
> मा । आपः ॥ १३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इदमुर्वशीवाक्यं । हे पुरूरवः त्वां प्रति ब्रवाणि । प्रतिवच्चि । त्वदपत्यमश्रु
> बाष्पं वर्तयते । वर्तयिष्यति । आध्य आध्याते वस्तुनि शिवाय्कॆ शिवे कल्याणे
> समुपस्थिते सति चक्रन्द्रदन्नशूणि विमुंचन्न क्रंदत् । नकारश्चार्थे । रोत्सृति
> चेत्यर्थः । तत्त्वदपत्यं ते तुभ्यं हिनव प्रहिणोमि यदपत्यं ते तव संबंध्यस्मे
> अस्मासु निहितं । त्वं परेह्यस्तं । अस्तमिति गृहनाम । स्वगृहं प्रतिगच्छ । हे मूर
> मूढ मा मां न ह्यापः । न प्राप्नोषि ॥ हिनवेत्यत्र हिनोतेश्चंदसि लुज्लज्लिट
> इति भविष्यदर्थे लजि मिष आमादेशः । गुणः । अंत्यलोपश्चांदसः । बहुलवचनादडभावः । आपः । अप्ल व्याप्तौ । लिटि तिजां तिजो भवंतीति थलो णल् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This is Urvaśī's speech. "O Purūravas, I shall answer you. Your offspring will shed tears; when the auspicious, desired moment arrives, he will cry, shedding tears." न (*na*) is in the sense of "and". "I send to you that offspring of yours which is placed in me. Go back to your own house (अस्तं (*astaṃ*) is a name for house). O fool, you do not reach me." Grammar notes on हिनव (*hinava*) and आपः (*āpaḥ*) follow.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Purūravas) प्रति ब्रवाणि (*prati bravāṇi*)—I shall answer your question
- (your son) अश्रु (*aśru*)—tears
- वर्तयते (*vartayate*)—will shed
- आध्यॆ (*ādhyè*)—desired
- शिवाय्कॆ (*śivāykè*)—when that auspicious time arrives
- चक्रन् (*cakran*)—shedding tears
- न क्रंदत् (*na kraṃdat*)—he will weep
- यत् ते (*yat te*)—which child of yours
- अस्मे (*asme*)—is placed in me
- तत् (*tat*)—that
- ते (*te*)—to you
- प्र हिनव (*pra hinava*)—I shall send
- अस्तं (*astaṃ*)—to your house
- परेहि (*parehi*)—go back
- मूर (*mūra*)—O fool
- मा (*mā*)—me
- नहि आप्य (*nahi āpya*)—you cannot approach

### Printed p. 44
#### Ṛk 13 (continued)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(Urvaśī's words) O Pururavas, I will answer your question. As soon as the proper and auspicious time draws near, your son will come to you shedding tears and addressing you as father. The child lying in my womb I will send to you as soon as he is born. Return to your house. O fool, you cannot approach me.

##### English translation
(Urvashi :) Let me reply. (Your son) will shed tears, crying out and calling aloud when the expected auspicious time arrives; I will send you that (child) which is yours in me, depart to your house, you cannot, simpleton, detain me.

#### Ṛk 14

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सु॒दे॒वो अ॒द्य प्र॒पते॒दना॑वृत्परा॒वतं॑ पर॒मां गंत॒वा उ॑ ।  
> अधा॒ शयी॑त॒ निर्ऋ॑तेरु॒पस्थेdधै॑नं॒ वृका॑ रभ॒सासो॑ अ॒द्युः॥१४॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सुदेवः । अद्य । प्रपतेत् । अनावृत् । परावतं । परमां । गंतवै ।  
> ऊं इति ।  
> अध । शयीत । निःऋतेः । उपस्थे । अध । एनं । वृकाः । रभसासः । अद्युः ॥ १४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अथ परिदूनः पुरूरवा उवाच । सुदेवस्त्वया सह सुक्रीडः पतिरद्य
> प्रपतेत् । अत्रैव प्रपततु । अथवानावृदनावृत्तः सन् परमां परावतं दूरादपि
> दूरदेशं गंतवै गंतुं महाप्रस्थानगमनं कुर्यात् । अधाथवा यत्रकुत्रापि
> गंतुं समर्थो निर्ऋतेः पृथिव्या उपस्थे शयीत । शयनं कुर्यात् । यद्वा ।
> निर्ऋतिः पापदेवता । तस्या उपस्थ उत्संगे संनिधौ म्रियतामित्यर्थः । अधाथवैनं
> वृका आरण्याः श्वानो रभसासो वेगवंतोऽद्युः । भक्षयंतु । अत्र वाजसनेयकं ।
> सुदेवोऽद्योद्या बद्धीत प्र ना पतेत्तदेनं वृका वा श्वानो वाद्युरिति हैव
> तदुवाच । शत. ११-५-१-८ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Then Pururavas, distressed, spoke. Let your husband, who sported well with you, fall down right here today; or, never turning back, let him make the great departure to the farthest distance; or, able to go anywhere, let him lie in the lap of the earth (निर्ऋति (*nirṛti*) as the earth). Alternatively, निर्ऋतिः (*nirṛtiḥ*) is the goddess of sin: let him die in her lap, that is, in her presence. Or let wolves, wild dogs, swift and eager, devour him. Sāyaṇa cites the Vājasaneyaka: सुदेवोऽद्योद्या बद्धीत प्र ना पतेत्तदेनं वृका वा श्वानो वाद्युरिति हैव तदुवाच । (*sudevo 'dyodyā baddhīta pra nā patettadenaṃ vṛkā vā śvāno vādyuriti haiva taduvāca |*) Śa. Brā. 11-5-1-8.

### Printed p. 45

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सुदेवः (*sudevaḥ*)—your husband who sported with you
- अद्य (*adya*)—right now
- प्रपतेत् (*prapatet*)—let him fall down on the ground (or)
- अनावृत् (*anāvṛt*)—without returning
- परमां परावतं (*paramāṃ parāvataṃ*)—to the farthest distant region
- गंत वै (*gaṃta vai*)—let him go out of sight (or let him depart from worldly life)
- अध (*adha*)—or
- निर्ऋतेः (*nirṛteḥ*)—of the goddess of sin named Nirṛti
- उपस्थे (*upasthe*)—on the lap
- शयीत (*śayīta*)—let him lie down
- अथ (*atha*)—or
- एनं (*enaṃ*)—him
- रभसासः (*rabhasāsaḥ*)—swift-moving
- वृकाः (*vṛkāḥ*)—wolves
- अद्युः (*adyuḥ*)—let them devour

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(Pururavas's words) O Urvaśī, let your husband who sported with you fall on the ground right now; or let him go, never to return, to the farthest distance (leaving worldly life and vanishing); or let him lie on the lap of the goddess of sin named Nirṛti; or let swift-moving wolves devour him.

##### English translation
(Pururavas :) (Your husband) who sports with you may now depart) never to return (depart) to proceed to a distant region. Either let him sleep upon the lap of Nirriti, or let the swift-moving wolves devour him.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Unable to bear the grief of separation from Urvaśī, Pururavas suggests that it is better to give up life, subjected to manifold afflictions, than to live consumed by sorrow, and he expresses his despair in various laments. This the Ṛk says movingly. Yāska cites this Ṛk as an example of a Ṛk expressing despair or sorrow (Ni. 7-3).
The main sense of the Ṛk: O Urvaśī, how can I, who sported with you till now, live tormented by the pain of separation from you? Should this body of mine not fall lifeless on the ground? Or should I not go to the farthest region, as written earlier? Or lie on a deathbed and be subject to death? Or should wolves not eat me? To Pururavas lamenting thus, Urvaśī in the next Ṛk will reply with words of the same kind and console him.
