#### Ṛk 15

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> पुरू॑रवो॒ मा मृ॑था॒ मा प्र प॑स्तो॒ मा त्वा॒ वृका॑सो॒ अति॑वास उ क्षन् ।  
> न वै स्रैणा॑नि स॒ख्यानि॑ संति सालावृ॒काणां॒ हृद॑यान्ये॒ता ॥१५॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> पुरूरवः । मा । मृथाः । मा । प्र । पप्तः । मा । त्वा । वृकासः । अशिवासः । ऊं इति ।  
> क्षन् ।  
> न । वै । सृणानि । सख्यानि । सॆंति । सालावृकाणां । हृदयानि । एता ॥ १५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> तमितरा प्रत्युवाच । हे पुरूरवः त्वं मा मृथाः । मृतिं मा प्राप्पुहि ॥
> म्रियतेर्लुजि थासि ह्रस्वादंगादिति सिचो लोपः ॥ तथा मा प्र पप्तः । अत्रैव
> पतनं मा कार्षिः ॥ पतेर्लुजि ल्गदित्वात्पुषादीत्यादिना चेरज् । पतः पुमिति
> पुम्॥ तथा त्वा त्वामशिवासोऽशुभा वृकासो वृका मा उ क्षन्। उ इत्येवकारार्थे!
> अक्षन् । माभ्यवहारयंतु ! किमित्येवमस्मदुपर्याग्रहं करोषि । मा कार्षिरित्यर्थः ॥ अदेर्लुजि लुज्‌सनोर्घस्ल । पा. २-४-३७ । इति घस्ला देशः । मंत्रे
> घसेति चेर्लुक् । गमहनेत्यादिनोपधालोपः । शासिवसीत्यादिना षत्वं । खरि चेति
> चर्त्वं । बाहुलकावडभावः ॥ अथ स्वस्नेहस्यासारतामाह । स्रैनानि स्त्रीणां कृतानि
> सख्यानि न वै संति । न संति खलु । अभावे कारणमाह । एतानि सख्यानि सालावृकाणां
> हृदयानि यथा वत्सादीनां विश्वासापन्नानां घातुकानि तद्वत् । अत्र वाजसनेयकं।
> मैतदादृथा न वै स्रैनग्ं सख्यमस्ति पुनर्गृहानिहीति हैवैनं तदुवाच । शत. ११-५-
> १९ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The other (Urvaśī) replied to him. O Pururavas, do not die; do not meet death. Likewise do not fall forth; do not fall right here. Likewise let inauspicious wolves not devour you; the particle उ (*u*) is used in the sense of "indeed". Why do you press upon us so? Do not do so. Then she speaks of the unreliability of such affection: friendships made with women do not exist, they are not lasting. The reason: these friendships are like the hearts of jackals (सालावृकाणां (*sālāvṛkāṇāṃ*)), which kill, as one kills calves and the like that have come to trust. On this the Vājasaneyaka: मैतदादृथा न वै स्रैनग्ं सख्यमस्ति पुनर्गृहानिहीति हैवैनं तदुवाच । (*maitadādṛthā na vai srainagṃ sakhyamasti punargṛhānihīti haivainaṃ taduvāca |*) Śa. Brā. 11-5-19 **NOTE**.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पुरूरवः (*purūravaḥ*)—O Pururavas
- मा मृधाः (*mā mṛdhāḥ*)—do not die
- मा प्र पप्तः (*mā pra paptaḥ*)—do not fall down either
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- अशिवासः (*aśivāsaḥ*)—inauspicious
- वृकासः (*vṛkāsaḥ*)—wolves
- मा उक्षन् (*mā ukṣan*)—let them not devour
- सृणानि (*sṛṇāni*)—made with women
- सख्यानि (*sakhyāni*)—friendships
- न वै संति (*na vai saṃti*)—are not lasting
- एता (*etā*)—these
- सालावृकाणां (*sālāvṛkāṇāṃ*)—of the animals called sālāvṛka
- हृदयानि (*hṛdayāni*)—cruel like their hearts (not worthy of trust)

### Printed p. 47

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(Urvaśī's words) O Pururavas, do not die and do not fall down. Let inauspicious wolves not devour you. Friendship with women is not lasting. These are cruel and unworthy of trust, like the hearts of the animals called sālāvṛka (jackals).

##### English translation
(Uruashi:) Die not, Pururavas, fall not, let not the hideous wolves devour you. Female friendships do not exist, their hearts are the hearts of jackals.

#### Ṛk 16

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यद्विरू॒पाच॑रं॒ मर्त्ये॒ष्वव॑सं॒ रात्रिः॑ श॒रद॒श्चत॑स्रः ।  
> घृ॒तस्य॑ स्तो॒कं स॒कृदह्न॑ आश्नां॒ तादे॒वेदं ता॑तृपा॒णा च॑रामि॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत् । विरूपा । अचरं । मर्त्येषु । अवसं । रात्रीः । शरदः । चतस्रः ।  
> घृतस्य । स्तोकं । सकृत् । अह्नः । आश्नां । तात् । एव । इदं । ततृपाणा । चरामि ॥१६॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यद्यदा विरूपा मनुष्यसंपर्काद्विगतसहजभूतदेवरूपापत्यानुकूल्येन
> नानारूपा वा मर्त्येषु मनुष्येष्टचरं तदानीं रात्रिः पूरयित्रिश्चतस्रः
> शरदोऽवसं । न्यवसं ॥ अत्यंतसंयोगे द्वितीया ॥ तदानीं धृतस्य स्तोकं
> सकृदह्न आश्नां । तादेव तेनैव स्तोकेनाहमिदं संप्रति तातृपाणा तृप्ता सती
> चरामि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

When I, विरूपा (*virūpā*), having left my natural divine form through contact with men, or in various forms suited to my offspring, moved among mortals, men, then I lived four nights-and-autumns (the nights fulfilling), अवसं (*avasaṃ*) = न्यवसं (*nyavasaṃ*); the accusative expresses continuous duration. Then I ate once a day a small drop of ghee; with just that I now move about satisfied.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत् (*yat*)—when
- विरूपा (*virūpā*)—I, leaving my natural divine form
- मर्त्येषु (*martyeṣu*)—among humans
- आ चरं (*ā caraṃ*)—moved about, then
- रात्रिः (*rātriḥ*)—delight-giving
- चतस्रः (*catasraḥ*)—four

### Printed p. 48
- शरदः (*śaradaḥ*)—years
- अवसं (*avasaṃ*)—I spent with you
- सकृत् अह्नः (*sakṛt ahnaḥ*)—only once in a day
- घृतस्य स्तोकं (*ghṛtasya stokaṃ*)—a small portion of ghee
- आश्नां (*āśnāṃ*)—I ate
- तादेव (*tādeva*)—by that alone
- तातृपाणा (*tātṛpāṇā*)—satisfied
- इदं (*idaṃ*)—now
- चरामि (*carāmi*)—I move

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(Urvaśī's words) Leaving my natural divine form, I spent among humans four years that gave me joy. Once a day I ate only a small portion of ghee. Satisfied with that alone, I now depart.

##### English translation
When changed in form I wandered amongst mortals, I dwelt (with them) four delightful years. I ate once a day a small quantity of butter; satisfied with that I now depart.

#### Ṛk 17

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अं॒त॒रि॒क्ष॒प्रां रज॑सो वि॒मानी॒मुप॑ शिक्षाम्यु॒र्वशीं॒ वसि॑ष्ठः ।  
> उप॑ त्वा रा॒तिः सु॑कृ॒तस्य॒ तिष्ठा॒न्नि व॑र्तस्व॒ हृद॑यं तप्यते मे ॥  
> ॥ पवनाति ॥  
> अंतरिक्षप्रां । रजसः । विमानीं । उप । शिक्षामि । उर्वशीं । वसिष्ठः ।  
> उप॑ । त्वा । रा॒तिः । सु॑कृ॒तस्य॒ । तिष्ठा॒त् । नि । व॑र्तस्व॒ । हृद॑यं । तप्यते । मे ॥ १७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अंतरिक्षप्रां स्वतेजसांतरिक्षस्य पूरयित्रीं तथा रजसो रंजकस्योदकस्य
> विमानीं निर्मात्रिमुर्वशीं वसिष्ठः समानानां मध्येऽतिशयेन वासयिताहमुप
> शिक्षामि । वशं नयामि । सुकृतस्य शोभनकर्मणो रातिर्दाता पुरूरवास्त्वा त्वामुप
> शिष्ठात् । उपतिष्ठतु । मे हृदयं तप्यते । अतो नि वर्तस्व । एवं राजोवाच ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

I, Vasiṣṭha, the one who most causes my companions to dwell well among them, उप शिक्षामि (*upa śikṣāmi*): bring under my control Urvaśī, who fills the atmosphere with her own radiance and is the maker (विमानीं (*vimānīṃ*)) of the colouring रजसः (*rajasaḥ*), water. Let Pururavas, the giver (रातिः (*rātiḥ*)) of good rites, सुकृतस्य (*sukṛtasya*), stand near you. My heart burns; therefore turn back. So the king spoke.

### Printed p. 49

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अंतरिक्षप्रां (*aṃtarikṣaprāṃ*)—she who fills the whole atmosphere with her radiance
- रजसः (*rajasaḥ*)—for water
- विमानीं (*vimānīṃ*)—maker
- उर्वशीं (*urvaśīṃ*)—Urvaśī
- वसिष्ठः (*vasiṣṭhaḥ*)—I, the best of men (or: I, who have an excellent life together with companions)
- उप शिक्षामि (*upa śikṣāmi*)—I take under my control
- सुकृतस्य (*sukṛtasya*)—of excellent rite
- रातिः (*rātiḥ*)—the giver (performer of the rite)
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- उपतिष्ठात् (*upatiṣṭhāt*)—let him approach and sit with you
- मे (*me*)—my
- हृदयं (*hṛdayaṃ*)—heart
- तप्यते (*tapyate*)—is burning (therefore, O Urvaśī)
- नि वर्तस्व (*ni vartasva*)—turn back

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(Pururavas's words) I, the best of men, take under my control Urvaśī, who fills the whole atmosphere with her radiance and is the maker of water (I will make her subject to me). Let Pururavas, the performer of excellent rites, approach you and enjoy happiness with you. My heart burns from separation from you. Turn back.

##### English translation
(Pururavas:) I, Vasistha, bring under subjection Urvashi who fills the firmament (with lustre) and measures out the rain. May (Pururavas), the bestower of the auspicious rite, abide near you; come back—my heart is burning.

#### Ṛk 18

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इति॑ त्वा दे॒वा इ॒म आ॑हुरैळ॒ यथे॑मे॒तद्भव॑सि मृ॒त्युबं॑धुः ।  
> प्र॒जा ते॑ दे॒वान्ह॒ विष्णा॑ यजाति स्व॒र्ग उ॒ त्वमपि॑ मादयासे ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इति । त्वा । देवाः । इमे । आहुः । ऐळ । यथा । ईं । एतत् । भवसि । मृत्युबंधुः।  
> प्रजा । ते । देवान् । हविषा । यजाति । स्वःगे । ऊं इति । त्वं । अपि । मादयासे॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे ऐळ पुरूरवः त्वा त्वामिमे देवा इत्याहुः । मृत्युबंधुर्मृत्योर्बंधको मृत्योर्बंधुभूतो ना मृत्युवशमप्राप्नु वंस्त्वं यथें यथैत-
> 4

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aiḷa Pururavas, these gods say this to you: you are मृत्युबंधुः (*mṛtyubaṃdhuḥ*), a binder of death, a kinsman of death, a man who has not come under death's power, as ….

### Printed p. 50

> द्भवसि भविष्यसि प्रजा प्रकर्षेण जायमानस्त्वं ते तव संबंधिनो यष्टव्यान्देवान्नविषा यजासि । यजसि । स्वर्ग उ स्वर्ग एव त्वमपि मादयासे । मादयसेऽस्माभिः
> सह। एवमाहुरित्यर्थः । यस्मादेवं करोषि तस्मादभिलाषं हित्वा सुखी भवेति सेयं
> पुरूरवसं प्रत्युवाच ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

(Continuing the verse's explanation.) Aila, the gods say this to you: since you are bound to death, you will be, being born as progeny, one who with oblation worships your related gods who are to be worshipped; and you too will rejoice in heaven with us. This is the meaning: they say so. Because you act in this way, give up your longing and be happy. So she (Urvaśī) replied to Purūravas.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ऐळ (*aiḷa*)—O Purūravas, son of Iḷā
- त्वा (*tvā*)—to you
- इमे देवाः (*ime devāḥ*)—these gods
- इति (*iti*)—in this manner
- आहुः (*āhuḥ*)—have said
- यथें (*yatheṃ*)—because
- मृत्युबंधुः (*mṛtyubaṃdhuḥ*)—you, having become subject to death
- भवसि (*bhavasi*)—you are
- एतत् (*etat*)—this is certain
- ते (*te*)—your
- प्रजा (*prajā*)—son (you who are born in the form of a son)
- देवान् (*devān*)—the gods
- हविषा (*haviṣā*)—with oblation
- यजाति (*yajāti*)—worships (let him worship)
- त्वं अपि (*tvaṃ api*)—you for your part
- स्वर्गे (*svarge*)—in heaven
- मादयासे (*mādayāse*)—you will join us and enjoy delight

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(Urvaśī's words) "O Purūravas, (some day) you will become subject to death. This is certain. Your son will worship the gods with oblation. You will join us in heaven and enjoy delight." So the gods have told you.

##### English translation
(Urvashi :) These gods said to you, Aila, since you are indeed subject to death, let your progeny propitiate your gods with oblations, you shall rejoice (with me) in heaven.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Some Western scholars say that nowhere in the Ṛgveda is heaven clearly mentioned, and that it does not explain what connection human beings have with heaven. But in this Ṛk it is clearly said स्वर्ग उ त्वमसि मादयासे (*svarga u tvamasi mādayāse*), "you will enjoy happiness in heaven"; and because भवसि मृत्युबंधुः (*bhavasi mṛtyubaṃdhuḥ*) is said, the matter is clearly stated that a man devoted to good works, having become subject to death and given up this earthly body, then (taking another body) enjoys happiness in heaven.

## Sūkta 96 (Maṇḍala 10)
The ninety-sixth sūkta

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अथ प्र ते मह इति त्रयोदशर्चं षष्ठं सूक्तं । बरुर्नामांगिरस ऋषिः
> इंद्रस्य पुत्रः सर्वहरिर्वा नाम । द्वादशीत्रयोदश्यौत्रिष्टुभौ शिष्टा एकादश जगत्यः ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Now the sixth sūkta, "Pra te mahe", of thirteen Ṛks. The seer is Baru of the Āṅgirasa line, or else Sarvahari, a son of Indra. The twelfth and thirteenth Ṛks are in the triṣṭubh metre; the remaining eleven are jagatī.

### Printed p. 51

> इंद्रस्याश्व हरी । तयोरत्र स्तूयमानत्वात्तद्देवताकमिदं । तथा चानुक्रांतं ।
> प्र ते सप्तोना बरुः सर्वहरिर्वैंद्रो हरिस्तुतिर्द्वित्रिष्टुबंतं ॥ अतिरात्रे तृतीये
> पर्याये ब्राह्मणाच्छंसिन एतत्सूक्तं । सूत्रितं च । प्र ते मह ऊती शचीवस्तव
> वीर्येणेति याज्या । आ. ६-४ । इति ॥ षोडशिशस्त्रेऽप्याद्यस्तृचः शंसनीयः । सूत्रितं
> च । प्र ते महे विदथे शंसिषं हरी इति तिस्रो जगत्यः । आ. ६-२ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The deity is the two horses (Hari) of Indra; since they are praised here, the sūkta has them as its deity. So the Anukramaṇī says: प्र ते सप्तोना बरुः सर्वहरिर्वैंद्रो हरिस्तुतिर्द्वित्रिष्टुबंतं (*pra te saptonā baruḥ sarvaharirvaiṃdro haristutirdvitriṣṭubaṃtaṃ*). In the Atirātra, in the third paryāya, the Brāhmaṇācchaṃsin recites this sūkta. It is also given in the sūtra: प्र ते मह ऊती शचीवस्तव वीर्येणेति याज्या (*pra te maha ūtī śacīvastava vīryeṇeti yājyā*) (Ā. 6-4). In the Ṣoḍaśin śastra too the first three Ṛks are to be recited; the sūtra says: प्र ते महे विदथे शंसिषं हरी इति तिस्रो जगत्यः (*pra te mahe vidathe śaṃsiṣaṃ harī iti tisro jagatyaḥ*) (Ā. 6-2).

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta "Pra te mahe" is the sixth sūkta in the eighth anuvāka. It has thirteen Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Baru of the Aṅgirasa gotra, or else Sarvahari, a son of Indra. The twelfth and thirteenth Ṛks of this sūkta are in the triṣṭubh metre; the remaining eleven Ṛks are in the jagatī metre. Since the two horses of Indra called Hari are praised in this sūkta, those horses of Indra are its deity. The Anukramaṇī says what has been quoted above. It is explained by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra (Ā. 6-4), which says that in the third paryāya of the sacrifice called Atirātra the priest called Brāhmaṇācchaṃsin should recite this sūkta; and (Ā. 6-2) that at the time of reciting the Ṣoḍaśin śastra mantras the first three Ṛks of this sūkta should be recited.

Sūkta 96
Maṇḍala 10; Anuvāka 8; Sūkta 96.
Aṣṭaka 8; Adhyāya 5; Vargas 5, 6, 7.
Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 13.
Seer: Baru Sarvahari, son of Indra.
Deity: praise of the two Haris.
Metre: 1–11 jagatī; 12, 13 triṣṭubh.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र ते॑ म॒हे वि॒दथे॑ शंसिषं॒ हरी॒ प्र ते॑ वन्वे व॒नुषो॑ ॒ ॑
> हर्यतं मदं ।  
> घृ॒तं न यो हरि॑भि॒श्चारु॒ सेच॑त॒ आ त्वा॑ विशंतु॒ हरि॑वर्पसं॒  ॑
> गिरः ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 52

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । शे । महे । विदथे । शंसिषं । हरी इति । प्र । ते । वन्वे । वनुषः । हर्यतं ।  
> मदु ।  
> धृतं । न । यः । हरिभिः । चारु । सेचते । आ । त्वा । विशंतु । हरिवर्पसं । गिरः ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र ते तव हरी अश्वा महे महति विदथे यज्ञे प्र शंसिषं । अशंसिषं।
> अस्ताविषं । तथा वनुषः ॥ वनु हिंसायां ॥ हिंसकस्य ते तव हर्यतं ॥ हर्य
> गतिकांतोः । तस्यौणादिकोऽतच् । चित्स्वरेणांतोदात्तः ॥ कमनीयं मदं प्र
> वन्ने । प्रयाचेऽस्मदभिमतं ॥ वनु याचने ॥ य. इंद्रो हरिभिर्हरितवर्णैरश्वैर्मद्यागं गश्वा चारु चरणीयं धृतं न धृतमिव सुपूतमुदकं सेचते वर्षति
> तं तादृशं हरिवर्पसं । वर्प इति रूपनाम । हरितरूपं त्वा त्वामा विशंतु
> गिरोऽस्मदीयाः स्तुतिवाचस्तव मदाय ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, I have praised your two bay horses at the great sacrifice. Further, I ask for your agreeable exhilaration, for you are the destroyer (of foes); the word हर्यतं (*haryataṃ*) means "desirable". I ask, I solicit, what we desire. Indra is he who, with his horses of yellow colour, pours down चारु (*cāru*), graceful, धृतं न (*dhṛtaṃ na*) "like clarified butter", that is, well-purified water. वर्प (*varpa*) is a name for form. Let our praise-words enter you, whose form is yellow, for your exhilaration.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ते (*te*)—your
- हरी (*harī*)—horses
- महे (*mahe*)—great
- विदथे (*vidathe*)—at the sacrifice
- प्र शंसिषं (*pra śaṃsiṣaṃ*)—I praise
- वनुषं (*vanuṣaṃ*)—destroyer of foes
- ते (*te*)—from you
- हरतं (*harataṃ*)—attractive
- मदं (*madaṃ*)—delight
- प्रवन्वे (*pravanve*)—I ask
- यः (*yaḥ*)—you who
- हरिभिः (*haribhiḥ*)—with your horses
- चारु (*cāru*)—beneficial
- धृतं न (*dhṛtaṃ na*)—water that is like clarified butter
- सेचते (*secate*)—do you pour down; such a one
- हरिवर्पसं (*harivarpasaṃ*)—having a golden-coloured form
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- गिरः (*giraḥ*)—our praises
- आ विशंतु (*ā viśaṃtu*)—let them enter

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, I praise your horses at the great sacrifice. I ask from you, the destroyer of foes, attractive delight. May our praises enter you, golden-coloured, who together with your horses pour down beneficial water like clarified butter.

##### English translation
I glorified your bay horses, Indra, at the great sacrifice, I solicit the agreeable exhilaration of you, the destroyer of foes. May my praises attain to you, whose form is yellow, who, with your horses, shower (water) like beautiful butter.

### Printed p. 53

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Though here and there a few Ṛks praise Indra's horses, in this sūkta Indra's horses alone are praised throughout. The style is of a high standard. Because the word Hari is used in every pāda of every Ṛk, the recitation of the mantras sounds rhymed and pleasant to hear; this is the distinctive feature of this sūkta.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> हरिं॒ हि योनि॑म॒भि ये स॒मस्व॑रन्हि॒न्वंतो॒ हरी॑ दि॒व्यं यथा॒  ॑
> सदः॑ ।  
> आ यं पृ॒णंति॒ हरि॑भि॒र्न धे॒नव॒ इंद्रा॑य शू॒षं हरि॑वंतमर्चत ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> हरिं । हि । योनिं । अभि । ये । संऽअस्वरन् । हिन्वंतः । हरी इति । दिव्यं । यथा ।  
> सदः ।  
> आ । यं । पृणंति । हरिभिः । न । धेनवः । इंद्राय । शूषं । हरिवंतं । अर्चत ॥२॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ये पूर्वे स्तोतार ऋषयो योनिमिंद्रस्य स्थानभूतं हरिमश्वमभि
> समस्वरन् ॥ स्वृ शब्दोपतापयोः ॥ अभिसंस्तुवंति । किं कुर्वंतः । दिव्यं देवसंबंधि सदो यागगृहं यथा प्रेरयंतींद्रं तथा हरी आश्वा हिन्वंतः प्रेरयंतः।
> सोमेऽभिषुते सति स्वयमेवेंद्रो रथेऽश्वा प्रेरयति अतः स्तोत्यणां
> हरिप्रेरणत्वं । यद्वा । दिव्यं सद इंद्रः प्राप्नुयाद्यथा तथा हरी स्तुतिभिर्हिन्वंतः
> प्रेरयंतः । यमिंद्रं धेनवो नवप्रसूतिका गावो यथा पृणंति क्षीरादिभिः ।
> अत्र पुरस्तादुपचारोऽपि नकार उपमार्थीयः । हरिभिर्हरितवर्णैः सोमैरा
> पृणंति पूरयंति च । तथा यूयमसींद्रायेंद्रस्य हरिवंतं शूषं बलमर्चित ।
> पूजयत हे स्तोतारः स्तुतिभिः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The ancient praising seers who समस्वरन् (*samasvaran*) (the root स्वृ (*svṛ*) in the sense of sound and heat) praised together the horse Hari, the seat of Indra: doing what? Urging the two horses, as one urges Indra to the divine sacrificial house, the house belonging to the gods. When Soma has been pressed, Indra himself drives the horses on his chariot; hence the praisers are said to urge the horses. Or else: the praisers urge the two horses with praises so that Indra may reach the divine house. The Indra whom, as newly calved cows fill with milk and the like, they fill and satisfy with Soma of yellow colour (here the particle न (*na*) of the preceding clause also has the sense of comparison). So, O praisers, with praises worship the horse-possessing might of that Indra.

### Printed p. 54

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ये (*ye*)—the praising seers
- दिव्यं सदः (*divyaṃ sadaḥ*)—to the venerable sacrificial house
- यथा (*yathā*)—in the manner of bringing Indra
- हरी (*harī*)—the horses
- हिन्वंतः (*hinvaṃtaḥ*)—urging
- योनिं (*yoniṃ*)—the seat of Indra
- हरिं (*hariṃ*)—the horse
- अभि समस्वरन् (*abhi samasvaran*)—have praised
- धेनवः न (*dhenavaḥ na*)—as cows satisfy with milk and the like
- यं (*yaṃ*)—which Indra
- हरिभिः (*haribhiḥ*)—with Soma of green colour
- आ पृणंति (*ā pṛṇaṃti*)—they fill and satisfy
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—of that same Indra
- हरिवंतं (*harivaṃtaṃ*)—horse-possessing
- शूषं (*śūṣaṃ*)—the might
- अर्चत (*arcata*)—worship

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The praising seers, urging the horses as if to bring Indra to the venerable sacrificial house, have praised the horse that carries Indra. As cows satisfy with milk and the like, so worship (praise) the horse-possessing might of that Indra whom they fill and satisfy with green-coloured Soma juices.

##### English translation
You, who have praised the horses, the seat of Indra, urging the horses so that (Indra may reach) the divine assembly, do you worship the horse-possessing might of Indra, whom men delight with the golden-tinted Soma juices as milch cows (with their milk.)

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सो अ॑स्य॒ वज्रो॒ हरि॑तो॒ य आ॑य॒सो हरि॒र्निका॑मो॒ हरि॒रा  ॑
> गभस्तोः ।  
> द्यु॒म्नो सु॑शि॒प्रो हरि॑मन्युसायक॒ इंद्रे॒ नि रू॒पा हरि॑ता  
> मिमिक्षिरे ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । अस्य । वज्रः । हरितः । यः । आयसः । हरिः । निकामः । हरिः । आ । गभस्त्योः ।  
> द्युम्ना । सुशिप्रः । हरिमन्युसायकः । इंद्रे । नि । रूपा । हरिता । मिमिक्षिरे ॥ ३ ॥

### Printed p. 55

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अस्येंद्रस्य स वज्रो हरितो हरितवर्णो य आयसोऽयःसारभूतोऽस्ति
> निर्मितः । स च हरिर्हरितवर्णो वज्रो निकामो नितरां कमनीयः । स आ हंता
> शत्रूणां ! तादृशो वज्रो गभस्त्योर्हस्तयोर्वर्तत इति शेषः । अयमिंद्रो
> द्युम्ना । द्युम्नं द्योतमानं धनं । तद्वान् सुशिप्रः शोभनहनुर्हरिमन्युसायकः ।
> यस्य मन्युः सायकः शत्रुहंताभिगंता ना भवति । यद्वा । शत्रुहंता कोपः
> सायकश्च यस्य स तादृशो भवति । किं बहुना ! इंद्रे।रूपा रूपाणि हरिता हरितानि
> नि मिमिक्षिरे । निषिक्तानि बभूवुः ॥ मिहेः सनंतात्मर्मणि लिटि रूपं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

That thunderbolt of this Indra is yellow, and is made of iron, being of the essence of metal; that yellow thunderbolt is निकामः (*nikāmaḥ*), exceedingly desirable. It is a striker (आ हंता (*ā haṃtā*)) of enemies. Such a thunderbolt is in his two hands (गभस्त्योः (*gabhastyoḥ*)); "is" is to be supplied. This Indra is द्युम्ना (*dyumnā*): द्युम्न (*dyumna*) is shining wealth, and he has it. He is सुशिप्रः (*suśipraḥ*), fair-jawed, हरिमन्युसायकः (*harimanyusāyakaḥ*): one whose anger and arrow are slayers of foes and go against them. Or else: one who has anger, the slayer of foes, and the arrow. What more need be said? The forms in Indra are yellow, नि मिमिक्षिरे (*ni mimikṣire*), they were infused; this is the form of the root *mih* in the desiderative-based perfect, middle.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अस्य (*asya*)—of Indra
- यः वज्रः (*yaḥ vajraḥ*)—which thunderbolt
- हरितः (*haritaḥ*)—being of green colour
- निकामः (*nikāmaḥ*)—exceedingly attractive
- आयसः (*āyasaḥ*)—made of steel
- सः (*saḥ*)—that thunderbolt
- अस्य (*asya*)—of Indra
- गभस्त्योः (*gabhastyoḥ*)—being in the hands
- आ (हंता) (*ā (haṃtā)*)—is a destroyer of foes
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- द्युम्ना (*dyumnā*)—wealthy
- सुशिप्रः (*suśipraḥ*)—having excellent cheeks
- हरिमन्युसायकः (*harimanyusāyakaḥ*)—having anger and arrows that destroy foes
- हरिता रूपा (*haritā rūpā*)—golden-coloured forms of many kinds
- इंद्रे (*iṃdre*)—in Indra
- नि मिमिक्षिरे (*ni mimikṣire*)—have joined together

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

That thunderbolt of Indra which is green in colour, exceedingly attractive and made of steel, being in Indra's hands is a destroyer of foes. Indra is wealthy, has excellent cheeks, and has anger and arrows that destroy foes. Golden-coloured forms of many kinds have joined together in Indra.

##### English translation
That is his thunderbolt, yellow, made of iron, the destroyer (of foes) desirable, it is in his hands; (he is) possessed of riches handsome-jawed, possessed of a shaft and anger which destroys (his foes)-many golden-tinted forms are mixed in Indra.

### Printed p. 56
#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> दि॒वि न के॒तुरधि॑ धायि हर्य॒तो वि॒व्यच॒द्वज्रो॒ हरि॑तो॒ न  ॑
> रंह्या ।  
> तु॒ददहिं॒ हरि॑शिप्रो॒ य आ॑य॒सः स॒हस्र॑शोका अभवद्धरिंभ॒रः ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> दिवि । न । केतुः । अधि । धायि । हर्यतः । विव्यचत् । वज्रः । हरितः । न । रंह्या ।  
> तुदत् । अहिं । हरिशिप्रः । यः । आयसः । सहस्रशोकाः । अभवत् । हरिंभरः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> दिव्यंतरिक्षे केतुः प्रकाशेन सर्वस्य प्रज्ञापक आदित्य इव स यथाधि निहितः
> एवमयमिंद्रोऽधि धायि । अध्यधायि । स्तोतृभिरधि निहितः । किंचेंद्रस्य वज्रो
> हर्यतः स्पृहणीयः सन् विव्यचत्। व्याप्नोति वाप्तव्यं शत्रुसंघं । तत्र दृष्टांतः।
> हरितो न हरितवर्णा अध्वाहर्तारो वाश्वा आदित्यसंबंधिनः । ते यथा रंह्या
> रंहणेन वेगेन व्याप्पुवंति व्याप्तव्यं । योऽस्य वज्र आयसोऽयोविकारोऽहिं
> वृत्रं मेघं वा तुदत् हिनस्ति । योऽयं महानुभावो हरिशिप्रः सोमपानरभसेन हरितवर्णनासिकस्तद्वर्णहनुर्वा हरिंभरो हर्योर्भर्तेंद्रः सहस्रशोका
> अभवत् ॥ शुच दीप्तौ li अपरिमितदीप्तिर्भवति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Like the sun, which is a केतुः (*ketuḥ*) (a banner), making everything known by its light, set in the sky, so Indra has been set up, placed by his worshippers. Further, Indra's thunderbolt, being desirable (हर्यतः (*haryataḥ*)), pervades, that is, reaches, the host of foes it is to reach. The illustration: as the green-tinted horses that carry the sun along the road traverse by their speed what is to be traversed, so the bolt does. The bolt of iron, a product of iron, strikes Ahi, that is, Vṛtra or the cloud. This great Indra, हरिशिप्रः (*hariśipraḥ*), has either a green-tinted nose from the exhilaration of drinking Soma, or green-tinted jaws; he is हरिंभरः (*hariṃbharaḥ*), the bearer (lord) of the two bay horses, and he became सहस्रशोकाः (*sahasraśokāḥ*): he is of boundless splendour.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- दिवि (*divi*)—in the sky
- केतुः न (*ketuḥ na*)—like the sun, who is a banner
- अधि धायि (*adhi dhāyi*)—Indra too has been established
- रंह्या (*raṃhyā*)—by their speed
- हरितो न (*harito na*)—as the sun's horses reach their goal
- हर्यतः (*haryataḥ*)—attractive
- वज्रः (*vajraḥ*)—the thunderbolt
- विव्यचत् (*vivyacat*)—approaches the host of foes
- यः (*yaḥ*)—of which Indra
- आयसः (*āyasaḥ*)—the thunderbolt made of steel
- अहिं (*ahiṃ*)—the enemy
- तुदत् (*tudat*)—strikes, such
- हरिशिप्रः (*hariśipraḥ*)—having cheeks green from drinking Soma
- हरिंभरः (*hariṃbharaḥ*)—the lord of horses, Indra
- सहस्रशोकाः (*sahasraśokāḥ*)—possessing lustre of many kinds
- अभवत् (*abhavat*)—has become

### Printed p. 57

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As the sun, the banner-like illuminator of the world, is established in the sky, so Indra too is established. As the sun's horses reach their goal by their speed, the attractive thunderbolt approaches the host of foes. Indra, whose steel thunderbolt strikes the enemy, who has cheeks green from Soma-drinking and is the lord of horses, has become possessed of lustre of many kinds.

##### English translation
He has been placed (by his worshippers) in the sky, like the banner
(of light, the sun). His desirable bolt reaches (the foe) as bay horses (of the
sun reach their aim) through speed. The iron (thunderbolt), of indra with
yellow jaws, which smote Ahi, (he Indra), lord of horses, shone with a
thousand lustres.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्वंत्व॑महर्यथा॒ उप॑स्तुतः॒ पूर्वे॑भिरिंद्र हरिकेश॒ यज्ज॑भिः ।  
> त्वं ह॑र्यसि॒ तव॒ विश्व॑मु॒क्क्य१॒॑गमसा॑मि॒ राधो॑ हरिजात  ॒
> हर्यतं ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> त्वंत्वं । अहर्यथाः । उपस्तुतः । पूर्वेभिः । इंद्र । हरिजीश । यज्वभिः ।  
> त्वं । हर्यसि । तव । विश्वं । उक्त्यं । असामि । राधः । हरिजात । हर्यतं ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र हे हरिकेश हरितरोमवदश्व त्वंत्वं त्वमेव सर्वत्र यज्ञेऽहर्यथाः । अकामयथाः स्तोत्रं हविर्वा । कीदृशस्त्वं । पूर्वभिः पूर्वतनैर्यज्वभिर्यजमानैरुपस्तुतः सन् । हे हरिजात हरितवर्णः सन्प्रादुर्भूत हारकप्रादुर्भाव वा । शत्रुवधार्थं प्रादुर्भूतेत्यर्थः । हे तादृशेंद्र त्वं तव । स्वभूतमिति
> शेषः । विश्वं व्याप्तं सोमचरुपुरोडाशादिरूपं सर्वं ना । यद्वा! यत्र यानि
> यानि हवींषि दीयंते तत्सर्वं वा । तथोक्थ्यं प्रशस्यमसाम्यसाधारणमनमं
> कृत्सं हर्यतं कांतं राधोऽन्नं हविर्लक्षणं हर्यसि । कामयसे ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, O हरिकेश (*harikeśa*) (horse with green-tinted hair), you alone, in every sacrifice, have desired the praise-song or the oblation, praised as you are by the ancient sacrificers. O हरिजात (*harijāta*), who have appeared in a green-tinted form, or whose appearance is that of one who carries off (the foe), that is, who have appeared for the slaying of enemies: you desire your own whole offering, pervading all, in the form of Soma, cooked offerings, cakes and the like; or else whatever offerings are given at a sacrifice, all of that. Likewise you desire the praiseworthy, unequalled, undiminished, delightful food (राधः (*rādhaḥ*)), whose nature is the oblation.

### Printed p. 58

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- हरिकेश (*harikeśa*)—having horses with golden hair
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- पूर्वेभिः (*pūrvebhiḥ*)—ancient
- यज्विभिः (*yajvibhiḥ*)—by the sacrificers
- उपस्तुतः (*upastutaḥ*)—praised
- त्वं त्वं (*tvaṃ tvaṃ*)—you alone
- आहर्यथाः (*āharyathāḥ*)—you desire the oblation in every sacrifice **NOTE**
- हरिजात (*harijāta*)—O Indra, who have appeared in a golden form
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- तव (*tava*)—to be offered to you
- विश्वं (*viśvaṃ*)—all
- उक्थ्यं (*ukthyaṃ*)—the praise-song
- असामि (*asāmi*)—unequalled
- हरतं (*harataṃ*)—and attractive **NOTE**
- राधः (*rādhaḥ*)—the oblation-food
- हरसि (*harasi*)—you desire **NOTE**

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, lord of horses with golden hair, praised by the sacrificers, you alone desire the oblation in every sacrifice. O Indra, who have appeared in a golden form, you desire all the praises that are to be offered to you, and all the unequalled and attractive oblations.

##### English translation
Indra, with horses of yellow locks, when praised by ancient
worshippers, you desired the oblation; you who are manifested of a golden hue
desire your universal praise, your peculiar and delightful (sacrificial) food.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ता व॒ज्रीणं॑ मं॒दिनं॒ स्तोम्यं॒ मद॒ इद्रं॒ रथे॑ वहतो ॒ ॑
> हर्य॒ता हरी॑ ।  
> पु॒रूण्य॑स्मै॒ सव॑नानि॒ हर्य॑त॒ इंद्रा॑य॒ सोमा॒ हर॑यो  
> दधन्विरे ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ता । वज्रेणं । मंदिनं । स्तोम्यं । मदे । इंद्रं । रथे । वहतः । हर्यता । हरी  
> इति ।  
> पुरूणि । अस्मै । सवनानि । हर्यते । इंद्राय । सोमाः । हरयः । दधन्विरे ॥ ६ ॥

### Printed p. 59

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ता तौ प्रसिद्धौ हर्यता हर्यतौ गंतारौ कांतौ वा हरी हरितवर्णावश्नौ
> मुंदिनं मोदमानं स्तोम्यं स्तुत्यर्हं वज्रीणमिंद्रं मदे निमित्ते रथे वहतः ।
> धारयतः । यज्ञमस्मदीयं प्रापयतः । अस्मै हर्यते कांतायेंद्राय पुरूणि
> बहूनि सवनानि प्रातरादीनि हरयो हरितवर्णाः सोमा दधन्विरे । निधीयंते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Those well-known, desirable (going or lovely) two bay horses, green-tinted, carry in the chariot Indra, the rejoicing one, worthy of praise, the wielder of the thunderbolt, for the exhilaration (मदे (*made*)); that is, they bear him and bring our sacrifice to him. For this lovely Indra many libations, the morning and the other ones, and green-tinted Soma-juices are stored up, deposited.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ता (*tā*)—those well-known
- हर्यता (*haryatā*)—attractive
- हरी (*harī*)—green-tinted horses
- मंदिनं (*maṃdinaṃ*)—the joyful
- स्तोम्यं (*stomyaṃ*)—the praiseworthy
- वज्रिणं (*vajriṇaṃ*)—the wielder of the thunderbolt
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- मदे (*made*)—for joy
- रथे (*rathe*)—in the chariot
- वहतः (*vahataḥ*)—bring
- हर ते (*hara te*)—having an attractive form **NOTE**
- अस्मै इंद्राय (*asmai iṃdrāya*)—for this Indra
- पुरूणि (*purūṇi*)—abundant
- हरयः (*harayaḥ*)—green-tinted
- सवनानि (*savanāni*)—fit for the sacrifice
- सोमाः (*somāḥ*)—Soma-juices
- दधन्विरे (*dadhanvire*)—are stored up

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The well-known, attractive, green-tinted horses carry Indra, the joyful, praiseworthy wielder of the thunderbolt, in the chariot for joy. For Indra of attractive form, abundant, green-tinted Soma-juices fit for the sacrifice are stored up.

##### English translation
These two delightful bay horses bring the thunderer, the rejoicing and laudable Indra, in his car to the exhilaration (of the soma); for him the beloved one, many libations of the yellow-tinted Soma-juices are stored up.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अरं॒ कामा॑य॒ हर॑यो दधन्विरे स्थि॒राय॑ हिन्व॒न्हर॑यो॒ हरी॑  ॒
> तुरा ।  
> अर्व॑द्भि॒र्यो हरि॑भि॒र्जोष॒मीय॑ते॒ सो अ॑स्य॒ कामं॒ हरि॑वंतमानशे ॥ ७ ॥

### Printed p. 60

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अरं । कामाय । हरयः । दधन्विरे । स्थिराय । हिन्वन् । हरयः । हरी इति । तुरा।  
> अर्वत्भिः । यः । हरिभिः । जोषं । ईयते । सः । अस्य । कामं । हरिवंतं  
> आनशे ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अरमलं पर्याप्तं कामायेंद्रकामनाय हरयो हरितवर्णाः सोमा
> दधन्विरे । ते च हरयः स्थिराय युद्धेऽपलयितायेंद्राय तुरा तुरौ त्वरमाणौ
> हरी अश्वा हिन्वन् । प्रेरयंति । योऽर्वद्भिररणकुशलैर्हरिभिरश्वैर्जोषं शूरैः
> सेव्यं संग्राममीयते गच्छति स रथोऽस्येंद्रस्य स्वभूतं कामं कमनीयं
> हरिवंतं सोमवंतं यज्ञमानशे । व्याप्प्नोति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Green-tinted Soma-juices are stored up in plenty for Indra's desire, that is, to satisfy him. Those green-tinted juices urge on the two swift horses for Indra, who is steadfast and does not flee in battle. The chariot that goes with spirited green-tinted horses to the battle, to which brave men resort (जोषं (*joṣaṃ*)), that same chariot reaches Indra's own desirable sacrifice, the one furnished with Soma (हरिवंतं (*harivaṃtaṃ*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अरं (*araṃ*)—complete
- कामाय (*kāmāya*)—for (Indra's) satisfaction
- हरयः (*harayaḥ*)—green-tinted Soma-juices
- दधन्विरे (*dadhanvire*)—are stored up
- हरयः (*harayaḥ*)—those same Soma-juices
- स्थिराय (*sthirāya*)—to carry Indra, who stands firm in battle
- तुरा (*turā*)—swift
- हरी (*harī*)—the horses
- हिन्सन् (*hinsan*)—urge on **NOTE**
- यः (*yaḥ*)—whose chariot of Indra
- अर्वद्भिः (*arvadbhiḥ*)—swift
- हरिभिः (*haribhiḥ*)—together with horses
- जोषं (*joṣaṃ*)—to the battle frequented by heroes
- ईयते (*īyate*)—goes
- सः (*saḥ*)—that same chariot
- अस्य (*asya*)—to Indra
- कामं (*kāmaṃ*)—the desired
- हरिवंतं (*harivaṃtaṃ*)—furnished with Soma, sacrifice
- आनशे (*ānaśe*)—enters

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

For Indra's complete satisfaction green-tinted Soma-juices are stored up. Those same juices urge the horses to carry Indra, who is steadfast in battle. The chariot of Indra that goes with swift horses to the battle frequented by heroes, that same chariot enters also the sacrifice desired by Indra and furnished with Soma.

##### English translation
Yellow-tinted (Soma-juices) are stored up sufficient for his desire, the yellow-tinted juices urge his two swift bay horses for the stalwart (Indra). His (chariot) which rushes with spirited bay horses to the conflict, has reached his desire (the sacrifice) in which the Soma is presented.

### Printed p. 61
#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> हरि॑श्मशारु॒र्हरि॑केश आय॒सस्तु॑र॒स्सेये॒ यो ह॑रि॒पा अव॑र्धत ।  
> अर्व॑द्भि॒र्यो हरि॑भिर्वा॒जिनी॑वसु॒रति॒ विश्वा॑ दुरि॒ता षारि॑ष॒द्धरी॑ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> हरिश्मशारुः । हरिःकेशः । आयसः । तुरःपेये । यः । हरिपाः । अवर्ध त ।  
> अर्वत्भिः । यः । हरिभिः । वाजनीवसुः । अति । विश्वा । दुःइता । पारिषत् ।  
> हरी इति ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हरिश्मशारुर्हरितवर्णश्मश्रुर्हरिकेशो हरितवर्णकेश अयसोऽयोमयहृदयः । शत्रूणां घातक इत्यर्थः । एतादृशो य इंद्रस्तुरस्सेये तूर्णं पातन्ये
> सोमे हरिपा हरितवर्णसोमपा अवर्धत वर्धते यश्चार्वद्भिर्गृंतृभिर्हरिभिरश्वैः
> सोमैर्वा वाजिनीवसुः । वाजिनमन्नं हविर्लक्षणं । तदस्या अस्तित्वि वाजिनी क्रिया ।
> सैव वसु धनं यस्य स तथोक्तः । यज्ञधन इत्यर्थः । यद्वा । वाजिनमेव वाजिनी ।
> तदेव धनं यस्य । स एवमुक्तलक्षण इंद्रो हरी रथे योजयित्वा विश्वा विश्वानि
> सर्वाणि दुरितास्माकं दुरितानि पारिषत् । पारयतु ॥ पारयतेर्लॆटि सिप्यडागमः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indra has green-tinted beard and green-tinted hair, and a heart of iron, that is, he is the slayer of enemies. This Indra grows strong at the swift drinking (तुरस्सेये (*turasseye*)) of Soma, drinking the green-tinted Soma. He is also वाजिनीवसुः (*vājinīvasuḥ*), through the praisers, the horses or the Soma-juices. *vājinaṃ* is food, the oblation; the action possessing it is वाजिनी (*vājinī*); that is his wealth: his wealth is the sacrifice. Or else *vājinaṃ* itself is वाजिनी (*vājinī*), and that itself is his wealth. Such an Indra, having yoked his two bay horses to the chariot, is to carry us across all our troubles.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- आयसः (*āyasaḥ*)—having a heart hard towards enemies **NOTE**
- हरिश्मशारुः (*hariśmaśāruḥ*)—having golden facial hair
- हरिकेशः (*harikeśaḥ*)—having golden hair
- यः (*yaḥ*)—which Indra
- तुरस्सेये (*turasseye*)—at the time of drinking Soma swiftly
- हरिपाः (*haripāḥ*)—drinking the green-tinted Soma
- अवर्धत (*avardhata*)—grows strong
- यः (*yaḥ*)—who
- अर्वद्भिः (*arvadbhiḥ*)—swift
- हरिभिः (*haribhiḥ*)—with the help of horses
- वाजिनीवसुः (*vājinīvasuḥ*)—who has wealth in the form of the oblation, that Indra
- हरी (*harī*)—having yoked his horses to the chariot
- विश्वा दुरिता (*viśvā duritā*)—all paths of sorrow
- पारिषत् (*pāriṣat*)—may he carry (us) across

### Printed p. 62
#### Ṛk 9 (continued)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, whose heart is hard toward his enemies, who has a golden-brown beard and golden-brown hair, who grows strong while drinking the green-hued Soma at the time of the quickly drunk Soma, and who is rich in wealth in the form of oblations through his swift horses: may that Indra yoke his horses to the chariot and carry us across our paths of sorrow.

##### English translation
The yellow-bearded, the yellow-haired, iron-hearted Indra, the drinker of the yellow (Soma-juice), who has been invigorated by the Soma which has to be quickly quaffed, who is rich in sacrificial food through his swift bay horses, may he drive his two bay horses safe through all difficulties.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स्रुवे॑व॒ यस्य॒ हरि॑णी विपे॒ततुः॒ शिप्रे॒ वाजा॑य॒ हरि॑णी॒ दवि॑ध्वतः ।  
> प्र यत्कृ॒ते च॑म॒से मर्मृ॑ज॒द्धरी॑ पी॒त्वा मद॑स्य हर्य॒तस्यांध॑सः ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> स्रुवाइव । यस्य । हरिणी इति । विपेततुः । शिप्रे इति । वाजाय । हरिणी इति ।  
> दविध्वतः ।  
> प्र । यत् । कृते । चमसे । मर्मृजत् । हरी इति । पीत्वा । मदस्य । हर्यतस ।  
> अंधसः ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यस्येंद्रस्य हरिणी हरितवर्णावश्वा विपेततुः रथ इंद्रमारोप्य विपततो
> यज्ञमस्मदीयं । यद्वा। यस्य हरिणी हरितवर्णॆ कनीनिके विपेततुः सोमं प्रति
> विपततः । तत्र दृष्टांतः । स्रुवेव । यथा स्रुवा स्रुनौ हविषा पूर्ण पात्रविशेषौ
> होमार्थं विपततः तद्वत् । तथा यस्य च हरिणी हरितवर्णॆ शिप्रे हनू वाजाय

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa explains: the two bay (green-hued) horses of Indra "fly forth", that is, they set Indra on the chariot and speed to our sacrifice. Or else: his two green-hued eye-pupils dart toward the Soma. An illustration is given: as the two ladles (sruva), vessels filled with oblation, are poured out for the offering, so his two golden jaws shake for the food, the Soma, the offering made to him.

### Printed p. 63

> सोमलक्षणायान्नाय दविध्वतः कंपयतः पुरतः प्रत्तस्य सोमस्य प्रीत्या चलतः ।
> तथा यद्यदा कृते संस्कृते चमसे वर्तमानं मदस्य मदकरं हर्यतस्य
> कांतमंधसोऽन्नं सोमं पीत्वा हरी अश्नौ प्र मर्मृजत् प्रमार्ष्टि । तदानीं
> स्तुत इत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The jaws tremble for the food in the form of Soma, moving with pleasure at the Soma offered before him. And when, from the Soma that is in the well-prepared cup, the intoxicating, desirable food, he has drunk, he strokes his two horses. At that time, the meaning is, he is praised.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- स्रुवेव (*sruveva*)—as vessels full of oblation are poured out in haste to offer the oblation
- यस्य (*yasya*)—of which Indra
- हरिणी (*hariṇī*)—the green-hued horses
- विपेततुः (*vipetatuḥ*)—run toward the sacrifice
- (यस्य (*yasya*)—of which Indra)
- हरिणी (*hariṇī*)—golden
- शिप्रे (*śipre*)—jaws
- वाजाय (*vājāya*)—to eat the oblation
- दविध्वतः (*davidhvataḥ*)—tremble; that Indra
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- कृते (*kṛte*)—well prepared
- चमसे (*camase*)—in the cup (camasa)
- मदस्य (*madasya*)—intoxicating
- हरतस्य (*haratasya*)—attractive
- अंधसः (*aṃdhasaḥ*)—the food (Soma)
- पीत्वा (*pītvā*)—having drunk
- हरी (*harī*)—his horses
- प्र मर्मृजत् (*pra marmṛjat*)—strokes; then (he is praised)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As vessels full of oblation are poured out in haste to offer the oblation, so the green-hued horses of Indra run toward the sacrifice, and his golden jaws tremble to eat the oblation. That Indra, having drunk the food (Soma), which is in the well-prepared camasa cup, intoxicating and attractive, and being satisfied, is praised as he strokes his horses.

##### English translation
He whose bay horses alight (upon the soma) like two ladles, whose yellow jaws tremble (with eagerness) for the sacrificial food. (is praised) when having drunk of the delicious exhilarating beverage in the prepared cup. he rubs down his bay horses.

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒त स्म॒ सद्म॑ हर्य॒तस्य॑ प॒स्तो॒३॒॑ऽतिरत्यो॒ न वाजं॒ हरि॑वा अचिक्रदत् ।  
> म॒ही चि॒द्दि धि॒षणाह॑र्य॒दोज॑सा बृ॒हद्वयो॑ दधिषे हर्य॒तश्चि॒दा ॥ १० ॥

### Printed p. 64

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उत । स्म । सद्म । हर्यतस्य । पस्तोः । अत्यः । न । वाजं । हरिवान् । अचिक्रदत् ।  
> मही । चित् । हि । धिषणा । अहर्यत् । ओजसा । बृहत् । वयः । दधिषे । हर्यतः ।  
> चित् । आ ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उतापि च हर्यतस्य कमनीयस्येंद्रस्य सद्म सदनं पस्त्योर्द्यावापृथिव्योः
> संबंधि । सोऽयमत्तो नाश्व इव वाजं संग्रामं हरिवानश्ववानचिक्रदत् । गच्छति ।
> तथा हि यस्माद्दे इंद्र त्वां मही महती धिषणा स्तुतिरोजसा बलेन युक्तमिंद्रमहर्यत् कामयते । चिदिति पूरणः । अतो वाजमचिक्रदत् । तथा सति हर्यतः
> कामयमानस्य यजमानस्य बृहन्महद्वयोऽन्नमा दधिषे । धारयसि । आप्रयच्छसि ।
> चिदिति पूरणः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

And the dwelling of the desirable Indra belongs to heaven and earth. That same Indra, owning horses, goes to battle (वाजं (*vājaṃ*)) like a horse. For, Indra, the great praise (धिषणा (*dhiṣaṇā*)) desires you, endowed with might and strength; चित् (*cit*) is a filler. Therefore he goes वाजं (*vājaṃ*) roaring. That being so, you bestow great food on the sacrificer who desires you; here too चित् (*cit*) is a filler.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उत (*uta*)—and
- हर्यतस्य (*haryatasya*)—of Indra of attractive form
- सद्म (*sadma*)—the dwelling
- पस्योः (*pasyoḥ*)—belongs to both heaven and earth
- हरिवान् (*harivān*)—that same Indra, owning horses
- अत्यो न (*atyo na*)—like a horse
- वाजं (*vājaṃ*)—to battle
- अचिक्रदत् (*acikradat*)—rushes forward
- मही (*mahī*)—full of greatness
- धिषणा (*dhiṣaṇā*)—praise
- ओजसा (*ojasā*)—Indra endowed with strength
- अहर्यत् (*aharyat*)—desires
- (O Indra) हर्यतः (*haryataḥ*)—to the sacrificer, your devotee
- बृहत् (*bṛhat*)—great
- वयः (*vayaḥ*)—food
- आ दधिषे (*ā dadhiṣe*)—you provide

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The dwelling of Indra of attractive form belongs to both heaven and earth. That same Indra, owning horses, rushes to battle like a horse. Praise full of greatness desires the mighty Indra. O Indra, you provide great food to the sacrificer, your devotee.

##### English translation
The dwelling of the gracious (Indra) is in heaven and earth; exhilarated by the Soma he rushes impetuously as a charger to battle. The earnest prayer desires (Indra endowed) with might; you bestow ample food on the devout (worshippcr).

#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ रोद॑सी॒ हर्य॑माणो महि॒त्वा नव्यं॑नव्यं हर्यसि॒ मन्म॒ नु  ॒
> प्रियं ।  
> प्र प॒स्य॑मसुर हर्य॒तं गोरा॒विष्कृ॑धि॒ हर॑ये॒ सूर्या॑य ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । रोदसी इति । हर्यमाणः । महित्वा । नव्यंनव्यं । हर्यसि । मन्म । नु ।  
> प्रियं ।  
> प्र । पस्यं । असुर । हर्यतं । गोः । अविः । कृधि । हरये । सूर्याय ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र हर्यमाणः कामयमानो महित्वा महत्वेन रोदसी द्यावापृथिव्यावा । पूरयसीति शेषः । तथा नव्यंनव्यं नवतरं प्रियं प्रियकरं मन्म
> मननीयं स्तोत्रं नु क्षिप्रं हर्यसि । कामयसे । हे असुर बलवन्। असुः प्राणः।
> तद्वन् ॥ मत्वर्थीयो रः ॥ तादृशींद्र गोः । जात्येकवचनं । गवां हर्यतं
> स्पृहणीयं पस्त्यं गृहं गोरुदकस्योक्तगुणकं स्थानं वा हरय उदकस्य
> हर्त्रॆ सूर्याय प्र प्रकर्षेणाविष्कृधि । प्रकटीकुरु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, desiring, you fill by your greatness the two worlds, heaven and earth ("fill" is to be supplied). You also quickly desire ever newer, pleasing praise worthy of reflection (मन्म (*manma*)). O असुर (*asura*), mighty one: असुः (*asuḥ*) is breath, and the one having it; the affix रः (*raḥ*) is in the sense of possession. Such an Indra: गोः (*goḥ*) is a singular of the class, "of the waters"; make manifest abundantly the desirable dwelling or the place, of the waters possessing the stated qualities, to the sun, the taker of water.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Indra) हर्यमाणः (*haryamāṇaḥ*)—you of attractive form
- महित्वा (*mahitvā*)—by your greatness
- रोदसी (*rodasī*)—both heaven and earth
- आ (पूरयसि) (*ā (pūrayasi)*)—you fill
- नु (*nu*)—always
- नव्यं नव्यं (*navyaṃ navyaṃ*)—ever new
- प्रियं (*priyaṃ*)—beneficial
- मन्म (*manma*)—praise
- हरसि (*harasi*)—you desire
- असुर (*asura*)—O mighty Indra
- हरये (*haraye*)—to the water-drinking
- सूर्याय (*sūryāya*)—sun
- गोः (*goḥ*)—of the waters
- हरतं (*harataṃ*)—attractive
- पस्यं (*pasyaṃ*)—dwelling (or place)
- प्र (*pra*)—greatly
- अविष्कृधि (*aviṣkṛdhi*)—make manifest

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, you of attractive form fill both heaven and earth by your greatness. You always desire praise that is ever new and beneficial.

### Printed p. 66
O mighty Indra, make manifest to the water-drinking sun the attractive dwelling, or the place, of the waters.

##### English translation
You (Indra) who are the object of desire (fill) heaven and earth with your greatness, you desire greatful praise new and new ; O powerful one, make manifest the desirable dwelling of the water to the water-seizing sun.

#### Ṛk 12

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ त्वा॑ ह॒र्यंतं॑ प्र॒युजो॒ जना॑नां॒ रथे॑ वहंतु॒ हरि॑शिप्रमिंद्र ।  
> पिबा॒ यथा॒ प्रति॑भृतस्य॒ मध्यो॒ हर्य॑न्य॒ज्ञं स॑ध॒मादे॒  ॑
> दशोणिं ॥ १२ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । त्वा । हर्यंतं । प्रयुजः । जनानां । रथे । वहंतु । हरिशिप्रं । इंद्र ।  
> पिब । यथा । प्रतिभृतस्य । मध्वः । हर्यन् । यज्ञं । सधर्मादे । दशओणिं ॥१२॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र हरिशिप्रं हरितवर्णशिप्रं त्वा त्वां हर्यंतं यज्ञं कामयमानं
> प्रयुजो रथे प्रयुक्ता अश्वा रथे स्थापयित्वा जनानामृत्विग्यजमानानामंतिकं
> वहंतु । प्रापयंतु । प्रवहंति । यथा येन प्रकारेण प्रतिभृतस्य ग्रहादिषु
> संभृतं मध्वो मधु सोमरसं यज्ञं यागसाधनं दशोणिं । ओणयोऽंगुलयः । दशभिरंगुलिभिः संपादितं सोमं हर्यन् कामयमानः सन् पिब पिबसि
> सधमादे संग्रामे जयार्थं तथावहंत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, you of green-hued jaws (हरिशिप्रं (*hariśipraṃ*)), desiring the sacrifice: let the horses yoked to the chariot (प्रयुजः (*prayujaḥ*)), having placed you on the chariot, carry you to the sacrificers, the priests and patrons (जनानां (*janānāṃ*)). In the manner in which, desiring the sweet Soma juice collected in the cups and so on, the sacrificial means, prepared by the ten fingers (दशोणिं (*daśoṇiṃ*); *oṇayaḥ* are the fingers), you drink, so let them carry you to the battle (सधमादे (*sadhamāde*)) for victory.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- हरिशिप्रं (*hariśipraṃ*)—having golden cheeks
- हर्यतं (*haryataṃ*)—desiring the sacrifice
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- रथे (*rathe*)—having seated on the chariot
- प्रयुजः (*prayujaḥ*)—the horses yoked to the chariot

### Printed p. 67
- जनानां (*janānāṃ*)—to the sacrificers
- वहंतु (*vahaṃtu*)—let them bring
- दशोणिं (*daśoṇiṃ*)—prepared with the ten fingers
- प्रतिभृतस्य (*pratibhṛtasya*)—collected in the vessels
- मध्वः (*madhvaḥ*)—sweet
- यज्ञं (*yajñaṃ*)—the Soma juice, the accomplisher of the sacrifice
- हर्यन् (*haryan*)—desiring
- यथा (*yathā*)—in what manner
- पिब (*piba*)—you drink, so
- सधमाधे (*sadhamādhe*)—for victory in battle let the horses bring you

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, you of golden cheeks, desiring the sacrifice: let the horses yoked to the chariot, having seated you on the chariot, bring you to the sacrificers. As you drink, desiring, the Soma juice prepared by the ten fingers, collected in the vessels, sweet and the accomplisher of the sacrifice, so (then) let the horses bring you for victory in battle.

##### English translation
Let your horses yoked to the chariot bring you, Indra, the yellow-jawed, desiring (the sacrifice), to (the vicinity) of the worshippers; when you desiring the sacrifice, prepared by the ten fingers, drink of the collected sweet-flavoured Soma, (Let them bring you) to the battle.

### Printed p. 68
#### Ṛk 13 (continued)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अपाः पूर्णेषामित्येषा षोडशिशस्त्रस्य याज्या । सूत्रितं च । एवा हि
> शक्रो वशी हि शक्र इति जपित्वापाह पूर्वेषां हरिवः सुतानामिति यजति ।
> आ. ६-२ । इति ॥
> हे इंद्र सुतानामभिसुतानां पूर्वॆषां प्रातःसवनसंपादितानां ।
> कर्मणि षष्ठ्यावेते । अभिषुतान्ना तःसवनिकान्नोमानित्यर्थः । तानपाः । अपिबः । हे
> हरिव इतींद्रसंबोधनं । हरिभ्यामश्वाभ्यां तद्वन् । यद्वा । ऋक्सामात्मकाभ्यां
> हरिभ्यां युक्त । ऋक्सामे वा इंद्रस्य हरी । ऐ- ब्रा. २-२४ । तै. सं. ६-५-९-२
> ताभ्यामेष हरतीति ब्राह्मणं । अथो अपि चेदं माध्यंदिनं सवनं केवलं ते
> तवैनासाधारणं । माध्यंदिनं सवनं केवलं ते । ऋ. ४-३५-७ । इति हि मंत्रांतरं ।
> तस्मिन्सवने हे इंद्र मधुमंतं माधुर्योपेतं सोमं ममद्धि । पिब । आस्वादयेत्यर्थः । मदिरत्रास्वादनकर्मा । पिबंतु मदंतु वियंत्विति च मंत्रः । सत्रा
> वृषन् । सत्राशब्दो भूयिष्ठवचनः । हे भूयिष्ठ वर्षितरिंद्र जठर आ वृषस्व ।
> आसिंचस्व ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Ṛk is the yājyā of the Ṣoḍaśi-śastra. The sūtra says: एवा हि शक्रो वशी हि शक्र इति जपित्वापः पूर्णेषां हरिवः सुतानामिति यजति (*evā hi śakro vaśī hi śakra iti japitvāpaḥ pūrṇeṣāṃ harivaḥ sutānāmiti yajati*) (Ā. 6-2). "O Indra, of the previous pressed offerings", that is, of the Soma pressed and made ready for the morning pressing (the genitive is in the sense of the object), you drank those. हरिवः (*harivaḥ*) is a vocative of Indra: one who has the two bay horses; or else one yoked with the two bays that are the Ṛk and the Sāman. Ai. Brā. 2-24 and Tai. Saṃ. 6-5-9-2 say: "The Ṛk and the Sāman are Indra's two bays; with them he carries (the offering)", so says the Brāhmaṇa. अथो (*atho*): moreover, this midday pressing is yours alone, not shared. Another mantra is quoted: माध्यंदिनं सवनं केवलं ते (*mādhyaṃdinaṃ savanaṃ kevalaṃ te*) (Ṛ. 4-35-7). At that pressing, O Indra, drink the sweet Soma, the one endowed with sweetness; ममद्धि (*mamaddhi*) means drink, enjoy; the root मदि (*madi*) has the sense of tasting; the mantra says *pibaṃtu madaṃtu viyaṃtu*. सत्रा (*satrā*) means in great abundance. O abundant showerer, Indra, pour (it) into your belly.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- हरिवः (*harivaḥ*)—O Indra, who are yoked with horses
- पूर्वेषां (*pūrveṣāṃ*)—formerly, prepared for the morning pressing
- सुतानां (*sutānāṃ*)—of the pressed Soma juices
- अपाः (*apāḥ*)—you have drunk
- अथो (*atho*)—likewise
- इदं सवनं (*idaṃ savanaṃ*)—the juices pressed for this midday pressing also
- केवलं ते (*kevalaṃ te*)—have been pressed with you alone in view
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- मधुमंतं (*madhumaṃtaṃ*)—the tasty
- सोमं (*somaṃ*)—Soma juice
- ममद्धि (*mamaddhi*)—drink it and be gladdened
- सत्रा (*satrā*)—in great abundance
- वृषन् (*vṛṣan*)—O Indra, who pour down rain
- जठरे (*jaṭhare*)—in your belly
- आ वृषस्व (*ā vṛṣasva*)—pour this in

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, yoked with horses, you have drunk the pressed Soma juices that were formerly prepared for the morning pressing. Likewise the juices pressed for this midday pressing have been pressed with you alone in view. O Indra, drink the tasty Soma and be gladdened. O Indra, who pour down rain in great abundance, pour this into your belly.

##### English translation
Lord of horses, you drank of previous libations, this sacrifice is for you alone; exhilarate yourself, Indra, with sweet Soma; showerer of copious rain, shower it into your belly.

### Printed p. 69

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

That this Ṛk beginning असाः पूर्णेषां (*asāḥ pūrṇeṣāṃ*) **NOTE** is to be recited as the yājyā mantra of the Ṣoḍaśi-śastra is explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra: एवा हि शक्रो वशी हि शक्र इति जपित्वापः पूर्णेषां हरिवः सुतानामिति यजति (*evā hi śakro vaśī hi śakra iti japitvāpaḥ pūrṇeṣāṃ harivaḥ sutānāmiti yajati*) (Ā. 6-2).

The ninety-seventh sūkta is on the Jīrike (cumin).

In this sūkta are praised the herbs and trees (oṣadhi-vanaspati) used to destroy diseases and the like: that is, small plants, creepers, roots, vines and so on, and also a few large trees such as the aśvattha and the pālāśa. The same sūkta, with some variant readings, is also recited in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā (Tai. Saṃ. 4-2-6). From some Ṛks of this sūkta, and from the mantras of the 145th sūkta of this same maṇḍala beginning इमां खनाम्योषधिं (*imāṃ khanāmyoṣadhiṃ*), it is evident that these herbs were used not only as medicine to cure diseases but also in magical (ābhicārika) rites such as wound-charming, subjugation and harming enemies. The seers believed that the power of these herbs and trees is very great. Soma, or the Soma plant, is said to be the king of these herbs and trees; that is why it is customary to call Soma by the word "king". Although other herbs and trees are described and praised here and there, this is the only sūkta in which these herbs are the main object of praise. The ninth maṇḍala of the Ṛgveda is set apart for praising the Soma plant and Soma, its presiding deity; and in many Ṛks of many sūktas, as the context requires, there is praise of the deity Soma as well. In the introduction to the ninth maṇḍala (Ṛ. Saṃ. part 26, pp. 1-98) there is a detailed account of the deity Soma and the Soma plant. In many places in the Ṛks of this sūkta, things regarded as lifeless, that is, creepers and the like that have no power to speak like human beings, are addressed as if they were human beings, and are seen speaking like human beings. But, as the Bhāṣyakāra has said in the Ṛgveda-bhāṣya-bhūmikā, it should be understood that in sentences such as ओषधे त्रायस्यैनं (*oṣadhe trāyasyainaṃ*) ("O herb, protect this man") and in the mantras of the present sūkta, the herbs and trees are not thought of merely as plants but are praised as their presiding deities.

Giving the etymology of the word ओषधि (*oṣadhi*), Yāska says: ओषधयः । ओषद्वयंतीति वा । ओषत्येना भयंतीति वा । दोषं भयंतीति वा (*oṣadhayaḥ | oṣadvayaṃtīti vā | oṣatyenā bhayaṃtīti vā | doṣaṃ bhayaṃtīti vā*) (Ni. 9-27). That is: here the two words ओषत् (*oṣat*) and धयंति (*dhayaṃti*) together make the word ओषधयः (*oṣadhayaḥ*). ओषत् (*oṣat*) is disease pervading the body, such as the heat of fever; धयंति (*dhayaṃti*) means they drink up or destroy. Because it drinks up (destroys) and demolishes the diseases that arise in the body, it gets the name ओषधयः (*oṣadhayaḥ*). Or, because it is drunk by going into the belly, it gets this name. Or, because it destroys the faults (doṣa) in the body, that is, the faults such as vāta and pitta, it gets the name ओषधि (*oṣadhi*). Thus he has given three kinds of meaning.

### Printed p. 70
## Sūkta 97 (Maṇḍala 10)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> या ओषधीरिति त्रयोविंशत्यृचं सप्तमं सूक्तं । अथर्वणः पुत्रस्य
> भिषग्नाम्न आर्ष० । आनुष्टुभनोषधिदेवताकं । तथा चानुक्रांतं । या ओषधीस्त्र्यधिकाधर्वणो भिषगोषधिस्तुतिरानुष्टुभमिति ॥ दीक्षितानां ज्वराद्युपसंतापे
> संजातेऽनेन सूक्तेन मार्जयेत् । सूत्रितं च । ओषधिसूक्तेन चाप्लाव्यानुम्मृजेत् । आ. ६-९ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The seventh sūkta, या ओषधीः (*yā oṣadhīḥ*), of twenty-three Ṛks. Its seer is Bhiṣak, son of Atharvan; its deity is the Oṣadhis; its metre is Anuṣṭubh. The Anukramaṇī says: या ओषधीस्र्यधिकाधर्वणो भिषगोषधिस्तुतिरानुष्टुभं (*yā oṣadhīsryadhikādharvaṇo bhiṣagoṣadhistutirānuṣṭubhaṃ*). When fever or other affliction has arisen among those consecrated for a rite (dīkṣita), one should sprinkle them with this sūkta. The sūtra says: ओषधिसूक्तेन चाप्लाव्यानुमृजेत् (*oṣadhisūktena cāplāvyānumṛjet*) (Ā. 6-9).

The Anuvāda: this sūkta beginning या ओषधीः (*yā oṣadhīḥ*) is the seventh sūkta in the eighth anuvāka; it has twenty-three Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is the one named Bhiṣak (physician), son of Atharvan; its deity is the herbs; its metre is Anuṣṭubh. In the Anukramaṇī it is said: या ओषधीस्र्यधिकाधर्वणो भिषगोषधिस्तुतिरानुष्टुभं (*yā oṣadhīsryadhikādharvaṇo bhiṣagoṣadhistutirānuṣṭubhaṃ*). If those who have taken the dīkṣā in sacrificial rites are afflicted by fever or other troubles in the middle of the rite, they are to be sprinkled (prokṣaṇa) with the mantras of this sūkta; this is explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, ओषधिसूक्तेन चाप्लाव्यानुमृजेत् (*oṣadhisūktena cāplāvyānumṛjet*) (Ā. 6-9).

Maṇḍala 10; Anuvāka 8; Sūkta 97. Aṣṭaka 8; Adhyāya 5; Vargas 8, 9, 10, 11. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 23. Ṛṣi: Bhiṣag Ātharvaṇa. Devatā: praise of the Oṣadhis. Chandas: Anuṣṭubh.

### Printed p. 71
#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> या ओष॑धीः॒ पूर्वा॑ जा॒ता दे॒वेभ्य॑स्त्रियु॒गं पु॒रा ॥  
> मन्यॆ॒ नु ब॒भ्रूणा॑म॒हं श॒तं धामा॑नि स॒प्त च॑ ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> याः । ओषधीः । पूर्वाः । जाताः । देवेभ्यः । त्रियुगं । पुरा ।  
> मन्यै । नु । बभ्रूणां । अहं । शतं । धामानि । सप्त । च ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> या ओषधयः पूर्वा पुरातन्यो जाता उत्पन्नाः । केभ्यः सकाशात् ।
> देवेभ्यो जगन्निर्मातृभ्यः । यद्वा । देवा द्योतमाना ऋतवः । तेभ्यः ।
> कस्मिन्काले । त्रियुगं त्रिषु युगेषु विशेषेण प्रादुर्भानापेक्षया कृतादियुगत्रयमुक्तं कलौ त्वत्यंताल्पत्वादुपेक्षितं । अथवा त्रिषु युगेषु वसंते प्रावृषि शरदि
> चेत्यर्थः । अहं बभ्रूणां बभ्रुवर्णानां सोमाद्योषधीनां शतं सप्त च
> धामान्यनुलेपमार्जनाभिषेकादिरूपेणाश्रयभूतानि स्थानानि नु क्षिप्रं मन्यॆ । मन्ये ।
> संभावयामीत्यर्थः । अत्र वाजसनेयकं । या ओषधीः पूर्वा जाता देवेभ्यस्त्रियुगं पुरेत्यृतवो नै देवास्त्रेभ्य एतास्त्रिः पुरा जायंते वसंते प्रावृषि शरदि
> मनै नु बभ्रूणामहमिति सोमो वै बभ्रुः सोम्या औषधॆय औषधः पुरुषः शतं
> धामानीति यदिदग्० शतायुः शतार्घः शतवीर्य एतानि हास्य तानि शतं धामानि सप्त
> चेति य एवेमे सप्त शीर्षन्नाणास्तानेतदाह । शत. ७-२-४-२६ । इति ! अत्र निरुक्तं
> च । या ओषधयः पूर्वा जाता देवेभ्यस्त्रीणि युगानि पुरा मन्यॆ नु तद्बभ्रूणामहं बभ्रुवर्णानां भरणानां हरणानामिति ना शतं धामानि सप्त चेति । धामानि
> त्रयाणि भवंति स्थानानि नामानि जन्मानीति । जन्मान्यत्राभिप्रेतानि सप्तशतानि सप्तशतं
> पुरुषस्य मर्मणां तेष्टेना दधति । नि. ९-२५ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The herbs that were born first, in ancient times, before the gods. "Gods" means either the world-makers, or else the shining seasons. "In three yugas": with regard to special manifestation, the three yugas beginning with Kṛta are meant; the Kali age is passed over because it is very short. Or else the three "yugas" are spring, the rains and autumn. "I" think quickly of the hundred and seven abodes of the brown-hued (बभ्रु (*babhru*)) herbs such as Soma, abodes that serve as supports in the form of anointing, sprinkling, bathing and so on; मन्ये (*manye*) means "I conceive". The Vājasaneyaka (Śa. Brā. 7-2-4-26) is cited here: the Ṛk is quoted, and then: the seasons are the gods; for them these (herbs) are born thrice, in spring, the rains and autumn. "I think of the brown ones": Soma is the brown one; the herbs are Soma's. The man is made of herbs. "A hundred abodes": this man lives a hundred years, is worth a hundred, has a hundred-fold vigour; these are his hundred abodes. "And seven": this refers to the seven vital breaths in the head. The Nirukta also (Ni. 9-25) is cited: the herbs that were born first, three yugas before the gods, "I think of the brown ones", of brown-coloured ones, of bearers or of takers (*bharaṇānāṃ haraṇānāṃ*); "a hundred abodes and seven". Abodes are of three kinds: places, names, births. Here births are meant: the seven hundred of the vital spots (marman) of a man, with which they support him.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देवेभ्यः (*devebhyaḥ*)—than the gods
- पुरा त्रियुगं (*purā triyugaṃ*)—three yugas before. Or त्रियुगं (*triyugaṃ*)—in the spring, rainy and autumn seasons
- पुरा (*purā*)—formerly
- जाताः (*jātāḥ*)—born
- पूर्वाः (*pūrvāḥ*)—and ancient
- या ओषधीः (*yā oṣadhīḥ*)—whichever herbs there are, such
- बभ्रूणां (*babhrūṇāṃ*)—of the golden-brown herbs such as Soma
- शतं सप्त च (*śataṃ sapta ca*)—a hundred and seven in number
- धामानि (*dhāmāni*)—abodes (anointing, or applications)
- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I
- नु (*nu*)—quickly
- मन्ये (*manye*)—have understood

### Printed p. 72

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Three yugas back, called Kṛta, Tretā and Dvāpara, or formerly in the spring, rainy and autumn seasons, whichever herbs were born and are ancient, such golden-brown herbs, Soma and the others: I have quickly understood the abodes (the uses) of a hundred and seven kinds of them.

##### English translation
I think of the hundred and seven applications of the brown-tinted plants, which are ancient, being generated for the gods before the three ages.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

या ओषधीः पूर्वा जाताः देवेभ्यः त्रियुगं पुरा (*yā oṣadhīḥ pūrvā jātāḥ devebhyaḥ triyugaṃ purā*): here the Bhāṣyakāra has given two kinds of meaning for the word त्रियुगं (*triyugaṃ*): त्रियुगं त्रिषु युगेषु विशेषेण प्रादुर्भानापेक्षया कृतादियुगत्रयमुक्तं कलौ त्वत्यंताल्पत्वादुपेक्षितं । (*triyugaṃ triṣu yugeṣu viśeṣeṇa prādurbhānāpekṣayā kṛtādiyugatrayamuktaṃ kalau tvatyaṃtālpatvādupekṣitaṃ |*). That is, "in the three yugas called Kṛta, Tretā and Dvāpara"; since the duration of the Kali age is very small, it is passed over here (that is, not worth counting). "Formerly" (पुरा (*purā*)), that is, the herbs born in the three yugas beginning with Kṛta, which are earlier than the Kali age: this is one kind of meaning.

अथवा त्रिषु युगेषु वसंते प्रावृषि शरदि चेत्यर्थः (*athavā triṣu yugeṣu vasaṃte prāvṛṣi śaradi cetyarthaḥ*): or, the herbs that are born in the three seasons that come in one year, spring (summer), the rains (rainy season) and autumn (cold season): this is the other kind of meaning. To support this meaning the Bhāṣyakāra has cited this passage of the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa: या ओषधीः पूर्वा जाता देवेभ्यस्त्रियुगं पुरेत्यृतवो वै देवास्त्रेभ्य एतास्त्रिः पुरा जायंते वसंते प्रावृषि शरदि मनै नु बभ्रूणामहमिति सोमो वै बभ्रुः सोम्या ओषधयः औषधः पुरुषः शतं धामानीति यदिदग्ं शतायुः शतार्घः शतवीर्य एतानि हास्य तानि शतं धामानि सप्त चेति । य एवेमे सप्न शीर्षन्प्राणास्तानेतदाह । (*yā oṣadhīḥ pūrvā jātā devebhyastriyugaṃ puretyṛtavo vai devāstrebhya etāstriḥ purā jāyaṃte vasaṃte prāvṛṣi śaradi manai nu babhrūṇāmahamiti somo vai babhruḥ somyā oṣadhayaḥ auṣadhaḥ puruṣaḥ śataṃ dhāmānīti yadidagṃ śatāyuḥ śatārghaḥ śatavīrya etāni hāsya tāni śataṃ dhāmāni sapta ceti | ya eveme sapna śīrṣanprāṇāstānetadāha |*).

### Printed p. 73
According to this Śruti passage, त्रियुगं (*triyugaṃ*) means the three seasons spring, rains and autumn; in all these three seasons these herbs were produced by the gods. They are brown-hued like the Soma plant. The chief import is that these herbs destroy a man's diseases and so make him live up to a hundred years, make him endowed with many kinds of vigour and the like (strength, capacity and so on), and make him healthy, endowed with the seven kinds of vital breath (air). We have already explained the meaning of the word युग (*yuga*) earlier, that is, in the 72nd sūkta of the tenth maṇḍala.

For the sentence देवेभ्यस्त्रियुगं पुरा जाताः (*devebhyastriyugaṃ purā jātāḥ*) the Bhāṣyakāra has explained that these herbs were produced by the gods, that is, created, in the three yugas beginning with Kṛta or in the three seasons beginning with spring. But for the sentence देवेभ्यस्त्रियुगं पुरा जाताः (*devebhyastriyugaṃ purā jātāḥ*) Yāska has explained the meaning thus: the herbs were produced three yugas of time before the creation of the gods; that is, the herbs were produced first, and then, after the time of three yugas had passed, the gods were produced. This meaning appears to be correct and consistent. For the plant kingdom, which is the food of all living beings, must have been produced first; then the creation of living beings. Otherwise, if living beings were produced first, what would they eat as food? For this reason too, to say that the creation of the herbs and trees (the plant kingdom) took place long before the gods, that is, three yugas before, is consistent and reasonable.

Yāska's Nirukta for this reasoning is as follows: या ओषधयः पूर्वा जाता देवेभ्यस्त्रीणि युगानि पुरा मन्ये नु तद्वभ्रूणामहं बभ्रूवर्णानां हरणानां भरणानामिति वा । शतं धामानि सप्त च । धामानि त्रयाणि भवंति स्थानानि नामानि जन्मानीति । जन्मान्यत्राभिप्रेतानि सप्तशतं पुरुषस्य मर्मणां तेष्टेना दधतीति वा ॥ (*yā oṣadhayaḥ pūrvā jātā devebhyastrīṇi yugāni purā manye nu tadvabhrūṇāmahaṃ babhrūvarṇānāṃ haraṇānāṃ bharaṇānāmiti vā | śataṃ dhāmāni sapta ca | dhāmāni trayāṇi bhavaṃti sthānāni nāmāni janmānīti | janmānyatrābhipretāni saptaśataṃ puruṣasya marmaṇāṃ teṣṭenā dadhatīti vā ‖*).

In explaining this Nirukta it is necessary to take each phrase of this Ṛk separately and explain it.

या ओषधयः पूर्वा जाता देवेभ्यस्त्रीणि युगानि पुरा मन्ये (*yā oṣadhayaḥ pūrvā jātā devebhyastrīṇi yugāni purā manye*): the seer says here, "I have understood that the herbs, that is, the plant kingdom of small plants, creepers, vines and so on, were produced, that is, created, three yugas of time before the gods were created." Long before (three yugas before) gods, men or other animals are born on this earth, the plant kingdom that is their food must be produced. For no animals can live without food. The food first

### Printed p. 74

The Lord's rule is to prepare food first and only afterwards to create living beings. Since the gods came into being before men and animals, it is said here that the plants, or herbs, came into being even before those gods, that is, before everyone.

तद्भभूणामहं शतं धामानि सप्त च (*tadbhabhūṇāmahaṃ śataṃ dhāmāni sapta ca*)—For the word बभ्रूणां (*babhrūṇāṃ*) Yāska gives two meanings, हरणानां (*haraṇānāṃ*) or भरणानां (*bharaṇānāṃ*). As to हरणानां (*haraṇānāṃ*): ता हि क्षुदादि हरंति (*tā hi kṣudādi haraṃti*)—these herbs, in the form of food, remove the hunger of living beings. Or बभ्रूणां भरणानां (*babhrūṇāṃ bharaṇānāṃ*): these herbs, being of a brown (babhru) colour, are bearers, भर्त्रस्ताः सर्वस्यभूतग्रामस्य वा (*bhartrastāḥ sarvasyabhūtagrāmasya vā*)—they bear the whole multitude of living beings; that is, by supplying food they nourish them and make it possible for them to live. In the phrase शतं धामानि सप्त च (*śataṃ dhāmāni sapta ca*), for the word धामानि (*dhāmāni*) Yāska says: धामानि त्रयाणि भवंति स्थानानि नामानि जन्मानीति (*dhāmāni trayāṇi bhavaṃti sthānāni nāmāni janmānīti*). धामानि (*dhāmāni*) means places; they are three: places, names, births. That is, the herbs are born in a hundred and seven places or regions. शतं सप्त च (*śataṃ sapta ca*) may be the definite number one hundred and seven; or the word शत (*śata*) may be taken to mean "many": these herbs are born and grow in many places (earth, mountains, river banks and many other regions). Likewise the names of these herbs are a hundred and seven, or of many kinds. "Births" means that the manner of their birth or growth is also of many kinds: some are small, some large; some spread and grow as creepers; some grow slowly, some quickly, and so on. The meaning is that they are of many kinds in form, in quality and in manner of growth.

Or the phrase शतं धामानि सप्त च (*śataṃ dhāmāni sapta ca*) is also to be understood as Yāska says: जन्मान्यत्राभिप्रेतानि सप्तशतं पुरुषस्य मर्मणां तेष्टेना दधतीति ना (*janmānyatrābhipretāni saptaśataṃ puruṣasya marmaṇāṃ teṣṭenā dadhatīti nā*). Besides the variety in the origin and growth of the herbs, there is variety also in the manner of using them. By applying these herbs to the seven hundred vital or joint places of the human body and so treating it, the seer describes them as having "a hundred and seven" (here, seven hundred and seven), or many kinds of use in treatment and the like. **NOTE**

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> श॒तं वो॑ अंब॒ धामा॑नि स॒हस्र॑मु॒त वो॒ रुहः॑ ।  
> अधा॑ शतक्रत्वो यू॒यमि॒मं मे॑ अग॒दं कृ॑त ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 75

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> शतं । वः । अंब । धामानि । सहस्रं । उत । वः । रुहः ।  
> अध । शतक्रत्वः । यूयं । इमं । मे । अगदं । कृत ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अंब मातर ओषधयः वो युष्माकं धामानि स्थानानि जन्मानि वा शतमपरिमितानि । उतापि च वो युष्माकं रुहः प्ररोहः प्रोद्गमः सहस्रमपरिमितः ।
> अधापि च हे शतक्रत्वः शतकर्माणः यूयमिमं मे मां मदीयं ना जनमामयग्रस्तमगदं । गदो रोगः । तद्रहितं कृत । कुरुत ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O mothers, herbs! Your places, or births, are a hundred, that is, unlimited; and your growth, your sprouting forth, is a thousand, also unlimited. And, O you of a hundred works (शतक्रत्वः (*śatakratvaḥ*)), make this my person, or my kin, who is seized by disease, अगदं (*agadaṃ*), that is free from गद (*gada*), which is disease.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अंब (*aṃba*)—O herbs, who are of the form of mothers
- वः (*vaḥ*)—your
- धामानि (*dhāmāni*)—birth-places (or uses)
- शतं (*śataṃ*)—are unlimited
- उत (*uta*)—and
- वः (*vaḥ*)—your
- रुहः (*ruhaḥ*)—growth also
- सहस्रं (*sahasraṃ*)—is exceedingly great
- शतक्रत्वः (*śatakratvaḥ*)—O herbs of many works
- यूयं (*yūyaṃ*)—you
- मे (*me*)—my
- इमं (*imaṃ*)—these sons and others
- अगदं (*agadaṃ*)—free from disease
- कृत (*kṛta*)—make

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O herbs, who are of the form of mothers, your birth-places are unlimited. Your growth too is exceedingly great. O herbs, who are used in many works, make my sons and other people free from disease.

##### English translation
Mothers (of mankind), a hundred are your applications, a thousandfold is your growth; do you who fulfil a hundred functions make this my (people) free from disease.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

शतक्रत्वः (*śatakratvaḥ*)—शत (*śata*) means many, क्रतु (*kratu*) means work, deed. Hence शतक्रत्वः (*śatakratvaḥ*) means that these herbs are useful for many tasks. The meaning is that they are used in various works.

### Printed p. 76

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ओष॑धीः॒ प्रति॑ मोदध्वं॒ पुष्प॑वतीः प्र॒सूव॑रीः ।  
> अश्वा॑ इव स॒जित्व॑रीर्वी॒रुधः॑ पारयि॒ष्ण्वः॑ ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ओषधीः । प्रति । मोदध्वं । पुष्पवतीः । प्रसूवरीः ।  
> अश्वाः इव । सजित्वरीः । वीरुधः । पारयिष्णुः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे ओषधीरोषधयः प्रति मोदध्वं । इमं रुग्णं प्रति मुदिता हृष्टा
> भवत । कीदृश्यो यूयं । पुस्पवतीः पुष्पवत्यः प्रसूवरीः । प्रकर्षेण सूयंत
> उपभोगायेति प्रसवाः फलानि । तद्वत्यः किंचाश्वा इवाश्नुवाना हया इव
> सजित्वरीः सह रोगं जयंत्यः वीरुधो विरोहंत्यः पारयिष्णो रुग्णं पुरुषं
> पारयंत्यो रोगात् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O herbs, rejoice towards this sick man; be glad. What are you like? पुष्पवतीः (*puṣpavatīḥ*), flowered; प्रसूवरीः (*prasūvarīḥ*), that is, bearing fruits, which are "brought forth" (प्रसवाः (*prasavāḥ*)) for enjoyment, and possessing them. Moreover, like horses that pervade (अश्वा इव (*aśvā iva*)), सजित्वरीः (*sajitvarīḥ*), together conquering disease; वीरुधः (*vīrudhaḥ*), growing in many ways; पारयिष्णो (*pārayiṣṇo*), carrying the sick man across, out of disease.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ओषधीः (*oṣadhīḥ*)—O herbs
- पुष्पवतीः (*puṣpavatīḥ*)—you who are possessed of flowers
- प्रसूवरीः (*prasūvarīḥ*)—and abundantly fruitful
- अश्वाइव (*aśvāiva*)—like horses
- सजित्वरीः (*sajitvarīḥ*)—conquering disease
- वीरुधः (*vīrudhaḥ*)—having the growth of many branches
- पारयिष्ण्वः (*pārayiṣṇvaḥ*)—carrying all across out of disease
- प्रति मोदध्वं (*prati modadhvaṃ*)—be givers of joy

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O herbs, you who are possessed of flowers and abundantly fruitful, being like horses conquerors of disease, having the growth of many branches, and carrying all across out of disease, be givers of joy.

##### English translation
Rejoice, plants, bearing abundant flowers and fruit, triumphing together (over disease) like (victorious) horses, sprouting forth, bearing (men safe) beyond (disease).

### Printed p. 77

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

पुष्पवती प्रसूवरीः (*puṣpavatī prasūvarīḥ*)—herbs that bear flowers and bear fruits.

अश्वा इव सजित्वरीः (*aśvā iva sajitvarīḥ*)—As horses bring about victory in battles, so these herbs conquer, that is, remedy, diseases and other afflictions.

वीरुधः (*vīrudhaḥ*)—For the word वीरुध (*vīrudha*) Yāska gives the etymology: वीरुध ओषधयो भवंति विरोहणात्। वीरुधः पारयिष्णवः इत्यपि निगमो भवति । (*vīrudha oṣadhayo bhavaṃti virohaṇāt| vīrudhaḥ pārayiṣṇavaḥ ityapi nigamo bhavati |*) वीरुधः (*vīrudhaḥ*) means herbs. Because they are born and grow in the earth they are called वीरुधः (*vīrudhaḥ*), herbs; this is the explanation of the word. (Ni. 6-3). **NOTE**

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ओष॑धी॒रिति॑ मातर॒स्तद्वो॑ देवी॒रुप॑ब्रुवे ।  
> स॒नेय॒मश्वं॒ गां वास॑ आ॒त्मानं॒ तव॑ पूरुष ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ओषधीः । इति । मातरः । तत् । वः । देवीः । उप । ब्रुवे ।  
> सनेयं । अश्वं । गां । वासः । आत्मानं । तव । पूरुष ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे ओषधीरोषधयो देवीर्देव्यो द्योतनादिगुणका हे मातरो
> जनानां मातृभूताः । मातृवद्धितकारित्वान्मातृत्वोपचारः । अथवा मातर अरोग्यनिर्मात्रैः वो युष्माकं संबंधिनं भिषजं तद्वक्ष्यमाणमितीत्थमुप ब्रुवे ।
> उपब्रवीमि । किं तदिति चेत् उच्यते । ओषध्यर्थमहमश्वं गां पासोंशुकं किं
> बहुनात्मानमपि हे पुरुष चिकित्सक तव तुभ्यं सनेयं । ददामि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O herbs, divine (देवीः (*devīḥ*), having the quality of shining and the like), O mothers, being mothers of people; the designation "mother" is figurative, since they benefit as a mother does. Or else मातरः (*mātaraḥ*) means makers of health. I speak to you thus about your physician, what is to be said. If it is asked what that is: for the sake of the herbs I give to you, O man, O healer, a horse, a cow, a cloak, and, what need of more, even myself.
**NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देवीः (*devīḥ*)—divine in nature
- मातरः (*mātaraḥ*)—like mothers to people
- ओषधीः (*oṣadhīḥ*)—O herbs
- वः (*vaḥ*)—your greatness
- तत् इत् (*tat it*)—as will be said hereafter
- उप ब्रुवे (*upa bruve*)—I praise
- पुरुष (*puruṣa*)—O physician
- तव (*tava*)—to you
- अश्वं (*aśvaṃ*)—a horse
- गाः (*gāḥ*)—cows
- वासः (*vāsaḥ*)—garments
- आत्मानं (*ātmānaṃ*)—even my (service)
- सनेयं (*saneyaṃ*)—I offer

### Printed p. 78

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O herbs, divine in nature and like mothers to people, I praise your greatness as will be said hereafter. O physician, I offer to you a horse, cows, garments, and finally even my service.

##### English translation
Plants! thus I hail you, the mothers (of mankind). I will give to you, oh physician, a horse, a cow, a garment—even myself.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒श्व॒त्थे वो॑ नि॒षद॑नं प॒र्णे वो॑ वस॒तिष्कृ॒ता ।  
> गो॒भाज॒ इत्किला॑सथ॒ यत्स॒नव॑थ॒ पूरु॑षं ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अश्वत्थे । वः । निसदनं । पर्णे । वः । वसतिः । कृता ।  
> गोऽभाजः । इत् । किल । असथ । यत् । सनवथ । पुरुषं ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे ओषधिदेवताः वो युष्माकमश्वत्थे निषदनं नितरां वर्तनं । तथा वो
> युष्माकं पर्णे पलाशे वसतिर्निवासः कृता । तृतीयस्यामितो दिवि सोम आसीत्तं
> गायत्राहरत्तस्य पर्णमच्छिद्यत तत्पर्णोऽभवत् तत्पर्णस्य पर्णत्वमिति । तै. ब्रा.
> १-१-३-१० । ब्राह्मणात्पलाशस्य पर्णत्वप्रसिद्धिः । अश्वत्थपलाशयोर्यज्ञयोग्यत्वप्राधान्यापेक्षयोपादानं । किंच गोभाज इत्किल गवां भाजयित्र्य एवासथ ! भवथ
> खलु । यद्यदि सनवथ संभजद्वे पुरुषं तर्ह्येवं भवथेति ॥ वन षण संभक्तौ ।
> लेट्यडागमः ! व्यत्ययेनोप्रत्ययॆः । यद्वा! औत्सर्गिकः शष्टेति द्विविकरणता ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O herbal deities, your sitting-place is in the अश्वत्थ (*aśvattha*) (pipal), your constant abiding; and your dwelling is made in the पर्ण (*parṇa*), the पलाश (*palāśa*). He cites: तृतीयस्यामितो दिवि सोम आसीत्तं गायत्राहरत्तस्य पर्णमच्छिद्यत तत्पर्णोऽभवत् तत्पर्णस्य पर्णत्वमिति (*tṛtīyasyāmito divi soma āsīttaṃ gāyatrāharattasya parṇamacchidyata tatparṇo 'bhavat tatparṇasya parṇatvamiti*) (Tai. Brā. 1-1-3-10); from this Brāhmaṇa the name पर्ण (*parṇa*) for the palāśa is established. The अश्वत्थ (*aśvattha*) and the palāśa are mentioned because of their pre-eminent fitness for sacrifice. Further, गोभाज इत्किल (*gobhāja itkila*): you indeed become the givers of cows' share, if you favour the man (physician).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O herbal deities) वः (*vaḥ*)—your
- निषदनं (*niṣadanaṃ*)—abode
- अश्वत्थे (*aśvatthe*)—in the pipal tree
- वः (*vaḥ*)—your
- वसतिः (*vasatiḥ*)—dwelling-place
- पर्णॆ (*parṇè*)—in the palāśa
- कृता (*kṛtā*)—is fixed

### Printed p. 79

- यत् (*yat*)—when
- पुरुषं (*puruṣaṃ*)—to the physician who resorts to us
- सवनथ (*savanatha*)—you show favour
- गोभाज इत् किल (*gobhāja it kila*)—to him you are indeed givers of cows
- असथ (*asatha*)—you are

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O herbal deities, your abode is in the pipal tree and your dwelling in the palāśa (the Butea). When you favour the physician who resorts to you, then you are indeed givers of cows to him.

##### English translation
Your abode is in the Ashwatha, your dwelling is established in the Palasa, you are assuredly the distributors of cattle, insmuch as you bestow them on the physician.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यत्रौष॑धीः स॒मग्म॑ृत॒ राजा॑नः॒ समि॑ताविन ।  
> विप्रः॒ स उ॑च्यते भि॒षग्र॑क्षो॒हामि॑व॒ाचात॑नः ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत्र । ओषधीः । संअग्मत । राजानः । समितौ इव ।  
> विप्रः । सः । उच्यते । भिषक् । रक्षःहा । अमीवचातनः ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यत्र यस्मिन्देश ओषधीरोषधयः समग्मत संगच्छंते । राजानः समिताविव
> संग्रामे यथा संगता भवंति तद्वत् । तासां नानाविधानामोषधीनां संगमनं
> यस्मिन्देशेऽस्ति तत्र विप्रः प्राज्ञो ब्राह्मणो भिषगुच्यते रक्षोहा रक्षोहंता ।
> अमीवाचातनः । अमीवा व्याधिः । तस्य चातनश्चातयिता नाशयिता च भवति तदानीं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In whatever region the herbs come together, as kings gather in a battle, as they are assembled there; in whatever region there is such a meeting of the many kinds of herbs, there the learned Brāhmaṇa, the wise man, is called a physician: slayer of demons (रक्षोहा (*rakṣohā*)) and अमीवाचातनः (*amīvācātanaḥ*), अमीवा (*amīvā*) being disease, he becomes its driver-away and destroyer then.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- समितौ (*samitau*)—in battle
- राजान इव (*rājāna iva*)—as kings assembled
- यत्र (*yatra*)—in whatever region
- ओषधीः (*oṣadhīḥ*)—herbs
- समग्मत (*samagmata*)—are gathered together, there
- विप्रः (*vipraḥ*)—the wise man
- भिषक् (*bhiṣak*)—as a physician
- उच्यते (*ucyate*)—is called
- सः (*saḥ*)—he
- रक्षोहा (*rakṣohā*)—is the destroyer of demons
- अमीवचातनः (*amīvacātanaḥ*)—and the destroyer of disease

### Printed p. 80

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Where the plants are gathered together, like kings assembled in one place in battle, there the wise physician is so called. That man is the destroyer of demons and the destroyer of disease.

##### English translation
Where, plants, you are congregated like princes (assembled) in battle there the sage is designated a physician, the destroyer of evil spirits, the extirpator of disease.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ॒श्वा॒व॒तीं सो॑माव॒तीमू॒र्जयं॑ती॒मुदो॑जसं ।  
> आवि॑त्सि॒ सर्वा॒ ओष॑धीर॒स्मा अ॑रि॒ष्टता॑तये ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अश्ववतीं । सोमवतीं । ऊर्जयंतीं । उत्ओजसं ।  
> आ । अवित्सि । सर्वाः । ओषधीः । अस्मै । अरिष्टतातये ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अश्वावत्यादयः प्रधानभूता ओषधयश्चतस्रः । ताः सर्वा ओषधीरावित्सि ।
> आजाने । सौमीत्यर्थः । अस्मा अरिष्टतातये । आमुं रोगं विनाशयितुमित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Aśvāvatī and the rest are four principal plants. "I praise all those plants": आवित्सि (*āvitsi*) means "I praise" (आजाने (*ājāne*)), that is, I address them with praise. अस्मा अरिष्टतातये (*asmā ariṣṭatātaye*) means: in order to destroy this आमुं (*āmuṃ*) disease.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अश्वावतीं (*aśvāvatīṃ*)—that named Aśvāvatī
- सोमवतीं (*somavatīṃ*)—that bearing the name Somavatī
- ऊर्जयंतीं (*ūrjayaṃtīṃ*)—that named Ūrjayantī
- उदोजसं (*udojasaṃ*)—that known as Udojas
- सर्वा ओषधीः (*sarvā oṣadhīḥ*)—all (four) kinds of plants
- अस्मै अरिष्टतातये (*asmai ariṣṭatātaye*)—for the destruction of this disease
- आवित्सि (*āvitsi*)—I praise

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

I praise all (four kinds of) plants—that named Aśvāvatī, that named Somavatī, that named Ūrjayantī, and that known as Udojas—for the destruction of this disease.

### Printed p. 81

##### English translation
The Ashwavathi, the Somavathi, the Urjayanti, the Udojasa—all these plants I praise for the purpose of overcoming this disease.

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उच्चुष्मा॒ ओष॑धीनां॒ गावो॑ गो॒ष्ठादि॑वेरते ।  
> धनं॑ सनि॒ष्यंती॑नामा॒त्मानं॒ तव॑ पूरुष ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)
