"O Indra, what wonderful, desirable special wealth do you send to us (iṣaṇyasi, 'you impel'), knowing that it is to be sent in every way? What kind is it? One that extends widely, taking a broad form; one that is to be sung, praiseworthy. For what purpose? For our growth (vavṛdhadhyai). And further: when the strength of that Indra dawns, what gift comes to us?" ("comes to us" is to be supplied.) "The thunderbolt, destroyer of Vṛtra, of the obstructing evil, Tvaṣṭṛ fashioned (takṣat) and made to flow (apinvat)." The connection is "in the dawning of his strength." Alternatively: "O Indra, what is the gift of that thunderbolt which he fashioned for Indra?" This is to be construed so. Another mantra: "Tvaṣṭṛ fashioned for me an iron thunderbolt" (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-48-3).
**NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Indra) चिकित्वान् (*cikitvān*)—you who know our condition
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- पृथुग्मानं (*pṛthugmānaṃ*)—that has taken many forms
- वाश्रः (*vāśraḥ*)—praiseworthy
- चित्रं (*citraṃ*)—and attractive
- कं (*kaṃ*)—wealth
- ववृदध्यै (*vavṛdadhyai*)—for our growth
- इषण्यसि (*iṣaṇyasi*)—you supply
- तस्य (*tasya*)—of Indra
- शवसः (*śavasaḥ*)—of the strength
- व्युष्पौ (*vyuṣpau*)—at the time of its shining
- कत् दातु (*kat dātu*)—what kind of gift is obtained by us
- वृत्रतुरं (*vṛtraturaṃ*)—the destroyer of Vṛtra
- वज्रं (*vajraṃ*)—the thunderbolt weapon
- तक्षत् (*takṣat*)—he fashioned
- अपिन्वत् (*apinvat*)—he caused rain to fall

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, you who know our condition, you supply for our growth wealth that is of many kinds, praiseworthy and attractive. He fashions the thunderbolt weapon and causes rain to fall. At the time when its strength shines, what kind of gift do we obtain?

##### English translation
What marvellous (wealth), comprehensive and laudable, do you, Indra knowing (what is needed). bestow upon us for our advantage? What gift (has been made to us) upon the development of his strength? He fabricated the Vritra-slaying thunderbolt, and let the waters flow.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स हि द्यु॒ता वि॒द्युता॒ वेति॒ साम॑ पृ॒थुं योनि॑मसुर॒त्वा स॑साद।  
> स.सवी॑ळेभिः प्रसहा॒नो अ॑स्य॒ भ्रातु॒र्न ऋ॒ते स॒प्तथ॑स्य मा॒याः॥

### Printed p. 121

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । हि । द्युता । विद्युता । वेति । साम । पृथुं । योनिं । असुरत्वा । आ । ससाद ।  
> सः । सनीळेभिः । प्रसहानः । अस्य । भ्रातुः । न । ऋते । सप्तथस्य । मायाः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> स हि स खल्विंद्रो द्युता द्योतमानेन विद्युत्तैतन्नामकेनायुधेन युक्तः
> सन् साम स्तोत्रात्मकं यज्ञसंबंधि वेति । गच्छति । तथासुरत्वासुरत्वेन बलेन
> युक्तः सन् पृथुं विस्तीर्णं योनिं फलस्योत्पादकं यज्ञं ससाद । संगतो
> भवति । स इंद्रः सनीळीभिः । नीडं विमानं । सविमानैर्मरुद्धिर्युक्तः प्रसहानोऽभिभवन्भवति । तस्य सप्तथस्यादित्यानां धात्रादीनां मध्यॆ सप्तमस्येंद्रस्य भ्रातुर्भागैर्भक्तव्यस्य मायासुर्यृते यज्ञे न संभवतीति शेषः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"That Indra, endowed with the shining weapon named Vidyut ('lightning'), goes (veti) to the sacrificial sāma, which is of the nature of a hymn of praise. Likewise, endowed with the strength of asuratva, he has sat down in the wide yoni, the sacrifice that produces fruit; he comes to be in company with it. That Indra, with the sanīḷas (nīḍa meaning 'vimāna', a dwelling): accompanied by the Maruts who share his dwelling, he becomes the overcomer (prasahāna)." "Of him, the seventh among the Ādityas, Dhātṛ and the rest, Indra, the brother ... the māyā of the Asuras does not occur in the sacrifice" (to be supplied).
**NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- स हि (*sa hi*)—that same Indra
- द्युता (*dyutā*)—with the shining
- विद्युता (*vidyutā*)—weapon called lightning, united
- साम (*sāma*)—to the sacrificial region full of praise
- वेति (*veti*)—he goes
- आसुरत्वा (*āsuratvā*)—endowed with strength
- पृथुं (*pṛthuṃ*)—in the spacious
- योनिं (*yoniṃ*)—place of sacrifice
- ससाद (*sasāda*)—he sits
- सः (*saḥ*)—he
- सनीळेभिः (*sanīḷebhiḥ*)—with the Maruts who go with him
- प्रसहानः (*prasahānaḥ*)—he becomes possessed of strength to defeat enemies
- सप्तथस्य (*saptathasya*)—with Indra, the seventh among the Ādityas
- भ्रातुः (*bhrātuḥ*)—who takes part
- माया (*māyā*)—the Asura power
- ऋते (*ṛte*)—in the sacrifice
- न (*na*)—none exists

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, united with the shining weapon called lightning, goes to the sacrificial region full of praise. Endowed with strength he sits in the place of sacrifice. With the Maruts who go with him he becomes possessed of strength to defeat enemies. There can be no Asura power that takes part in the sacrifice together with Indra, the seventh among the sons of Aditi.

##### English translation
Armed with the bright lightning he repairs to the (scene of) adoration; endowed with might he has seated himself on the spacious place (of sacrifice). He (is) triumphant with his companions (the Maruts); the wiles of his brother do not (prevail) at the rite.

### Printed p. 122

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स वाजं॒ याताप॑दुष्पदा॒ यन्त्व॑र्षाता॒ परि॑ षदत्सनि॒ष्यन् ।  
> अ॒न॒र्वा यच्छ॒तदु॑रस्य॒ वेदो॒ घ्नइा॒श्नदे॑वा अ॒भि वर्प॑सा॒  
> भूत् ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । वाजं । याता । अपदुःपदा । यन् । स्वःसाता । परि । सदत् । सनिष्यन् ।  
> अनर्वा । यत् । शतदुरस्य । वेदः । घ्नन् । शिश्नदेवान् । अभि । वर्षसा । भूत्॥३॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वाजं शूरैर्गंतव्यं संग्रामं याता स इंद्रः ॥ न लोकाव्ययेति षष्ठीप्रतिषेधः ॥ अपदुष्पदापगतदुष्टपतनेन यन् गच्छन् सनिष्यंस्तत्र शत्रुधनानि
> संभक्तुमिच्छन् परि षदत् । परिषीदति । कुत्रेति उच्यते । स्वर्षाता स्वर्षातौ सर्वलाभोपेते संग्रामे । किंचानर्वा युद्धेऽप्रत्यृत इंद्रः शतदुरस्य शत्रुपुरस्यांतर्निहितं यद्वेदो धनमस्ति तद्धनं वर्षसावरकेण बलेनाभि भूत् । अभिभवति ।
> किं कुर्वन् । शिश्नदेवानब्रह्मचर्याइतद्वारेषु शत्रुपुरसंबंधिषु वर्तमानान्
> घ्नन् हिंसन् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

वाजं (*vājaṃ*): Indra, who goes to the battle (the place to which heroes must go). The sixth case is barred by the rule "na lokāvyaya…". अपदुष्पदा (*apaduṣpadā*): going with the fall of what is bad removed, i.e. with faultless gait. सनिष्यन् (*saniṣyan*): desiring to share out the enemy's wealth there, परि षदत् (*pari ṣadat*): he sits around. Where? In स्वर्षाता (*svarṣātā*), the battle in which every gain is obtained. Further, अनर्वा (*anarvā*): Indra, unopposed in battle. The wealth that lies hidden within शतदुरस्य (*śatadurasya*), the enemy's city, he overcomes by his encompassing strength (वर्षसा (*varṣasā*)). What is he doing? Striking down the शिश्नदेवान् (*śiśnadevān*), those who live in the enemy city's gates in a state of non-celibacy.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वाजं (*vājaṃ*)—to the battle
- याता (*yātā*)—who goes
- सः (*saḥ*)—Indra
- अपदुष्पदा (*apaduṣpadā*)—with faultless gait
- यन् (*yan*)—going
- सनिष्यन् (*saniṣyan*)—desiring to obtain the enemy's wealth
- स्वर्षाता (*svarṣātā*)—in battle
- परिषदत् (*pariṣadat*)—goes and sits
- अनर्वा (*anarvā*)—Indra, unopposed in battle
- शिश्नदेवान् (*śiśnadevān*)—the Asuras who worship the male organ
- घ्नन् (*ghnan*)—destroying
- शतदुरस्य (*śatadurasya*)—in the enemy cities with hundreds of gates
- यत् वेदः (*yat vedaḥ*)—whatever wealth is hidden, that
- वर्पसा (*varpasā*)—by his pervading power
- अभिभूत् (*abhibhūt*)—brings under his control

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, who goes to battle, goes with faultless gait, desiring to obtain the enemy's wealth, and takes part in the battle. Invincible in battle, destroying the Asuras who worship the male organ, he takes into his control by his pervading power the wealth hidden in the enemy cities with hundreds of gates.

### Printed p. 123

##### English translation
Going to the battle, marching with easy gait, desiring the spoil, he set himself to the acquisition of all (wealth). Invincible, destroying the Phallus-worshippers, he won by his prowess whatever wealth (was concealed in the city) with the hundred gates.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स य॒हूव्यो॒३॒॑ऽवनी॒र्गोर्ष्टर्वा जु॑होति प्रध॒न्या॑सु॒ सस्रिः॑ ।  
> अ॒पादो॒ यत्र॒ युज्या॑सोर॒था द्रो॒ण्य॑श्वास॒ ईर॑ते घृ॒तं  
> वाः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । यह्व्यः । अवनीः । गोषु । अर्वा । आ । जुहोति । प्रधन्यासु । सस्रिः ।  
> अपादः । यत्र । युज्यासः । अरथाः । द्रोणि अश्वासः । ईरते । घृतं । नारिति वाः ।

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> स इंद्रोऽर्वा मेघेष्वभिगंता सस्रिः सरणकुशलः प्रधन्यासु प्रकृष्टधननिमित्तासु गोषु भूमिषु यह्वः । महन्नामैतत् । महतीरवनीः । अवंतीत्यवनय
> आपः । ता आ जुहोति । आक्षिपति । यत्र यासु भूमिष्वपादः पादरहिता अरथा
> रथवर्जिताः । पादरहिताः केचन रथेन गच्छंति । केनापि शून्या द्रोण्यश्वासो
> द्रुतव्यापना युज्यासो युज्या इंद्रस्य सख्यो नद्यो वार्वारकं धृतमुदकमीरते प्रेरयंति तत्राजुहोति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

That Indra is अर्वा (*arvā*), one who goes among the clouds, सस्रिः (*sasriḥ*), skilled in moving, and यह्वः (*yahvaḥ*) in the गोषु (*goṣu*), the lands which are प्रधन्यासु (*pradhanyāsu*), productive of excellent wealth. यह्व (*yahva*) is a name for "great". He आ जुहोति (*ā juhoti*), pours in, the great अवनीः (*avanīḥ*), the waters (so called because they protect). In those lands where the अपादः (*apādaḥ*) (footless), अरथा (*arathā*) (chariotless) rivers, Indra's friends, द्रोण्यश्वासः (*droṇyaśvāsaḥ*) (swift-moving) and युज्यासः (*yujyāsaḥ*), send forth the घृतं वाः (*ghṛtaṃ vāḥ*), the water, there he pours in. Some footless beings go by chariot, but these are devoid of any such.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत्र (*yatra*)—in which regions
- अपादः (*apādaḥ*)—footless
- आरथाः (*ārathāḥ*)—chariotless
- द्रोण्यश्वासः (*droṇyaśvāsaḥ*)—swift in motion
- युज्यासः (*yujyāsaḥ*)—rivers friendly to Indra
- वाः (*vāḥ*)—flowing
- धृतं (*dhṛtaṃ*)—water
- ईरते (*īrate*)—send flowing, such
- गोषु (*goṣu*)—in the regions
- अर्वा (*arvā*)—moving among the clouds
- सस्रिः (*sasriḥ*)—of excellent movement
- सः (*saḥ*)—Indra
- प्रधन्यासु (*pradhanyāsu*)—for the attainment of excellent wealth
- यज्ञ (*yajña*)—great
- अवनीः (*avanīḥ*)—the waters
- आ जुहोति (*ā juhoti*)—supplies

### Printed p. 124

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

In those regions where the rivers, unable to walk or travel by chariot, yet swift and friendly to Indra, send forth waters, Indra, who moves among the clouds, supplies great waters for the attainment of excellent wealth.

##### English translation
The Victorious Indra, Coming amongst the clouds quickly moving, offers the abundant waters on the rich (plains) where his associates (the rivers) footless, and without conveyances, having pitchers for horses pour out water like ghi.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स रु॒द्रेभि॒रश॑स्तवार॒ ऋभ्वा॑ हि॒त्वी गय॑मा॒रेअ॑वद्य॒ आगा॑त् ।  
> व॒म्रस्य॑ मन्ये मिथु॒ना विव्॑री॒ अन्न॑म॒भीत्या॑रोदयनृषा॒यन् ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । रुद्रेभिः । अशस्तवारः । ऋभ्वा । हित्वी । गयं । आरेऽअवद्यः । आ । अगात् ।  
> वम्रस्य । मन्ये । मिथुना । विवव्री इति विवव्री । अन्नं । अभिइत्य । अरोदयत् ।  
> मुषायन् ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> स इंद्रो रुद्रेभी रुद्रपुत्रैर्मरुद्भिः सहित आगात् । अगच्छतु । कीदृशः ।
> अशस्तवारः स्तोतृभिरप्रार्थितधनः । स्वयमेव प्रदातेत्यर्थः । तथर्भ्या महान्
> गयं हित्वी हित्वा स्वस्थानं परित्यज्यागात् । आगच्छतु । तत्र संबंधः । आरेअवद्यो
> दूणेरितगर्ष्यः । किंच वम्रस्यॆतन्नामकस्यर्षेर्मम मिथुना मिथुनौ मातापितरौ
> विषद्वी विगतज्वरौ मन्ये । अवगच्छामि । अयं वनोऽन्नं शत्रुसंबंध्यभीत्याभिप्राप्न मुतायन्नुष्टन्नरोदयत् । रोदयति ॥ छंदसि शायजपीत्यहावपि मुषेः
> श्वः शायजादेशः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

May that Indra come with the Rudras, the Maruts, sons of Rudra. Of what kind? अशस्तवारः (*aśastavāraḥ*): one whose wealth is not begged for by the praisers, i.e. he gives of himself. Likewise ऋभ्वा (*ṛbhvā*): great. Having left his own abode (गयं हित्वी (*gayaṃ hitvī*)), let him come. आरेअवद्यः (*āreavadyaḥ*): one whose blame is far off. Further, I, the seer named Vamra, think (मन्ये (*manye*)) that my two parents (मिथुना (*mithunā*)) are free from fever. This Indra, having approached (अभीत्य (*abhītya*)) the enemy's food, stole it and made it cry out (अरोदयत् (*arodayat*)). **NOTE**

### Printed p. 125

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अशस्तवारः (*aśastavāraḥ*)—one who gives wealth unasked
- यभ्वा (*yabhvā*)—the great one
- आरेअवद्यः (*āreavadyaḥ*)—who stays far from blame
- सः (*saḥ*)—Indra
- गयं हित्वी (*gayaṃ hitvī*)—leaving his house
- रुद्रेभिः (*rudrebhiḥ*)—with the Maruts, sons of Rudra
- आगात् (*āgāt*)—may he come
- वम्रस्य (*vamrasya*)—of me, named Vamra
- मिथुना (*mithunā*)—the parents
- विवप्री (*vivaprī*)—as being free from fever
- मन्ये (*manye*)—I understand
- अन्नं (*annaṃ*)—the food belonging to the enemy
- अभीत्य (*abhītya*)—having obtained
- मुषायन् (*muṣāyan*)—stealing it
- अरोदयत् (*arodayat*)—makes a loud noise

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May Indra, who gives wealth to devotees unasked, the great one, free from fault, leave his house and come to us with the Maruts, the sons of Rudra. I, named Vamra, understand that my parents are free from fever. Indra, having carried off the enemy's food, makes a loud noise with delight.

##### English translation
May he, our unsolicited benefactor, the mighty one, from whom blame is far removed, come with the Rudras, having quitted his dwelling. I think of the two (parents) of Vamra, who are free from fever. Having obtained (the enemy's) food, he called aloud whilst stealing it.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स इद्दासं॑ तुवी॒रवं॒ पति॒र्दन्न॑ळ॒क्षं त्रि॑शी॒र्षाणं॑ दमन्यत् ।  
> अ॒स्य त्रि॒तो न्वोज॑सा वृधा॒नो वि॒पा व॑रा॒हमयो॑अग्रया हन् ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । इत् । दासं । तुविरवं । पतिः । दन् । षट्ऽअक्षं । त्रिशीर्षाणं । दमन्यत् ।  
> अस्य । त्रितः । नु । ओजसा । वृधानः । विपा । वराहं । अयःऽग्रया । हन्निति हन् ॥६॥

### Printed p. 126

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> स इत् स एवेंद्रः पतिः सर्वस्य स्वामी दासमुपक्षपयितारं तुवीरवं
> बहुशब्दं संग्रामे भयंकरं शब्दं कुर्वाणं वृत्रं दन् दमयन् षळक्षमक्षिषट्कोपेतं त्रिशीर्षाणं त्रिशिरस्कं त्वष्टुः पुत्रं विश्वरूपं दमन्यत् । दमितं प्रहर्तुमैच्छत् । अवधीदित्यर्थः । यद्वा! दमयतीति दमनः ॥ नंद्यादित्वाल्लुः ॥ स
> इवाचरति ॥ उपमानादाजारे । पा. ३-१-१० । इति क्यच्यंतलोपश्चांदसः । ततो
> लजि बहुलं छंदसीत्यडभावः ॥ किंच त्रित एतन्नामा महर्षिरस्येंद्रसौजसा बलेन
> वृधानो वर्धमानो विपांगुल्या ! कीदृश्या ! आयोअग्रयायोवत्कठिननखया
> वराहं वराहारमुदकवंतं मेघं व्यहन् । विहतवान् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

That very Indra, lord (पतिः (*patiḥ*)) of all, subduing (दन् (*dan*)) the दासं (*dāsaṃ*), the harasser, the तुवीरवं (*tuvīravaṃ*), the loud-shouting one who makes a terrible noise in battle, namely Vṛtra, wished (दमन्यत् (*damanyat*)) to strike down the six-eyed (षळक्षं (*ṣaḷakṣaṃ*)), three-headed (त्रिशीर्षाणं (*triśīrṣāṇaṃ*)) son of Tvaṣṭṛ, Viśvarūpa; that is, he slew him. Or else, दमन (*damana*) means one who subdues. Further, the great seer named Trita, growing (वृधानः (*vṛdhānaḥ*)) by this Indra's strength, with his finger (विपा (*vipā*)), which has an iron-hard nail (आयोअग्रया (*āyoagrayā*)), struck apart the वराहं (*varāhaṃ*), the cloud full of water.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पतिः (*patiḥ*)—lord of all
- स इत् (*sa it*)—that same Indra
- दासं (*dāsaṃ*)—the harasser
- तुवीरवं (*tuvīravaṃ*)—the one who shouts loudly, Vṛtra
- दन् (*dan*)—subduing
- षळक्षं (*ṣaḷakṣaṃ*)—the six-eyed
- त्रिशीर्षाणं (*triśīrṣāṇaṃ*)—the three-headed Viśvarūpa
- दमन्यत् (*damanyat*)—wished to destroy
- त्रितः (*tritaḥ*)—Indra, who strode over the three worlds
- अस्य (*asya*)—his own
- ओजसा (*ojasā*)—by strength
- वृधानः (*vṛdhānaḥ*)—growing greatly
- अयोअग्रया (*ayoagrayā*)—with nails like steel
- विपा (*vipā*)—with the finger
- वराहं (*varāhaṃ*)—the cloud full of water
- हन् (*han*)—pierced

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, lord of all, subduing Vṛtra the harasser and loud shouter, wished to destroy the six-eyed, three-headed Viśvarūpa. Indra, who strides over the three worlds, growing greatly by his strength, pierced with his finger, with nails like steel, the water-laden cloud.

##### English translation
The sovereign Indra attacking him overcame the loud-shouting, six-eyed, three-headed Dasa, and Trita, invigorated by his strength, smote the water-laden (cloud) with his iron-tipped finger.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स द्रुह्व॑णे॒ मनु॑ष्य ऊर्ध्वस्था॒न आ सा॑विषदर्शस्था॒नाय॒ शरुं॑ ।  
> स नृत॑मो॒ नहु॑षो॒ऽस्मत्सुजा॑तः॒ पुरो॑ऽभिन॒दर्ह॑न्दस्यु॒हत्ये॑॥७॥

### Printed p. 127

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । द्रुह्वणे । मनुषे । ऊर्ध्वसानः । आ । साविषत् । अर्शसानाय । शरुं ।  
> सः । नृतमः । नहुषः । अस्मत् । सुजातः । पुरः । अभिनत् । अर्हन् । दस्युहत्ये॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> स इंद्रो द्रुह्मणे द्रुह्मणाय दृढं शत्रुभिर्ह्वातव्याय मनुषे मनुष्याय योद्ध्रे स्वभक्तायोर्ध्वसानः । ऊर्ध्व उच्चृतः शौर्यादिभिर्गुणैरधिकः
> सन् स्मृति शत्रूणामंतं करोतीतूर्ध्वसः । स इवाचरन्॥ ऊर्ध्वसशब्दादाचारार्थे
> क्विबंताच्छानच् ॥ अर्शसानाय शत्रूणां हिंसित्रे शरुं हिंसकमायुधमा साविषत् ।
> अभिमुख्येन प्रसौति । यच्छति । यद्वा! द्रुह्मणे मनुषे द्रोग्धव्याय मनुष्यायार्शसानाय स्वभक्तहिंसित्रे तस्य वधार्थमूर्ध्वसानः सञ्करुमा साविषत् । वज्रं
> प्रेरयति । स एवेंद्रो नहुषो मनुष्यान्नृतमो मनुष्याणां नेतृतमः
> संग्रामे शूराणां गमयितृतमः । यद्वा । नृतमो नहुषो बंधकश्च । अस्मदस्मदर्थं सुजातः सुष्टु प्रादुर्भूतोऽर्हन् पूज्यः सन् दस्युहत्ये । दस्यव उपक्षपयितारः शत्रवः । तेषां हत्यं हननं यस्मिन् तादृशे संग्रामे पुरः शत्रूणां
> शरीराण्यभिनत् । भिन्नवान् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

That Indra, for द्रुह्वणे (*druhvaṇe*), one firmly pulled about by enemies, मनुषे (*manuṣe*), a man, a warrior, his own devotee, being ऊर्ध्वसानः (*ūrdhvasānaḥ*) (raised up, superior through heroism and other qualities; or one who puts an end to enemies; he acts like that), sends forth शरुं (*śaruṃ*), the weapon that harms, for अर्शसानाय (*arśasānāya*), the destruction of enemies; he thrusts it forward against them. Or else: for the man to be wronged, who is अर्शसान (*arśasāna*), a harmer of his own devotee, Indra, rising up for his slaying, hurls the thunderbolt. That same Indra is नहुषः (*nahuṣaḥ*), नृतमः (*nṛtamaḥ*), the best leader of men, who most makes heroes advance in battle. Or else नृतमो नहुषो (*nṛtamo nahuṣo*) is also "binder". Born well for us (अस्मत् सुजातः (*asmat sujātaḥ*)), worthy of worship (अर्हन् (*arhan*)), in the battle where the दस्यव (*dasyava*), the destroying enemies, are slain, he split (अभिनत् (*abhinat*)) the bodies (पुरः (*puraḥ*)) of the enemies.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सः (*saḥ*)—Indra
- द्रुह्वणे (*druhvaṇe*)—for one firmly pulled about by enemies
- मनुषे (*manuṣe*)—for his devotee
- ऊर्ध्वसानः (*ūrdhvasānaḥ*)—possessing exceedingly great qualities such as heroism
- अर्शसानाय (*arśasānāya*)—for the destruction of enemies
- शरुं (*śaruṃ*)—the harming weapon
- आ साविषत् (*ā sāviṣat*)—employs
- नहुषः (*nahuṣaḥ*)—more than human beings
- नृतमः (*nṛtamaḥ*)—the most excellent guide
- सः (*saḥ*)—Indra
- अस्मत् (*asmat*)—for us
- सुजातः (*sujātaḥ*)—of excellent birth
- अर्हन् (*arhan*)—worthy of worship
- दस्युहत्ये (*dasyuhatye*)—in the battle that destroys demons
- पुरः (*puraḥ*)—the bodies of the enemies
- अभिनत् (*abhinat*)—splits

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

For his devotee, who is pulled about firmly by enemies, Indra, possessed of the greatest qualities such as heroism, employs a harming weapon for the destruction of the enemies. Indra, the most excellent guide, more than human beings, of excellent birth, worthy of worship, splits for us the bodies of the enemies in the battle that destroys demons.

### Printed p. 128

##### English translation
Raising himself on high, he launched his arrow against the malignant aggressive foe. He, the chief leader of men, manifested favourably for our (aid), laudable, slew the bodies (of enemies) in the slaughter of the Dasyus.

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सो अ॒भ्रियो॒ न यव॑स उद॒न्यन्पया॑य गा॒तुं वि॒दन्नो॑  ॒
> अस्मे ।  
> उप॒ यत्सीद॒दिंदुं॒ शरी॑रैः श्ये॒नोऽयो॑पाष्टि र्हंति॒  ॑
> दस्यून् ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । अभ्रियः । न । यवसे । उदन्यत् । क्षयाय । गातुं । विदत् । नः । अस्मे इति ।  
> उप । यत् । सीदत् । इंदुं । शरीरैः । श्येनः । अयःऽअपाष्टिः । हंति । दस्यून् ॥८॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> स इंद्रोऽभ्रियो न । न भ्राजंतेऽपो बिभ्रतीति वाभ्राणि मेघाः । तेषां
> संघोऽभ्रियः । स इव यवसे गवादिभक्षणसाधनाय तृणायोदन्यन्नुदकं
> दातुमिच्छन् । तथा क्षयाय गमनाय निवासाय वास्मे अस्माकं गातुं
> मार्गं विदल्लंभयन् । न इति पूरणः । तादृशः सन् यद्यदेंदुं सोमं
> शरीरैः स्वशरीरावयवैरंगैरुप सीदत् उपगच्छति ॥ यच्छब्दयोगादनिघातः ॥
> तदानीं श्येनः । सादृश्यप्रधानोऽयं निर्देशः । श्येनसदृशः श्येनवच्छंसनीयगमनोऽयोपाष्टिः । अपयष्टिर्व्याप्तिर्यस्य स प्रदेशोऽपाष्टिः पाष्णि ९ । अयोमयोऽपाष्टिः पाष्णिर्यस्य सः । दस्यूइत्यून्हंति । हिनस्ति ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

That Indra is like an *abhriya*. Clouds (*abhra*) are so called either because they do not shine or because they bear water, and their multitude is the *abhriya*. Like it, he desires to give water to the *yavasa*, the grass that serves as fodder for cattle and the like. Likewise he finds for us (*asme*) the way (*gātu*) for going to, or dwelling in, our home (*kṣaya*). The word "na" is only a filler. Being such, when (*yat*) he approaches Soma (*indu*) with his own limbs (*śarīraiḥ*), then he is "श्येन (*śyena*)". This is a statement based on likeness: he is like a hawk, with a praiseworthy manner of going. He is *ayopāṣṭi*: his heel (*pāṣṇi*) is made of iron. **NOTE**. He smites (*hanti*) the Dasyus, that is, he destroys them.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यवसे (*yavase*)—for the grass that is food for our cows
- उदन्यत् (*udanyat*)—desiring to give water
- अभ्रियोन (*abhriyona*)—like a mass of clouds
- सः (*saḥ*)—Indra
- अस्मे (*asme*)—our
- क्षयाय (*kṣayāya*)—for coming to the home
- गातुं (*gātuṃ*)—the path
- विदत् (*vidat*)—he has known
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- इंदुं (*iṃduṃ*)—

### Printed p. 129
- इंदुं (*iṃduṃ*)—(continued) Soma
- शरीरैः (*śarīraiḥ*)—with his own body
- उपसीदत् (*upasīdat*)—approaches
- श्येनः (*śyenaḥ*)—one who goes like a hawk
- अयोपाष्टिः (*ayopāṣṭiḥ*)—one whose heel is made of iron (firm like iron), namely Indra
- दस्यून् (*dasyūn*)—the enemies
- हंति (*haṃti*)—destroys

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Like a mass of clouds that wishes to give water to the grass fit to be eaten by our cows, Indra knows the way to come to our home. When he approaches the Soma with his own body, then Indra, who goes like a hawk and whose heel is firm like iron, destroys the enemies.

##### English translation
Like the aggregated cloud desiring to pour water on the pasturage, he found the way to our dwelling : when he approaches the Soma with his limbs like a hawk with heel of iron he smites the Dasyus.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स व्राध॑तः शवसा॒नेभि॑रस्य॒ कुत्सा॑य॒ शुष्णं॑ कृ॒पणे॒ परा॑दात् ।  
> अ॒यं क॒विम॑नयच्छ॒स्यमा॑न॒मत्कं॒ यो अ॑स्य॒ सनि॑तो॒त नृ॒णां ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । व्राधतः । शवसानेभिः । अस्य । कुत्साय । शुष्णं । कृपणे । परा । अदात् ।  
> अयं । कविं । अनयत् । शस्यमानं । अत्कं । यः । अस्य । सनिता । उत । नृणां ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> स इंद्रो व्राधतः । महन्नामैतत् । महतोऽपि शत्रूञ्यवसानेभिर्बलमाचरद्भिरायुधैरस्य । अस्यतु ॥ असु क्षेपणे । व्यत्ययेन मध्यमः ॥ कुत्सायैतन्नामकाय कृपणे स्तोत्रे । कृपतिः स्तुतिकर्मा । शुष्णं शोषकमेतन्नामकमसुरं परादात् । पराभूय खंडितवान् । कुत्साय शुष्मशुषं नि बर्हीः ।
> ऋ. ४-१६-१२ । इति मंत्रांतरं । किंचायमिंद्रः कविमुशनसं । कविरिति पितृनाम्म
> पुत्रस्यापि व्यवहार उपचारात् । शस्यमानं स्तुवंतमनयत् । वशं प्रापयत्तस्य
> 9

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

That Indra overthrows (*asyati*, from the root *asu* in the sense of throwing; the middle-person ending stands by *vyatyaya*) even the mighty enemies (*vrādhataḥ* is a name for "mighty") with his powerful weapons (*śavasānebhiḥ*). For Kutsa, so named, a praiser (*kṛpaṇa*; *kṛpati* means the act of praising), he defeated (*parādāt*) and broke the asura named Śuṣṇa, the "drier". Another mantra says: कुत्साय शुष्मशुषं नि बर्हीः । (*kutsāya śuṣmaśuṣaṃ ni barhīḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-16-12). Further, this Indra brought Kavi (Uśanas) under his control. *Kavi* is a name for the father, but is used also of the son by transference. He was praising Indra (*śasyamānam*), and Indra made his opponent submit to him.

### Printed p. 130

> विरोधिनं । यद्वा । कविं भार्गवमेव स्तोतृभिः शस्यमानं स्ववशमनयत् । यः
> कविरस्येंद्रस्यात्मं रूपं सनिता संभक्ता भवति ॥ न लोकाव्ययेति षष्ठिप्रतिषेधः ॥
> उतापि च नृणां वृष्टादिनेत्यणामिंद्रानुचराणां मरुतां यः कविः सनिता ।
> तमनयदिति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Or else: Indra brought under his own control Kavi Bhārgava himself, who was being praised by the singers. This Kavi is the one who becomes devoted (*sanitā*) to the form (*atkam*) of this Indra. And also of the Maruts, Indra's followers, "of the men" (*nṛṇām*): whichever Kavi is devoted to them, him he led.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सः (*saḥ*)—Indra
- व्राधतः (*vrādhataḥ*)—even the mighty enemies
- शवसानेभिः (*śavasānebhiḥ*)—with mighty weapons
- अस्य (*asya*)—he throws down
- कृपणे (*kṛpaṇe*)—who is a praiser
- कुत्साय (*kutsāya*)—for Kutsa
- शुष्णं (*śuṣṇaṃ*)—the "drier", the asura Śuṣṇa
- परादात् (*parādāt*)—he split apart
- यः (*yaḥ*)—which Uśanas
- अस्य (*asya*)—of Indra
- उत (*uta*)—and
- नृणां (*nṛṇāṃ*)—of the Maruts
- अत्कं (*atkaṃ*)—the form
- सनिता (*sanitā*)—honoured with praises
- शस्यमानं (*śasyamānaṃ*)—who is a praiser
- कविं (*kaviṃ*)—to Uśanas
- अयं (*ayaṃ*)—Indra
- अनयत् (*anayat*)—made the enemies come under his control

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra throws down even mighty enemies with his own powerful weapons. For Kutsa, the praiser, he had the drying asura Śuṣṇa split apart. For that praiser Uśanas, who with his praises refined the form of Indra and of the Maruts, his followers, Indra brought all enemies under control.

##### English translation
Let him overthrow the migthy with powerful (weapons) ; he destroyed Shushna for the sake of the liberal Kutsa ; he humiliated Kavi, who praised him, who was the giver of form to Indra and his men.

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒यं द॑श॒स्यन्नर्ये॑भिरस्य द॒स्मो दे॒वेभि॒र्वरु॑णो॒ न मा॒या।  
> अ॒यं क॒नीन॑ ऋतु॒पा अ॑वे॒द्यमि॑माता॒ररुं॒ यश्चतु॑ष्पात्॥१०॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> २ ३८ ' वचन् ' नर्येभिः । अस्य ! दस्यः । देवेभिः । वरुणः । न । मायी ।  
> अयं । कनीनः । ययुपाः । अवेदि । अमिमात । अररुं । यः । चतुःपात् ॥ १० ॥

### Printed p. 131

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अयमिंद्रो दशस्यन् स्तोतृभ्यो धनं प्रयच्छन् । दशस्यतिर्दानकर्मा ।
> नर्येभिर्नैन्रहितैर्मरुद्धिरस्य अस्यति ॥ छांदसस्तिपो लोपः । यद्वा । व्यत्ययेन लोण्मध्यमः ॥ तथा देवेभिर्द्योतमानैः स्वतेजोभिर्दस्मो दर्शनीयो
> वरुणो न वरुण इव । वरुणस्तमोवारक आदित्यो वरुण एव वा । स इव मायि
> मायानान् । तथायं कनीनः कमनीय ऋतुपा ऋत् पातावेदि । अज्ञायि ।
> तथाररुमसुरमेतन्ना मानममिमीत । अमिनात् । योऽररुश्चतुष्पात् पादचतुष्टयोपेतः ॥ मीज् हिंसायां । लजि बहुलं छंदसीति विकरणस्य श्लुः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Indra, bestowing (*daśasyan*) wealth on the praisers (*daśasyati* means the act of giving), with the Maruts, friendly to men (*naryebhiḥ*), "throws" (*asyati*; the ending is dropped in the Veda, or else the imperative middle-person stands by *vyatyaya*). **NOTE**. Likewise, with his shining (*devebhiḥ*) splendours he is *dasma*, lovely to behold, like Varuṇa. Varuṇa is the Āditya who keeps off darkness, or Varuṇa himself. Like him he is *māyin* (possessed of māyā). **NOTE**. Likewise this Indra, *kanīna* (lovely) and *ṛtupā* (protector of the seasons), was known (*avedi*). And he destroyed (*amimīta*, root *mīj* in the sense of harming) the asura named Araru, who is four-footed, having four feet.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अयं (*ayaṃ*)—this Indra
- दशस्यन् (*daśasyan*)—desiring to give wealth to the praisers
- नरैभिः (*naraibhiḥ*)—with the Maruts
- अस्य (*asya*)—comes
- देवेभिः (*devebhiḥ*)—with his own radiances
- दस्मः (*dasmaḥ*)—being lovely to behold
- वरुणो न (*varuṇo na*)—like Varuṇa
- मायिी (*māyiī*)—endowed with power
- अयं (*ayaṃ*)—Indra
- कनीनः (*kanīnaḥ*)—having an attractive form
- ऋतुपाः (*ṛtupāḥ*)—as one who protects in each season
- अवेदि (*avedi*)—is known
- यः (*yaḥ*)—which Araru
- चतुष्पात् (*catuṣpāt*)—who is four-footed
- अररुं (*araruṃ*)—the asura called Araru
- अमिमात (*amimāta*)—has harmed

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

This Indra comes with the Maruts, wishing to give wealth to the praisers. Beautiful to behold with his radiances, powerful like Varuṇa, with an attractive form, he is known by people as the one who protects in each season. Indra has destroyed the four-footed asura called Araru.

##### English translation
Bountiful (to his worshippers) with his (Maruts) friendly to men wonderful with his brilliancies, having magical power liko Varuna, he was known as desirable, protecting the seasons, he destroyed Araru, who was four-footed.

#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒स्य स्तोमे॑भिरौशि॒ज ऋ॒जिश्वा॑ व्र॒जं द॑रयद्वृष॒भेण॒ पिप्रोः॑ ।  
> सुत्वा॒ यद्य॑ज॒तो दी॒दय॒द्द्गीः पुर॑ इया॒नो अ॒भि वर्प॑सा॒  
> भूत् ॥ ११ ॥

### Printed p. 132

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अस्य । स्तोमेभिः । औशिजः । ऋजिश्वा । व्रजं । दरयत् । वृषभेण । पिप्रोः ।  
> सुत्वा । यत् । यजतः । दीदयत् । गीः । पुरः । इयानः । अभि । वर्पसा । भूत्॥११॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अस्येंद्रस्य स्तोमेभिः स्तोत्रैरौशिज उशिजः पुत्र ऋजिश्वैतन्नामा वृषभेण वज्रेण वृषभेण वा युक्तं व्रजं गोष्ठं पिप्रोरेतन्नामकस्यासुरस्य
> संबंधिनं तेनापहृत्य पालितं दरयत् । अदारयत् । यद्यदा सुत्वा सोमस्य
> सोता ॥ सुयजोर्ज्वनिबिति वनिष् । ह्रस्वस्य पितीति. तुक् ॥ यजतो यष्टौशिजो
> गीः स्तुतिवाचो दीदयत् दीपितवान् । तथा यद्यदेयानो गच्छन् पुरः
> शत्रुपुराणि । यद्वा । पुरः पुर इव संघीभूतानि पापानि । अभि भूत् । अभिभवति ।
> केन साधनेन । वर्षसा । रूपनामैतत् । रूपेणेंद्रानुगृहीतेन । यदैवमकरोत् तदा व्रजं दरयदिति संबंधः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

With the praises (*stomebhiḥ*) of this Indra, Rijiśvan, son (*aujija*) of Uśija, split (*darayat*) the cow-pen (*vraja*), belonging to the asura named Pipru, which was joined with the bull (*vṛṣabha*), that is, with the thunderbolt or with a bull, and which had been taken away and guarded by him. When (*yat*) the presser of Soma (*sutvā*) and sacrificer (*yajataḥ*), Aujija, made his praise-words (*gīḥ*) shine, and likewise as he went forward (*iyānaḥ*) he overcame (*abhi bhūt*) the cities (*puraḥ*) of the enemy. Or else: the sins gathered like cities. By what means? By his *varṣas* (**NOTE**), a name for form: by the form favoured by Indra. The construction is: when he did so, then he split the cow-pen.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अस्य (*asya*)—of Indra
- स्तोमेभिः (*stomebhiḥ*)—with praises
- औशिजः (*auśijaḥ*)—the son of Uśija
- यजिश्वा (*yajiśvā*)—Yajiśvan **NOTE**
- वृषभेण (*vṛṣabheṇa*)—with the powerful thunderbolt
- पिप्रोः (*piproḥ*)—of Pipru
- व्रजं (*vrajaṃ*)—the cow-pen
- दरयत् (*darayat*)—split apart
- सुत्वा (*sutvā*)—the presser of Soma
- यजतः (*yajataḥ*)—the sacrificer, Yajiśvan
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- गीः (*gīḥ*)—the praise-words
- दीदयत् (*dīdayat*)—he made shine, then
- इयानः (*iyānaḥ*)—going forward
- पुरः (*puraḥ*)—the cities of the enemies
- वर्षसा (*varṣasā*)—with his own form favoured by Indra
- अभिभूत् (*abhibhūt*)—he brought under control

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Rijiśvan, the son of Uśija, praised Indra and, with the help of his thunderbolt, split the cow-pen of Pipru. When Rijiśvan, the presser of Soma and sacrificer, praised Indra with words of praise, then, going forward with his own form favoured by Indra, he brought the cities of the enemies under his control.

##### English translation
Rijishwan, the son of Ushija with Indra's praises shattered the cow-pen of Pipru with the thunderbolt; when having expressed (the Soma), the

### Printed p. 133
venerable sage recited his praises, (Indra) proceeding against the cities (of the enemy) triumphed with his body.

#### Ṛk 12

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ए॒वा म॒हो अ॑सुर व॒क्षथा॑य वम्र॒कः प॒ड्भिरुप॑ सर्प॒दिंद्रं॑ ।  
> स इ॑या॒नः क॑रति स्व॒स्तिम॑स्मा॒ इष॒मूर्जं॑ सुक्षि॒तं विश्व॒माभाः॑ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> एव । महः । असुर । वक्षथाय । वम्रकः । पट्भिः । उप । सर्पत् । इंद्रं ।  
> सः । इयानः । करति । स्वस्तिं । अस्मै । इषं । ऊर्जं । सुक्षितिं । विश्वं । आ । अभारि.  
> त्यभाः ॥ १२ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अनया स्तुतिमुपसंहृत्याभिमतं वम्रकः पार्थयते । हे असुर बलवन्निंद्र
> त्वामेमैवमुक्तप्रकारेण: महो महतो हविषः स्तोत्रस्य ना वक्षथाय वहनाय
> महतः स्वर्गादेः प्रापणाय वा पड्भिः पादैरुप सर्पत् । उपागमत् । स इयानउपागम्यमानः सॆन् करति करोतु स्वस्तिमविनाशमस्मै वम्रकाय । तथेषमन्नमूर्जं रसं सुक्षितिं सुनिवासं सम्यग्विश्वं सर्वमाभाः । आहरतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Concluding the praise with this verse, Vamraka makes his request. O mighty Asura, Indra: in the manner just stated (*eva*), Vamraka approaches you with his feet, to carry (*vakṣathāya*) a great (*mahaḥ*) oblation or praise, or to obtain great things such as heaven. May Indra, being approached (*iyānaḥ*), bring about (*karati*) welfare (*svasti*), freedom from destruction, for this Vamraka. And may he bring (*ābhāḥ*) food (*iṣam*), sap (*ūrjam*), a good dwelling (*sukṣitim*) and everything (*viśvam*).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- असुर (*asura*)—O mighty Indra
- एव (*eva*)—in this way
- वम्रकः (*vamrakaḥ*)—Vamraka
- महः (*mahaḥ*)—of the great oblation
- वक्षथाय (*vakṣathāya*)—for obtaining
- पद्भिः (*padbhiḥ*)—with his feet
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- उप सर्पत् (*upa sarpat*)—approaches
- सः (*saḥ*)—Indra
- इयानः (*iyānaḥ*)—having been approached
- अस्मै (*asmai*)—for this Vamraka
- स्वस्तिं (*svastiṃ*)—welfare
- करति (*karati*)—may he bring about
- विश्वं (*viśvaṃ*)—all
- इषं (*iṣaṃ*)—food
- ऊर्जं (*ūrjaṃ*)—sap
- सुक्षितिं (*sukṣitiṃ*)—an excellent dwelling-place
- आभाः (*ābhāḥ*)—may he provide

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O mighty Indra, in this way Vamraka approaches you, the lord, with his feet, to obtain a great oblation. May Indra, thus approached, bring welfare to Vamraka. May he provide him with all food, sap and an excellent dwelling-place.

### Printed p. 134
In this manner, O mighty (Indra), Vamraka approached Indra on foot, to bring an ample oblation. May he, being approached, grant us prosperity, may he bring us food, drink, a secure dwelling and all (good things).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk Sāyaṇa has explained the word पद्भिः (*padbhiḥ*) as पादैः (*pādaiḥ*), "with the feet". But Yāska says:
पद्भिः । वम्रकः पद्भिरुपसर्पदिंद्रं । पानैरिति ना स्पाशनैरिति वा (स्पशनैरिति वा (*padbhiḥ | vamrakaḥ padbhirupasarpadiṃdraṃ | pānairiti nā spāśanairiti vā (spaśanairiti vā*) (Ni. 5-3).
That is, the sage approaches Indra with drinks such as Soma juice, or with praises; the meaning is that he prays to Indra and invites him. Since Indra is a deity not visible to the eye, to say that he approaches Indra on foot does not seem quite reasonable. Yāska's view, that he prays to and invites Indra with Soma juice fit for drinking or with praises, is the more acceptable.

## Sūkta 100 (Maṇḍala 10)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> नवमेऽनुवाके त्रयोदश सूक्तानि । तत्रेंद्र दृह्येति द्वादशर्चं प्रथमं
> सूक्तं वंदनपुत्रस्य दुवस्कोरार्षं वैश्वदेवं । द्वादशी त्रिष्टुप् । शिष्टा जगत्यः ।
> तथा चानुक्रांतं । इंद्र दुवस्युर्वांदनो वैश्वदेवं त्वंत्या त्रिष्टुबिति ॥ गतो
> विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The ninth anuvāka contains thirteen sūktas. Of them the first, beginning इंद्र दृह्य (*iṃdra dṛhya*), has twelve Ṛks; its seer is Duvasyu, son of Vandana, and its deity the Viśvedevas. The twelfth verse is in Triṣṭubh, the rest in Jagatī. He cites the Anukramaṇī: इंद्र दुवस्युर्वांदनो वैश्वदेवं त्वंत्या त्रिष्टुबिति (*iṃdra duvasyurvāṃdano vaiśvadevaṃ tvaṃtyā triṣṭubiti*). The viniyoga has already been stated.

##### Anuvāda

The tenth Maṇḍala's ninth anuvāka contains thirteen sūktas. Among them this sūkta beginning "Indra dṛhya" is the first; it has twelve Ṛks. Its seer is Duvasyu, son of the sage Vandana; its deity is the Viśvedevas. The twelfth Ṛk is in the Triṣṭubh metre, the remaining Ṛks in Jagatī. The Anukramaṇī says इंद्र दुवस्युर्वांदनो वैश्वदैवं त्वंत्या त्रिष्टुप् (*iṃdra duvasyurvāṃdano vaiśvadaivaṃ tvaṃtyā triṣṭup*). The viniyoga was stated earlier.

### Printed p. 135
Maṇḍala 10; Anuvāka 9; Sūkta 100. Aṣṭaka 8; Adhyāya 5; Varga 16, 17. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 12. Seer: Duvasyu Vāndana. Deity: Viśvedevas. Metre: 1–11 Jagatī; 12 Triṣṭubh.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्र॒ दृह्य॑ मघव॒न्ता व॒दिद्भु॒ज इ॒ह स्तु॒तः सु॑त॒पा बो॑धि नो  ॒
> वृधे ।  
> दे॒वेभि॑र्नः सवि॒ता प्राव॑तु श्रु॒तमा स॒र्वता॑ति॒मदि॑तिं वृणीमहे॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इंद्र । दृह्य । मघवन् । श्वावत् । इत् । भुजे । इह । स्तुतः । सुतपाः । बोधि ।  
> नः । वृधे ।  
> देवेभिः । नः । सविता । प्र । अवतु । श्रुतं । आ । सर्वतातिं । अदितिं । वृणीमहे ॥१॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र मघवन्थनवन् त्वं त्वावदित् त्वत्सदृशमेव शत्रुबलं दृह्य मारय ।
> किमर्थ० । भुजेऽस्माकं भोगाय । तदर्थमिहास्मिन्यज्ञे स्तुतः सन् सुतपाः
> सुतस्याभिषुतस्य सोमस्य पाता बोधि । बुध्यस्व! भवेत्यर्थः । किमर्थ० । नोऽस्माकं
> वृधे वर्धनाय । किंच देवेभिर्देवैः सह नोऽस्माकं श्रुतं विश्रुतं यज्ञं सविता
> सर्वस्य स्वस्वकर्मसु प्रेरको देवः प्रावतु । प्ररक्षतु । किंच सर्वतातिं ॥ स्वार्थिकस्तातिल् ॥ सर्वा० सर्वात्मिकां । यद्वा । सवर्ण कायंतेऽस्यामिति सर्तातिः ॥
> छांदसो दीर्घः ॥ तादृशीमदितिमखंडनीयां देवमातरमा वृणीमहे ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, Maghavan (wealthy one), destroy the enemy's strength that is like your own, for our enjoyment. Being praised here in this sacrifice, be the drinker of the pressed Soma, for our increase." Further: may Savitṛ, the god who impels all to their own works, together with the gods protect our renowned sacrifice. And we long for सर्वतातिं (*sarvatātiṃ*) (the affix tāti is svārthika), the all-embracing; or else, because all are desired in her, सर्तातिः (*sartātiḥ*) with a Vedic lengthening; such an Aditi, the indivisible mother of the gods, we long for.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मघवन् (*maghavan*)—wealthy
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- त्वावदित् (*tvāvadit*)—an enemy as mighty as you
- भुजे (*bhuje*)—for our happiness
- दृह्य (*dṛhya*)—destroy
- इह (*iha*)—in this sacrifice
- स्तुतः (*stutaḥ*)—you who are praised
- सुतर्पा (*sutarpā*)—as protector of Soma
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- वृधे (*vṛdhe*)—for growth
- बोधि (*bodhi*)—know (be mindful)
- सविता (*savitā*)—Savitṛ
- देवेभिः (*devebhiḥ*)—with the other gods
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- श्रुतं (*śrutaṃ*)—renowned sacrifice
- प्रावतु (*prāvatu*)—may he protect
- सर्वतातिं (*sarvatātiṃ*)—all-pervading
- अदितिं (*aditiṃ*)—Aditi
- आ वृणीमहे (*ā vṛṇīmahe*)—we respectfully desire and pray to

### Printed p. 136

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O wealthy Indra, destroy for our happiness the enemy who is as mighty as you. Praised in this sacrifice, be mindful, as protector of Soma, for our growth. May Savitṛ, together with the other gods, protect our renowned sacrifice. We pray to and call upon the all-pervading Aditi.

##### English translation
Consume, affluent Indra, (the foe that is) like you (in might); praised on this occasion, do you who are the drinker of the libation be favourable to our advancement. May Savitri, with the gods, defend our sacrifice; we long for the universal Aditi.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> भरा॑य॒ सु भ॑रत भा॒गमृ॒त्तियं॒ प्र वा॒यवे॑ शुचि॒पे क्रं॒ददि॑ष्टये ।  
> गौ॒रस्य॒ यः पय॑सः प्री॒तिमा॑न॒श आ स॒र्वता॑ति॒मदि॑तिं वृणीमहे ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> भराय । सु । भरत । भागं । ऋत्वियं । प्र । वायवे । शुचिपे । क्रंदत् ऽइष्टये ।  
> गौरस्य । यः । पयसः । प्रीतिं । आनशे । आ । सर्वतातिं । अदितिं । वृणीमहे ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> भराय संग्रामकारिणे सर्वेषां पोषकाय वेंद्रायर्त्वियं काले
> जातं प्राप्तकालं भागं सु भरत । सुष्टु संपादयत हे ऋत्विजः । तथा शुचिपे
> शुद्धस्य सोमस्य पात्रे क्रंददिष्टये शब्दितगमनाय । वायोः शीघ्रगमने हि शब्दः
> प्रत्यक्षः । तादृशाय वायवे देवाय प्र भरत भागमिति शेषः । यो देवो गौरस्य
> गौरवर्णस्य पशोः पयसः पीतिं पानमानशे प्राप्नोति तस्मै वायवे । शिष्टमुक्तं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O priests, bring well the seasonable share, the portion that has come in due time, to Bhara, the battle-doer and nourisher of all, that is Indra. Likewise to Vāyu, the drinker (शुचिपे (*śucipe*)) of the pure Soma in the cup, क्रंददिष्टये (*kraṃdadiṣṭaye*), whose going is noisy; for the sound of Vāyu's swift going is plain to the eye. Bring the share to such a god Vāyu, who obtains the drinking of the milk of a white-coloured animal." The rest is as already said.

### Printed p. 137

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- भराय (*bharāya*)—to Indra, nourisher of all
- ऋत्वियं (*ṛtviyaṃ*)—suited to each season
- भागं (*bhāgaṃ*)—the share of the sacrifice
- सु (*su*)—abundantly
- भरत (*bharata*)—procure and offer
- यः (*yaḥ*)—which Vāyu
- गौरस्य (*gaurasya*)—of the white cow
- पयसः (*payasaḥ*)—of the milk
- पितिं (*pitiṃ*)—the drinking
- आनशे (*ānaśe*)—obtains, such a one
- शुचिपे (*śucipe*)—the drinker of pure Soma
- देवायॆ (*devāyè*)—the divine one
- क्रंददिष्टये (*kraṃdadiṣṭaye*)—whose going makes a noise
- वायवे (*vāyave*)—to Vāyu
- प्र (भरत) (*pra (bharata)*)—offer the share of the sacrifice
- सर्वतातिं (*sarvatātiṃ*)—the all-pervading
- अदितिं (*aditiṃ*)—Aditi
- आ वृणीमहे (*ā vṛṇīmahe*)—we desire and call

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Offer abundantly to Indra, nourisher of all, the sacrificial share suited to each season. To Vāyu, who desires the drinking of the milk of a white cow, the drinker of pure Soma, divine, whose going makes a noise, offer the sacrificial share. We desire and call the all-pervading Aditi.

##### English translation
Offer to (Indra) the cherisher (all) the share suited to the season; (offer it) to Vayu, the drinker of the pure (Soma), who clamours as he travels, who obtains a draught of white milk; we long for the universal Aditi.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ नो॑ दे॒वः स॑वि॒ता सा॑विष॒द्वय॑ ऋजूय॒ते यज॑मानाय  ॒
> सुन्वते ।  
> यथा॑ दे॒वान्प्र॑ति॒भूषे॑म पाक॒वदा स॒र्वता॑ति॒मदि॑तिं वृणीमहे ॥ ३ ॥

### Printed p. 138

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । नः । देवः । सविता । साविषत् । वयः । ऋजुऽयते । यजमानाय । सुन्वते ।  
> यथा । देवान् । प्रतिऽभूषेम । पाकvत् । आ । सर्वऽतातिं । अदितिं । वृणीमहे॥३॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सविता सर्वस्य प्रेरकः सूर्यो नोऽस्माकं संबंधिन ऋजूयत ऋजुकामाय सुन्वतेऽभिषवं कुर्वते यजमानाय वयोऽन्नं पाकवत् पाकोपेतं ।
> व्यवहितमप्येतदत्र संबध्यते । आ साविषत् । अभिमुख्येन प्रसौति ॥ सौतेर्ण्यंताल्लेटि
> रूपं ॥ यथा येन प्रकारेण देवान्प्रतिभूषेम प्रतिभवेम ॥ भवतेर्लिजि सिष्यप् ।
> द्विविकरणता ॥ यद्वा ॥ भूष अलंकारे । भौवादिकः ॥ भूषयेमेत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Savitṛ, the impeller of all, the Sun, is to bring वयः (*vayaḥ*), food, पाकवत् (*pākavat*), cooked, to our sacrificer who desires uprightness and presses Soma; though separated in the verse, this is construed here. आ साविषत् (*ā sāviṣat*): he impels toward us (the form is a leṭ from sū with the causative). यथा (*yathā*): in such manner that we may प्रतिभूषेम (*pratibhūṣema*), come to be in favour with the gods. Or else, from भूष (*bhūṣa*), to adorn, of the bhū class: "that we may adorn".

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यथा (*yathā*)—in what manner
- देवान् (*devān*)—the gods
- प्रतिभूषेम (*pratibhūṣema*)—as we adorn (or become worthy of their grace)
- देवाः (*devāḥ*)—the divine
- सविता (*savitā*)—god Savitṛ
- ऋजूयते (*ṛjūyate*)—of upright conduct
- सुन्वते (*sunvate*)—who presses Soma
- नः (*naḥ*)—belonging to us
- यजमानाय (*yajamānāya*)—to the sacrificer
- पाकवत् (*pākavat*)—cooked (adorned with juices and so on)
- वयः (*vayaḥ*)—food
- आ साविषत् (*ā sāviṣat*)—may he supply
- सर्वतातिं (*sarvatātiṃ*)—the all-embracing
- अदितिं (*aditiṃ*)—Aditi
- आ वृणीमहे (*ā vṛṇīmahe*)—we desire and pray to

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

In the manner that we become worthy of the gods' favour, may the divine Savitṛ supply cooked food, adorned with juices and so on (prepared), to our sacrificer, who is of upright conduct and presses Soma. We desire and pray to the all-embracing Aditi.

##### English translation
May the divine Savitri generate well-dressed food for our sincere yajamana as he offers the libation, so that we may be in good favour with the gods; we long for the universal Aditi.

### Printed p. 139
#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्रो॑ अ॒स्मे सु॒मना॑ अस्तु वि॒श्वहा॒ राजा॒ सोमः॑ सुवि॒तस्याध्ये॑तु नः ।  
> यथा॑यथा मि॒त्रधि॑तानि संद॒धुरा स॒र्वता॑ति॒मदि॑तिं वृणीमहे ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इंद्रः । अस्मे इति । सुमनाः । अस्तु । विश्वहा । राजा । सोमः । सुवितस्य । अधि ।  
> एतु । नः ।  
> यथायथा । मित्रधितानि । संदधुः । आ । सर्वतातिं । अदितिं । वृणीमहे ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इंद्रो देवोऽस्मे अस्माकं सुमनाः सुमनस्कोऽनुग्रहचेता अस्तु । भवतु ।
> विश्वहा सर्वेष्टप्यहःसु । राजा सोमश्च नोऽस्माकं सुवितस्य सुवितं स्तोत्रमध्येतु
> अधिगच्छतु । यथायथा येन येन प्रकारेणास्माकं मित्रधितानि मित्रनिहितानि धनानि
> संदधुः तथाधिगच्छतु सोमः । तथेंद्रोऽपि सुमना अस्तित्वि समन्वयः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

May the god Indra be well-minded toward us, of gracious mind, on all days. And King Soma also: may he अध्येतु (*adhyetu*), take up, our सुवित (*suvita*), praise. In whatever manner मित्रधितानि (*mitradhitāni*), wealth laid up by friends, they bestow upon us, so may Soma take up our praise; so also may Indra be well-minded: this is the construction.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यथायथा (*yathāyathā*)—in whatever manner (the gods)
- मित्रधितानि (*mitradhitāni*)—wealth beneficial like friends
- संदधुः (*saṃdadhuḥ*)—they provide
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- अस्मे (*asme*)—toward us
- विश्वहा (*viśvahā*)—on all days
- सुमनाः (*sumanāḥ*)—of gracious mind
- अस्तु (*astu*)—let him be
- राजा (*rājā*)—the lord
- सोमः (*somaḥ*)—Soma
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- सुवितस्य (*suvitasya*)—praise
- अध्येतु (*adhyetu*)—let him accept
- सर्वतातिं (*sarvatātiṃ*)—the all-embracing
- अदितिं (*aditiṃ*)—Aditi
- आ वृणीमहे (*ā vṛṇīmahe*)—we desire

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

In the manner that Indra and Soma bestow on us wealth beneficial like friends, so may Indra be of gracious mind toward us on all days. May Soma, the lord, accept our praise. We desire the all-embracing Aditi.

### Printed p. 140
#### Ṛk 4 (continued)

##### English translation
May Indra be favourably disposed towards us every day, may the royal Soma accept our praise, so that they may bestow upon us friendly trea-sures; we long for the universal Aditi.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्र॑ उ॒क्तेन॒ शव॑सा॒ परु॑र्दधे॒ बृह॑स्पते प्रतरी॒तास्यायु॑षः ।  
> य॒ज्ञो मनुः॒ प्रम॑तिर्नः पि॒ता हि क॒मा स॒र्वता॑ति॒मदि॑तिं  
> वृणीमहे ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इंद्रः । उक्तेन । शवसा । परुः । दधे । बृहस्पते । प्रतरीता । असि । आयुषः ।  
> यज्ञः । मनुः । प्रमतिः । नः । पिता । हि । कं । अ । सर्वतातिं । अदितिं । वृणीमहे ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अयमिंद्र उक्तेन शवसा स्तुत्येन बलेन परुः पर्व यज्ञियमस्मदीयं
> वा दधे । धारयति । हे बृहस्पते आयुषो ममायुष्यस्य प्रतरीता प्रवर्धयितासि।
> भव । तथा यज्ञो मनुर्मंता प्रमतिः प्रकृष्टा मतिर्यस्य स नः पिता पालकः सन्
> कं सुखं प्रयच्छतु । यद्वा! यज्ञो मंत्रो बुद्धिश्च प्रत्येकं सुखं प्रयच्छतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Indra, by his praised strength (उक्तेन शवसा (*uktena śavasā*) = स्तुत्येन बलेन (*stutyena balena*)), holds (दधे (*dadhe*) = धारयति (*dhārayati*)) the joint (परुः (*paruḥ*) = पर्व (*parva*)), either the sacrificial joint or ours. O Bṛhaspati, you are the one who prolongs, that is, increases, my life; so be it. Likewise let the sacrifice, Manu the thinker, and Pramati (he whose mind is excellent), being our protector, grant happiness. Or else: let the sacrifice, the mantra and the intellect each grant happiness.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- उक्तेन (*uktena*)—with praiseworthy
- शवसा (*śavasā*)—strength
- परुः (*paruḥ*)—all my limbs
- दधे (*dadhe*)—has supported
- बृहस्पते (*bṛhaspate*)—O Bṛhaspati
- आयुषः (*āyuṣaḥ*)—of my life
- प्रतरीता (*pratarītā*)—as the one who increases
- असि (*asi*)—be
- यज्ञः (*yajñaḥ*)—the sacrifice
- मनुः (*manuḥ*)—Manu
- प्रमतिः (*pramatiḥ*)—the excellent intellect
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- पिता (*pitā*)—being protectors
- कं (*kaṃ*)—let them grant happiness
- सर्वतातिं (*sarvatātiṃ*)—the universal
- अदितिं (*aditiṃ*)—Aditi
- आ वृणीमहे (*ā vṛṇīmahe*)—we pray and invoke

### Printed p. 141

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra has filled all my limbs with strength by his praiseworthy might. O Bṛhaspati, be the one who increases my life. May the sacrifice, Manu and the excellent intellect, being our protectors, grant us happiness. We pray to and invoke Aditi, who is the all-embracing one.

##### English translation
By his laudable strength Indra supports my limb; you, Brihaspati, are the prolonger of my life. May the sacrifice, the sage Manu, (being) our protector, grant us happiness. We long for the Universal Aditi.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्र॑स्य॒ नु सुकृ॑तं॒ दैन्यं॒ सहो॒ग्निगृ॒हे ज॑रि॒ता मेधि॑रः क॒विः।  
> य॒ज्ञश्च॑ भूद्वि॒दथे॒ चारु॒रंत॑म॒ आ स॒र्वता॑ति॒मदि॑तिं वृणीमहे ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इंद्रस्य । नु । सुकृतं । दैव्यं । सहः । अग्निः । गृहे । जरिता । मेधिरः । कविः ।  
> यज्ञः । च । भूत् । विदथे । चारुः । अंतमः । आ । सर्वतातिं । अदितिं । वृणीमहे॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> दैव्यं देवानां हितं सहो बलं यदस्ति तदिंद्रस्य सुकृतं । इंद्रेण खलु
> सुष्टु संपादितं । यद्वा । यत्सुकृतं दैव्यं सहो बलं मरुत्संज्ञकमस्ति तदिंद्रस्य
> नु । इंद्रस्य संबंधि खलु । अथवा । इंद्रस्य नु संबंधि सुकृतं दैव्यं सहो
> देवार्हं बलमग्निर्गृहेऽस्मदीये यागगृहे वर्तत इति शेषः । इंद्रस्य संबंधी
> सुष्टु संपादितो देवानां बलभूतोऽग्निर्गार्हपत्यः सॆन् वर्तत इत्यर्थः । स
> चाग्निर्जरिता देवानां स्तोता मेधिरः । मेधो यज्ञो हविर्वा । तद्वान् कविः
> क्रांतप्रज्ञो यज्ञो यष्टव्यश्च भूत् । भवति । कुत्र । विदथे यज्ञे । किंचॆ
> सोऽग्निश्चारुश्चरणेयोऽंतनोऽस्माकमंतिकतमश्च ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The divine strength (दैव्यं (*daivyaṃ*), beneficial to the gods, सहः (*sahaḥ*) = strength) that exists is Indra's, well made (सुकृतं (*sukṛtaṃ*)), that is, well produced by Indra. Or else: the well-made divine strength called Marut is Indra's, belonging to Indra. Or else: the well-made divine strength of Indra, fit for the gods, namely Agni, is present in our house, that is, in our sacrificial house (the verb to be supplied is "is present"). The sense is that Agni Gārhapatya, well produced by Indra and being the strength of the gods, is present. That Agni is the praiser (जरिता (*jaritā*)) of the gods and मेधिरः (*medhiraḥ*): मेध (*medha*) is the sacrifice or the oblation, and he possesses it. He is कविः (*kaviḥ*), of far-reaching wisdom, and has become (भूत् (*bhūt*)) the sacrifice, to be worshipped. Where? In the विदथे (*vidathe*), the sacrifice. Moreover that Agni is चारुः (*cāruḥ*), glossed *caraṇeyaḥ*, and अंतमः (*aṃtamaḥ*), glossed *aṃtanaḥ* and "nearest to us". **NOTE**

### Printed p. 142

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- दैव्यं (*daivyaṃ*)—beneficial to the gods
- इंद्रस्य (*iṃdrasya*)—of Indra
- सहः (*sahaḥ*)—the strength
- सुकृतं (*sukṛtaṃ*)—is excellently obtained
- जरिता (*jaritā*)—the praiser of the gods
- मेधिरः (*medhiraḥ*)—the wise one
- कविः (*kaviḥ*)—the sagacious one
- यज्ञः (*yajñaḥ*)—the one worthy of worship **NOTE**
- चारुः (*cāruḥ*)—the one of attractive form
- अंतमः (*aṃtamaḥ*)—the one nearest to us
- अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—Agni
- गृहे (*gṛhe*)—in our house
- विदथे (*vidathe*)—in the sacrificial place
- भूत् (*bhūt*)—has been established
- सर्वतातिं (*sarvatātiṃ*)—the universal
- अदितिं (*aditiṃ*)—Aditi
- आ वृणीमहे (*ā vṛṇīmahe*)—I respectfully long for and invoke

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The strength of Indra, beneficial to the gods, is excellently obtained. Agni, the praiser of the gods, the wise, the sagacious, the one worthy of worship, of attractive form and nearest to us, is established in our house, in the sacrificial place. We respectfully long for and invoke Aditi, who is the all-embracing one.

##### English translation
The divine force of Indra is well constructed; Agni (abides) in ou dwelling, the praiser of the gods, the reciever of oblations, the sage, and is worthy of sacrifice at the altar, beautiful, and most nigh (to us). We long for the universal Aditi.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> न वो॒ गुहा॑ चक्रम॒ भूरि॑ दुष्कृ॒तं नाविष्ट्यं॑ वसवो देव॒हेळ॑नं॥  
> माकि॑र्नो देवा॒ अनृ॑तस्य॒ वर्ष॑स॒ आ स॒र्वता॑ति॒मदि॑तिंवृणीमहे ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> न । वः । गुहा । चक्रम । भूरि । दुःकृतं । न । अवित्यं । वसवः । देवहेळनं ।  
> माकिः । नः । देवाः । अनृतस्य । वर्पसः । आ । सर्वतातिं । अदितिं । वृणीमहे ॥ ७ ॥

### Printed p. 143

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे देवाः वो युष्माकं गुहा गुहायां प्रच्छन्ने देशे भूरि प्रभूतं
> दुष्कृतं पापं । द्रोहमित्यर्थः । न चक्रम । तथाविष्ट्यमाविः संभूतं देवहेळनं देवानां क्रोधनं तन्निमित्तं द्रोहं न चक्रम हे वसवो वासका देवाः ।
> किंच हे देवाः नोऽस्माकमनृतस्य वर्पसो रूपस्य प्राप्तिर्माकिः । मा भूत् ।
> इतः परं मानुषं रूपं मा भूदित्यर्थः । शिष्टमुक्तं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O gods, we have not done abundant (भूरि (*bhūri*)) evil (दुष्कृतं (*duṣkṛtaṃ*)), that is, treachery, toward you in a hidden place (गुहा (*guhā*)). Likewise, O Vasus, dwelling-givers among the gods, we have not done any treachery that is manifest and causes the anger of the gods (देवहेळनं (*devaheḷanaṃ*)). Moreover, O gods, let there be no (माकिः (*mākiḥ*) = मा भूत् (*mā bhūt*)) attainment for us of an unreal (अनृतस्य (*anṛtasya*)) form (वर्पसः (*varpasaḥ*) = रूपस्य (*rūpasya*)); the sense is that from now on there should be no human form. The rest has been explained.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वसवः (*vasavaḥ*)—givers of wealth
- देवाः (*devāḥ*)—O gods
- वः (*vaḥ*)—in regard to you
- गुहा (*guhā*)—in secret
- भूरि (*bhūri*)—abundant
- दुष्कृतं (*duṣkṛtaṃ*)—sin
- न चक्रम (*na cakrama*)—we have not committed
- देवहेळनं (*devaheḷanaṃ*)—so as to incur the anger of the gods
- आविष्ट्यं (*āviṣṭyaṃ*)—openly also
- न (*na*)—we do no wrong at all
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- अनृतस्य (*anṛtasya*)—of an unreal nature
- वर्पसः (*varpasaḥ*)—of a form
- माकिः (*mākiḥ*)—let there be no attainment
- सर्वतातिं (*sarvatātiṃ*)—the universal
- अदितिं (*aditiṃ*)—Aditi
- आ वृणीमहे (*ā vṛṇīmahe*)—we respectfully long for and invoke

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O gods, givers of wealth, we have not done any hidden wrong to you in abundance. We have not done any wrong openly either, so as to incur your anger. May the attainment of an unreal form not befall us. We respectfully pray to and invoke Aditi, who is the all-embracing one.

##### English translation
We have not done any great evil in secret from you, nor any open
(action) causing the anger of the gods O you givers of wealth; let not, O
gods, (the possession) of an unreal form be our (lot). We long for the univer-
sal Aditi.

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अपामी॑वानां सवि॒ता सा॑विष॒न्य१॒॑ १ ग्वरी॑य॒ इदस॑ सेधं॒त्वद्र॑यः ॥  
> ग्रावा॒ यत्र॑ मधु॒षुदु॒च्यते॑ बृ॒हदा स॒र्वता॑ति॒मदि॑तिं  
> वृणीमहे ॥ ८ ॥

### Printed p. 144

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अप । अमीवां । सविता । साविषत् । न्यक् । वरीयः । इत् । अप । सेधंतु । अद्रयः ॥  
> ग्रावा । यत्र । मधुसुत् । उच्यते । बृहत् । आ । सर्वतातिं । अदितिं । वृणीमहे॥८॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सविता सर्वस्य प्रेरकः सवितृदेवतास्माकं संबंधिनममीवां रोगमप साविषत् । अपप्रेरयतु । अपगमयत्वित्यर्थः । तथा वरीय इदुरुतरमपि पापं
> न्यग्नीचीनमप सेधंत्वद्रयः पर्वताभिमानिनो देवा अभिषवाश्मानो वा । एतत्सर्वं कुत्र भवत्विति चेत् उच्यते । यत्र यस्मिन्प्रदेशे यस्मिन्यागे वा मधुरस्य
> सोमस्य सोता ग्रावा बृहत्प्रवृद्धमुच्यते स्सूयते तत्र ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Savitṛ, the impeller of all, is to drive away (अप साविषत् (*apa sāviṣat*) = अपप्रेरयतु (*apaprerayatu*), *apagamayatu*) the disease (अमीवां (*amīvāṃ*)) that is ours. Likewise let the अद्रयः (*adrayaḥ*), the gods who preside over mountains, or the pressing-stones, drive downward (न्यक् (*nyak*) = न्यग्नीचीनमप (*nyagnīcīnamapa*)) even the greater (वरीयः (*varīyaḥ*)) sin. If it is asked where all this is to take place, the answer is: in the place or at the sacrifice where the stone (ग्रावा (*grāvā*)) that presses the sweet Soma is greatly (बृहत् (*bṛhat*)) praised, that is, pressed (उच्यते (*ucyate*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सविता (*savitā*)—the god Savitṛ
- अमीवां (*amīvāṃ*)—our disease
- अप साविषत् (*apa sāviṣat*)—let him destroy
- यत्र (*yatra*)—in which sacrificial place
- मधुसुत् (*madhusut*)—that presses the sweet Soma
- ग्रावा (*grāvā*)—the stone
- बृहत् (*bṛhat*)—greatly
- उच्यते (*ucyate*)—is praised (there)
- अद्रयः (*adrayaḥ*)—the mountain-presiding deities
- न्यक् (*nyak*)—our sin
- वरीय इत् (*varīya it*)—even though it is great
- अप सेधंतु (*apa sedhaṃtu*)—let them drive far away and destroy
- सर्वतातिं (*sarvatātiṃ*)—the universal
- अदितिं (*aditiṃ*)—Aditi
- आ वृणीमहे (*ā vṛṇīmahe*)—we respectfully pray and invoke

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May the god Savitṛ destroy our disease. In whatever sacrificial place the stone that presses the sweet Soma is greatly praised, there may the mountain-presiding deities destroy our abundant and despicable sin. We respectfully pray to and invoke Aditi, who is all-pervading.

##### English translation
May Savitri drive away our disease, may the mountains keep off our
most heinous (sin), where the stone (the effuser) of the sweet juice is abun-
dantly praised. We long for the universal Aditi.

### Printed p. 145
#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ऊ॒र्ध्वो ग्रावा॑ वसवोऽस्तु सो॒तरि॒ विश्वा॒ द्वेषां॑सिसनु॒तर्यु॑योत ॥  
> स नो॑ दे॒वः स॑वि॒ता पा॒युरीड्य॒ आ स॒र्वता॑ति॒मदि॑तिं वृणीमहे ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ऊर्ध्वः । ग्रावा । वसवः । अस्तु । सोतरि । विश्वा । द्वेषांसि । सनुतः । युयोत ।  
> सः । नः । देवः । सविता । पायुः । ईड्यः । आ । सर्वतातिं । अदितिं । वृणीमहे ॥९॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे वसवो देवाः सोतरि सोमाभिषवकर्तरि मयि ग्रावोर्ध्व उन्नतोऽस्तु ।
> तेनोच्छ्रितेन विश्वा द्वेषांसि सर्वानपि द्वेष्ट्यन्सनुतर्निगूढान्युयोत । पृथक्कुरुत । स सविता देवो नोऽस्माकं पायुः पालयिता ॥ कृवापाजीत्यादिनोण् ।
> प्रत्ययस्वरः ॥ स ईड्यः स्तोतव्यश्च ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Vasus, gods, in me, the one who presses Soma (सोतरि (*sotari*)), let the pressing-stone be raised up (ऊर्ध्वः (*ūrdhvaḥ*), *unnataḥ*). By that raised stone separate out (युयोत (*yuyota*) = पृथक्कुरुत (*pṛthakkuruta*)) all hatreds, that is, all hateful ones, hidden (सनुतः (*sanutaḥ*) = निगूढान् (*nigūḍhān*)). That god Savitṛ is our protector (पायुः (*pāyuḥ*) = पालयिता (*pālayitā*)), and is to be praised (ईड्यः (*īḍyaḥ*) = *stotavyaḥ*).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वसवः (*vasavaḥ*)—O gods, givers of dwelling
- सोतरि (*sotari*)—when I press Soma
- ग्रावा (*grāvā*)—the pressing-stone
- ऊर्ध्वः अस्तु (*ūrdhvaḥ astu*)—let it rise upward
- सनुतः (*sanutaḥ*)—hidden in secret
- विश्वा द्वेषांसि (*viśvā dveṣāṃsi*)—all our enemies
- युयोत (*yuyota*)—destroy
- सः (*saḥ*)—the famous
- सविता देवः (*savitā devaḥ*)—the god Savitṛ
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- पायुः (*pāyuḥ*)—protector
- ईड्यः (*īḍyaḥ*)—worthy of worship
- सर्वतातिं (*sarvatātiṃ*)—the universal
- अदितिं (*aditiṃ*)—Aditi
- आ वृणीमहे (*ā vṛṇīmahe*)—we respectfully pray and invoke

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O gods, givers of dwelling, when I press Soma, let the crushing stone rise upward. Destroy our enemies who lie hidden in secret. The famous god Savitṛ is both our protector and worthy of worship. We respectfully pray to and invoke Aditi, who is the all-embracing one.

### Printed p. 146

##### English translation
May the stone be uplifted, gods, when I make the libation ; disperse all my secret adversaries; the god Savitri is our adorable protector. We long for the universal Aditi.

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उर्जं गावो यवसे पीवो अत्तन ऋतस्य याः सदने  
> कोशे अंगै ॥  
> त॒नूरे॒व त॒न्नो॑ अस्तु भेष॒जमा स॒र्वता॑ति॒मदि॑तिं वृणीमहे ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ऊर्जं । गावः । यवसे । पीवः । अत्तन । ऋतस्य । याः । सदने । कोशे । अंगै ।  
> तनूः । एव । तन्नः । अस्तु । भेषजं । आ । सर्वतातिं । अदितिं । वृणीमहे ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे गाव आशिरार्थाः यूयं यवसे तृणवति देशे पीवः प्रवृद्धमूर्जं
> रसमत्तन । अत्त । या ऋतस्य यज्ञस्य सदने गृहे कोशे गोष्ठे दोहनस्थानेऽज्ध्वे व्यंजयथ ता अत्तन । तनूरेव ! जन्वे जनकशब्दः । तनूरेव क्षीरमेव
> तन्नः सोमरसस्य भेषजमस्तु । यद्वा । पृथगेव वाक्यं । अस्माकं तन्नो भेषजमस्तु । किंच तनूरेव पशुशरीरमेव । पशुना यागे सति स्वर्गस्य संपादयितुं
> शक्यत्वात् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa addresses the cows (हे गाव (*he gāva*)): "You, in a grassy place, eat the abundant, swollen sap (ऊर्जं (*ūrjaṃ*))." Those cows which are adorned in the house of the ṛta, that is, of the sacrifice, in the cowshed, the milking place, are to eat it. Then comes तनूरेव (*tanūreva*), with a remark on a word meaning "begetter" (जन्वे जनकशब्दः (*janve janakaśabdaḥ*)) **NOTE**. "Their body itself, that is, the milk, shall be a remedy for our Soma-juice." Or else it is a separate sentence: "May it be a remedy for us." And further, "the body" is the animal's body itself, because when an animal is offered in the sacrifice heaven can be secured.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- गावः (*gāvaḥ*)—O cows
- याः (*yāḥ*)—you who
- ऋतस्य सदने (*ṛtasya sadane*)—which is a part of the sacrificial house
- कोशे (*kośe*)—in the cowshed
- अज्‌ध्वै (*ajdhvai*)—are adorned with unguents and the like; you such as these
- यवसे (*yavase*)—in a place where there is grass
- पीवः (*pīvaḥ*)—abundant
- ऊर्जं (*ūrjaṃ*)—the sap (water)
- अत्तन (*attana*)—drink
- तनूः एव (*tanūḥ eva*)—the milk itself produced from your body

### Printed p. 147
- तन्नः (*tannaḥ*)—for the Soma-juice (or for our body)
- भेषजं (*bheṣajaṃ*)—as a medicine
- अस्तु (*astu*)—let it be
- सर्वतातिं (*sarvatātiṃ*)—the universal
- अदितिं (*aditiṃ*)—Aditi
- आ वृणीमहे (*ā vṛṇīmahe*)—we invoke with prayer

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O cows, standing adorned with unguents and the like in the cowshed that is part of the sacrificial house, drink the abundant water in the place where there is grass. May the milk produced from your body be a medicine for our body. We invoke with respect and prayer the universal Aditi.

##### English translation
Eat abundant fodder in the pasture. O cows, who are anointed in the hall of sacrifice in the cow stall; may (your) body be the remedy for (our) body. We long for the universal Aditi.

#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> क्र॒तु॒प्रावा॑ जरि॒ता शश्व॑ता॒मव॒ इंद्र॒ इद्भ॒द्रा प्रम॑तिः सु॒तान॑तां।  
> पू॒र्णमूध॑र्दि॒व्यं यस्य॑ सि॒क्तय॒ आ स॒र्वता॑ति॒मदि॑तिं वृणीमहे ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> क्रतुऽप्रावा । जरिता । शश्वतां । अवः । इंद्रः । इत् । भद्रा । प्रमतिः । सुतvवतां ।  
> पूर्णं । ऊधः । दिव्यं । यस्य । सिक्तये । आ । सर्वऽतातिं । अदितिं । वृणीमहे॥११॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> क्रतुप्रावा कर्मणां पूरकः ॥ प्रा पूरणे । आतो मनिन्नित्यादिना वनिप् ॥
> जरिता स्तोता । यजमानं साधु सोमः कृत इति स्तौति । यद्वा शश्वतां सर्वॆषां
> जरियितेंद्र इदिंद्र एव सुतावतां सोमवतां यजमानानामवो रक्षको भद्रा
> भद्रया स्तुत्या प्रमतिः । यस्येंद्रस्य सिक्तये सेकाय पानायोधरुद्धततरं
> द्रोणकलशं पूर्णं सोमेन पूर्णं भवति स एवावः स एव प्रमतिरिति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

क्रतुप्रावा (*kratuprāvā*) means the fulfiller of rites. जरिता (*jaritā*) is the praiser: he praises the sacrificer, saying "Soma has been well prepared." Or else, शश्वतां (*śaśvatāṃ*) means "of all", and Indra, the praiser, is himself the protector (अवः (*avaḥ*)) of the sacrificers who have Soma pressed, and the auspicious forethought (प्रमतिः (*pramatiḥ*)) by his auspicious praise. For the sake of the sprinkling, that is, the drinking, of that Indra, the divine udder, the Droṇa vessel, becomes full, filled with Soma; he alone is the protector, he alone is the forethought.

### Printed p. 148

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यस्य (*yasya*)—of which Indra
- सिक्तये (*siktaye*)—for drinking
- दिव्यंऊधः (*divyaṃūdhaḥ*)—the Droṇa vessel
- पूर्णं (*pūrṇaṃ*)—is filled with Soma-juice, such a one
- क्रतुप्रावा (*kratuprāvā*)—who fulfils the rite
- शश्वतां (*śaśvatāṃ*)—all sacrificers
- जरिता (*jaritā*)—who praises
- इंद्र इत् (*iṃdra it*)—Indra himself
- सुतावतां (*sutāvatāṃ*)—to the sacrificers who have Soma
- अवः (*avaḥ*)—protector
- भद्रा (*bhadrā*)—auspicious
- प्रमतिः (*pramatiḥ*)—he is of the nature of forethought
- सर्वतातिं (*sarvatātiṃ*)—the universal
- अदितिं (*aditiṃ*)—Aditi
- आ वृणीमहे (*ā vṛṇīmahe*)—we invoke with respect and prayer

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, for whose drinking the Droṇa vessel is filled with Soma-juice, who fulfils the rite and praises all the sacrificers, is the protector of the sacrificers who have Soma, and is auspicious, of the nature of forethought. We invoke with respect and prayer the universal Aditi.

##### English translation
Indra is the fulfiller of pious acts, the glorifier of all, the guardian of the offerers of libations, the auspicious forethought, for his libation the divine pitcher is filled (with Soma). We long for the universal Aditi.

#### Ṛk 12

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> चि॒त्रस्ते॑ भा॒नुः क्र॑तु॒प्रा अ॑भि॒ष्टिः संति॒ स्पृधो॑ जरिणि॒प्रा अधृ॑ष्टाः ।  
> रजि॑ष्ठया॒ रज्या॑ प॒श्व आ गोस्तूतू॑र्षति॒ पर्यग्रं॑ दुव॒स्युः ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> चित्रः । ते । भानुः । क्रतुप्राः । अभिष्टिः । संति । स्पृधः । जरणिप्राः । अधृष्टाः ।  
> रजिष्ठया । रज्या । पश्वः । अ । गोः । तूतूर्षति । परि । अग्रं । दुवस्युः ॥ १२ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र ते भानुः प्रकाशश्चित्र आश्चर्यभूतश्चायनीयो ना । तथा क्रतुप्रा
> स भानुरस्माकं कर्मणां पूरकोऽभिष्टिरभ्येषणीयः । हे इंद्र ते जरणिप्राः

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, your radiance (भानुः (*bhānuḥ*)) is wonderful (चित्र (*citra*)), a marvel, to be worshipped." That radiance fulfils (क्रतुप्रा (*kratuprā*)) our rites and is desirable (अभिष्टि (*abhiṣṭi*)).

### Printed p. 149

> स्तोत्कणां धनस्य पूरयित्र्योऽधृष्ट्वा अन्यैरप्रधृष्याः स्पृधः स्पर्धाः संति ।
> यस्मादेवं तस्माद्रजिष्ययर्जुतमया रज्या रज्जा गोः पश्वः पशोरग्रं दुवस्युः
> परिचरणमिच्छन्नेतन्नामर्षिः हरि तूतूर्षति । अभिश्वरयति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, your efforts (स्पृधः (*spṛdhaḥ*)), which fill the wealth of the praisers (जरणिप्राः (*jaraṇiprāḥ*)), are unassailable (अधृष्टाः (*adhṛṣṭāḥ*)) by others. Because this is so, Duvasyu, the seer of that name, desiring to serve, hastens (तूतूर्षति (*tūtūrṣati*), he urges on) to the front of the animal, the cow, with a straightest cord (रज्या (*rajyā*), a rope) **NOTE**.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Indra) ते (*te*)—your
- भानुः (*bhānuḥ*)—radiance
- चित्रः (*citraḥ*)—is wonderful
- क्रतुप्राः (*kratuprāḥ*)—protects our rites
- अभिष्टिः (*abhiṣṭiḥ*)—grants what we desire
- स्पृधः (*spṛdhaḥ*)—your efforts
- अधृष्टाः (*adhṛṣṭāḥ*)—are unobstructed
- जरणिप्राः (*jaraṇiprāḥ*)—fill with wealth the praisers
- संति (*saṃti*)—they are
- (therefore) दुवस्युः (*duvasyuḥ*)—the seer named Duvasu
- रजिष्ठया (*rajiṣṭhayā*)—straight
- रज्या (*rajyā*)—held by a rope
- पश्वः (*paśvaḥ*)—the sacrificial animal
- गोः (*goḥ*)—of the cow
- अग्रं (*agraṃ*)—to the front
- परि तूतूर्षति (*pari tūtūrṣati*)—is going in haste

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, your radiance is exceedingly wonderful; it protects our rites and grants what we desire. Your efforts are unobstructed and fill the praisers with wealth. Therefore the seer Duvasyu hastens to the front of the sacrificial cow held by a straight rope. (He is making it ready for the sacrifice.)

##### English translation
Your splendour, Indra, is wonderful, fulfilling our pious acts, desirable; your efforts replenishing the wealth of your worshippers are irresistible; (therefore Duvasyu hastens in front of the victim cow, (leading it) with a straight cord.

## Sūkta 101 (Maṇḍala 10)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उद्बुध्यध्वमिति द्वादशर्चं द्वितीयं सूक्तं । सोमपुत्रस्य बुधस्यार्षं । आ
> वो धियमिति नवमी कपृन्नर इत्यंता च द्वे जगत्यौ । चतुर्थीषष्ट्यौ गायत्र्यौ।
> निराहावानिति पंचमी बृहती । शिष्टास्त्रिष्टुभः । विश्वे देवा देवता । ऋत्विक्सुतिरूपोऽर्थो वा देवता । तथा जानुक्रांतं । उद्बुध्यध्वं बुधः सौम्य ऋत्विक्सुतिर्वा
> नवम्यंत्ये जगत्यौ गायत्र्योर्मध्यॆ प चमी बृहतीति ॥ गतः सूक्तविनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The second sūkta, उद्बुध्यध्वं (*udbudhyadhvaṃ*), has twelve ṛks. The seer is Budha, son of Soma. The ninth आ वो धियं (*ā vo dhiyaṃ*) and the last, कपृन्नर (*kapṛnnara*), are Jagatī; the fourth and sixth are Gāyatrī; the fifth, निराहानान् (*nirāhānān*), is Bṛhatī; the rest are Triṣṭubh. The deity is the Viśve Devāḥ, or the subject, the praise of the priests (ṛtviks). Likewise the Anukramaṇī: उद्बुध्यध्वं बुधः सौम्य ऋत्विक्सुतिर्वा नवम्यंत्ये जगत्यौ गायत्र्योर्मध्यॆ प चमी बृहती (*udbudhyadhvaṃ budhaḥ saumya ṛtviksutirvā navamyaṃtye jagatyau gāyatryormadhyè pa camī bṛhatī*). The use of the sūkta has been stated.

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta beginning उद्बुध्यध्वं (*udbudhyadhvaṃ*) is the second sūkta in the ninth anuvāka. It has twelve ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Budha, son of Soma.

### Printed p. 150
In this sūkta the ninth ṛk, आ वो धियं (*ā vo dhiyaṃ*), and the last (twelfth) ṛk, कपृन्नरः (*kapṛnnaraḥ*), are in the Jagatī metre. The fourth and the sixth ṛks are in Gāyatrī. The fifth ṛk, निराहानान् (*nirāhānān*), is in Bṛhatī. The remaining ṛks are in Triṣṭubh. The deity is the Viśve Devāḥ, or the priests (ṛtviks) are praised here, that is, the praise of the priests is the deity. In the Anukramaṇī it is said: उद्बुध्यध्वं बुधः सौम्य ऋत्विक्सुतिर्वा नवम्यंत्ये जगत्यौ गायत्रोर्मध्यॆ पंचमी बृहती (*udbudhyadhvaṃ budhaḥ saumya ṛtviksutirvā navamyaṃtye jagatyau gāyatrormadhyè paṃcamī bṛhatī*). The use (viniyoga) has been stated earlier.

##### Particulars

- Maṇḍala 10; Anuvāka 9; Sūkta 101
- Aṣṭaka 8; Adhyāya 5; Vargas 18, 19
- Number of ṛks in the sūkta: 12
- Seer: Budha Saumya
- Deity: the Viśve Devāḥ, or the priests
- Metre: 1–3, 7, 8, 10, 11 Triṣṭubh; 4, 6 Gāyatrī; 5 Bṛhatī; 9, 12 Jagatī

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उद्बु॑ध्यध्वं॒ सम॑नसः सखायः॒ सम॒ग्निमिं॑ध्वं ब॒हवः॒ सनी॑ळाः 1  
> द॒धि॒क्राम॒ग्निमु॒षसं॑ च दे॒वीमिंद्रा॑व॒तोऽव॑से॒ नि ह्व॑ये वः॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उत् । बुध्यध्वं । समनसः । सखायः । सं । अग्निं । इंध्वं । बहवः । सनीळाः ।  
> दधिक्रां । अग्निं । उषसं । च । देवीं । इंद्रवतः । अवसे । नि । ह्वये । वः ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अग्निं चेष्यमाणा ऋत्विजो वदंति । हे सखायः सखिभूता ऋत्विजः समनसः
> समानमनस्काः परस्परमद्रोग्धारः । अन्नोन्यं कर्मानुतूला इत्यर्थः । तापृशा
> यूयमुदुत्कृष्टं [बुध्यध्वं । जानीध्वं । उत्तरत्र यूष्माकं कर्तव्यमेतोप.
> दिशति । तथाग्निं समिंध्वं । सम्यग्दीपयध्वं ॥ इञइंधी: डीप्तौ दौधादिकः । लोट
> श्चसोरल्लोलोप इत्यल्लोलोपः ॥ बहवोऽनेके सनीळाः समाननिवासा एकस्यामेव

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The priests who are about to pile the fire speak. "O friends, priests who are friends, of one mind, not injuring one another, that is, favourable to one another's work, awake (बुध्यध्वं (*budhyadhvaṃ*), know) the excellent;" he thus instructs what is to be done by you hereafter. Likewise "kindle (समिंध्वं (*samiṃdhvaṃ*)) Agni well, make him blaze up." Many, सनीळाः (*sanīḷāḥ*), of one dwelling, living in one and the same hall.

### Printed p. 151

> शालायां निवसंतः । अहं च दधिक्रामेतन्नामिकां देवतामग्निमुषसं च
> देवीमेतांस्त्रीन्देवानिंद्रावत इंद्रेण युक्तान्नो युष्मानवसेऽस्माकं रक्षणाय
> नि नितरां ह्वये । आह्वयामि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"And I invoke (नि ह्वये (*ni hvaye*)) the deity of this name, Dadhikrā, Agni and the divine Uṣas, these three gods, and you, joined with Indra, for our protection."

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सखायः (*sakhāyaḥ*)—O priests who are friends
- समनसः (*samanasaḥ*)—being of one mind
- उत् (*ut*)—your excellent duties
- बुध्यध्वं (*budhyadhvaṃ*)—know
- बहवः (*bahavaḥ*)—you who are many
- सनीळाः (*sanīḷāḥ*)—dwelling in one house
- अग्निं (*agniṃ*)—Agni
- समिध्वं (*samidhvaṃ*)—cause to blaze up
- (O gods) इंद्रावतः (*iṃdrāvataḥ*)—accompanied by Indra
- दधिक्रां (*dadhikrāṃ*)—the deity named Dadhikrā
- अग्निं (*agniṃ*)—Agni
- देवीं उषसं (*devīṃ uṣasaṃ*)—the goddess Uṣas
- वः (*vaḥ*)—all of you
- अवसे (*avase*)—for protection
- नि ह्वये (*ni hvaye*)—I call

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O priests who are friends, being of one mind, know your excellent duties. Many of you, living in one house, cause the fire to blaze up. O gods, I invoke with prayer, for protection, the deity Dadhikrā joined with Indra, Agni, the divine Uṣas, and all of you.

##### English translation
Awake, friends, being all agreed; many in number, abiding in one dwelling, kindle Agni. I invoke you, Dhadhikra, Agni and the divine Ushas, who are associated with Indra, for our protection.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In some ṛks of this sūkta there is mention of matters connected with agriculture: yoking oxen to the plough, ploughing the earth, conducting water to the field, reaping the crop, and the like. For some ṛks a meaning connected with the sacrifice can also be given.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> मं॒द्रा कृ॑णुद्वं॒ धिय॒ आ त॑नुध्वं॒ नाव॑मरित्र॒पर॑णीं कृणुद्वं।  
> इष्कृ॑णुद्व॒मायु॒धारं॑ कृणुद्वं॒ प्रांचं॑ य॒ज्ञं प्रण॑यता  
> सखायः ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 152

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> मंद्रा । कृणुध्वं । धियः । आ । तनुध्वं । नावं । अरित्रसरणीं । कृणुध्वं ।  
> इष्पृणुध्वं । आयुधा । अरं । कृणुध्वं । प्रांचं । यज्ञं । प्र । नयत । सखायः ॥२॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> मंद्रा मंद्राणि मदकराणि स्तोत्राणि कृणुध्वं । कुरुध्वं हे सखायः ॥
> कृवि हिंसाकरणयोः । धिन्विकृण्वोरच्चेत्युप्रत्ययः । अकारश्चांतादेशः । यद्वा ।
> करोतेर्व्यत्ययेन श्नुः ॥ तथा धियः कर्माणि चयनप्रदेशकर्षणादीन्यातनुध्वं ।
> विस्तारयत । तथारित्रपरणीं कर्षणादिरूपेणारित्रेण पारयितव्यां नावं चयनाख्यां कृणुध्वं । कुरुध्वं । तथायुधायुधानि सीरयुगादीन्यरमलं कृणुध्वं।
> हे सखाय ऋत्विजः प्रांचं प्रागंचनं यज्ञं यष्टव्यमग्निं प्रणयत । प्रकर्षेण
> नयत । आदौ कर्षणं प्रांचं कुरुतेत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"Make (कृणुध्वं (*kṛṇudhvaṃ*)) exhilarating (मंद्रा (*maṃdrā*)) praises, O friends." Likewise, extend (आतनुध्वं (*ātanudhvaṃ*)) the rites (धियः (*dhiyaḥ*)), such as the piling site and the ploughing. Make the boat called the piling (चयन (*cayana*)), which is to be carried across by the oar (अरित्र (*aritra*)) in the form of ploughing and the like. Likewise make the implements, the plough, yoke and the rest, sufficient (अरं (*araṃ*)). "O friends, priests, lead forward (प्रणयत (*praṇayata*)) the fire, the object of worship, eastward (प्रांचं (*prāṃcaṃ*))." The meaning is: first make the ploughing face the front.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सखायः (*sakhāyaḥ*)—O priests who are friends
- मंद्रा (*maṃdrā*)—gladdening praises
- कृणुध्वं (*kṛṇudhvaṃ*)—compose
- धियः (*dhiyaḥ*)—the prescribed rites
- आ तनुध्वं (*ā tanudhvaṃ*)—extend
- अरित्रपरणीं (*aritraparaṇīṃ*)—to be carried on by ploughing and the like
- नावं (*nāvaṃ*)—the boat in the form of the piling
- कृणुध्वं (*kṛṇudhvaṃ*)—construct
- आयुधा (*āyudhā*)—the weapons
- इष्टृणुध्वं (*iṣṭṛṇudhvaṃ*)—make ready
- अरं कृणुध्वं (*araṃ kṛṇudhvaṃ*)—adorn
- प्रांचं (*prāṃcaṃ*)—adorned with unguent
- यज्ञं (*yajñaṃ*)—worthy of worship, Agni
- प्रणयत (*praṇayata*)—lead forth

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O priests who are friends, compose gladdening praises. Extend the prescribed rites. Construct the boat in the form of the fire-piling, to be carried on by ploughing and the like. Make the weapons ready. Adorn. Lead forth Agni, adorned with unguent and worthy of worship. Make all these preparations for the sacrifice.

##### English translation
Construct exhilarating (hymns), spread forth praises, construct the ship which is propelled by oars, prepare your weapons, make ready, lead forth, O friends, the herald, the adorable (Agni).

### Printed p. 153

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> य॒नक्त॒ सीरा॒ वि यु॒गा त॑नुध्वं कृ॒ते योन्॑ वपते॒ह बीजं॑ ।  
> गि॒रा च॑ श्रु॒ष्टिः सभ॑रा॒ अस॑न्नो॒ नेदी॑य॒ इत्सृ॒ण्यः॑ प॒क्वमेया॑त् ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> युनक्त । सीरा । वि । युगा । तनुध्वं । कृते । योन् । वपत । इह । बीजं ।  
> गिरा । च । श्रुष्टिः । सभराः । असत् । नः । नेदीयः । इत् । सृण्यः । पक्वं । आ । इयात् ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे सखायः यूयं सीरा सीराणि युनक्त । युंग्वमनडुद्भिः सह ॥
> युजेर्लोटि तप्तनप्तनथनाश्चति तबादेशः । आत एवाजित्वाच्छसोरल्लोप
> इत्यल्लोपाभावः ॥ तदर्थं युगा युगानि वि तनुध्वं । विस्तारयध्वं । कृते च
> योनाविह सीतायां बीजं ग्राम्यमारण्यं च वपत निधत्त । तिलमाषव्रीह्यादिकं
> ग्राम्यसप्तकं वेणुश्यामाकनीवारादिकमारण्यबीजसप्तकं च कृष्णाकृष्टयोर्निवपतेत्यर्थः । सप्त ग्राम्याः कृष्टे सप्तारण्या अकृष्ट इत्यापस्तंबः । तथा नोऽस्माकं
> गिरा स्तुत्या प्रशस्ता सहास्माकं श्रुष्टिरन्नं सभराः सभरमसत् । भवति । भवतु । तथा
> नेदीय इदंतिकमेव सृण्यः । सृणिरंकुशः । अंकुशवद्वक्रो लवित्रः । पक्वं
> स्तंबमेयात् । अभिमुख्येन गच्छतु ॥ श्रुष्टिः सभरा असत् सृण्यः पक्वमेयादिति
> चशब्दानुवृत्तेश्ववायोगे प्रथमेति प्रथमस्य निघाताभावः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa addresses the friends (the priests): yoke the ploughs सीरा (*sīrā*) together with the bulls, and for this purpose stretch out the yokes. In the prepared womb (the furrow) here, sow the seed, both village and forest kinds. He explains this as sesame, māṣa beans, rice and the like (the seven village grains), and bamboo-rice, śyāmāka millet, wild rice and the like (the seven forest seeds); one is to sow them on ploughed and unploughed ground. He cites Āpastamba: सप्त ग्राम्याः कृष्टे सप्तारण्या अकृष्ट (*sapta grāmyāḥ kṛṣṭe saptāraṇyā akṛṣṭa*). Then: through our praise, may our food (श्रुष्टिः (*śruṣṭiḥ*) = food) be abundant. May the sickle सृण्यः (*sṛṇyaḥ*) (सृणिः (*sṛṇiḥ*) is a goad; the sickle is curved like a goad) come very near, and may the ripe stalk come directly towards it.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (Addressed to the priests, friends)
- सीरा (*sīrā*)—the ploughs for tilling
- युनक्त (*yunakta*)—join them with the bulls
- युगा (*yugā*)—the yokes
- वि तनुध्वं (*vi tanudhvaṃ*)—spread out
- कृते च योन्सौ (*kṛte ca yonsau*)—as soon as the field is made ready
- इह (*iha*)—in this ploughed place
- बीजं (*bījaṃ*)—the seed
- वपत (*vapata*)—sow
- गिरा (*girā*)—by praise
- श्रुष्टिः (*śruṣṭiḥ*)—our food
- सभराः (*sabharāḥ*)—abundantly
- असत् (*asat*)—may it grow
- नः सृण्यः (*naḥ sṛṇyaḥ*)—our sickles
- नेदीय इत् (*nedīya it*)—quite near
- पक्वं (*pakvaṃ*)—the ripe ear
- आ इयात् (*ā iyāt*)—may it approach

### Printed p. 154

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O friends, priests, yoke the bulls to the ploughs. Spread out the yokes. As soon as the field is ready, sow the seed in the ploughed place. By praise may our food (grain) grow abundantly. May the ripe ear come to our sickle.

##### English translation
Harness the ploughs, fit on the yokes, now that the womb of earth is ready, sow the seed therein, and through our praise may there be abundant food; may (the gain) fall ripe towards the sikle.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

सृण्यः (*sṛṇyaḥ*)—In explaining the word सृणिः (*sṛṇiḥ*) Yāska gave an etymology for it: सृणिरंकुशो भवति सरणात् । अंकुशोऽञ्चितेराकुचितो भवतीति वा । नेदीय इत्सृण्यः पक्वमेयात् इत्यपि निगमो भवति । अंतिकतममंकुशादायात्पक्वमौषधमागच्छत्वित्यागच्छत्त्विति ॥ (*sṛṇiraṃkuśo bhavati saraṇāt | aṃkuśo 'ñciterākucito bhavatīti vā | nedīya itsṛṇyaḥ pakvameyāt ityapi nigamo bhavati | aṃtikatamamaṃkuśādāyātpakvamauṣadhamāgacchatvityāgacchattviti ‖*) (Ni. 5-28). सृणि (*sṛṇi*) means a goad or hook. The word is formed from the root सृ (*sṛ*), meaning "go, enter" (सरणात् (*saraṇāt*)), because the goad goes, that is, enters, into the elephant's head. The word अंकुश (*aṃkuśa*) is formed from the root अंचति (*aṃcati*), "go, enter"; or, because it is slightly bent (आकुचितो भवतीति (*ākucito bhavatīti*)), it is named अंकुश (*aṃkuśa*). The word सृणि (*sṛṇi*) is used in the Vedic sentence नेदीय इत्सृण्यः पक्वमेयात् (*nedīya itsṛṇyaḥ pakvameyāt*). The well-ripened ear of grain comes near this goad, that is, the sickle (सृणि (*sṛṇi*)); the meaning is that the ear of grain is cut with the sickle.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)
