> सीरा॑ युंजंति क॒वयो॑ यु॒गा वि त॑न्वते॒ पृथ॑क् ।  
> धीरा॑ दे॒वेषु॑ सुम्म॒या ॥ ४ ॥

### Printed p. 155

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सीरा । युंजंति । कवयः । युगा । वि । तन्वते । पृथक् ।  
> धीराः । देवेषु । सुम्मया ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> कवयो मेधाविन ऋत्विजः सीरा सीराणि कर्षणसाधनानि युंजंति । योजयंति । युगा युगान्यपि पृथक् परस्परं वि तन्वते । भिन्नप्रदेशानि कुर्वंति ।
> कीदृशाः कवयः । देवेषु विषये धीरा धीमंतः । किमर्थं । सुम्नया । सुम्नमिति
> सुखनाम । सुखेच्छया ॥ सुम्मशब्दात्क्यजंताद्भावे आ प्रत्ययादित्यकारप्रत्ययः ।
> सुपां सुलुगिति तृतीयाया आकारः ॥ अथवा । धीरा धीमंतो देवेषु सुम्न या
> सुम्नेन । देवेषु सुखं भूयादिति ॥ सुपां सुलुगिति विभक्तेर्याजादेशः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The wise priests (कवयः (*kavayaḥ*)) yoke the ploughs, the implements of tilling, and spread the yokes apart, setting them in separate places. What kind of seers? Wise (धीराः (*dhīrāḥ*)) in regard to the gods. For what purpose? सुम्नया (*sumnayā*): सुम्न (*sumna*) is a name for happiness; so, from desire for happiness. Alternatively: the wise, by means of सुम्न (*sumna*) (happiness), among the gods, that is, "may there be happiness among the gods."

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सुम्मया (*summayā*)—for the sake of happiness
- देवेषु (*deveṣu*)—in the gods
- धीराः (*dhīrāḥ*)—endowed with wisdom, devoted
- कवयः (*kavayaḥ*)—the priests
- सीरा (*sīrā*)—the ploughs
- युंजंति (*yuṃjaṃti*)—join
- युगा (*yugā*)—the yokes
- पृथक् (*pṛthak*)—separating them
- वि तन्वते (*vi tanvate*)—spread out

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Desiring happiness, the priests, wise and devoted to the gods, join the ploughs. They separate the yokes and spread them out.

##### English translation
The wise (priests) harness the ploughs, they lay the yokes apart,
firmly devoted through the desire of happiness.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> निरा॑हा॒वान्कृ॑णोतन॒ सं व॑र॒त्रा द॑धातन ।  
> सिं॒चाम॑हा अव॒तमु॒द्रिणं॑ व॒यं सु॒षेक॒मनु॑पक्षितं ॥ ५ ॥

### Printed p. 156

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> निः । आहावान् । कृषोतन । सं । वरत्राः । दधातन ।  
> सिंचामहै । अवतं । उद्रिणं । वयं । सुसेकं । अनुपक्षितं ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे सखायः आहावान्! आहूयंते पानार्थं गावोऽत्रेत्याहावा निपाणानि ।
> अत्रौचित्यादग्नि चयनप्रदेशकर्षणार्थगोपानसाधनद्रुममयपानपात्राण्युच्यंते
> तानाहावान् निष्कृणोतन । निष्टुरुत ॥ आहूयतेर्निपानमाहावः । पा. ३-३-७४ ।
> इति निपातितः । थाथादिस्वरः । कृणोतन । तप्तनबित्यादिना तनप् । तेनाजित्त्वाद्गुणः ।
> श्नाभ्यस्तयोरात इत्याकारलोपः ॥ किमर्थमाहावकरणं वरत्रस्थापनं चेति ।
> अत्रोच्यते । वयमवतमवटं सिंचामहै । कीदृशमवटं । उद्रिणमुद्द्राववंतं
> सुषेकं सुष्टु सेक्तुं शक्यं अनुपक्षितमनुपक्षीणं कदाचिदुदकोपक्षयरहितं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O friends, make आहावान् (*āhāvān*)": आहाव (*āhāva*) means drinking troughs, places where cattle are called to drink. Here, as is fitting, he says these are drinking vessels made of wood, for the cattle that plough the fire-piling site. Make them (निष्कृणोतन (*niṣkṛṇotana*) = निष्टुरुत (*niṣṭuruta*)). He cites Pā. 3-3-74 for आहाव (*āhāva*) as an irregular formation from आहूयते (*āhūyate*) meaning "drinking place". To what end the making of the troughs and the fixing of the straps? He answers: we shall pour out the well (अवतं (*avataṃ*) = *avaṭaṃ*), which is उद्रिणं (*udriṇaṃ*) (full of springing water), सुषेकं (*suṣekaṃ*) (easily drawn from), and अनुपक्षितं (*anupakṣitaṃ*) (not diminished, never without water).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O priests)
- आहावान् (*āhāvān*)—troughs that show water to the bulls
- निष्कृणोतन (*niṣkṛṇotana*)—construct
- वरत्राः (*varatrāḥ*)—the neck-straps of the bulls
- सं दधातन (*saṃ dadhātana*)—join and tie
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we
- उद्रिणं (*udriṇaṃ*)—provided with a spring of water
- सुषेकं (*suṣekaṃ*)—having water that can be easily drawn
- अनुपक्षितं (*anupakṣitaṃ*)—not dried up however much water is taken
- अवतं (*avataṃ*)—the water of the well
- सिंचामहै (*siṃcāmahai*)—let us draw and pour out

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O priests, construct troughs that show water to the bulls. Join and tie neck-straps to the bulls. Let us draw and pour out the water of the well, which has a spring of water, has water that can be easily drawn, and is not dried up however much water is drawn.

##### English translation
Set up the cattle-troughs, bind the straps to it; let us pour out (the water of) the well, which is full of water, fit to be poured out, and not easily exhausted.

### Printed p. 157

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इष्कृ॑ताहावमव॒तं सु॑वर॒त्रं सु॑षेच॒नं ।  
> उ॒द्रिणं॑ सिंचे॒ अक्षि॑तं ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इष्टतआहावं । अवतं । सुवरत्रं । सुसेचनं ।  
> उद्रिणं । सिंचे । अक्षितं ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इष्कृताहावं संस्कृताहावमवतमवटं द्रोणं सिंचे । सेचयामि । पुनः
> कीदृशमवटं । सुवरत्रं शोभनवरत्रोपेतं सुषेचनं शोभनोदकसेकोपेतं
> उद्रिणमुद्द्राववंतं अक्षितमक्षीणं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"I pour out the well (अवतं (*avataṃ*) = *avaṭaṃ*), the trough (द्रोणं (*droṇaṃ*)), whose drinking-place is prepared (इष्कृताहावं (*iṣkṛtāhāvaṃ*) = *saṃskṛtāhāvaṃ*)." Again, of what kind? सुवरत्रं (*suvaratraṃ*): provided with good straps; सुषेचनं (*suṣecanaṃ*): provided with good pouring-out of water; उद्रिणं (*udriṇaṃ*): full of springing water; अक्षितं (*akṣitaṃ*): inexhaustible.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इष्कृताहावं (*iṣkṛtāhāvaṃ*)—prepared for the bulls to drink
- सुवरत्रं (*suvaratraṃ*)—furnished with an excellent bank
- सुषेचनं (*suṣecanaṃ*)—that is to be drawn easily
- उद्रिणं (*udriṇaṃ*)—having water like a spring
- अक्षितं (*akṣitaṃ*)—holding much water
- अवतं (*avataṃ*)—the trough that is like a well
- सिंचे (*siṃce*)—I fill with water

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

I fill with water the trough, like a well, which is prepared for the bulls to drink, furnished with an excellent bank, to be drawn easily, having water like a spring, and holding much water.

##### English translation
I pour out (the water of) the well, whose cattle troughs are prepared,
well fitted with straps, fit to be poured out, full of water, inexhaustible.

### Printed p. 158

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्री॒णी॒ताश्वा॑न्हि॒तं ज॑याथ स्वस्ति॒वाहं॒ रथ॒मित्कृ॑णुध्वं ।  
> द्रोणा॑हामव॒तमश्म॑चक्र॒मंस॑त्रकोशं सिंचता नृ॒पाणं॑ ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्रीणीत । अश्वान् । हितं । जयाथ । स्वस्तिवाहं । रथं । इत् । कृणुध्वं ।  
> द्रोणआहावं । अवतं । अश्मचक्रं । अंगत्रकोशं । सिंचत । नृपानं ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे ऋत्विजः यूयमश्वान्व्यापनशीलान्बलिवर्दान्रीणीत । उचितघासोदकादिप्रदानेन प्रीणयत । यथा क्षेत्रकर्षणाय प्रभवंति तथा कुरुतेत्यर्थः । तथा
> कृत्वा हितं चयनायोजितं कर्षणं जयाथ । जयथ । संपादयथ । तथा रथं
> चयनाख्यं हलाख्यं वा स्वस्तिवाहमित् सुखस्य वाहकमेव कृणुध्वं । कुरुध्वं ।
> तदर्थं द्रोणाहावं । आहूयंत्यत्र पानार्थं बलिवर्दानित्याहावो जलाधारः
> पात्रविशेषः । स च द्रोणमयो द्रुममय आहावो यस्य तादृशमवतमवटवन्निम्नभूतमश्मचक्रं व्याप्तक्रमणमश्ममयचक्रं वांसत्रकोशं अंसत्रं कवचं
> यथा कायं रक्षति तद्वदुदकस्य कोशं कोशस्थानीयं नृपाणं नृणां कर्मनेतृणां पानयोग्यवादृशमवतं सिंचत हे ऋत्विजः । एवमृत्विक्सुतिर्वेति पक्षे ।
> सूक्तस्य वैश्वदेवपक्ष एवं योजना ! प्रीणीत प्रीणयताश्वान् । स्नानपानयोग्यान्नैः संग्रामयोग्यान्कुरुतेत्यर्थः । हितं जयाथ । हितमिति क्रियाविशेषणं ।
> कथं जयथ । बंधुसुहृद्यो यथा हितं भवति तथा जयथ संग्रामं । स्वस्तिवाहं!
> स्वस्तित्यविनाशनाम । अविनाशवाहनं रथमिद्रथं च कृणुध्वं । इदिति चार्थे । अश्वरथं
> च दृढं कृत्वा संग्रामभूमिं गत्वा द्रोणाहावमित्यादीनि द्वितीयांतानि कूपाख्यसंग्रामविशेषणानि सिंचतेत्यनेन संबध्यते ! द्रोणो द्रुममयः स एव रथ
> आहाव आहावस्थानीयो यस्य संग्रामस्य तं । अवतं । कूपनामैतत् । कूपं,
> संग्रामाख्यं । अश्मचक्रं कूपप्रांतिनिबद्धात्मसदृशप्रहरणायुधवंतं ! असनानि वा
> क्षेपणानि ना चक्राख्यायुधविशेषा यस्मिन् तं । व्याप्तचरणवंतं व्याप्तक्रमणवंतं
> वा । चक्रं चकतेवा चरतेर्वा क्रामतेर्वेति निरुक्तं । ४-२७ । अंसत्रकोशं

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

First explanation (the priests' cultivation): "O priests, satisfy the horses, that is, the pervading bulls, by giving suitable fodder, water and the like, so that they are capable of ploughing the field. Having done so, win हितं (*hitaṃ*), the ploughing arranged for the fire-piling. Make the chariot, whether the one called चयन (*cayana*) or the plough, स्वस्तिवाहं (*svastivāhaṃ*), a bearer of well-being." For this purpose: आहाव (*āhāva*) is a water-holding vessel from which bulls are called to drink; its द्रोण (*droṇa*) is of wood. अवतं (*avataṃ*) is the well-like hollow; अश्मचक्रं (*aśmacakraṃ*) is one with a stone wheel, extensively moving. अंसत्रकोशं (*aṃsatrakośaṃ*): as armour protects the body, so a store of water, नृपाणं (*nṛpāṇaṃ*), fit for drinking by the men who lead the rite. "Pour out such a well, O priests." This applies if the hymn is a praise of the priests.
Second explanation (if the hymn is Vaiśvadeva, concerning battle): प्रीणीत (*prīṇīta*): satisfy the horses, making them fit for bathing, drinking and battle. हितं जयाथ (*hitaṃ jayātha*): हितं (*hitaṃ*) is adverbial; win the battle in such a way as is beneficial to kinsmen and friends. स्वस्तिवाहं (*svastivāhaṃ*): स्वस्ति (*svasti*) is a name for non-destruction; make the chariot a carrier of what is not destroyed; इत् (*it*) has the sense of "and". Having made horses and chariot firm and gone to the battlefield, pour out the accusatives द्रोणाहावं (*droṇāhāvaṃ*) etc., epithets of the battle called a well (कूप (*kūpa*)). अवतं (*avataṃ*) is a name for a well. द्रोण (*droṇa*) is wooden, that is, the chariot itself stands in the place of the आहाव (*āhāva*) of that battle. अश्मचक्रं (*aśmacakraṃ*) is explained as having missile weapons resembling oneself fastened at the edge of the well, or as one in which the weapons called चक्र (*cakra*) are hurled, or as having extended movement. He cites the Nirukta (4-27): चक्रं चकतेवा चरतेर्वा क्रामतेर्वेति (*cakraṃ cakatevā caratervā krāmaterveti*).

### Printed p. 159

> अंसत्राणि धनूंषि कवचानि च कोशस्थानीयानि यस्मिन् तं सिंचत । नृपाणं यस्मिन्नोद्धार उदकवत्पीयंते मार्यंते तं । ईदृशं कूपसदृशं संग्रामं सिंचत
> हे अस्मदीया योद्धारः । सूक्तस्य वैश्वदेवत्वाद्दे सैनिका विश्वेषां देवानां
> प्रसादेन सर्वमेतत्कुरुतेत्यर्थः । एवं निराहावानित्यादिषु कूपरूपव्याजेन
> संग्रामवर्णनमवगंतव्यं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa completes his gloss of the preceding verse, taking it as a description of a battle compared to a well. The "bows and armours" are the अंसत्राणि (*aṃsatrāṇi*), which stand in the place of a treasury (kośa) in that battle. The warriors are to "pour out" (सिंचत (*siṃcata*)) this battle, which is like a well, and in which the lives of men are drunk up like water. Because the sūkta is addressed to the Viśvedevas, the soldiers are to do all this through the favour of all the gods. In verses such as *nirāhāvān* the description of the battle is to be understood as made under the guise of a well.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अश्वान् (*aśvān*)—(O priests) the oxen that move about everywhere
- प्रीणीत (*prīṇīta*)—satisfy them by giving grass and the like
- हितं (*hitaṃ*)—the work such as ploughing
- जयाथ (*jayātha*)—do and complete
- रथं (*rathaṃ*)—the car
- स्वस्तिवाहं इत् (*svastivāhaṃ it*)—so that it bears auspicious wealth
- कृणुध्वं (*kṛṇudhvaṃ*)—make
- अवतं (*avataṃ*)—that which is deep like a well
- अश्मचक्रं (*aśmacakraṃ*)—that which has a wheel made of stone
- अंसत्रकोशं (*aṃsatrakośaṃ*)—that which protects the water like armour
- नृपाणं (*nṛpāṇaṃ*)—that which has water fit for men to drink
- द्रोणाहावं (*droṇāhāvaṃ*)—the water trough
- सिंचत (*siṃcata*)—fill with water

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O priests, satisfy the oxen, which can move about everywhere, by giving them grass and the like. Do and complete the work such as ploughing. Fill the car with auspicious wealth. Fill with water the water trough, which is deep like a well, has a wheel made of stone, protects the water like armour, and has water fit for men to drink.

##### English translation
Satisfy the horses, accomplish the good work (of ploughing), equip a car laden with good fortune, pour out (the water of) the well having a stone rim, having a receptacle like armour, fit for the drinking of men.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The commentator has given two kinds of meaning for this Ṛk: one the priests' view, the other the Viśvedeva view. What is said addressing the priests is the priests' (sacrificial) view, and that has been given above. The Viśvedeva view (the battle view) is as follows.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अश्वान् (*aśvān*)—(O soldiers) the horses
- प्रीणीत (*prīṇīta*)—satisfy them with food and water and make them fit to take part in the battle
- हितं (*hitaṃ*)—so that it is beneficial to kinsmen and friends

### Printed p. 160
- जयाथ (*jayātha*)—win victory in the battle
- स्वस्तिवाहं (*svastivāhaṃ*)—so that it is able to bear auspicious wealth
- रथमित् (*rathamit*)—the car
- कृणुध्वं (*kṛṇudhvaṃ*)—prepare
- द्रोणाहावं (*droṇāhāvaṃ*)—that in which cars made of wood move about
- अश्मचक्रं (*aśmacakraṃ*)—that which is well equipped with weapons such as the stone pulley-wheel of a well and discus-like weapons hurled at enemies
- अंसत्रकोशं (*aṃsatrakośaṃ*)—that which has means of protection such as bows and armour
- नृपाणं (*nṛpāṇaṃ*)—that which is full of chiefs (leaders) who are the guides in the works of battle
- अवतं (*avataṃ*)—the battlefield
- सिंचत (*siṃcata*)—resort to

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O soldiers, give the horses grass and water and make them ready to fight in battle. Win victory so that kinsmen and well-wishers may be happy. Make ready the car so that it can bring back the wealth and so forth of the enemies, after defeating them in battle. Cars made of wood are moving about on the battlefield. Discus-like weapons will be used against the enemies. Armour will be used to protect oneself, and bows to harm the enemies. An assembly of leaders skilled in the works of battle will be there. Go to such a battlefield, join it, conquer the enemies and show your valour and the like.

Of these two kinds of meaning, the first describes the work of agriculture; the second describes the battlefield. For this Ṛk Yāska has given the Nirukta:
प्रीणीताश्वान् । सुहितं जयथ जयनं वो हितमस्तु स्वस्तिवाहनं रथं कुरुध्वं द्रोणाहावं द्रोणं द्रुममयं भवति । आहाव आह्वानादावह आवहनादवतोऽवातितो महान् भवत्यश्मचक्रमसनचक्रमिति वा अंगत्रकोशमंसत्राणि वः कोशस्थानीयानि संतु कोशः कुष्णातेर्निकुषितो भवति । अयमपीतरः कोश एतस्मादेव संचय अचितमात्रो महान् भवति सिंचत नृपाणं नरपाणं कूपकर्मणा संग्राममुपमिमिते ॥ ( (*prīṇītāśvān | suhitaṃ jayatha jayanaṃ vo hitamastu svastivāhanaṃ rathaṃ kurudhvaṃ droṇāhāvaṃ droṇaṃ drumamayaṃ bhavati | āhāva āhvānādāvaha āvahanādavato 'vātito mahān bhavatyaśmacakramasanacakramiti vā aṃgatrakośamaṃsatrāṇi vaḥ kośasthānīyāni saṃtu kośaḥ kuṣṇāternikuṣito bhavati | ayamapītaraḥ kośa etasmādeva saṃcaya acitamātro mahān bhavati siṃcata nṛpāṇaṃ narapāṇaṃ kūpakarmaṇā saṃgrāmamupamimite ‖ (*) (Ni. 5-26)
In this Nirukta Yāska (or the seer of the sūkta) describes the battlefield by comparing it to a (water) well.

प्रीणीताश्वान् (*prīṇītāśvān*)—to make ready for battle, supply the horses with grass and water and make them glad.

### Printed p. 161
सुहितं जयथ जयनं वो हितमस्तु (*suhitaṃ jayatha jayanaṃ vo hitamastu*)—Among the soldiers who take part in battles there are certainly kinsmen and friends. At least some of them will die in the battle, so although battle is somewhat harmful, victory is beneficial to all. Therefore win victory so that it is beneficial to all.

स्वस्ति वाहनं रथं कुरुध्वं (*svasti vāhanaṃ rathaṃ kurudhvaṃ*)—Equip the car well to bring back the wealth obtained through excellent victory.

द्रोणाहावं द्रोणं द्रुममयं भवति । आहाव आह्वानादावह आवहनादवतोऽवातितो महान् भवति (*droṇāhāvaṃ droṇaṃ drumamayaṃ bhavati | āhāva āhvānādāvaha āvahanādavato 'vātito mahān bhavati*)—Droṇa means made of wood (a car, or a wooden trough or vat for filling with water). Āhāva means a wooden vat (ā-hāva). Āvaha means a means of bearing, a cart and so on; this word has the sense of bearing, carrying. Avata means a deep well; this word indicates the sense of ava-atita, very deep below.

अश्मचक्रमशनचक्रमसनचक्रमिति ना (*aśmacakramaśanacakramasanacakramiti nā*)—Aśmacakra means a stone wheel. It is spread everywhere on the battlefield, or its use frightens the enemies. In this sense it is a special weapon used in battle (stone-axe).

अंसत्रकोशमंसत्राणि वः कोशस्थानीयानि संतु । कोशः कुष्णातेर्विकुषितो भवति । अयमपीतरः कोश एतस्मादेव (*aṃsatrakośamaṃsatrāṇi vaḥ kośasthānīyāni saṃtu | kośaḥ kuṣṇātervikuṣito bhavati | ayamapītaraḥ kośa etasmādeva*)—Aṃsa means the limbs of the body. Because it protects them (tāyate), the (steel) armour and bows used in battle are called aṃsatra. The sheath or leather bag in which they are kept is called aṃsatrakośa. The word kośa is derived from the root kuṣ, meaning to pull, to draw out. Because the armour, bow and so on in it (in the bag) are drawn out, it is called kośa. Or kośa has another meaning, a place where wealth and the like are gathered and stored; when this meaning is indicated, the word is likewise derived from the root kuṣ.

संचय अचितमात्रो महान् भवति (*saṃcaya acitamātro mahān bhavati*)—Sañcaya means a collection, the storing together in one place of many things.

नृपाणं नरपाणं कूपकर्मणा संग्राममुपमिमाते (*nṛpāṇaṃ narapāṇaṃ kūpakarmaṇā saṃgrāmamupamimāte*)—Nṛpāṇa means a trough or vat, deep like a well, for filling with water fit for men to drink. The meaning is: fill this with water. Here the battlefield is described by comparison with a deep well.

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> व्र॒जं कृ॑णुध्वं॒ स हि वो॑ नृ॒पाणो॒ वर्म॑ सीव्यध्वं बहु॒ला  ॒॑
> पृथूनि ।  
> पुरः॑ कृणुद्व॒माय॑सी॒रधृ॑ष्टा॒ मा वः॑ सुस्रोच्चम॒सो द्रंह॑ता॒  
> तं ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> व्रजं । कृणुध्वं । सः । हि । वः । नृपानः । वर्म । सीव्यध्वं । बहुला । पृथूनि ।  
> पुरः । कृणुध्वं । आयसीः । अधृष्टाः । मा । वः । सुस्रोत् । चमसः । दृंहत । तं ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे ऋत्विजः व्रजं कृणुध्वं । व्रजं गोष्ठं कुरुध्वं । आशिरदोहार्थं
> गोस्थानं कुरुत । स हि स खलु वो नृपाणो नेतृणां देवानां पातव्यः । देवपानसाधन इति । सांनाय्यरूपेण वासिररूपेण वा गोः पयआदिकं देवाः पिबंति
> खलु । वर्म वर्माणि सीव्यध्वं । वर्मवत्वधानस्योभयत आच्छादकत्वात्पयाजादीनि
> प्राच्यानि प्रतीच्यानि चांगानि वर्माणि । तानि सीव्यध्वं । दलयध्वं । कुरुध्वमित्यर्थ॥
> कीदृशानि वर्माणि । बहुला बहुलान्यनेकानि । तानि च पृथूनि विस्तीर्णानि । तथा पुरः
> पुराणि धिष्टादीनि यष्टव्यानां देवानां वा पुरः शरीराण्यायसीरयोमयवत्
> सारभूता अधृष्टा अन्यैरधृष्याः । पूरणीया ग्रहा वा पुर इत्युच्यंते । तानुक्तलक्षणान्कुरुत । वो युष्मदीयश्चमसो यज्ञाख्यो भक्षणसाधनश्चमस एव ना ।
> चमसपक्षे सामान्येनैकवचनं । मा सुस्रोत् । मा स्रवेत् ॥ स्रवतेर्लजि बहुलं
> छंदसीति तपः श्लुः ॥ तं दृंहत । दृढीकुरुत । यथा विकलो न भवति तथा कुरुत ॥
> दृह दृहि वृदौ भौवादिकः । तस्माल्लोटीदित्वान्नुम् ॥ ऋत्विक् स्तुतिपक्ष एवं ।
> वैत्वदेवपक्षे! आस्मदीया योद्धारो यूयं। नरो योद्धारः पिबंति प्रत्यर्थिनां
> प्राणानिति नृपाणः । स तादृशो यष्मदीयः खलु । गोष्ठपक्षॆ नराणां पानसाधनः
> स खलु ॥ पातेः करणाधिकरणयोश्चेति ल्युट् ॥ वर्म वर्माणि कवचानि सीव्यध्वं
> बहुलानि पृथूनि च यथा भवंति तथा । पुरः पुरीः कृणुध्वमायसीरन्यैरधृष्टाश्च ।
> वश्चमसोऽदनसाधनो मा सुस्रोत् । मा स्रवेत् । तं दृंहतेति । इति
> देवतानुग्रहलब्ध बलो वदति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O priests, make a pen (vraja)": make a cow-station for the milking of the āśir. "That is the nṛpāṇa of you": the drinking-place of the gods, the leaders, a means of the gods' drinking; for the gods drink the cow's milk and the like in the form of sāṃnāyya or of vāsira. "Sew the armours": since the vardhana-like covering covers on both sides, the prayājas and the other forward and backward limbs of the rite are the armours; sew, that is make, them. Such armours are many and broad. The "cities" (puraḥ) are the dhiṣṇya hearths and the like of the gods to be worshipped, or the bodies of the gods, made like iron, strong and unassailable by others; or the cups to be filled (grahas) are called "cities". Make them of the kind described. "Your cup", the cup that is the means of drinking: let it not leak, and make it firm so that it does not become defective. This is the priests' praise view.

On the Viśvedeva view: you are our warriors. Nṛpāṇa means "they, the men, the warriors, drink the lives of the enemies"; such a one is yours. On the cow-pen view it is the means of the men's drinking. Sew armours, so that they are many and broad; make cities of iron, unassailable by others. Let your cup, the means of eating, not leak, and make it firm. So speaks one who has gained strength through the favour of the gods.

### Printed p. 163

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- व्रजं (*vrajaṃ*)—(O priests) the cow-pen
- कृणुद्वं (*kṛṇudvaṃ*)—prepare
- स हि (*sa hi*)—that very
- वः (*vaḥ*)—your
- नृपाणः (*nṛpāṇaḥ*)—the place where the gods, the leaders, drink milk and the like
- वर्म (*varma*)—the prayājas and the like, which cover like armour
- सीव्यध्वं (*sīvyadhvaṃ*)—weave and prepare
- बहुला (*bahulā*)—many and extensive (pṛthulā)
- पुरः (*puraḥ*)—the sacrificial grounds
- आयसीः (*āyasīḥ*)—like cities made of iron
- अधृष्ट्वा (*adhṛṣṭvā*)—make them unassailable
- वः (*vaḥ*)—your
- चमसः (*camasaḥ*)—the sruk-vessel
- मा सुसोत् (*mā susot*)—let it have no hole
- तं (*taṃ*)—that
- दृंहत (*dṛṃhata*)—make firm

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O priests, prepare the cow-pen. It is the place where your leaders, the gods, drink milk and the like. Weave and prepare the armours in the form of the prayājas and the like. Make the many and extensive sacrificial ground unassailable, like cities made of iron. Let your vessel have no hole. Make it firm.

##### English translation
Construct the cow-stall, for that is the drinking-place of your leaders (the gods), fabricate armour, manifold and ample; make cities of iron and impregnable; let not the ladle leak, make it strong.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

For this Ṛk too the commentator has given two kinds of meaning: one the priests' view or the cup (camasa) view, the second the battle view.

व्रजं कृणुद्धं (*vrajaṃ kṛṇuddhaṃ*)—O priests, prepare the cow-pen for milking the cows, for use in sacrifices and the like. On the battle view: vraja means a multitude. For the sake of battle, equip the multitude of armies or soldiers with weapons and other equipment. The meaning is: gather the soldiers in one place for fighting, give them horses, cars, weapons and other equipment of war, and make them ready for battle.

स हि वः नृपाणि (*sa hi vaḥ nṛpāṇi*)—The milk drawn in that pen is fit to be drunk by the gods, the leaders of the sacrifice. Besides, the cow's milk is used in the form of sāṃnāyya, or as the āśira substance mixed with the soma juice. On the battle view: *naro yoddhāraḥ pibaṃti

### Printed p. 164
pratyarthināṃ prāṇāniti nṛpāṇaḥ | sa tādṛśo yuṣmadīyaḥ khalu |* Nṛpāṇa means the warriors who drink, that is take, the lives of the enemies who face them in battle.

वर्म सीव्यध्वं बहुला पृथूनि (*varma sīvyadhvaṃ bahulā pṛthūni*)—Varma means armour. O priests, as armour protects the body, weave extensively, that is recite well and offer, the prayāja and other oblations offered in the fire to protect the sacrifice from the asuras. On the battle view: the meaning is, O warriors, use (steel) armour freely in battle to protect your bodies from the enemies' arrows.

पुरः कृणुध्वमायसीरधृष्टाः (*puraḥ kṛṇudhvamāyasīradhṛṣṭāḥ*)—O priests, by your mantras of praise make the bodies of the gods like iron, firm, and not to be harmed by others. On the battle view: O soldiers, make our cities impenetrable to the enemies, like iron.

मावः सुस्रोत् चमसः दृंहत तं (*māvaḥ susrot camasaḥ dṛṃhata taṃ*)—O priests, make your camasa vessels not leak and make them firm. On the battle view: O warriors, make the food and the like that you eat not run short and make it strengthening; or, by the favour of the gods, make your strength firm. That is the meaning.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ वो॒ धियं॑ य॒ज्जियां॑ वर्त ऊ॒तये॒ देवा॑ दॆ॒वीं य॑ज॒तां य॒ज्जिया॑मि॒ह ।  
> सा नो॑ दुहीय॒द्यव॑सेव ग॒त्वी स॒हस्र॑धारा॒ पय॑सा म॒ही गौः॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । वः । धियं । यज्ञियां । वर्ते । ऊतये । देवाः । देवीं । यजतां । यज्ञियां । इह ।  
> सा । नः । दुहीयत् । यवसा इव । गत्वी । सहस्रधारा । पयसा । मही । गौः ॥ ९ ॥

### Printed p. 165

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे देवाः स्तोतार ऋत्विजः वो युष्माकमूतये यज्ञियां यज्ञार्हां
> धियं बुद्धिमा वर्ते । प्रवर्तये । यद्वा ! हे देवा ऋत्विजः वो यज्ञियां
> धियं युष्माकं कर्तव्यत्वेन संबंधिनीमस्माकमूतये रक्षणाया वर्ते । अपि च
> देवा एव संबोध्याः । हे देवाः वो युष्माकं धियं बुद्धिमा वर्ते । आवर्तयामि । किमर्थं । ऊतयेऽस्माकं रक्षणाय । कीदृशीं धियं । यज्ञियां
> यज्ञार्हां देवीं द्योतमानां यजतां पूज्यां ! इहास्मिन्यागेऽस्यां भूमौ
> ना । एको यज्जियामिति शब्दः पूरणः ! सा धीर्नोऽस्माकं दुहीयत् । दुह्यात् ।
> तत्र दृष्टांतः । यवसेव यवसानि भक्षयित्त्वेव गत्वी पुनर्गोष्णं गत्वा! पीतीतिवत् ।
> पयसा सहस्रधारा मही महती गौर्यथा दुग्धे तद्वत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa addresses the gods as the officiating priests who are the praisers. "O gods, for your protection I turn towards us your sacrificially worthy thought." Alternatively: O gods, the priests, I turn towards us, for our protection, your thought that is to be done by you and belongs to you, a thought worthy of sacrifice. Or again the gods alone are addressed: "O gods, I bring round your thought, for our protection." The thought is described as worthy of sacrifice, shining (divine) and worthy of worship, here at this sacrifice, on this earth. The word "यज्ञियां (*yajñiyāṃ*)" is a filler. May that thought milk out for us. The illustration: like a large cow that has eaten fodder, gone back to the cow-pen, and yields milk in a thousand streams.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देवाः (*devāḥ*)—O gods
- इह (*iha*)—here
- ऊतये (*ūtaye*)—for protection
- वः (*vaḥ*)—your
- यज्ञियां (*yajñiyāṃ*)—worthy of worship
- देवीं (*devīṃ*)—shining
- यज्ञियां (*yajñiyāṃ*)—worthy of sacrifice
- यजतां (*yajatāṃ*)—fit for worship
- धियं (*dhiyaṃ*)—your gracious mind
- आ वर्ते (*ā varte*)—I draw towards us
- यवसा (*yavasā*)—having been satisfied with grass and the like
- गत्वी (*gatvī*)—having gone to the cow-pen
- पयसा (*payasā*)—in the form of milk
- सहस्रधारा (*sahasradhārā*)—having many streams
- मही (*mahī*)—great
- गौः इव (*gauḥ iva*)—like a cow
- सा (*sā*)—that gracious mind of yours
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- दुहीयत् (*duhīyat*)—may it pour out desired things

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O gods, I draw towards us, for our protection, your gracious mind, which is worthy of worship, shining and fit for sacrifice. Like a great cow that, satisfied with grass and the like, has gone to the cow-pen and pours out many streams of milk, may your gracious mind pour out desired things for us.

##### English translation
I attract, O gods for my protection, your adorable, divine mind, which is deserving of sacrifice and worship here; may it milk forth for us, like a large cow with milk, giving a thousand streams, (having eaten) fodder and returned.

### Printed p. 166

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ तू षिं॑च॒ हरि॑मां॒ द्रोरु॒पस्थे॒ वाशी॑भिस्तक्षताश्म॒न्मया॑भिः ।  
> परि॑ ष्वजध्वं॒ दश॑ क॒क्ष्या॑भिरु॒भे धुरौ॒ प्रति॒ वह्निं॑ युनक्त ॥१०॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । तु । सिंच । हरिं । ईं । द्रोः । उपस्थे । वाशीभिः । तक्षत । अश्मन्मयाभिः ।  
> परि । स्वजध्वं । दश । कक्ष्याभिः । उभे इति । धुरौ । प्रति । वह्निं । युनक्त ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अध्वर्यो त्वमेनं हरिं हरितवर्णं सोमं द्रोर्द्रुमविकारस्य
> पात्रस्कोपस्थ उपर्या सिंच । आसेकं कुरु । त्विति पूरणः । तदर्थं हे पात्रसंपादिनः अश्मन्मयीभिरयःशिलासारभूताभिर्वाशीभिस्तक्षत पात्राणि ॥ नकारोपजनश्चांदसः।
> मयटः टित्त्वाद्‌जेप् ॥ तानि पात्राणि सोमपूर्णानि दश दशसंख्याकानि कक्ष्याभिः
> प्रकार्यैः पर्यायेण परि ष्वजध्वं । संस्कुरुध्वं । आसादयत । यद्वा । वाशीभिरश्य
> न्मयीभिर्हविर्धानशकटे तक्षत । ते च दश कक्ष्याभिर्दशसंख्याकाभी रज्जुभिः परि ष्वजध्वंउभे धुरौ युगसंबंधिन्यौ प्रति वह्नि वाहकावनड्वाहौ युनक्त । अथवायं
> मंत्रश्चयनक्षेत्रकर्षणसाधनहलाविषयतया व्याख्येयः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Adhvaryu, pour this tawny (greenish-yellow) Soma into the lap of the wooden vessel, the "dru" (wood-product); "tu" is a filler. For that purpose, O makers of vessels, fashion the vessels with adzes of stone-like, iron-hard quality. Those vessels, filled with Soma, ten in number, should in turn be encircled with girths, i.e. arranged and set in place. Alternatively: fashion with the stone adzes the Havirdhāna waggon, and gird it with ten ropes; yoke the beast of burden to both poles of the yoke. Or else this mantra may be explained as referring to ploughing, the plough being the instrument for working the field.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Adhvaryu) ईं हरिं (*īṃ hariṃ*)—this Soma
- द्रोः उपस्थी (*droḥ upasthī*)—in a vessel made of wood
- आ सिंच (*ā siṃca*)—pour
- अश्मन्मयीभिः (*aśmanmayībhiḥ*)—made of stones like steel
- वाशीभिः (*vāśībhiḥ*)—with hammers
- तक्षत (*takṣata*)—prepare the vessels
- दश कक्ष्याभिः (*daśa kakṣyābhiḥ*)—with ten cords
- परिष्वजध्वं (*pariṣvajadhvaṃ*)—encircle, bind and adorn
- उभे धुरौ (*ubhe dhurau*)—on both sides of the yoke
- वह्निं (*vahniṃ*)—the ox that bears the load
- प्रति युनक्त (*prati yunakta*)—join and bind to the yoke

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Adhvaryu, pour this Soma into the vessel made of wood. Prepare the vessels with hammers made of steel-like stones. Encircle and adorn them with ten cords. Join and bind the load-bearing ox to the yoke on both its sides.

### Printed p. 167

##### English translation
Pour out the golden-tinted Soma into the bowl of the wooden cup, fabricate it with the stone axes, gird it with ten bands, harness the beast of burden to the two poles (of the waggon).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

To explain the meaning of the word "वाशी (*vāśī*)", Yāska has given an etymology for the first half of this Ṛk:
वाशी । आ तू षिंच हरिमीं द्रोरुपस्थे वाशीभिस्तक्षताश्मन्मयाभिः ॥ आसिंच हरिं द्रोरुपस्थे । द्रुममयस्य हरिः सोमो हरितवर्णः । अयमहीतरो हरिरेतस्मादेव । वाशीभिस्तक्षताश्मन्मयाभिः । वाशीभिरश्ममयाभिरिति ना वाग्निरिति ना ॥ (*vāśī | ā tū ṣiṃca harimīṃ drorupasthe vāśībhistakṣatāśmanmayābhiḥ ‖ āsiṃca hariṃ drorupasthe | drumamayasya hariḥ somo haritavarṇaḥ | ayamahītaro hariretasmādeva | vāśībhistakṣatāśmanmayābhiḥ | vāśībhiraśmamayābhiriti nā vāgniriti nā ‖*) (Ni. 4-19)

"Ā siñca hariṃ droḥ upasthe": (O Adhvaryu) pour the Soma-juice, of shining golden hue (haritavarṇa), into the vessel made of wood (or onto the pressing-boards). "Dru" means a tree. "Dror upasthe" means in the vessel made of wood, that is, in the wooden vessel in which the Soma-juice is collected and kept. It is called the Droṇakalaśa.

"हरिः सोमो हरितवर्णः अयमपीतरो हरिरेतस्मादेव (*hariḥ somo haritavarṇaḥ ayamapītaro hariretasmādeva*)": here "हरिं (*hariṃ*)" means the Soma-juice of tawny hue. When the juice is pressed from the Soma plants it gleams like the colour of gold. The word hari also means a monkey; as the monkey's colour is also tawny, it is customary to call the monkey "hari".

"वाशीभिरश्मयीभिरिति वा वाग्निरिति वा तक्षत (*vāśībhiraśmayībhiriti vā vāgniriti vā takṣata*)": this sentence can be given two kinds of meaning. The word "अश्मयीभिः (*aśmayībhiḥ*)", formed from the root "अशू व्याप्तौ (*aśū vyāptau*)", means "all-pervading". "वाशीभिः (*vāśībhiḥ*)" means "by vāgnis marked by praise", i.e. by speeches in the form of praise. Hence: O priests, prepare this Soma with all-pervading words in the form of praise. With mantras and hymns that extol the deity Soma, praise and prepare this Soma: this is one kind of meaning.

"वाशीभिः (*vāśībhiḥ*)" also means with hammers or chisels; "अश्मयीभिः (*aśmayībhiḥ*)" means made of stone. So for the first half of this Ṛk another meaning is: O priests, pour the tawny Soma-juice into the wooden vessel made with hammers made of stone, or made of metals and the like as hard as stone

### Printed p. 168

(continuing) …that are fashioned.

#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒भे धुरौ॒ वह्नि॑रा॒पिब्द॑मानो॒ऽंतर्योने॑व चरति द्वि॒जानिः॑ ।  
> वन॒स्पतिं॒ वन॒ आस्था॑पयध्वं॒ नि षू द॑धिध्व॒मख॑नंत॒ उत्सं॑ ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उभे इति । धुरौ । वह्निः । आपिब्दमानः । अंतः । योनाइव । चरति । द्विजानिः ।  
> वनस्पतिं । वने । आ । अस्थापयध्वं । नि । सु । दधिध्वं । अखनंतः । उत्सं ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हविर्धानशकटस्योभे धुरावापिब्बमान आपिशब्दमानो वह्निर्वहनिर्वाहकोऽनड्वान् योनावंतरिव द्विजानिर्द्विजायः । तयोरंतश्चरति । तं वनस्पतिं
> वनस्पतिविकारं शकटं वने वनसंबंधिनि वृक्षे । मेथीभूत इत्यर्थः । तत्रास्थापयध्वं।
> यद्वा । वने वननीये देश आस्थापयध्वं । तत्पश्चात्तत्रत्यं सोमं सु सुष्टु नि
> दधिध्वं । सुखं यथा भवति तथा स्थापयतासंद्यां । अभिषवप्रदेशे च पश्चादुत्सं
> तद्रसमखनंतः संपादयतेत्यर्थः ॥ अथवायमपि मंत्रः कर्षणपरो व्याख्येयः।
> यस्य लांगलस्योभे धुरावापिब्बमानोऽपिशब्दमानो वह्निरनड्वान् अंतर्योना
> विवेत्यादि समानं । तादृशं वनस्पतिं वनस्पतिविकारं लांगलं वने वननीये
> वपनार्थं कर्षणप्रदेश आस्थापयध्वं । सु सुष्टु सर्वत्र तं लांगलं नि दधिध्वं ।
> उत्सं सेकसाधनं गर्तं सिक्तं वा प्रदेशमखनंतः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The ox, the bearer, making a sound, goes between the two poles of the Havirdhāna waggon, like a man with two wives between the two ("द्विजानि (*dvijāni*)" = *dvijāyaḥ*). That waggon, a product of a tree, set it up in the forest, in a tree of the forest—so, joined with a tree. Or: set it up in a wooded, pleasing place. Afterwards place the Soma there well and comfortably, and at the pressing-place dig out the juice. Or else this mantra too may be explained as referring to ploughing: the ox yoked to both poles of the plough goes between them making a sound, as above; set up that wooden plough in a pleasing place for sowing, in the ploughing area; set it down everywhere, digging a trench ("utsa", a means of irrigation) or watered ground.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- द्विजानिः (*dvijāniḥ*)—as a man with two wives is between the two
- उभे धुरौ (*ubhe dhurau*)—between the two yokes, or between the two ends of the yoke (where yoked)
- वह्निः (*vahniḥ*)—the bull that draws the cart filled with the oblation, i.e. Soma plants
- आपिब्धमानः (*āpibdhamānaḥ*)—making a sound
- अंतः योनेव (*aṃtaḥ yoneva*)—in the middle of the sacrificial ground
- चरति (*carati*)—moves about (O priests)
- वनस्पतिं (*vanaspatiṃ*)—that cart (made of wood)
- वने (*vane*)—under a tree or in the forest

### Printed p. 169

- आस्थापयध्वं (*āsthāpayadhvaṃ*)—stop it (and in that cart)
- सु नि दधिध्वं (*su ni dadhidhvaṃ*)—fill the Soma plants so that it is full (afterwards, in the place where the Soma plants are crushed)
- उत्सं (*utsaṃ*)—that Soma-juice
- अखनंतः (*akhanaṃtaḥ*)—press out

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As a man with two wives moves between them, so the bull yoked between the two ends of the yoke comes into the sacrificial altar drawing the cart filled with the oblation, that is, Soma plants. O priests, to bring the Soma plants, take that wooden cart to the forest region where the Soma plants grow and stop it there, and fill the cart with Soma plants till full. Afterwards, when the Soma plants have been brought to the altar, crush those plants and press out the juice.

##### English translation
The beast of burden pressed within the two waggon-poles, moves as if on the womb of sacrifice having two wives. Place the chariot in the wood, without digging store up the juice.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The Soma-oriented meaning of this Ṛk has been explained above; the commentator has also explained another meaning, relating to the plough, as possible.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- द्विजानि (*dvijāni*)—as a man with two wives moves between the two
- उभे धुरौ (*ubhe dhurau*)—yoked between the two ends of the plough
- वह्निः (*vahniḥ*)—the bull
- आपिब्बमानः (*āpibbamānaḥ*)—making a sound
- अंतः योनाविव (*aṃtaḥ yonāviva*)—in the middle of the field (the earth)
- चरति (*carati*)—moves (O farmers)
- वनस्पतिं (*vanaspatiṃ*)—the plough made of wood
- वने (*vane*)—in the field, for ploughing
- आस्थापयध्वं (*āsthāpayadhvaṃ*)—take and set down
- सु (*su*)—well, or everywhere
- नि दधिध्वं (*ni dadhidhvaṃ*)—plough (the earth with the plough)
- उत्सं (*utsaṃ*)—a well of water, to channel water to the field
- अखनंतः (*akhanaṃtaḥ*)—dig

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As a man with two wives moves between them, the bull yoked between the two ends of the plough draws the plough in the middle of the field, making a sound. O farmers,

### Printed p. 170

take the wooden plough there to plough the ground, and plough the ground well. Dig a well there to channel water.

#### Ṛk 12

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> कपृ॑न्नरः कपृ॒थमुद्द॑धातन चो॒दय॑त खु॒दत॒ वाज॑सातये ।  
> नि॒ष्टि॒ग्र्यः॑ पु॒त्रमा च्या॑वयो॒तय॒ इंद्रं॑ स॒बाध॑ इ॒ह सोम॑पीतये ॥ १२ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कपृत् । नरः । कपृथं । उत् । दधातन । चोदयत । खुदत । वाजसातये ।  
> निष्टिग्र्यः । पुत्रं । आ । च्यवय । ऊतये । इंद्रं । सबाधः । इह । सोमपीतये॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> षष्ठेऽहनि ब्राह्मणाच्छंसिशस्त्रे कपृन्नर इत्येषा । कपृन्नरो यद्ध प्राचीरजगंतेति चैते । आ. ८-३ । इति ॥
> हे नरो नेतार ऋत्विजः एष इंद्रः कपृत् । सुखस्य पूरको यष्टणां
> स्तोतृणां च । कमिति सखनाम । तं कपृथं सुखस्य पूरयितारमिंद्रं चोदयत ।
> प्रेरयत । खुदत । खुर्दत । क्रीडयत ॥ खुर्द क्रीडायां भौवादिकः । व्यत्ययेन
> शः । रेफलोपश्चांदसः ॥ संपिंष्ट! संस्कुरुतेत्यर्थः । किमर्थ० । वाजसातयेऽन्नस्य
> लाभाय । तदेवाह । हे होतः निष्कग्र्यः पुत्रं । निष्ठिं दितिं स्वसपत्नीं गिरतीति
> निष्टिग्रीरदितिः । तस्याः पुत्रमिंद्रं सबाधः । सह पापैः प्रयासरूपैः कर्मभिर्बाध्यंत
> इति सबाध ऋत्विजः । तथाविधा यूयं सोमपीतये तस्य सोमपानायोतयेऽस्माकं रक्षणाय वा च्यवय ॥ व्यत्ययेनैकवचनं । आच्यावयतेत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Use: on the sixth day, in the Brāhmaṇācchaṃsin's śastra, the verse "kapṛn naraḥ", and "kapṛn naro yad dha prācīr ajagantā" are these (Ā. 8-3). O leaders, priests: this Indra is "कपृत् (*kapṛt*)", the fulfiller of happiness of sacrificers and praisers ("kam" is a name for happiness). Impel (codayata) that Indra, the fulfiller of happiness. "Khudata" is "khurdata": make sport. "Khurda" is a root of the bhū class meaning play; the ending *śa* by vyatyaya, and loss of r in the Veda. Or: grind together and prepare. For what? For the winning of food. He says this: O Hotṛ, the son of Niṣṭigrī: Niṣṭigrī is Aditi, who swallows her co-wife Diti. Indra, her son, with the priests "सबाधः (*sabādhaḥ*)", who are troubled together with sins by toilsome rites. Such as you are, bring him over for the drinking of Soma, or for our protection; "cyavaya" is a singular by vyatyaya, meaning "make him come".

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- नरः (*naraḥ*)—O priests, leaders of rites; this Indra
- कपृत् (*kapṛt*)—is the giver of happiness
- कपृथं (*kapṛthaṃ*)—this Indra, the bestower of happiness
- चोदयत (*codayata*)—impel
- उद्दधातन (*uddadhātana*)—rouse up
- वाजसातये (*vājasātaye*)—for gaining food
- खुदत (*khudata*)—sport with him
- सबाधः (*sabādhaḥ*)—O priests
- निष्टिग्र्यः (*niṣṭigryaḥ*)—of Aditi
- पुत्रं (*putraṃ*)—the son
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra

### Printed p. 171

ʻAh, leaders of the rite, bring him down here for the soma-drinking and for protection.ʼ (This completes the Pratipadārtha begun on the previous page.)

- नन्नु (*nannu*)—(Indra, the son of Aditi, continuing from the previous page)
- सोमपीतये (*somapītaye*)—for the drinking of soma
- ऊतये (*ūtaye*)—for protection
- इह (*iha*)—here
- आ च्यवय (*ā cyavaya*)—make him come down

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O priests, leaders of the rite, Indra is the giver of happiness to us. Urge on this giver of happiness, stir him up. Sport with him for the gaining of food. O priests, make Indra, the son of Aditi, come down here both for the drinking of soma and for our protection.

##### English translation
Indra, you leaders, is the giver of happiness; excite the giver of happiness, stimulate him, sport with him for the acquisition of food, bring down here, O priests, Indra, the son of Nishtigri, to drink the soma juice.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

On the sixth day of the sacrifice, among the śastra-mantras to be recited by the priest called Brāhmaṇācchaṃsin, this Ṛk beginning कपृन्नरः (*kapṛnnaraḥ*) is to be recited. This is set out by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra in the sūtra कपृन्नरो यद्ध प्राचीरजगन्तेति चैते (*kapṛnnaro yaddha prācīrajaganteti caite*) (Ā. 8-3).

## Sūkta 102 (Maṇḍala 10)

**Introduction**
The seer of this sūkta is Mudgala, son of Bhramyaśva. There is a story about this seer. Once thieves carried off all the cows that Mudgala had. They left behind only one old ox, useless for any work. Mudgala did not lose heart; he yoked that very old ox to his cart and pursued the thieves. He threw ahead a club or cudgel that he had. By the power of his austerity it followed the very path the thieves had taken and led him to the place where they were. Then Mudgala beat the thieves down with that same club and brought his cows back. The seer himself tells this in the ninth Ṛk of this sūkta.

Yāska too has spoken thus on this matter: तत्रेतिहासमाचक्षते । मुद्गलो भार्म्यश्व ऋषिर्वृषभं च द्रुघणं च युक्त्वा संग्रामे व्यवहृत्याजिं जिगायॆ ॥ (*tatretihāsamācakṣate | mudgalo bhārmyaśva ṛṣirvṛṣabhaṃ ca drughaṇaṃ ca yuktvā saṃgrāme vyavahṛtyājiṃ jigāyè ‖*) (2. 9-24)

### Printed p. 172

Once the seer Mudgala, son of Bhṛmyaśva, yoked to his cart an ox and, together with it, a wooden club, and with its help won victory in battle. This is told in the ninth Ṛk of this same sūkta. It will be explained there, in the Special Topics of that Ṛk, when the nirvacana is set out. The nirvacana of the words Mudgala and Bhārmyaśva will also be given there.

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> प्र ते रथमिति द्वादशर्चं तृतीयं सूक्तं । भर्मश्वपुत्रो मुद्दलऋषिः ।
> आद्या तृतीयांत्या चेति तिस्रो बृहत्यः । शिष्टा नव त्रिष्टुभः । द्रुघणो नाम
> मुद्दरः । तद्देवत्यमिदमिंद्रदेवत्यं वा । तथा चानुक्रांतं । प्र ते मुद्दलो
> भार्म्यश्व ऋषभेण द्रुघणेन चाजिं जिगायेति दौघणं वाद्या तृतीयांत्या
> च बृहत्य इति ॥ गतो विनियोगः ॥ अत्राहुः । मुद्दलस्य हृता गावश्चोरैस्त्यक्ता
> जरद्गवं । स शिष्टं शकटे कृत्वा गत्वैक ऋजुराहवं । द्रुघणं युयुजेऽन्यत्र
> चोरमार्गनुसारकः । द्रुघणं जाग्रतः क्षिप्ता चोरेभ्यो जगृहे स्वगा इति ॥
> तथा निरुक्तेऽपीयं कथा सूचिता । मुद्दलो भार्म्यश्व ऋषिर्वृषभं च द्रुघणं
> च युक्त्वा संग्रामे व्यवहृत्याजिं जिगाय ।नि ९-२३ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta प्र ते रथं (*pra te rathaṃ*) has twelve Ṛks and is the third of its anuvāka. The seer is Mudgala, son of Bhṛmyaśva. The first, third and last verses are Bṛhatī; the remaining nine are Triṣṭubh. Drughaṇa is a name for the club (mudgara); the deity is that, or Indra. The Anukramaṇī is quoted: प्र ते मुद्दलो भार्म्यश्व ऋषभेण द्रुघणेन चाजिं जिगायेति दौघणं वाद्या तृतीयांत्या च बृहत्य इति ॥ (*pra te muddalo bhārmyaśva ṛṣabheṇa drughaṇena cājiṃ jigāyeti daughaṇaṃ vādyā tṛtīyāṃtyā ca bṛhatya iti ‖*) The viniyoga has been given already. Of this the verses say: Mudgala's cows were stolen, and the thieves left only an old ox. He put that leftover ox to his cart, went on and yoked the club to the other side; following the thieves' track he threw the club ahead against them while they were awake, and took back his cows from the thieves. The same tale is indicated in the Nirukta: मुद्दलो भार्म्यश्व ऋषिर्वृषभं च द्रुघणं च युक्त्वा संग्रामे व्यवहृत्याजिं जिगाय । (*muddalo bhārmyaśva ṛṣirvṛṣabhaṃ ca drughaṇaṃ ca yuktvā saṃgrāme vyavahṛtyājiṃ jigāya |*) (Ni. 9-23).

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta प्र ते रथं (*pra te rathaṃ*) is the third sūkta of the ninth anuvāka, with twelve Ṛks. Its seer is Mudgala, son of the seer Bhramyaśva. The first, third and last Ṛks are in the Bṛhatī metre; the other nine are Triṣṭubh. Drughaṇa means mudgara, that is, the (wooden) club. The deity of the sūkta is this club, or Indra. In the Anukramaṇī it is said: प्र ते मुद्गलो भार्म्यश्व ऋषभेण द्रुघणेन चाजिं जिगायेति द्रौघणं वाद्या तृती यांत्या च बृहत्यः (*pra te mudgalo bhārmyaśva ṛṣabheṇa drughaṇena cājiṃ jigāyeti draughaṇaṃ vādyā tṛtī yāṃtyā ca bṛhatyaḥ*). The viniyoga was stated earlier.

Concerning Mudgala, the seer of this sūkta, it is said: मुद्गलस्य हृता गावश्चोरैस्त्यक्त्वा जरद्गवं । स शिष्टं शकटे कृत्वा गत्वैक ऋजुराहवं ॥ द्रुघणं युयुजेऽन्यत्र चोरमार्गनुसारकः । द्रुघणं चाग्रतः क्षिप्त्वा चोरेभ्यो जगृहे स्वगाः ॥ इति ॥ (*mudgalasya hṛtā gāvaścoraistyaktvā jaradgavaṃ | sa śiṣṭaṃ śakaṭe kṛtvā gatvaika ṛjurāhavaṃ ‖ drughaṇaṃ yuyuje 'nyatra coramārganusārakaḥ | drughaṇaṃ cāgrataḥ kṣiptvā corebhyo jagṛhe svagāḥ ‖ iti ‖*)

Once all the cows of this seer Mudgala were taken away by thieves; they left only one old ox. Mudgala yoked that old ox to one side of his cart and the wooden club he had to the other, sat in that cart, pursued the thieves

### Printed p. 173

and fought them with that very club, conquered them and brought his cows back. Such is the tradition. In the Nirukta too it is said: मुद्दलो भार्म्यश्व ऋषिर्वृषभं च द्रुघणं च युक्त्वा संग्रामे व्यवहृत्याजिं जिगाय (*muddalo bhārmyaśva ṛṣirvṛṣabhaṃ ca drughaṇaṃ ca yuktvā saṃgrāme vyavahṛtyājiṃ jigāya*) (Ni. 9-23). We have explained the sense of this in the introduction to this very sūkta.

Sūkta 102
Maṇḍala 10; Anuvāka 9; Sūkta 102.
Aṣṭaka 8; Adhyāya 5; Varga 20, 21.
Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 12.
Seer: Mudgala Bhārmyaśva.
Deity: Drughana, or Indra.
Metre: 1, 3, 12 Bṛhatī; 2, 4–11 Triṣṭubh.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र ते॒ रथं॑ मिथू॒कृत॒मिंद्रो॑ऽवतु धृष्णु॒या ।  
> अ॒स्मिन्ना॒जौ पु॑रुहूत श्र॒वाय्ये॑ धनभ॒क्षेषु॑ नोऽव ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । ते । रथं । मिथुकृतं । इंद्रः । अवतु । धृष्णुया ।  
> अस्मिन् । आजौ । पुरुहूत । श्रवाय्ये । धनभक्षेषु । नः । अव ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मुद्दल ते रथं मिथूकृतं मिथःकृतमसहायं कृतं । अथवा मिथुरिति
> मिथ्यानाम । अश्वादिभिः शून्यं कृतं धृष्णुया धर्षणशील इंद्रोऽवतु । रक्षतु ।
> यद्वा । धृष्णुया धर्षणशीलेन वज्रेण ॥ उभयत्र सुपां सुलुगिति- सुपो
> याजादेशः ॥ अथ प्रत्यक्षकृतः । हे पुरुहूत बहुभिराहूतेंद्र अस्मिन्नाज्
> संग्रामेऽपहृतगोभिश्चोरैः कृते संग्रामे । कीदृशे श्रवाय्कॆ श्रोतव्ये विश्रुत
> इत्यर्थः ॥ श्रुदक्षीत्यादिना । उ-३-९६ । आय्यप्रत्ययः ॥ धनभक्षेष्टस्मासु गोरूपधनभजनकामेषु सत्सु । यद्वा । गोरूपधनस्य भक्षकेषु चौरेषु जेतव्येषु
> सत्सु । नोऽस्मानव । रक्ष ॥

**NOTE**

> (एलैमुद्गलने)के-निन्न । मिथूकृतं-हूडलु अश्वादिगळिल्लदॆ असहायकस्थितियल्लिरुव
> रथं-रथवन्नु अथवा गाडियन्नु ! इंद्रः-इंद्रनु:। धृष्णुया-धर्षणशीलवाद ऎंदरॆ
> शत्रुगळन्नु ऎदुरिसबल्ल वज्रायुधदिंद ! अवतु-रक्षिसलि । पुरुहूत-अनेकर आह्वानक्कॆ ।
> पात्रनाद ऎलै इंद्रने । श्रवाय्ये-प्रसिद्धवाद ! धनभक्षेषु गोरूपवाद धनप्राप्तिदाय
> कवाद अथवा गोरूपधनवन्नु पहरिसिद चोररॊडनॆ नडॆयबेकागिरुव । अस्मिन् आजौई युद्धदल्लि । वः-नम्मन्नु । अव-रक्षिसु !
> ॥ ८४ ॥
> ऎलै (मुद्दल ऋषिये) हूडलु अश्वादिगळिल्लदिरुव मत्तु असहायकस्थितियल्लिरुव निन्न
> रथवन्नु इंद्रनु तन्न शत्रुधर्षकवाद वज्रायुधद सहायदिंद रक्षिसलि. अनेकर आह्वानक्कॆ
> पात्रनाद ऎलै इंद्रने, गोरूपवाद धनवन्न पहरिसिद चोररॊडनॆ नडॆयुव प्रसिद्धवाद ई
> युद्धदल्लि नम्मन्नु (मुद्दल मॊदलादवरन्नु) रक्षिसु.

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Mudgala, may Indra, the daring one, protect your chariot मिथूकृतं (*mithūkṛtaṃ*)—made helpless, without help; or *mithuḥ* is a name meaning "false", so made empty of horses and the like. Or else, धृष्णुया (*dhṛṣṇuyā*) is the daring thunderbolt (by it). Then, addressing him directly: O Indra, called by many, in this battle fought against the thieves who carried off the cows, a battle that is श्रवाय्ये (*śravāyye*), to be heard of, renowned; while we are eager to win the wealth in the form of cows; or else while the thieves, devourers of the cow-wealth, are to be conquered—protect us.

### Printed p. 174

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मिथूकृतं (*mithūkṛtaṃ*)—left helpless, without horses and the like to yoke
- रथं (*rathaṃ*)—the chariot or cart
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- धृष्णुया (*dhṛṣṇuyā*)—with the daring thunderbolt, able to face enemies
- अवतु (*avatu*)—may he protect
- पुरुहूत (*puruhūta*)—O Indra, worthy of the invocation of many
- श्रवाय्ये (*śravāyye*)—renowned
- धनभक्षेषु (*dhanabhakṣeṣu*)—in the winning of wealth in the form of cows, or in the fight to be waged with thieves who seized the cow-wealth
- अस्मिन् आजौ (*asmin ājau*)—in this battle
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- अव (*ava*)—protect

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mudgala, may Indra protect your chariot, which is without horses and the like to yoke and is in a helpless state, with his thunderbolt that overcomes enemies. O Indra, worthy of the invocation of many, protect us (Mudgala and the others) in this famous battle waged with the thieves who carried off the wealth of cows.

##### English translation
May (Indra) by his prowess protect your waggon (Mudgala) that has no companion, defend us, O invoked of many, in this memorable conflict in the recovery of our wealth.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The story of Mudgala, and why he had to fight the thieves, is explained in the introduction to this sūkta. In such a battle the seer prays Indra to help and protect him.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उत्स्म॒ वातो॑ वहति॒ वासो॑ अस्मा॒ अधि॑रथं॒ यदज॑यत्स॒हस्रं॑ ।  
> र॒थीर॑भून्मुद्ग॒लानी॒ गवि॑ष्टौ॒ भरे॑ कृ॒तं व्य॑चेदिंद्रसे॒ना ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उज् । स्त । वातः । वहति । वासः । अस्माः । अधिरथं । यत् । अजयत् । सहस्रं ।  
> रथीः । अभूत् । मुद्दलानी । गोइष्टौ । भरे । कृतं । वि । अजेत् । इंद्रसेनाः ॥२

### Printed p. 175

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अस्या रथीरभून्मुद्गलानीति वक्षमाणत्वादस्य मुद्गलस्य पत्न्याः सारथिभूताया वासोऽंशुकं वातो वायुरद्वहति । ऊर्ध्व० प्रापयति ।श्रमजनितस्वेदापनयनाय । अथवा शीघ्ररथधावनजनितो वायुरंशुकं चालयतीत्यर्थः ।
> कदेति उच्यते । यद्यदाधिरथमधिकं रथमारुह्य सहस्रं सहस्रसंख्याकं गोसंघमजयत् तवा । यव्या गोसहस्रमजयदस्या इति वा योज्यं ॥ यद्योगाज्जयतेर्न निघातः ॥ गविष्टौ गवामपहृतानामेषणे मुद्गलानी मुद्गलस्य स्त्री
> रथीरभूत् । सारथिरभवत् ॥ छांदस आनुक् ॥ इंद्रसेना स्तुत्या परितुष्टस्येंद्रस्य
> सेना । यद्वा । इंद्रस्य शत्रूणां दारयितुर्मुद्गलस्य सेनानीरूपा मुद्गलानी ।
> भरे संग्रामे कृतं संपादितं गोसंघं व्यचेत् । व्यचिनोत् । शत्रुभ्यः
> पृथक्तवती ॥ चयतेर्लजि बहुलं छंदसीति विकरणस्य लुक् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Because the verse will say रथीरभून्मुद्गलानी (*rathīrabhūnmudgalānī*), the wind lifts upward the garment of this Mudgala's wife, who has become the charioteer, to remove the sweat born of exertion; or: the wind produced by the swift running of the chariot makes the garment flutter. When? When, mounting the great chariot, she won a thousand, a herd of a thousand cows; अस्या (*asyā*) may be construed either way (she won for him, or the thousand cows were won of him). In the quest of the stolen cows Mudgalānī, Mudgala's wife, became the charioteer. इंद्रसेना (*iṃdrasenā*) is the army of Indra, pleased by praise; or else, as the destroyer of enemies, she is Mudgala's army-leader. In battle she parted from the enemies the herd that had been won, and separated it.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत् (*yat*)—when Mudgala's wife
- अधिरथं (*adhirathaṃ*)—mounting the great chariot
- सहस्रं (*sahasraṃ*)—the many cows taken by the thieves
- अजयत् (*ajayat*)—won and brought back, then
- अस्याः (*asyāḥ*)—of this charioteer, Mudgalānī
- वासः (*vāsaḥ*)—the garment
- वातः (*vātaḥ*)—the wind
- उत् स्मवहति (*ut smavahati*)—kept blowing upward
- गविष्टौ (*gaviṣṭau*)—in the battle waged to seek and bring back the stolen cows
- मुद्दलानी (*muddalānī*)—Mudgala's wife
- रथीः (*rathīḥ*)—as the charioteer driving the chariot
- अभूत् (*abhūt*)—was
- इंद्रसेना (*iṃdrasenā*)—the army of Indra, pleased with praise; or Mudgala's wife, like Mudgala's army-leader, splitting enemies
- भरे (*bhare*)—in battle
- कृतं (*kṛtaṃ*)—the herd of cows gained
- व्यचेत् (*vyacet*)—freed from the enemies and brought back

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

In the fight with the thieves, waged to recover the cows they had stolen from Mudgala, Mudgala's wife was the charioteer driving the chariot. As she drove, the wind caused by the chariot's speed kept lifting her garment. In that battle for the cows, his wife, who was like Mudgala's army-leader, won the cows back from the thieves.

##### English translation
The wind raised her vesture when she won a thousand waggon-loads. Mudgalani was the charioteer in the quest of the cattle, the army of Indra recovered (the spoil) taken in battle.

### Printed p. 176

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अं॒तर्य॑च्छ॒ जिघां॑सतो॒ वज्र॑मिंद्राभि॒दास॑तः ।  
> दास॑स्य वा मघव॒न्नार्य॑स्य ना सनु॒तर्य॑वया व॒धं ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अंतः । यच्छ । जिघांसतः । वज्रं । इंद्र । अभिदासतः ।

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> दासस्य । वा । मघवन् । आर्यस्य । वा । सनुतः । यवय । वधं ॥ ३ ॥
> हे इंद्र जिघांसतो हंतुमिच्छतोऽभिदासतोऽभिद्रुह्यतः शत्रोर्वज्रमंतर्यच्छ । अंतर्गमय । तदेवाह । हे मघवन्धनवन्निंद्र दासस्य वोपक्षेणस्याल्पस्यार्यस्याभिगंतव्यस्य महतो ना शत्रोर्वधं । वज्रनामैतत् । हननसाधनं
> वज्रं सनुतः । अंतर्हितनामैतत् । अंतर्हितं गूढं प्रयुज्यमानं यवय ।
> पृथक्कुरु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, hold back within the thunderbolt of the enemy who wishes to kill and is hostile; make it go within. He says it: O Maghavan, wealthy Indra, whether the enemy is a दास (*dāsa*) (or an upakṣepaṇa, a petty one) or an आर्य (*ārya*) (one to be approached, a great one), separate the वधं (*vadhaṃ*) of the man. वध (*vadha*) is a name of the thunderbolt, an instrument of killing. सनुतः (*sanutaḥ*) is a name meaning "hidden": the weapon hidden, secretly used—separate it, keep it apart.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- जिघांसतः (*jighāṃsataḥ*)—of the enemy who wishes to kill us
- अभिदासतः (*abhidāsataḥ*)—who does us harm
- वज्रं (*vajraṃ*)—the weapon like a thunderbolt
- अंतः यच्छ (*aṃtaḥ yaccha*)—hold it back there (so that it does not fall upon us)
- मघवन् (*maghavan*)—O wealthy Indra
- दासस्य (*dāsasya*)—even if the enemy is a murderous dasyu
- आर्यस्य वा (*āryasya vā*)—or even one of the Āryas, of such an enemy
- सनुतः (*sanutaḥ*)—hidden away, concealed
- वधं (*vadhaṃ*)—the weapon (thunderbolt), the instrument of killing
- यवय (*yavaya*)—separate it so that it does not come to us, that is, ward it off

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, hold back there, so that it does not fall upon us, the thunderbolt-like weapon of the enemy who is hostile to us and wishes to kill us. O wealthy Indra, whether the enemy who wishes to kill us is a dasyu or an Ārya, check there the weapon he has hidden away, so that it does not come to us.

### Printed p. 177

##### English translation
Restrain, Indra, the thunderbolt of the malignant threatening (foe); ward off, Maghavan, the secret weapon (of our foe), be he Dasa or Arya.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒द्यो ह्र॒दम॑पिब॒ज्ज हृ॑र्षाणः॒ कूटं॑ स्म तृं॒हद॒भिमा॑तिमेति ।  
> प्र मु॒ष्कभा॑रः॒ श्रव॑ इ॒च्छमा॑नोऽजि॒रं बा॒हू अ॑भर॒त्सिषा॑सन् ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उद्नः । ह्रदं । अपिबत् । जर्हृषाणः । कूटं । स्म । तृंहत् । अभिमातिं । एति ।  
> प्र । मुष्कभारः । श्रवः । इच्छमानः । अजिरं । बाहू इति । अभरत् । सिसासन् ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अत्र वृषभः स्तूयते । वृषभ उद्य उदकस्य प्रदमगाधजलाशयमपिबज्जर्हृषाणोऽत्यंतं हृषितः सन् । तथा कूटं पर्वतशृंगं चोर्रैर्गॊपिधानार्थं
> निहितं तृंहत्स्म ! शृंगेण विहिंसितवान् । व्यदारयदित्यर्थः । तथा कृत्वाभिमातिमेति ।
> शत्रुं गच्छति हंतुं । पश्चान्मुष्कभारः प्रवृद्धमष्कः । प्रवृद्धस्य वृषभस्य हि मुष्कवृद्विर्भवति । तथा प्रवृद्धो वृषभः श्रवः । श्रूयत इति श्रवो यशः । इच्छमान
> इच्छन्नजिरं गमनशीलं शत्रुं बाहू बाहुभ्यां बाह्वर्वा प्राभरत् । प्रहृतवान् ।
> यद्वा । अजिरमिति क्षिप्रनाम । क्षिप्रं सिषासन् संभक्तुमिच्छन् बाहू प्राभरत् ।
> प्रसारितवानित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Here a bull is praised. The bull, greatly delighted, drank the deep pool of water. In the same way it struck with its horn and split the mountain peak (कूटं (*kūṭaṃ*)) that thieves had placed there to hide cows. Having done so, it goes against the enemy in order to kill him. Afterwards the bull is मुष्कभारः (*muṣkabhāraḥ*), "with grown testicles"; for in a full-grown bull the testicles grow. So the grown bull, desiring श्रवः (*śravaḥ*), that is, fame (what is heard), attacked the swift-moving enemy with its two arms, that is, with its forelegs. Alternatively: अजिरं (*ajiraṃ*) is a name for "quick"; desiring to obtain (सिषासन् (*siṣāsan*)) quickly, it stretched out its arms.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उद्नः (*udnaḥ*)—filled with water (the bull yoked to Mudgala's cart)
- ह्रदं (*hradaṃ*)—the very pool
- अपिबत् (*apibat*)—drank
- जर्हर्षाणः (*jarharṣāṇaḥ*)—joyfully (that is, from excess of delight)
- कूटं (*kūṭaṃ*)—the mountain peak (which thieves had set up as a cover to hide cows), that is, a great rock
- तृंहत् स्म (*tṛṃhat sma*)—gored it with its horn and threw it down (and then)
- अभिमातिं एति (*abhimātiṃ eti*)—charged forward against the enemy
- प्र मुष्कभारः (*pra muṣkabhāraḥ*)—that bull, intoxicated with mushka, that is, intoxicated with strength
- श्रवः (*śravaḥ*)—fame, that is, the approval of its master
- इच्छमानः (*icchamānaḥ*)—

### Printed p. 178

- desiring
- अजिरं (*ajiraṃ*)—the enemy who was rushing forward
- सिषासन् (*siṣāsan*)—approaching
- बाहू (*bāhū*)—with its arms, that is, with its feet (hooves)
- प्र अभरत् (*pra abharat*)—seized and harmed

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

That bull (ox), yoked to Mudgala's cart, drank all the water in the pool. Delighted by this, it gored with its horns and threw down the boulder that lay across the mouth of the cave in which thieves had hidden the cows, and charged forward against the enemy. The bull, intoxicated with excess of strength, rushed upon the enemy to win its master's approval, and trampled and harmed him with the hooves of its feet.

##### English translation
Rejoicing, he drank up the pool of water; he cleft the mountain peak, he went against the enemy; endowed with vigour, eager for fame, assailing the quick--moving (foe), he scized him with his two arms.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk is described the daring deed of the bull yoked to the cart of the sage Mudgala, which charged upon the enemy and wounded him.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> न्य॑क्रंदयन्नु प॒यंत॑ एन॒ममे॑हयन्वृष॒भं मध्य॑ आ॒जेः ।  
> तेन॒ सूभ॑र्वं श॒तव॑त्स॒हस्रं॒ गवां॒ मुद्ग॑लः प्र॒धने॑ जिगाय ॥५॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> नि । अक्रंदयन् । उपयंतः । एनं । अमेहयन् । वृषभं । मध्ये । आजेः ।  
> तेन । सूभर्वं । शतवत् । सहस्रं । गवां । मुद्गलः । प्रधने । जिगाय ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> मुद्गल आह । तेनेति तच्छब्दश्रुतेर्यच्छब्दाध्याहारः । यमेनं वृषभं
> न्यक्रंदयन् नितरां क्रंदनमकुर्वन् । के । उपयंतः समीपे गच्छंतो नरः ।
> यथा प्रचंडेन क्रंदनेन शत्रवो भीताः पलायंते तथाकुर्वन् । किं । वृषभं

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Mudgala speaks. Since the word तेन (*tena*) is heard, the word "yat" (which) must be supplied: the bull which the men, approaching, made to bellow (न्यक्रंदयन् (*nyakraṃdayan*)) repeatedly. They did so in such a way that enemies, frightened by the fierce bellowing, flee. What did they make bellow? The bull

### Printed p. 179

> वर्षकं सेक्तारमाजेः संग्रामस्य मध्येमेहयन् मूत्रपुरीषोत्सर्गं विश्रामार्थं कारितवंतः सूभर्वं । भर्वतिरत्तिकर्मा । शोभनचर्वणादिभक्षकं । तादृशो
> यथा भवति तथा कृत्वेत्यर्थः । यद्वा । एतद्द्योसहस्रविशेषणं । तेनोक्तरूपेण
> वृषभेण सूभर्वं सुष्टु यवसास्वादि शतवच्छतोपेतं गवां सहस्रं मुद्गल एतन्नामाहं प्रधने संग्रामे जिगाय । अजैषं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

— the one who sprinkles (the shedder of seed) — in the midst of the battle (आजेः (*ājeḥ*)). They made him pass urine and dung (अमेहयन् (*amehayan*)), having him do so for rest. सूभर्वं (*sūbharvaṃ*): the root भर्वति (*bharvati*) has the sense of eating; hence "a good chewer", one who eats well; the sense is that they did this so that he became such. Alternatively, this is an epithet of the thousand: by that bull as described, I, Mudgala by name, won in battle (प्रधने (*pradhane*)) a thousand cows, with a hundred added, well-fed on fodder. जिगाय (*jigāya*) means "I won".

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- आजेः (*ājeḥ*)—of the battlefield
- मध्यॆ (*madhyè*)—in the middle
- एनं वृषभं (*enaṃ vṛṣabhaṃ*)—this bull (men)
- उपयंतः (*upayaṃtaḥ*)—approaching
- नि अक्रंदयन् (*ni akraṃdayan*)—made it bellow loudly (to frighten the enemies)
- अमेहयन् (*amehayan*)—also made it pass urine and dung
- तेन (*tena*)—by this act of the bull, done in this way
- मुद्गलः (*mudgalaḥ*)—I, named Mudgala
- प्रधने (*pradhane*)—in battle
- सूभर्वं (*sūbharvaṃ*)—well-fed
- गवां शतवत् सहस्रं (*gavāṃ śatavat sahasraṃ*)—hundreds and thousands of cows
- जिगाय (*jigāya*)—have won and brought

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(Mudgala's words:) My people took this bull that was with me to the battlefield, and there made him bellow and also pass urine and dung. The enemies were thrown into confusion by the bull's bellowing. By this action of the bull, I, Mudgala, won in battle hundreds and thousands of cows, well-fed on grass, and brought them back.

##### English translation
Approaching the bull, they made him roar in the midst of the battle. I, Mudgala, have thereby gained in war hundreds and thousands of cattle well-pastured.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

For this Ṛk Yāska has given an etymology:

वृषभः । प्रजां वर्षतीति वातिबृहति रेत इति ना तद्वृषकर्मा वर्षणाद्वृ षभः ॥ तस्यैषा भवति ॥ (*vṛṣabhaḥ | prajāṃ varṣatīti vātibṛhati reta iti nā tadvṛṣakarmā varṣaṇādvṛ ṣabhaḥ ‖ tasyaiṣā bhavati ‖*) (Ni. 2 9-22)**NOTE**

"वृषभ (*vṛṣabha*)" means one who pours out. What does he pour out? He pours out the offspring, or the seed (semen) which is the cause of the birth of offspring, into the womb (of the female); hence the name vṛṣabha. Or, because he pours out seed in abundance, he is called vṛṣabha. "From pouring, vṛṣabha": the pouring out of seed is called vṛṣakarma; because he does it, he is called vṛṣabha. The Ṛk that contains this word is cited here:

### Printed p. 180

न्यक्रंदयन्नुपयंत एनमिति व्याख्यातं । अमेहयन् वृषभं मध्य आजेराजयनस्याजवनस्यॆति वा । तेन तं सूभर्वं राजानं । भर्वतिरत्तिकर्मा । तद्वा सूभर्वं सहस्रं गवां मुद्गलः प्रधने जिगाय । प्रधन इति संग्रामनाम प्रकीर्णान्यस्मिन् धनानि भवंति ॥ (*nyakraṃdayannupayaṃta enamiti vyākhyātaṃ | amehayan vṛṣabhaṃ madhya ājerājayanasyājavanasyèti vā | tena taṃ sūbharvaṃ rājānaṃ | bharvatirattikarmā | tadvā sūbharvaṃ sahasraṃ gavāṃ mudgalaḥ pradhane jigāya | pradhana iti saṃgrāmanāma prakīrṇānyasmin dhanāni bhavaṃti ‖*) (Ni. 9-23)

The meaning of the words न्यक्रंदयन्नुपयंत एनं (*nyakraṃdayannupayaṃta enaṃ*) has been explained earlier. "They made this bull pass urine and dung in the middle of the battlefield" means they made him rest for a moment. Āji means battlefield. The derivation of the word āji follows the meanings of the words आजयन (*ājayana*), a place where enemies are conquered, or आजवन (*ājavana*), a place where one moves swiftly. For this battlefield is helpful for conquering enemies, or on this battlefield soldiers rush swiftly one upon another and fight. Therefore the word āji is derived from आजयन (*ājayana*) or आजवन (*ājavana*), which carry these meanings. The sense of the sentence is that they made that bull, which was lording it like a king on such a battlefield, roar. भर्वतिरत्तिकर्मा (*bharvatirattikarmā*): the root भर्वति (*bharvati*) has the meaning "to eat". Therefore the word सूभर्वं (*sūbharvaṃ*), formed from this root, means a bull that has eaten (grass) well, one that has grazed well. With the help of such a bull the sage Mudgala won thousands of cows in battle and brought them. Prādhana means battle. For in this battle it is possible to conquer enemies and plunder the abundant wealth they possess; hence battle also has the name pradhana.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> क॒कर्द॑वे वृष॒भो यु॒क्त आ॑सी॒दवा॑वची॒त्सार॑थिरस्य के॒शी ।  
> दुधे॑युर्यु॒क्तस्य॒ द्रव॑तः स॒हान॑स ऋ॒च्छंति॑ ष्मा नि॒ष्पदो॑ मुद्द॒लानीं॑ ॥ ६ ॥

### Printed p. 181

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ककर्दवे । वृषभः । युक्तः । आसीत् । अनावचीत् । सारथिः । अस्य । केशी ।  
> दुधेः । युक्तस्य । द्रवतः । सह । अनसा । ऋच्छंति । स्म । निःपदः । मुद्दलानीं ॥६॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ककर्दवे हिंसनाय शत्रूणां वृषभो वर्षिता गौर्युक्त आसीत् । रथे
> संबद्धोऽभूत् । केशी केशवान् प्रग्रहवानस्य राज्यो रथस्य वा सारथिः । मुद्दलानीत्यर्थः । सारथ्यभिप्रायेण केशीति पुल्लिंगता । अवावचीत् । वृषभभयजनकं शत्रुभयजनकं वाक्रोशमकरोत् । अथवा । केशी केशिनी सारथिरस्य । अमुमित्यर्थः ।
> अमुं वृषभं शब्दमकारयत् ॥ वच परिभाषण इत्यस्य केवलस्यांतर्णीतण्यर्थस्य
> यज्लुगंतस्य लुजि रूपं ॥ अथवा । अस्य सारथिः सहायभूतः केशी प्रकृष्टकेशो
> वृषभोऽवावचीत् । भृशमशब्दयत् । एवंभूतस्य दुधेर्दुर्धरस्यानसा सह द्रवतः
> शीघ्रं शत्रुमध्यॆ रथेन सह धावतो वृषभस्य भयंकरशब्देन निष्पदो निर्गच्छंतो
> योद्धारो मुद्दलानीं मुद्दलस्य योषितं सारथिभूतां प्रत्यृच्छंति स्म ।
> अगच्छन्नित्यर्थः ॥ पाप्रेत्यादिनर्चादेशः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

For the harming of enemies (ककर्दवे (*kakardave*)) the bull, the sprinkler, was yoked; it was fastened to the chariot. केशी (*keśī*) means one having hair, or one holding the reins; the charioteer of this king or of this chariot, that is, Mudgalānī. The masculine form is used with the sense of "charioteer". अवावचीत् (*avāvacīt*): she uttered a cry causing fear to the bull, or causing fear to the enemies. Alternatively: केशी (*keśī*) is feminine, a woman with hair, the charioteer of this (bull); the sense is that she made this bull sound. The form is the aorist of the root वच (*vaca*) ("to speak") in the simple (non-causative) sense, with an internal causative, ending in yaṅluk. Or else: the long-maned bull, the helper of this charioteer, bellowed loudly. Of such a bull, irresistible (दुधेः (*dudheḥ*)), running swiftly with the cart in the midst of the enemy, the warriors, going out (निष्पदः (*niṣpadaḥ*)) at its fearful sound, went toward Mudgalānī, the wife of Mudgala, who was the charioteer. The sense is that they went. (A remark on the substitution of ṛc follows, which is grammar.)

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ककर्दवे (*kakardave*)—for the sake of harming enemies
- वृषभः (*vṛṣabhaḥ*)—the bull
- युक्तः आसीत् (*yuktaḥ āsīt*)—was yoked to the chariot
- केशी (*keśī*)—having long hair
- अस्य सारथिः (*asya sārathiḥ*)—Mudgalānī, the charioteer of this chariot
- अवावचीत् (*avāvacīt*)—made a loud sound to frighten the bull or the enemies
- दुधेः (*dudheḥ*)—of the unbearable
- अनसा सह (*anasā saha*)—with the chariot (cart)
- द्रवतः (*dravataḥ*)—by the fearful bellowing of this bull, which was running swiftly in the midst of the enemies
- निष्पदः (*niṣpadaḥ*)—the warriors who were running away
- मुद्दलानीं (*muddalānīṃ*)—Mudgalānī the charioteer
- ऋच्छंति स्म (*ṛcchaṃti sma*)—followed after (pursued)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

When the chariot was got ready in the battle to kill the enemies, the bull was yoked to it. Then the charioteer Mudgalānī, resplendent with her long hair, made a loud sound to urge on the bull. As that bull yoked to the cart, advancing with unbearable speed, plunged into the midst of the enemies dragging the cart,

### Printed p. 182

the soldiers, terrified by that sound and running away, followed Mudgalānī the charioteer. Or else the enemy soldiers rushed upon Mudgalānī the charioteer.

##### English translation
The bull has been yoked for the destruction of the enemy ; his long-haired yoke-fellow made him roar; rushing on with the waggon of the irresistible yoked (bull), the warriors comming forth went to Mudgalani.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The meaning of this Ṛk is somewhat difficult. It is the custom to use chariots when fighting to kill enemies. In the battle that Mudgala fought with the thieves, the single bull he had was yoked to the chariot. That chariot was driven by Mudgala's wife Mudgalānī as charioteer. Her long hair then shone so that all could see it. Those who drive a cart frighten and drive their oxen on with words like "hey"; so too the charioteer Mudgalānī, urging her chariot's bull to go swiftly, made a loud sound. This sound not only urged on her bull but also caused fear in the enemy soldiers. Or, for the sentence केशी अवावचीत् (*keśī avāvacīt*), one may say either that the long-haired bull yoked to this chariot bellowed fearfully and made a noise, or that the charioteer Mudgalānī made her bull bellow; either meaning may be given for the first half of this Ṛk.

For the second half of this Ṛk, beginning दुधेयुक्तस्य (*dudheyuktasya*), the meaning may be given thus: when the bull yoked to this chariot, dragging the chariot, was running swiftly in the midst of the enemies, the enemy soldiers, unable to withstand its force, ran off in various directions, and also rushed upon Mudgalānī, who was driving the chariot.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒त प्र॒धिमुद॑हन्नस्य वि॒द्वानुपा॑युन॒ग्वंस॑ग॒मत्र॒ शिक्ष॑न् ।  
> इंद्र॒ उदा॑व॒त्पति॒मघ्न्या॑ना॒मरं॑हत॒ पद्या॑भिः क॒कुद्मा॑न् ॥ ७ ॥

### Printed p. 183
#### Ṛk 7 (continued)

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उत । प्रधिं । उत् । अहन् । अस्य । विद्वान् । उप । आयुनक् । वंसगं । अत्र ।  
> शिक्षन् ।  
> इंद्रः । उत् । आवत् । पतिं । अघ्न्यानां । अरंहत । पद्याभिः । ककुत्ऽमान् ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उतापि च विद्वान् स मुद्दलोऽस्य रथस्य प्रधिं । प्रधिरक्षच्छिद्रकाष्ठः स च
> रथावयवानामुपलक्षकः। रथावयवमुदहन्। उदगमयत्। रथं संननाहेत्यर्थः ।
> तदनंतरमत्र रथे वंसगं वननीयगमनं वृषभं शिक्षञ्चयन्न्रज्जुषु स्थापयन्नुपायुनक् । रथसमीपे युगेऽयोजयत् ॥ युजेर्लजि तिपो हल्यादिलोपः ॥
> एवं सतींद्रो देवोऽघ्यानामहंतप्यानां गवां पतिमुदावत् । उत्कृष्टमावत् । रक्षामकरोत् । पश्चात्तकुद्मान्वृषभः पद्याभिः सरणिभिररंहत । वेगेनागमत्।
> रहिर्गत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says that the wise Mudgala raised the प्रधिं (*pradhiṃ*) of this chariot. A prādhi is the wooden piece pierced with a hole for the axle, and here it stands for the parts of the chariot generally. "Raised" (*udahan*) means he made it rise, that is, he fitted the chariot up. Then, in this chariot, he trained the bull that goes beautifully (वंसगं (*vaṃsagaṃ*)), placed it in the ropes, and yoked it (*upāyunak*) to the yoke beside the chariot. This being so, the god Indra protected (*udāvat*, "protected excellently") the lord of the inviolable cows. Afterwards the humped bull went swiftly along its paths (पद्याभिः (*padyābhiḥ*) = "by ways"); अरंहत (*araṃhata*) means "went with speed", the root rah having the sense of motion.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उत (*uta*)—and
- विद्वान् (*vidvān*)—that discerning Mudgala
- अस्य (*asya*)—of his own chariot
- प्रधि (*pradhi*)—the linchpins of the wheel
- उत् अहन् (*ut ahan*)—joined and made ready
- अत्र (*atra*)—in this chariot
- वंसगं (*vaṃsagaṃ*)—his bull of excellent gait
- शिक्षन् (*śikṣan*)—binding with a rope
- उप आयुनक् (*upa āyunak*)—yoked to the yoke (and then)
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- अघ्न्यानां (*aghnyānāṃ*)—of the cows that must not be killed
- पतिं (*patiṃ*)—to this bull, the lord
- उत् आवत् (*ut āvat*)—gave excellent protection (and then)
- ककुद्मान् (*kakudmān*)—that bull
- पद्याभिः (*padyābhiḥ*)—with its footfalls
- अरंहत (*araṃhata*)—rushed forward swiftly

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Moreover, that discerning Mudgala fitted the linchpins near the wheels of his chariot and made the chariot ready. He tied his bull of excellent gait with a rope and yoked it to the yoke of the chariot. Then Indra gave excellent protection to that bull, who is called lord of the cows. Afterwards that bull, with its excellent gait, rushed forward swiftly on the battlefield.

##### English translation
The sage (Mudgala) has fitted up the frame of the waggon, he has harnessed the bull, training him to his work : Indra protected the lord of the inviolable (cows). the buffalo rushed along with speed.**NOTE**

### Printed p. 184
#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> शु॒नम॑ष्ट्रा॒व्य॑चरत्कप॒र्दी व॑र॒त्रायां॒ दार्वा॒नह्य॑मानः ।  
> नृ॒म्मानि॑ कृ॒ण्वन्ब॒हवे॒ जना॑य॒ गाः प॑स्पशा॒नस्तवि॑षीरधत्त ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> शुनं । अष्ट्रावी । अचरत् । कपर्दि । वरत्रायां । दारु । आनह्यमानः ।  
> नृम्मानि । कृण्वन् । बहवे । जनाय । गाः । पस्पशानः । तविषीः । अधत्त ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> शुनमिति सुखनाम । सुखं यथा भवति तथा । अष्टावी । अष्टा प्रतोदः ।
> तद्वान् ॥ छांदसो विनिः ॥ कपर्दि कपर्दवानचरत् । संचरति । किं कुर्वन् ।
> वरत्रायां वध्र्यां दारु काष्ठं रथांगभूतमानह्यमानः सर्वतो बध्नन् । पुनः
> किं कुर्वन् । बहवे जनाय स्वपुत्रभृत्यादिलक्षणाय नृम्मानि धनानि शत्रुसंबंधीनि
> सुखानि वा कृण्वन् । गाः पस्पशानः स्पृशन् ॥ स्पश बाधनस्पर्शनयोः । छांदसो लिटि
> कानच् ॥ एवं कुर्वंस्तविषीर्बलान्यधत्त । धत्ते । जयं प्राप्नोतीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

शुनं (*śunaṃ*) is a name for happiness: "in such a way that there is happiness". अष्ट्रावी (*aṣṭrāvī*) is one who has the goad (aṣṭrā = pratoda), the word being formed in the Veda with the affix vini. कपर्दि (*kapardi*) is one who has braided hair. He moves about (अचरत् (*acarat*)). Doing what? Binding all round the wood, a part of the chariot, with the strap (वरत्रायां (*varatrāyāṃ*)). And again doing what? Making for the many people, that is, for his own sons, servants and the like, नृम्मानि (*nṛmmāni*), namely riches belonging to the enemy, or comforts. पस्पशानः (*paspaśānaḥ*) means touching the cows. Doing thus, he took up (अधत्त (*adhatta*)) powers (तविषीः (*taviṣīḥ*)), that is, he wins victory.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- कपर्दि (*kapardi*)—he of beautiful hair (Indra)
- शुनं (*śunaṃ*)—easily
- अष्टावी (*aṣṭāvī*)—holding the whip
- वरत्रायां दारु (*varatrāyāṃ dāru*)—the (wooden) part of the chariot, with a rope
- आनह्यमानः (*ānahyamānaḥ*)—having bound it tight
- बहवे जनाय (*bahave janāya*)—for many people
- नृम्मानि (*nṛmmāni*)—riches (or happiness)
- कृण्वन् (*kṛṇvan*)—providing
- गाः (*gāḥ*)—the cows
- पस्पशानः (*paspaśānaḥ*)—touching with affection
- अचरत् (*acarat*)—he moves about everywhere (and)
- तविषीः (*taviṣīḥ*)—his strength and capacity
- अधत्त (*adhatta*)—he bears, that is, he increases them and wins victory

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, with his beautiful braided locks, goes about with a whip in his hand. He binds the loose parts of the chariot tight with a rope. He provides wealth or happiness to many people. He pats the cows with affection. He increases his strength and wins victory.

### Printed p. 185

##### English translation
Wielding the goad (Indra) with the braided hair proceeded happily, fastening the strap to the wood (of the waggon); distributing riches to many people, caressing the oxen he acquired vigour.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इ॒मं तं प॑श्य वृष॒भस्य॒ युंजं॒ काष्ठा॑या॒ मध्ये॑ द्रुघ॒णं  ॑
> शयानं ।  
> येन॑ जि॒गाय॑ श॒तव॑त्स॒हस्रं॒ गवां॒ मुद्ग॑लः पृत॒नाज्ये॑षु ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इमं । तं । पश्य । वृषभस्य । युंजं । काष्ठायाः । मध्ये । द्रुघनं । शयानं ।  
> येन । जिगाय । शतवत् । सहस्रं । गवां । मुद्गलः । पृतनाज्येषु ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> कंचन सखायॆं सेनादिसहायाभावेन जयविषय उपहासं कुर्वाणं प्रति
> ब्रवीति । राजा द्रुघणेन वृषभेण चाजिं जिगायेत्युक्तवान् । पूर्व० वृषभो
> वर्णितः । अत्र द्रुघणो वर्ण्यते । हे सखे तमिमं द्रुघणं पश्य! कीदृशमिममिति
> उच्यते ॥ युजेरसमासे । पा. ७-१-७१ । इति नुम् ॥ युंजं । युज्यत इति
> युक् सखा । वृषभस्य सखिभूतं । वृषभो यावंतमर्थं साधयति तावंतमेव
> साधयंतमित्यर्थः । पुनः कीदृशं । काष्टाया मध्यॆ । आज्यंतोऽपि काष्टोच्यत
> इति निरुक्तं । २-१५ । आज्यंतस्य मध्यॆ । संग्राम इत्यर्थः । तत्र शयानं हंतव्यान्सर्वान्हत्वा सुखेन स्वपंतं निर्व्यापारत्वेन वर्तमानं । ईदृशं द्रुममयं घनं
> पश्येत्यर्थः । येन द्रुघणेन शतवच्छतोपेतं गवां सहस्रं पृतनाज्येषु संग्रामेषु जिगाय अहमजैषं तमिमं पश्येति समन्वयः ॥ जयतेर्लिट् । सन्‌लिटोर्जेरित्यभ्यासादुत्तरस्य कुत्वं । मिपो णल् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Mudgala speaks to a friend who mocks him about victory because he had no army or other help. The king had said that he won the battle with the द्रुघण (*drughaṇa*) (the wooden club) and the bull. Earlier the bull was described; here the club is described. "Friend, look at this club." What is it like? युंजं (*yuṃjaṃ*): that which is yoked (yujyate), a companion; it is the bull's friend, that is, it accomplishes just as much as the bull accomplishes. Again, what is it like? काष्टाया मध्यॆ (*kāṣṭāyā madhyè*): the end of a course is also called kāṣṭhā (Ni. 2-15)**NOTE**; "in the middle of the kāṣṭhā" is the battle. Lying there (शयानं (*śayānaṃ*)): having slain all who were to be slain, sleeping at ease, being without work. "Look at such a club, a solid mass of wood." The construction is: look at this club, with which I won a thousand cows together with a hundred (शतवत् (*śatavat*)) in the battles (पृतनाज्येषु (*pṛtanājyeṣu*)).

### Printed p. 186

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- येन (*yena*)—with which wooden club (O friend)
- गवां शतवत् सहस्रं (*gavāṃ śatavat sahasraṃ*)—hundreds and thousands of cows
- पृतनाज्येषु (*pṛtanājyeṣu*)—in battles
- जिगाय (*jigāya*)—I won and brought
- युंजं (*yuṃjaṃ*)—that which is used as an aid in battle
- वृषभस्य (*vṛṣabhasya*)—that is like a friend of the bull (the ox)
- काष्ठायाः मध्ये (*kāṣṭhāyāḥ madhye*)—in the middle of the battle
- शयानं (*śayānaṃ*)—that lies at ease after destroying enemies
- तं इमं द्रुघणं (*taṃ imaṃ drughaṇaṃ*)—this club of mine (or cudgel)
- पश्य (*paśya*)—look

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Friend, look at this wooden club (cudgel) that I have. With its help I conquered my enemies in battle and won thousands of cows. In battle it is a help to me like a friend. Because it bears the cart together with my bull, it is a companion to the bull too. This club, having beaten down the enemies in the middle of the battle, now lies here as if resting. Look at such a club.

##### English translation
Behold this club, the ally of the bull, resting in the midst of the conflict, with which I, Mudgala, have won hundreds and thousands of cattle in war.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

For this ṛk Yāska has given the Nirukta: इमं तं पश्य वृषभस्य सहयुजं काष्ठाया मध्ये द्रुघणं शयानं येन (*imaṃ taṃ paśya vṛṣabhasya sahayujaṃ kāṣṭhāyā madhye drughaṇaṃ śayānaṃ yena*) … भ्रमयोऽस्या अश्वभरणाद्वा (*bhramayo 'syā aśvabharaṇādvā*) **NOTE** (Ni. 9-24)

इमं तं पश्य वृषभस्य सहयुजं काष्ठाया मध्ये द्रुघणं शयानं (*imaṃ taṃ paśya vṛṣabhasya sahayujaṃ kāṣṭhāyā madhye drughaṇaṃ śayānaṃ*)—The sage Mudgala, speaking to his friend about the druhghana, the wooden club or cudgel that he has with him, has praised it: "O friend, behold this club of mine. Since I have only one bull or ox, I have come to the battlefield after yoking the ox on one side of the yoke of the cart and this club on the other side. Therefore this club is called the ox's yoke-fellow (a friend)", वृषभस्य सहयुजं (*vṛṣabhasya sahayujaṃ*). "Since I have used this club freely against the enemies in battle, it has destroyed the enemies and lies in the middle of the battlefield as if resting (as if fallen). Look at this club of mine."

### Printed p. 187
येन जिगाय शतवत् सहस्रं गवां मुद्गलः पृतनाज्येषु (*yena jigāya śatavat sahasraṃ gavāṃ mudgalaḥ pṛtanājyeṣu*)—With the help of this club I, Mudgala, have beaten down the enemies in battles and won hundreds and thousands of cows.

पृतनाज्यमिति संग्रामनाम पृतनानाम जनाद्वा जयनाद्वा (*pṛtanājyamiti saṃgrāmanāma pṛtanānāma janādvā jayanādvā*)—Pṛtanājya means battle. Pṛtanā means people, armies. The place where such armies go together (अजंति गच्छंति (*ajaṃti gacchaṃti*)), that is, meet, is called pṛtanājya, battle. Or else, जयनाद्वा (*jayanādvā*), पृथनाद्वा (*pṛthanādvā*): because armies or people win (jayanāt) in it; since people or armies fight one another here and win, it is called pṛtanājya, that is, battle.

मुद्दलो मुद्दवान्- मुद्दगिलो ना मदनं गिलतीति वा मदंगिलो वा मुदंगिलो वा (*muddalo muddavān- muddagilo nā madanaṃ gilatīti vā madaṃgilo vā mudaṃgilo vā*)**NOTE**—Mudga is the name of a grain, the green gram. Because he has green gram, he is named Mudgala. Or मुद्दलो वा (*muddalo vā*): because he is fond of eating green gram, the name Mudgala came to him.
