> न॒व॒तिं स्रो॒त्या नव॑ च॒ स्रवं॑तीर्दे॒वेभ्यो॑ गा॒तुं मनु॑षे च  
> विंदः ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सप्त । आपः । देवीः । सुरणाः । अमृक्ताः । याभिः । सिंधुं । अतरः । इंद्र । पूःभित् ।  
> नवतिं । स्रोत्याः । नव । च । स्रवंतीः । देवेभ्यः । गातुं । मनुषे । च । विंदः ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र त्वदाज्ञया सप्तैतत्संख्याका आपोऽब्रूपा देवीर्देव्यो
> गंगाद्याः सुरणाः सुष्टु रममाणाः शोभनशब्दा ना ॥ रणिः शब्दार्थः । वशरण्योरुपसंख्यानं । पा. ३-३-५८-३ । इति भावेऽप् । बहुव्रीहावाद्युदात्तं द्व्यच्छंदसीत्युत्तरपदाद्युदात्तत्वं ॥ अमृक्ता अहिंसिताः प्रवहंतीति शेषः । हे इंद्र पूर्भित्
> पुरां भेत्ता त्वं याभिर्गंगादिभिः सप्तनदीभिः सिंधुं समुद्रमतरः । प्रावर्धय
> इत्यर्थः । यद्वा । सप्त सर्पणस्वभावा उक्तलक्षणा आपो देव्यस्त्वया सृष्टाः

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, by your command the seven waters, divine rivers such as the Gaṅgā, flow well and with fair sound (सुरणाः (*suraṇāḥ*)), unharmed (अमृक्ताः (*amṛktāḥ*)); 'they flow' is to be supplied. O Indra, breaker of cities, by these seven rivers, the Gaṅgā and the rest, you crossed the ocean (सिंधुं (*siṃdhuṃ*))", that is, you made it swell. Alternatively: the seven divine waters, of winding nature, were created by you and flow; by them you crossed the river Sindhu. (The sentence continues on the next page.)

### Printed p. 222

> प्रवहंति याभिः सिंधुं नदीमतरः । किंच त्वं नवतिं नव च नवोत्तरनवतिसंख्याकाः
> स्रवंतीः प्रवहंतीः स्रोत्याः । नदीनामैतत् । नदीर्देवेभ्यो हविरर्थं मनुषे
> मनुष्याय भोगार्थं च गातुं गंतव्यं तासां मार्गं च विंदः । अलभथाः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Further, you found the ninety-and-nine flowing rivers (स्रोत्याः (*srotyāḥ*) is a name for rivers) and their path to be travelled, for the gods, for the oblation, and for man, for enjoyment.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- पूर्भित् (*pūrbhit*)—you who split the enemy cities
- याभिः (*yābhiḥ*)—by which rivers, the Gaṅgā and others
- सिंधुं (*siṃdhuṃ*)—the ocean
- अतरः (*ataraḥ*)—you made vast; such
- देवीः (*devīḥ*)—divine in nature
- सुरणाः (*suraṇāḥ*)—having a gladdening sound
- सप्त आपः (*sapta āpaḥ*)—the seven rivers, the Gaṅgā and others
- अमृक्ताः (*amṛktāḥ*)—flow unharmed
- (त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you)
- नवतिं नव (*navatiṃ nava*)—numbering ninety-nine
- स्रवंतीः (*sravaṃtīḥ*)—flowing
- स्क्रोत्याः (*skrotyāḥ*)—of the rivers
- गातुं (*gātuṃ*)—the path
- देवेभ्यः (*devebhyaḥ*)—for the gods
- मनुषे (*manuṣe*)—for men
- विंदः (*viṃdaḥ*)—you have found

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, you who split the enemy cities: the seven divine rivers, the Gaṅgā and the rest, with gladdening sound, by which you made the ocean vast, flow unharmed. You have found the paths of the ninety-nine rivers for the gods and for men.

##### English translation
The seven divine meandering rivers with which you, Indra, the destroyer of cities, replenished the ocean, flow unimpeded; you discovered the nine-and-ninety flowing rivers (and their) path for gods and men.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒पो म॒हीर॒भिश॑स्तेरमुं॒चोजा॑गरा॒स्वधि॑ दे॒व एकः॑ ।  
> इंद्र॒ यास्त्वं वृ॑त्र॒तूर्ये॑ च॒कर्थ॒ ताभि॑र्वि॒श्वायु॑स्त॒न्वं॑  
> पुपुष्याः ॥ ९ ॥

### Printed p. 223

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अपः । महीः । अभिशस्तेः । अमुंचः । अजागः । आसु । अधि । देवः । एकः ।  
> इंद्र । याः । त्वं । वृत्रतूर्ये । चकर्थ । ताभिः । विश्वआयुः । तन्वं । पुपुष्याः ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र महीर्महतीरप उदकान्यभिशस्तेरभिशंसकाद्वृत्रादमुंचः । आसु
> मुक्तास्वप्नु देवस्त्वमेक एक एवासहायः सन्नध्यजागः । अप्रमत्तो भवसि ।
> यद्वा । अधीति सप्तम्यर्थानुवादी । हे इंद्र त्वं या अपो वृत्रतूर्ये
> वृत्रहत्यायां चकर्थ करोषि ताभिरद्विर्विश्वाभिः सर्वाभिस्तं तनुं स्वकीयं शरीरं
> पुपुष्याः । पोषयेः ॥ पुष पुष्पावित्यस्य व्यत्ययेन श्लुः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, you released the great waters from the abuser (अभिशस्तेः (*abhiśasteḥ*)), that is, from Vṛtra the afflicter. Over these released waters you, the one god, alone and without helper, kept watch (अजागः (*ajāgaḥ*)), that is, you are unheedless of nothing. Alternatively, अधि (*adhi*) merely restates the sense of the locative. O Indra, with those waters, all of them, which you made in the slaying of Vṛtra, may you nourish (पुपुष्याः (*pupuṣyāḥ*)) your own body.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- महीः (*mahīḥ*)—of great nature
- अपः (*apaḥ*)—the waters
- अभिशस्त्रेः (*abhiśastreḥ*)—from the grip of the wicked Asura
- अमुंचः (*amuṃcaḥ*)—you released
- आसु (*āsu*)—in regard to these released waters
- देवः (*devaḥ*)—you, the revered one
- एकः (*ekaḥ*)—alone, without helper (without a second)
- अधि अजागः (*adhi ajāgaḥ*)—you have been watchful
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- याः (*yāḥ*)—which waters
- वृत्रतूरै (*vṛtratūrai*)—for the destruction of Vṛtra
- चकर्थ (*cakartha*)—you made the means
- ताभिः विश्वाभिः (*tābhiḥ viśvābhiḥ*)—with all those waters
- तनुं (*tanuṃ*)—your body
- पुपुष्याः (*pupuṣyāḥ*)—make it grow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, you released the waters of great nature from the grip of the wicked Asura. In regard to these released waters you alone, without any other's help, remain watchful. With all those waters which you made the means for the destruction of Vṛtra, make your body grow.

##### English translation
You released the great waters from the malingnant (obstructer), you were the only god who watched over them; with those waters wich you have employed for the destruction of Vritra, may you, all vivifying Indra, cherish your own person.

### Printed p. 224
#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वी॒रेण्यः॒ क्रतु॒रिंद्रः॑ सुश॒स्तिरु॒तापि॒ धेना॑ पुरुहू॒तमी॑ट्टे ।  
> आर्द॑यद्वृ॒त्रमकृ॑णोदु लो॒कं स॑सा॒हे श॒क्रः पृत॑ना अभि॒ष्टिः॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वीरेण्यः । क्रतुः । इंद्रः । सुशस्तिः । उत । अपि । धेना । पुरुहूतं । ईट्टे ।  
> आर्दयत् । वृत्रं । अकृणोत् । ऊं इति । लोकं । ससहे । शक्रः । पृतनाः ।  
> अभिष्टिः ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इंद्रो वीरेण्यो वीरैर्गंतव्योऽतिशयेन वीरो वा क्रतुः कर्मवान्
> सुशस्तिः शोभनस्तुतिकः । उतापि च । अपीत्यनर्थकः । अपि च धेना । वाग्नामैतत् ।
> प्रीणयित्री स्तुतिवाक् पुरुहूतमिंद्रमाट्टे । स्तौति पूजयति ना । यत उक्तलक्षणः
> अतः स्तौतीत्यर्थः । किंचायं वृत्रमावरकमेतन्नामकमसुरमार्दयत् । हतवानित्यर्थः । उ अपि च लोकं । लोक्यत इति लोकः प्रकाशः । तमकृणोत् ।
> अकरोत्॥ अवरकमसुरं हत्वा प्रकाशं कृतवानित्यर्थः । न केवलं तमेकमेव अपि
> तु शक्र इंद्रोऽभिष्ठिः शत्रूणामभिगंता पृतनाः शत्रुसेनाः ससाहे । अभ्यभवत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indra is वीरेण्यः (*vīreṇyaḥ*), one to be approached by heroes, or exceedingly heroic; क्रतुः (*kratuḥ*), a doer of deeds; सुशस्तिः (*suśastiḥ*), one of fair praise. उत (*uta*) and अपि (*api*) mean "and also" (अपि (*api*) is without sense here). धेना (*dhenā*) is a name of speech: the gratifying word of praise extols (ईट्टे (*īṭṭe*)) Indra, the much-invoked (पुरुहूतं (*puruhūtaṃ*)); that is, since he is such as described, it praises him. Further, he slew (आर्दयत् (*ārdayat*)) Vṛtra, the Asura of that name, the obstructer. And he made लोकं (*lokaṃ*), light ("that which is seen"); that is, having slain the obstructing Asura he made light. Not only him: Śakra Indra, अभिष्टिः (*abhiṣṭiḥ*), the one who advances against enemies, overcame (ससाहे (*sasāhe*)) the hostile armies (पृतनाः (*pṛtanāḥ*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- वीरेण्यः (*vīreṇyaḥ*)—foremost among heroes
- क्रतुः (*kratuḥ*)—one of extraordinary deeds
- सुशस्तिः (*suśastiḥ*)—one of excellent praise
- धेना (*dhenā*)—praise
- पुरुहूतं (*puruhūtaṃ*)—Indra, worthy of the invocation of many
- ईट्टे (*īṭṭe*)—praises
- वृत्रं (*vṛtraṃ*)—Vṛtra
- आर्दयत् (*ārdayat*)—Indra has destroyed
- लोकं (*lokaṃ*)—light
- अकृणोत् (*akṛṇot*)—has produced
- अभिष्टिः (*abhiṣṭiḥ*)—the one who confronts enemies
- शक्रः (*śakraḥ*)—the able Indra
- पृतनाः (*pṛtanāḥ*)—enemy armies
- ससाहे (*sasāhe*)—has conquered

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra is foremost among heroes, a doer of extraordinary deeds, possessed of excellent praise. Praise extols Indra, who is worthy of the invocation of many. Indra has produced light. Indra, who confronts enemies and is able, has conquered the enemy armies.

### Printed p. 225

##### English translation
Indra is the chief of heroes, the doer of great deeds, to whom praise is well offered; and the voice of praise lauds him the invoked of many. He slew Vritra, he created light; Shakra, the conqueror, has overcome (hostile) hosts.

#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> शु॒नं हु॑वेम म॒घवा॑न॒मिंद्र॑म॒स्मिन्फरे॒ नृत॑मं॒ वाज॑सात् ।  
> शृ॒ण्वंत॑मु॒ग्रमू॒तये॑ स॒मत्तु॒ घ्नंतं॑ वृ॒त्राणि॑ सं॒जितं॒ धना॑नां ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> शुनं । हुवेम । मघ्नवानं । इंद्रं । अस्मिन् । भरे । नृतमं । वाजसात् ।  
> शृण्वंतं । उग्रं । ऊतये । समत्सु । घ्नंतं । वृत्राणि । संजितं । धनानां॥११॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र वाजसात् । वाजस्यान्नस्य सातिर्लाभो यस्मिन् सोऽयं वाजसातिः।
> अस्मिन्भरे । बिभ्रति जयलक्ष्मीमनेन योद्धार इति भरः संग्रामः । तस्मिन्संग्रामे
> शुनं शूनमुत्साहेन प्रवृद्धं । यद्वा शुनमिति सुखनाम । सुखकरं मघवानं धनवंतं अत एवेंद्रं निरतिशयैश्वर्यसंपन्नं नृतमं सर्वस्य जगतोऽतिशयेन नेतारं त्वां हुवेम । कुशिका वयं यज्ञार्थमाह्वयेम । तथा शृण्वंतमस्माभिः क्रियमाणां स्तुतिं शृण्वंतमुग्रं शत्रूणां भयंकरं समत्तु संग्रामेषु वृत्राणि वृत्रोपलक्षितानि सर्वाणि रक्षांसि घ्नंतं हिंसंतं धनानां शत्रुसंबंधिनां संजितं सम्यग्वेतारं त्वामूतये रक्षणाय वयमाह्वयेम ॥ शुनं । टुओश्वि गतिवृद्ध्योरित्यस्य
> निष्ठायां यस्य विभाषेतीट् प्रतिषेधः । यजादित्वात्संप्रसारणं । दीर्घाभावश्चांदसः
> प्रत्ययस्वरः । हुवेम । ह्वयतेर्बहुलं छंदसीति संप्रसारणे कृते शपो लुकि कृते
> च लिज् रूपं । निघातः । भरे । भृञ् भरणे । घः । वृषादित्वादाद्युदात्तः ; वाजसात्!
> षणु दाने । भावे क्तिन् । जनसनेत्यादिनानुनासिकस्यात्वं । बहुव्रीहौ पूर्वपदस्वरः ।
> समत्सु । आद भक्षणे । संपूर्वात् क्विप् । संभक्षयंति योद्धर्णामायूंषीति समदः
> 15

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, वाजसात् (*vājasāt*): he in whom there is gain (सातिः (*sātiḥ*)) of food (वाज (*vāja*)) is वाजसातिः (*vājasātiḥ*). In this भरे (*bhare*)", the battle, so called because warriors bear (बिभ्रति (*bibhrati*)) the fortune of victory by it; in that battle शुनं (*śunaṃ*) is "swollen", grown through zeal. Or शुनं (*śunaṃ*) is a name for happiness, so "happiness-giving". Sāyaṇa takes the verse to say: we call you, Indra, who are wealthy (मघवानं (*maghavānaṃ*)), endowed with unsurpassed lordship, and the best leader (नृतमं (*nṛtamaṃ*)) of the whole world. We Kuśikas call you for the sacrifice. Likewise we call you who hear (शृण्वंतं (*śṛṇvaṃtaṃ*)) the praise made by us, who are fierce (उग्रं (*ugraṃ*)), terrifying to enemies, who in battles (समत्सु (*samatsu*)) slay (घ्नंतं (*ghnaṃtaṃ*)) all the Vṛtras, the Rākṣasas marked by Vṛtra's nature, and who are the complete winner (संजितं (*saṃjitaṃ*)) of the enemies' wealth (धनानां (*dhanānāṃ*)). We call you for our protection (ऊतये (*ūtaye*)). The remainder of the block is grammatical and is cut off at the end of the page.

### Printed p. 226

> संग्रामाः कृदुत्तरपदस्वरः । घ्नंतं । हन हिंसागत्योरित्यस्य शतरि गमहनेत्यादिनोपधालोपः । हो हंतेरिति घत्वं संजितं । जि जये । क्विप् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This block is the grammatical tail of the bhāṣya on the preceding Ṛk. It gives the derivations of घ्नंतं (*ghnaṃtaṃ*) and संजितं (*saṃjitaṃ*) and the accent of the compound संग्रामाः (*saṃgrāmāḥ*). It has no other content.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- नृतमं (*nṛtamaṃ*)—the best among leaders
- अस्मिन् भरे (*asmin bhare*)—in this battle
- शुनं (*śunaṃ*)—the bringer of happiness
- मघवानं (*maghavānaṃ*)—the wealthy one
- शृण्वंतं (*śṛṇvaṃtaṃ*)—the one who hears our praises
- उग्रं (*ugraṃ*)—the one of exceeding power
- समत्सु (*samatsu*)—in battles
- वृत्राणि (*vṛtrāṇi*)—enemies
- घ्नंतं (*ghnaṃtaṃ*)—the destroyer
- धनानां (*dhanānāṃ*)—of the enemies' wealth
- संजितं (*saṃjitaṃ*)—the conqueror
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- वाजसातौ (*vājasātau*)—for the sake of food
- ऊतये (*ūtaye*)—for protection
- हुवेम (*huvema*)—we call upon (him) with prayer

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

We call upon Indra, who is the best among leaders, the bringer of happiness in this battle, wealthy, hearing our praises, of exceeding power, the destroyer of enemies in battles, the conqueror of the enemies' wealth — we call upon him with prayer, for food and for protection.

##### English translation
We invoke for our protection the opulent Indra, distinguished in this combat; the leader in the food-bestowing strife, hearing (our praises), terrible in battles, the destroyer of foes, the conqueror of wealth.

## Sūkta 105 (Maṇḍala 10)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> कदेत्येकादशर्चं षष्ठं सूक्तं । ऐंद्रकुत्सपुत्रो नाम्मा दुर्मित्रो
> गुणतः सुमित्रः यद्वा नाम्मा सुमित्रो गुणतो दुर्मित्र ऋषिः । हरी यस्य
> सुयुजा वज्रं यश्चक्र इति द्वे पिपीलिकमध्ये एकादशषट्कैकादशके उष्णिहौ! अंत्या
> त्रिष्टुप् । आद्या गायत्री वा । अक्षरव्यूहेन पक्षांतर उष्णिक् । शिष्टाभिः सर्वाभिः
> सप्तोष्णिहः । तथा चानुक्रांतं । कदा कौत्सो दुमिः श्रो नाम्मा सुमित्रो गुणतः
> सुमित्रो वा ना नाम्मा दुर्मित्रो गुणत औष्णिहं हरी वज्रं पिपीलिकमध्ये त्रिष्टुबं
> श्याद्या गायत्री वेति ॥ गतो विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning कदा (*kadā*) has eleven Ṛks and is the sixth sūkta. Its seer is Durmitra, son of Kutsa, who is Durmitra by name and Sumitra by quality; or else Sumitra by name and Durmitra by quality. The deity is Indra. The second Ṛk (हरी यस्य सुयुजा (*harī yasya suyujā*)) and the Ṛk *iti dve vajraṃ yaścakra* are in the Pipīlikamadhyā metre, which has three pādas of 11, 6 and 11 syllables, and both are Uṣṇih. The last Ṛk is a Triṣṭubh. The first is a Gāyatrī, or, by the arrangement of its syllables, according to another view an Uṣṇih. All the remaining seven are Uṣṇih. The Anukramaṇī is quoted: कदा कौत्सो दुमिः श्रो नाम्मा सुमित्रो गुणतः सुमित्रो वा ना नाम्मा दुर्मित्रो गुणत औष्णिहं हरी वज्रं पिपीलिकमध्ये त्रिष्टुबं श्याद्या गायत्री वेति ॥ (*kadā kautso dumiḥ śro nāmmā sumitro guṇataḥ sumitro vā nā nāmmā durmitro guṇata auṣṇihaṃ harī vajraṃ pipīlikamadhye triṣṭubaṃ śyādyā gāyatrī veti ‖*). He adds that the viniyoga has been stated.**NOTE**

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta beginning "कदा (*kadā*)" is the sixth sūkta in the ninth anuvāka. It has eleven Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Durmitra, the son of the seer Kutsa, or Sumitra from the point of view of quality;

### Printed p. 227

or else his name may be Sumitra and, if one looks at it from the point of view of quality, he may be Durmitra. The second Ṛk of this sūkta, "हरी यस्य सुयुजा (*harī yasya suyujā*)", and the seventh Ṛk, "वज्रं यश्चक्रे (*vajraṃ yaścakre*)", are in the metre called Pipīlikamadhyā. In a Ṛk of this metre there are three pādas, having 1, 6 and 11 syllables respectively. The last Ṛk is in the Triṣṭup metre; the first Ṛk may be in the Gāyatrī metre, and by the order of arrangement of the syllables may also be in the Uṣṇik metre. The remaining seven Ṛks are in the Uṣṇik metre. In the Anukramaṇī it is said: कदा कौत्सो (*kadā kautso*) … गायत्री वेति (*gāyatrī veti*) **NOTE**. The viniyoga has been stated earlier.

##### Particulars

Maṇḍala 10; Anuvāka 9; Sūkta 105. Aṣṭaka 8; Adhyāya 5; Varga 26, 27. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 11.
Ṛṣi: कौत्सो दुर्मित्रो नाम्ना दुर्मित्रो गुणतो वा नाम्ना दुर्मित्रो गुणतः (*kautso durmitro nāmnā durmitro guṇato vā nāmnā durmitro guṇataḥ*).
Deity: Indra.
Metre: 1. Uṣṇig-Gāyatrī or. 2, 7. Pipīlikamadhyā. 3–6, 8–10. Uṣṇik. 11. Triṣṭup.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> क॒दा व॑सो स्तो॒त्रं हर्य॑त॒ आव॑ श्म॒शा रु॑ध॒द्वाः ।  
> दी॒र्घं सु॒तं वा॒ताप्या॑य ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कदा । वसो इति । स्तोत्रं । हर्यते । आ । अव । श्मशा । रुधत् । वारिति वाः ।  
> दीर्घं । सुतं । वाताप्याय ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे वसो वासयितरिंद्र स्तोत्रमस्मत्कर्तृकं हर्यते कामयमानाय
> कामयमानं त्वां ॥ क्रियाग्रहणं कर्तव्यमिति कर्मणः संप्रदानत्वाच्चतुर्थी ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Vasu", that is, Indra who causes (men) to dwell: our praise is addressed to you who desire it. हर्यते (*haryate*) means "to you, the desiring one". Because of the rule that the verbal action must be taken into account, the object is a recipient (संप्रदान (*saṃpradāna*)), and so the dative is used.

### Printed p. 228

> कदा कस्मिन्कालेव रुधत् । अवरोत्सृति । अवरुध्य च कदा वाः । वारयिष्यति ।
> तादृशः कालः कदास्माकं संभविष्यतीत्याशास्त्रे ॥ रुधेर्लुर्जरितो वेति चेरजादेशः । वारिति वृणोतेर्न्यंताल्लजि च्छांदसः शपो लुक् ॥ छंदस्युभयथेति तिप
> अर्धधातुकत्वाण्णीरनिटीति लोपे हल्ख्याभ्य इति तिलोपः । तत्र दृष्टांतः ॥ अश्नुते
> क्षेत्रमिति स्मशा कुल्या । लुप्तोपममेतत् । यथा कुल्येशस्तत उदकान्यवरुणद्धि
> अवरुध्य च वारयति तथेत्यर्थः । किमुद्दिश्यावरोध इति तत्राह । दीर्घ० सवनत्रयरूपेणायतं सुतमभिषुतं सोमं प्रति । किमर्थमिति तदाह । वाताप्याय ।
> वातेनाप्यतेऽधस्तान्निपात्यत इति वाताप्यमुदकं । तस्य प्रदानायेत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

कदा (*kadā*) means "at what time". "When will he obstruct, and having obstructed, when will he release (वाः (*vāḥ*))?" Such a time, he says, is hoped for by us — when will it come? The word स्मशा (*smaśā*) means a channel, because it reaches (अश्नुते (*aśnute*)) the field. The comparison is one with the particle of comparison dropped: as the lord of a channel holds back the water from it and, having held it back, lets it go, so it is meant here. For what purpose is the holding back? For the सुतं (*sutaṃ*), the Soma pressed, दीर्घं (*dīrghaṃ*) stretched out in the form of the three pressings. For what end? वाताप्याय (*vātāpyāya*): वाताप्य (*vātāpya*) is water, which is brought down by the wind; so the meaning is "for the giving of that water".

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वसो (*vaso*)—O Indra, giver of dwelling
- स्तोत्रं (*stotraṃ*)—the praise composed by us
- हर्यते (*haryate*)—to you who desire it
- दीर्घं (*dīrghaṃ*)—prepared in the three pressings
- सुतं (*sutaṃ*)—for the drinking of the Soma juice
- वाताप्याय (*vātāpyāya*)—for the giving of rain
- श्मशा (*śmaśā*)—as the banks of a river hold back the water
- कदा (*kadā*)—when
- अव रुधत् (*ava rudhat*)—holds back (and, having held back)
- वाः (*vāḥ*)—when does it release?

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, giver of dwelling: the praise offered by us desires you — for the drinking of the Soma juice prepared in the three pressings, and for giving us rain — when will it hold you back, as the banks of a river hold back the water? And when, having held you back, will it release you?

##### English translation
When, Vasu, (will our) praise (reach) you desiring it? (when) will he (like) a dam, obstruct and let those the long-protracted libation for the sake of wind-driven (rain)?

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The meaning that Sāyaṇa has given for this Ṛk does not fit well. The explanation of the Nirukta given by Yāska for this Ṛk appears more suitable:
श्मशा । श्मशा शु अश्नुत इति ना श्माश्नुत इति वा । अव श्मशा रुधद्वाः । अनारुधच्छ्मशा वारिति ॥ (*śmaśā | śmaśā śu aśnuta iti nā śmāśnuta iti vā | ava śmaśā rudhadvāḥ | anārudhacchmaśā vāriti ‖*) (Ni. 5-13)

### Printed p. 229

Śmāśinī or Śvāśinī means a river. Śvāśinī is "शु अशिनी (*śu aśinī*)": because it flows swiftly, the river has the name Śvāśinī; or Śmāśinī is "श्मा अशिनी (*śmā aśinī*)": because it keeps cutting away its bank little by little, the river is also called Śmāśinī. Accordingly, the word स्मशा (*smaśā*) here, as "शु अश्नुत इति ना (*śu aśnuta iti nā*)", means "flowing swiftly", or, as "श्माश्नुत इति ना (*śmāśnuta iti nā*)", means the nāḍī, that which pervades the body. The meaning that Durgācārya, the commentator on the Nirukta, has given for this Ṛk is meaningful and acceptable:
हे वसो वसुमन् इंद्र । कदा कस्मिन्काले स्तोत्रं एतन्मयोदीरितं अस्य यजमानस्य हर्यतः कामयतः कामान् प्रार्थयतः त्वां रुधत् उपरोत्सृति । स्मशा इव कुल्येवोदकं विसर्पमाणं नाडीव वा अथवा आध्यात्मिकी नाडी शरीराश्रितं रसं ! आह-कतमत् स्तोत्रं ? इति । उज्यतॆ-यदेतत् दीर्घं सुतं सोमं सत्राख्यं सुतमभिषुतं प्रवर्तते वाताप्याय उदकार्थं । एवमेतस्मिन् स्मशा इत्येतत् नाड्यभिधानं कुल्याभिधानं वाः शब्दोपपत्तेश्च इति ॥ (*he vaso vasuman iṃdra | kadā kasminkāle stotraṃ etanmayodīritaṃ asya yajamānasya haryataḥ kāmayataḥ kāmān prārthayataḥ tvāṃ rudhat uparotsṛti | smaśā iva kulyevodakaṃ visarpamāṇaṃ nāḍīva vā athavā ādhyātmikī nāḍī śarīrāśritaṃ rasaṃ ! āha-katamat stotraṃ ? iti | ujyatè-yadetat dīrghaṃ sutaṃ somaṃ satrākhyaṃ sutamabhiṣutaṃ pravartate vātāpyāya udakārthaṃ | evametasmin smaśā ityetat nāḍyabhidhānaṃ kulyābhidhānaṃ vāḥ śabdopapatteśca iti ‖*) (Simukta-Durgā-vyākhyā 5-13)**NOTE**
The meaning stated following this commentary is as follows.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वसो (*vaso*)—O wealthy Indra
- कदा (*kadā*)—when
- स्तोत्रं (*stotraṃ*)—the praise that I offer
- दीर्घं सुतं (*dīrghaṃ sutaṃ*)—for the drinking of the Soma juice pressed in the sattra sacrifices that are performed over a long time
- वाताप्याय (*vātāpyāya*)—for the sake of rain
- हर्यतः (*haryataḥ*)—you who go to the sacrificer who desires his wishes
- वाः (*vāḥ*)—the water of the river
- श्मशा (*śmaśā*)—as the banks hold it back [or: वाः (*vāḥ*)—the juices (rasa) in the body; श्मशा (*śmaśā*)—as the nāḍīs hold them back]
- (त्वां (*tvāṃ*)—you)
- आ अव रुधत् (*ā ava rudhat*)—will it hold back?

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O wealthy Indra: for the drinking of the Soma juice pressed and prepared for you in the sattra sacrifices that are performed for many days, and for the sake of rain, the praise that I offer — to you who are going to the sacrificers who desire their wishes — when will it hold you back (so that you do not go elsewhere), as the banks of a river hold back the water of the river?

### Printed p. 230

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> हरी॒ यस्य॑ सु॒युजा॒ विव्र॑ता॒ वेरर्वं॒तानु॒ शेषा॑ ।  
> उ॒भा र॒जी न के॒शिना॒ पति॒र्दन् ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> हरी इति । यस्य । सुयुजा । विव्रता । वेः । अर्वंता । अनु । शेषा ।  
> उभा । रजी इति । न । केशिना । पतिः । दन् ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यस्येंद्रस्य हरी हरितवर्णावश्च सुयुजा सुष्टु नियोज्यौ । सुशिक्षितानित्यर्थः । विव्रता । व्रतमिति कर्मनाम । विशिष्टकर्माणावर्वंतावरणकुशलौ शेपा
> शेषवंत् । मत्त्वर्थो लुप्यते । प्रशस्तपुंस्त्वावित्यर्थः । उभोभौ रजी न रजसी
> द्यावापृथिव्याविव यद्वा महांतौ रंजकौ सूर्याचंद्रमसाविन केशिन् प्रकाशमानौ
> केशवंत् वा ईदृशावश्च हे इंद्र यस्य ते तव स्वभूत् तयोः पतिः स्वामिा त्वं
> दन् धनानि प्रयच्छन्ननु नेः । स्तुतशस्त्रादीन्यनुकामयस्व ॥ दन्निति ददातेः शतरि
> च्छंदस्युभयथेत्यार्थधातुकत्वादातो लोप इट चेत्याकारलोपः । अत एव
> श्लभावश्च ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indra's two bay (green-hued) horses are सुयुजा (*suyujā*), well to be yoked, that is, well trained. विव्रता (*vivratā*): *vrataṃ* is a name for action; so, of distinguished action, skilled in carrying. शेपा (*śepā*) means "having शेप (*śepa*)"; the suffix of possession is dropped; the meaning is "excellent males". उभा रजी न (*ubhā rajī na*): both, like two रजसी (*rajasī*), heaven and earth; or else like the sun and moon, which are great and delightful. केशिना (*keśinā*): shining, or having manes. Of such horses, O Indra, which are yours, you are the lord; giving riches, desire (अनु वेः (*anu veḥ*)) our praises, recitations and the rest.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यस्य (*yasya*)—to you, whose (O Indra)
- सुयुजा (*suyujā*)—well trained
- विव्रता (*vivratā*)—of excellent action
- अर्वंता (*arvaṃtā*)—swift-going
- शेषा (*śeṣā*)—possessing vigour
- रजी न (*rajī na*)—like heaven and earth
- केशिना (*keśinā*)—and shining
- उभा हरी (*ubhā harī*)—the two bay-coloured horses are your bearers, to them
- पतिः (*patiḥ*)—you, who are the lord
- दन् (*dan*)—giving us riches
- अनु वेः (*anu veḥ*)—look with favour upon our praises and the rest

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra: you who have two bay-coloured horses as your bearers, well trained, of excellent action, swift-going, possessing vigour, and shining like heaven and earth — you who are the lord of them, giving us riches, look with favour upon our praises and the rest.

### Printed p. 231

##### English translation
(O Indra), to whom belong the two bay horses, well-trained, active, swift, courageous. brilliant as the two worlds; you who are lord (of bay horses), bestowing (wealth upon us), desire (our praises).

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अप॒ योरिंद्रः॒ पाप॑ज॒ आ मर्तो॒ न श॑श्रमा॒णो बि॑भी॒वान् ।  
> शु॒भे यद्यु॑यु॒जे तनि॑षीवान् ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अप । योः ! इंद्रः । पापजे । आ । मर्तः । न । शश्रमाणः । बिभीवान् ।  
> शुभे । यत् । युयुजे । तविषीऽवान् ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> य इंद्रः पापजे । आ इत्यनर्थकः । पापाज्जायत इति पापजो वृत्रः ।
> तस्मिन्मर्तो न मनुष्य इव शश्रमाणस्तेन सह युद्धे श्राम्यन् बिभीवान् बिभ्यच्च
> भवति ॥ श्राम्यतेश्चंदसि लिट् । तस्य व्यत्ययेन कानच् ॥ स इंद्रो यद्यदा
> तविषीवान् मरुद्भिर्बलैर्बलवान्युयुजे युज्यते संपद्यते शुभे शोभार्थं
> तदा नीमपयोः । तस्य वृत्रस्यापयोरपयोजिता भवति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The Indra who is पापजे (*pāpaje*) — the particle आ (*ā*) has no meaning — that is, Vṛtra, who is born of sin (पापाज्जायत (*pāpājjāyata*)): against him, like a mortal man (मर्तो न (*marto na*)), wearying (शश्रमाणः (*śaśramāṇaḥ*)) in battle with him, he becomes afraid (बिभीवान् (*bibhīvān*)). When Indra, who is तविषीवान् (*taviṣīvān*), strong with the forces of the Maruts, is joined (युयुजे (*yuyuje*)), that is, becomes endowed, शुभे (*śubhe*) for splendour, then he becomes the one who drives away (*apayojitā*) that Vṛtra.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- पापजे (*pāpaje*)—with Vṛtra, born as the fruit of sin
- शश्रमाणः (*śaśramāṇaḥ*)—fighting in battle
- मर्तो न (*marto na*)—like a man
- बिभीवान् (*bibhīvān*)—became afraid
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- तविषीवान् (*taviṣīvān*)—with the Maruts
- शुभे (*śubhe*)—for strength (for welfare)
- युयुजे (*yuyuje*)—he joined and shone, then
- अप योः (*apa yoḥ*)—he became able to drive Vṛtra away

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

While fighting in battle with Vṛtra, who was born as the fruit of sin, Indra became afraid like a man. But when, for the accomplishment of his task, he joined with the Maruts, who are endowed with strength, then he became able to drive Vṛtra away.

### Printed p. 232

##### English translation
Indra repelled (him), being like a mortal tired and affrighted in (the combat with) the offspring of iniquity (Vritra), when being associated with the forces (of the Maruts). he has been prepared for splendor.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सचा॒योरिंद्र॒श्चर्कृ॑ष॒ आ उ॑पान॒सः स॑प॒र्यन् ।  
> न॒दयो॒र्विव्र॑तयोः॒ शूर॒ इंद्रः॑ ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सचा । आयोः । इंद्रः । चर्कृषे । आ । उपानसः । सपर्यन् ।  
> नदयोः । विव्रतयोः । शूरः । इंद्रः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आयोः । मनुष्यनामैतत् । मनुष्यस्य स्तोतुश्चर्कृषे पुनःपुनर्विलेखनाय । धनप्रदानायेत्यर्थः । सचा सह दातवैर्धनैः सहितः । यद्वा । आयोः
> सचा सहायभूतः । उपानसोऽन उपागतवान् ॥ अत्यादयः क्रांताद्यर्थॆ द्वितीयया । म- २-२-१८-४ । इति प्रादिसमासः । अनसंतान्न पुंसकात् । पा. ५-४-१०३ । इति
> समासांतष्टच् ॥ आरूढरथ इत्यर्थः । सर्पयन् धनैः स्तोतारं पूजयन् । यद्वा ।
> व्यत्ययेन कर्तृप्रत्ययः । स्तुतिभिर्हविर्भिश्च पूज्यमानः । उक्तगुणविशिष्टः सन्ना ।
> आगच्छति । उपसर्गश्रुतेरुचितक्रियाध्याहारः । कीदृश इंद्रः । नदयोर्हेषाशब्दं
> कुर्वतोर्निव्रतयोर्विशिष्टकर्मणोरश्वयोर्नियंता शूरो विक्रांतः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

आयोः (*āyoḥ*) is a name for "man". For the man, the worshipper, चर्कृषे (*carkṛṣe*) means "for repeated drawing", that is, for giving wealth. सचा (*sacā*) means "together with" the wealth to be given. Or else, सचा (*sacā*) means "a helper" of Āyu. उपानसः (*upānasaḥ*) means one who has come to the cart (अन (*ana*)), that is, one who has mounted his chariot. सपर्यन् (*saparyan*) means honouring the worshipper with wealth. Or else, by a change of the affix to the agent form, it means being honoured with hymns and oblations. Possessed of the qualities stated, Indra "comes" (आ (*ā*)). Because the preverb is heard, the fitting verb is supplied. What kind of Indra? He is the controller of the two horses that neigh and perform distinguished work, the hero, the valiant.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- नदयोः (*nadayoḥ*)—of the two (horses) that make the neighing sound
- विव्रतयोः (*vivratayoḥ*)—that have excellent action, the master of the horses
- शूरः (*śūraḥ*)—the valiant
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- सपर्यन् (*saparyan*)—being worshipped with praises
- उपानसः (*upānasaḥ*)—having mounted the chariot
- आयोः (*āyoḥ*)—to the man (the sacrificer)
- चर्क्कृषे (*carkkṛṣe*)—for the giving of wealth
- सचा (*sacā*)—with wealth
- आ (*ā*)—comes

### Printed p. 233

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, the master of the horses that make the neighing sound and have excellent action, the valiant one, being worshipped with praises and having mounted his chariot, comes with wealth to the sacrificer for the giving of wealth.

##### English translation
Indra, the friend of man, for his exhilaration, comes mounted in his chariot. granting him favour, the hero Indra, (the driver) of the two neighing and prancing (steeds).

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अधि॒ यस्त॒स्थौ केश॑वंता॒ व्यच॑स्वंता॒ न पु॒ष्ट्यै ।  
> व॒नोति॒ शिप्रा॑भ्यां शि॒प्रिणी॑वान् ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अधि । यः । तस्थौ । केशवंता । व्यचस्वंता । न । पुष्टॆति ।  
> वनोति । शिप्राभ्यां । शिप्रिणीऽवान् ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> य इंद्रः केशवंता केशिन् व्यचस्वंता व्यचनवंतौ व्याप्तिमंतौ । नेत्यनर्थको निपातः । ईदृशावश्व पुष्टै पोषणाय यजमानस्यात्मनो वाधि तस्थौ अधितिष्ठति
> आरोहति ॥ अधिशीज् स्थानां कर्म । पा.१-४-४६।इति कर्मणोऽधिकरणसंज्ञा ॥ यद्वा!
> नेति चार्थे । तेन गुणौ समुच्चीयेते। प्रशस्तकेश् व्यचस्वंतौ चेत्यर्थः । स शिप्राभ्यां
> हनूभ्यां शिप्रिणीनाञ्प्रवानिंद्रः । एको मत्वर्थीयोऽनुवादः। वनोति । सोमान्याचते ॥ वनु याचने ॥ यद्वा । वनुष्यतिर्हंतिकर्मा । शत्रून्हिनस्ति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The Indra who mounts the two horses that are केशवंता (*keśavaṃtā*) (maned) and व्यचस्वंता (*vyacasvaṃtā*) (pervading, wide-reaching) does so पुष्टै (*puṣṭai*), for the nourishment of the sacrificer or of himself; न (*na*) is here a meaningless particle. Or else न (*na*) has the sense of "and", so that the two qualities are combined: the horses are well-maned and pervading. That Indra, शिप्राभ्यां (*śiprābhyāṃ*), with his two jaws, being शिप्रिणी (*śipriṇī*) ... [the following words are damaged] **NOTE**. वनोति (*vanoti*) means he asks for Soma (root वनु (*vanu*) in the sense of asking). Or else, वनुष्यति (*vanuṣyati*) is a verb of killing: he strikes the enemies.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—which Indra
- केशवंता (*keśavaṃtā*)—(the horses) having excellent manes
- व्यचस्वंता (*vyacasvaṃtā*)—and pervading
- पुष्टये (*puṣṭaye*)—for the nourishment of the sacrificer
- अधि तस्थौ (*adhi tasthau*)—mounts, that very

### Printed p. 234
- इंद्रनु (*iṃdranu*)—Indra [continuing the preceding line]
- शिप्रिणीवान् (*śipriṇīvān*)—having excellent cheeks
- शिप्राभ्यां (*śiprābhyāṃ*)—opening his jaws (his mouth)
- वनोति (*vanoti*)—desires the Soma

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, who mounts the horses with excellent manes and wide-reaching, to bring nourishment to the sacrificer, having excellent cheeks, opens his jaws (his mouth) and asks for the Soma.

##### English translation
He who has mounted the two long-maned and all-pervading steeds for the nourishment (of the sacrificer) asks (for the Soma with his two jaws, possesing mighty jaws.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्रास्तौ॑दृ॒ष्ट्वा जा॑ ऋ॒स्वेभि॑स्त॒तक्ष॒ शूरः॒ शव॑सा ।  
> ऋ॒भुर्न क्रतु॑भिर्मात॒रिश्वा॑ ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । अस्तौत् । ऋष्वओजाः । ऋष्वेभिः । ततक्ष । शूरः । शवसा ॥  
> ऋभुः । न । क्रतुभिः । मातरिश्वा ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ऋष्टौजा दर्शनीयबलो व्याप्तबलो वर्षेभिर्दर्शनीयैर्मरुद्भिः सह
> प्रास्तौत् । स्तुत्यादिकं सम्यकृतमनेनेति प्रशंसति । यद्वा । कर्मणि कर्तृप्रत्ययः ।
> प्रकर्षेण स्तुतोऽभूत् । य इंद्रः शूरो विक्रांतो मातरिश्वा वृष्टेर्निर्मातर्यंतरिक्षे श्वसन्वर्तमानः सङ्ज्यवसा बलेन क्रतुभिः क्रियमाणैः कर्मभिः । यद्वा ।
> क्रतुरिति प्रज्ञानाम । बलेन प्रज्ञाभिश्च ततक्ष वृत्रवधादिकं चकार । तत्र दृष्टांतः ।
> ऋभुर्नर्भव इव ॥ सुपां सुलुगिति जसः सुरादेशः ॥ ऋभवो यथा कर्मभिः प्रज्ञाभिश्चानन्यसाधारणरथचमसादिनिर्माणमकुर्वन् तद्वदित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

ऋष्टौजा (*ṛṣṭaujā*): he who has beautiful strength, pervading strength, प्रास्तौत् (*prāstaut*), praised together with the beautiful Maruts: "the praise and the rest were well done by him", so he commends. Or else, with the agent affix in the passive sense, he was greatly praised. That Indra, the hero, the valiant, मातरिश्वा (*mātariśvā*), the maker of rain, who moves breathing in the atmosphere, ततक्ष (*tatakṣa*), wrought the slaying of Vṛtra and the like, with strength and with क्रतुभिः (*kratubhiḥ*), the actions that are performed. Or else क्रतु (*kratu*) is a name for wisdom, so: with strength and with wisdom. The illustration: ऋभुर्न (*ṛbhurna*), like the Ṛbhu(s): as the Ṛbhus by their actions and wisdom made the unequalled chariot, cups and the like, so he did.

### Printed p. 235

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- शूरः (*śūraḥ*)—the valiant
- ऋष्वजाः (*ṛṣvajāḥ*)—having attractive strength
- ऋष्वभिः (*ṛṣvabhiḥ*)—joined with the radiant Maruts, Indra
- प्रास्तौत् (*prāstaut*)—has been praised
- ऋभुः न (*ṛbhuḥ na*)—as the Ṛbhus accomplished an extraordinary work by their wisdom
- मातरिश्वा (*mātariśvā*)—Indra, abiding in the atmosphere
- शवसा (*śavasā*)—with his strength
- क्रतुभिः (*kratubhiḥ*)—and with excellent wisdom
- ततक्ष (*tatakṣa*)—accomplished the work of Vṛtra-slaying and the like

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, the valiant, of attractive strength, joined with the radiant Maruts, has been praised. As the Ṛbhus accomplished an extraordinary work by their wisdom, so Indra, abiding in the atmosphere, accomplished the work of Vṛtra-slaying and the like with his strength and with excellent wisdom.

##### English translation
Of glorious strength (associated) with the glorious (Maruts) he was re-
nowned; the hero abiding in the firmament wrought with his strength as the
Ribhus with their wonderful works.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वज्रं॒ यश्च॒क्रे सु॒हना॑य॒ दस्य॑वे हिरीम॒शो हिरी॑मान् ।  
> अरु॑तहनु॒रद्भु॑तं॒ न रजः॑ ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वज्रं । यः । चक्रे । सुहनाय । दस्यवे । हिरीमशः । हिरीमान् ।  
> अरुत्‌हनुः । अद्भुतं । न । रजः ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> य इंद्रः सुहनाय सुष्टु हननीयाय दस्यव उपक्षपयित्रे शत्रवे तस्य
> वधाय वज्रं प्रहरणसाधनमायुधं चक्रे कृतवान् स त्वं वृजिना शिशीहीत्युत्तरसंबंधः । कीदृश इंद्रः । हिरीमशो हरितश्मश्रुः ॥ पृषोदरादिः ॥ हिरीमान् हरि-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The Indra who made the vajra, the weapon, the instrument of striking, for the slaying of the दस्यवे (*dasyave*), the harassing enemy who is easily slain (सुहनाय (*suhanāya*)): that same one, "you destroy our sins" (वृजिना शिशीहि (*vṛjinā śiśīhi*)), this being the connection with the next verse. What kind of Indra? हिरीमशः (*hirīmaśaḥ*): green- (golden-) bearded.

### Printed p. 236

> ताश्वो हरितवर्णो ना अरुतहनुरबाधितहनुः । किमिव । अद्भुतं न रजः । अद्भुतमिति
> महन्नाम । रज इति रंजकमंतरिक्षमुच्यते । महदंतरिक्षमिन । तद्यथान्यैरबाध्यं
> भवति तद्वत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

हिरीमान् (*hirīmān*): possessing tawny horses, of tawny colour; अरुतहनुः (*arutahanuḥ*): of unobstructed jaws. Like what? अद्भुतं न रजः (*adbhutaṃ na rajaḥ*). अद्भुत (*adbhuta*) is a name for "great"; रजः (*rajaḥ*) is said to be the colour-giving atmosphere: like the great atmosphere. As that is unobstructed by others, so is he.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- हिरीमशः (*hirīmaśaḥ*)—having golden-coloured hair
- हिरीमान् (*hirīmān*)—yoked with tawny-coloured horses
- अद्भुतं (*adbhutaṃ*)—great
- रजः न (*rajaḥ na*)—like the atmosphere
- अरुतहनुः (*arutahanuḥ*)—having unharmed cheeks
- यः (*yaḥ*)—that very Indra
- सुहनाय (*suhanāya*)—who deserves to be slain
- दस्यवे (*dasyave*)—for Vṛtra
- वज्रं (*vajraṃ*)—the thunderbolt weapon
- चक्रे (*cakre*)—made sharp

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, who has golden-coloured hair, is yoked with tawny-coloured horses, and has unharmed cheeks like the great atmosphere, made his thunderbolt sharp for Vṛtra, who deserved to be slain.

##### English translation
The golden-bearded (Indra), lord of bay horses, who made the thunder-
bolt for the easy destruction of the Dasyu, who has irresistible jaws like the
vast firmament.

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अव॑ नो वृजि॒ना शि॑शीहि ॒चा व॑नेमा॒नृचः॑ ।  
> नाब्र॑ह्मा य॒ज्ञ ऋध॒गोष॑ति॒ त्वे ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अव । नः । वृजिना । शिशीहि । ऋचा । वनेम । अनृचः ।  
> न । अब्रह्मा । यज्ञः । ऋधक् । जोषति । त्वे इति ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र नोऽस्माकं वृजिना वृजिनानि वर्जनीयानि पापान्यव शिशीहि । अत्यर्थं
> तनूकुरु । विनाशयेत्यर्थः । वयं चर्चा स्तुत्या साधनेनानृचोऽस्तुतिकानयजमानान्वनेम । हिंसाम । अब्रह्मा । ब्रह्म परिवृढं स्तोत्रं । स्तुतिविरहितो यज्ञ

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, वृजिना (*vṛjinā*), our sins, which are to be avoided, अव शिशीहि (*ava śiśīhi*): make them exceedingly thin, that is, destroy them. We, by ऋचा (*ṛcā*), the hymn as the means, वनेम (*vanema*), may harm अनृचः (*anṛcaḥ*), the sacrificers who have no hymns. अब्रह्मा (*abrahmā*): ब्रह्म (*brahma*) is the enlarged hymn of praise; the sacrifice that is without praise,

### Printed p. 237

> ऋधक् सस्तुतिकाद्यज्ञात्प्रथग्भूतस्त्वे त्वयि न जोषति । न सेवते त्वां । न प्रीणयतीत्यर्थः ॥ जुषी प्रीतिसेवनयोः । व्यत्ययेन शप् परस्मैपदं च ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

ऋधक् (*ṛdhak*), being separate from the sacrifice with praise, does not delight in you (त्वे (*tve*)): it does not serve you, it does not please you.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- नः (*naḥ*)—our (O Indra)
- वृजिना (*vṛjinā*)—sins
- अव शिशीहि (*ava śiśīhi*)—destroy
- ऋचा (*ṛcā*)—together with our hymns
- अनृचः (*anṛcaḥ*)—those who are not praisers
- वनेम (*vanema*)—may we harm
- अब्रह्मा (*abrahmā*)—not accompanied by praise
- यज्ञः (*yajñaḥ*)—worship
- यधक् (*yadhak*)—being different from worship accompanied by praise
- तै (*tai*)—in you
- न जोषति (*na joṣati*)—does not produce satisfaction

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, destroy our sins. Together with our hymns, may we harm those who are not praisers. Because worship without praise is different from worship accompanied by praise, it does not produce satisfaction in you.

##### English translation
Comminute, Indra our iniquities, may we, by our hymns, exterminate those who offers no hymns. The sacrifice, unaccompained by prayer, distinct (from that which is performed with prayer), is not grateful to you.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ऊ॒र्ध्वा यत्ते॑ त्रे॒तिनी॒ भूद्य॒ज्ञस्य॑ धू॒र्षु सद्म॑न् ।  
> स॒जूर्नावं॒ स्वय॑शसं॒ सचा॒योः ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ऊर्ध्वा । यत् । ते । त्रेतिनी । भूत् । यज्ञस्य । धूःसु । सद्मन् ।  
> सजूः । नावं । स्वयशसं । सचा । आयोः ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र ते तव स्वभूता त्रेतिनी । त्रेताग्नित्रयं । तद्वती त्रेतिनी क्रियाः । सा
> यद्यदोर्ध्वर्न्नता प्रवृत्ता भूत् अभूत् ॥ यद्योगादनिघातः ॥ कुत्र स्थान इति: ।
> यज्ञस्य यागस्य सद्मन् सदने । केष्टिति । धूर्षु कर्मणां वोढृष्टृत्विसु । तदानीं

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, त्रेतिनी (*tretinī*), your own: त्रेता (*tretā*) is the triad of fires, and the rite possessing them is त्रेतिनी (*tretinī*). When it *ūrdhvā ... bhūt*, has risen up, comes forth. Where? *yajñasya ... sadman*, in the seat of the sacrifice; among whom? धूर्षु (*dhūrṣu*), among the priests, the bearers of the rites. Then

### Printed p. 238

> सजूर्यजमानेन मरुद्भिर्वा सह प्रीयमाणो वा सन्नायोर्मनुष्यस्य यजमानस्य सचा सह तेन साकं स्वयशसं स्वायत्तयशस्वं यशःसाधनं नावं तरणसाधनं
> तरणिं । आरोहसीति शेषः । एकमेव रथं यजमान आरोहति स्वतरणार्थं तमेव
> तस्य साधकमारोहसीत्यर्थः । यद्वा । नावं रथं स्वयशसमायोर्यजमानस्य
> सचा सहायभूतः सजूस्तेन सह प्रीयमाण आरोहेत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

सजूः (*sajūḥ*), being pleased together with the sacrificer or with the Maruts, together with आयोः (*āyoḥ*), the man, the sacrificer, सचा (*sacā*), with him, स्वयशसं (*svayaśasaṃ*), the vessel whose glory is in one's own hand, the means of glory, नावं (*nāvaṃ*), the means of crossing, the boat: "you ascend" is the remainder to be supplied. The sacrificer mounts one and the same chariot for his own crossing; that very instrument of his you mount. Or else: नावं (*nāvaṃ*) is the chariot; स्वयशसं (*svayaśasaṃ*); as the companion (सचा (*sacā*)) of the sacrificer Āyu, सजूः (*sajūḥ*), pleased together with him, mount it: such is the meaning.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यज्ञस्य (*yajñasya*)—of the sacrifice (O Indra)
- सद्मन् (*sadman*)—in the place
- धूर्षु (*dhūrṣu*)—among the priests who carry out the rites
- ते (*te*)—the rite that worships you
- त्रेतिनी (*tretinī*)—consisting of the three fires
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- ऊर्ध्वा भूत् (*ūrdhvā bhūt*)—becomes upward-turned (is performed), then
- सजूः (*sajūḥ*)—together with the Maruts
- आयोः सचा (*āyoḥ sacā*)—with the sacrificer
- स्वयशसः (*svayaśasaḥ*)—which is in one's own hand, the means of glory
- नावं (*nāvaṃ*)—you will mount the boat

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, when in the place of the sacrifice, among the priests who carry out the rites, the rite consisting of the three fires, which worships you, becomes upward-turned, then, together with the Maruts and with the sacrificer, you will mount the boat that is in one's own hand and is the means of glory.

##### English translation
When the ceremony of the three fires is instituted for you by those who support (the burden of the rite) in the hall of sacrifice, accompanied by the sacrifice, you, the friend of man, (ascend) your vessel of the glory.

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> श्रि॒ये ते॒ पृत्नि॑रुप॒सेच॑नी भूच्छ्रि॒ये दर्वि॑ररे॒पाः ।  
> यया॒ स्वे पात्रे॑ सिं॒चस॒ उत् ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> क्रिये । ते । पृश्निः । उपसेचनी । भूत् । क्रिये । दर्विः । अरेपाः ।  
> यया । स्वे । पात्रे । सिंचसे । उत् ॥ १० ॥

### Printed p. 239

#### Ṛk 10 (continued)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र ते तव संबंधिनः सोमस्य श्रिये श्रयणाय पृश्निर्गौः पृश्निवर्णोपसेचनी पयसो दॊग्नि भूत् । भवतु । तथा दर्विररणिररेपा अपापा सती
> ते श्रिये हविषः श्रयणाय भूत् । भवतु । यया दर्न्या स्वे स्वकीये पात्रे
> पानसाधने मुख उत्सिंचसे उपर्याकृष्ण पिबसि सा श्रिये भूदिति संबंधः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, for the sake of the "mixing" (श्रिये (*śriye*), "resorting to") of your Soma, let the spotted cow (पृश्निः (*pṛśniḥ*), a cow of dappled colour) be the one that yields milk for sprinkling it. Likewise let the ladle (दर्विः (*darviḥ*), the churning-stick), free from fault (अरेपाः (*arepāḥ*), "sinless"), serve for the mixing of your oblation. The ladle with which you pour the Soma upward into your own vessel, that is, into your mouth, the instrument of drinking, drawing it up and drinking it — let that be for mixing. This is how the sentence is connected.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ते (*te*)—with your Soma
- श्रिये (*śriye*)—for mixing
- पृश्निः (*pṛśniḥ*)—the cow of spotted colour
- उपसेचनी (*upasecanī*)—the giver of milk
- भूत् (*bhūt*)—let her be
- यया (*yayā*)—with which ladle
- स्वे पात्रे (*sve pātre*)—in your mouth, the means of drinking
- उत् सिंचसे (*ut siṃcase*)—you pour out the Soma
- दर्विः (*darviḥ*)—that same ladle
- अरेपाः (*arepāḥ*)—free from fault
- श्रिये (*śriye*)—fit for mixing the oblation
- भूत् (*bhūt*)—let it be

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, for mixing with your Soma, let the spotted cow give milk. Let the ladle, the means of pouring the Soma into your mouth, be free from fault and fit for drinking.

##### English translation
May the spotted cow yield milk for mixing with your (Soma), may the ladle for the mixture wherewith you pour it into your own vessel be free from defect.

#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> श॒तं वा॒ यद॑सुर्य॒ प्रति॑ त्वा सुमि॒त्र इ॒त्थास्ता॑द्दुर्मि॒त्र इ॒त्थास्तौ॑त् ।  
> आवो॒ यद्द॑स्यु॒हत्ये॑ कुत्सपु॒त्रं प्रावो॒ यद्द॑स्यु॒हत्ये॑ कुत्स्व॒त्सं ॥ ११ ॥

### Printed p. 240

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> शतं । वा । यत् । असुर्य । प्रति । त्वा । सुमित्रः । इत्था । अस्तौत् । दुःमित्रः ।  
> इत्था । अस्तौत् ॥  
> आवः । यत् । दस्युहत्ये । कुत्सपुत्रं । प्र । आवः । यत् । दस्युहत्ये । कुत्सवत्सं॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे असुर्य बलवन्निंद्र । अवध्यत्वेनासुरसंबंधित्वात् । त्वा त्वां प्रति शतं
> ना शतसंख्याकं धनं । वाशब्देनापरिमितं धनं ना । यद्यदा कामितवानित्यर्थः ।
> यद्यदा च दस्युहत्ये दस्युनने शत्रुवधे कुत्सपुत्रं दुर्मित्रमावः रक्षितवानसि ।
> तथा कुत्सवत्सं कुत्रपुत्रं प्रावः प्रकर्षण रक्षितवानसि ॥ अव रक्षणादौ । लज्याडजादीनामित्याडागमः । पादादित्वादनिघातः। पुनरुक्तिरादरार्थ०॥ तदानीं सुमित्रो
> नाम्मेष्ठमस्तौत् । तथा दुर्मित्रो गुणत इत्थमस्तौत् । तद्विपरीतं वा द्रष्टव्यं ।
> सुमित्रो नाम्मा दुर्मित्रो गुणत इति कात्यायनेन तथोक्तेः । स इत्थेष्ठमनेन
> कृतप्रकारेणास्तौत् । अस्तावीत् । इत्थास्तौदिति द्विरुक्तिः स्तुतिसमाप्त्यर्थ० ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O mighty Indra (असुर्य (*asurya*); as unslayable he belongs to the asura-power), "towards you" (त्वा प्रति (*tvā prati*)) a hundred, that is, a hundredfold wealth; the word वा (*vā*) shows that the wealth is immeasurable. "When" (यत् (*yat*)) he desired it — that is the meaning — and when, at the slaughter of the Dasyus, the killing of enemies, you protected Kutsa's son Durmitra; and likewise when you protected Kutsa's darling, the son of Kutsa, with special favour: then Sumitra praised you thus by name, and Durmitra praised you thus by quality. Or else it is to be seen the other way round, since Kātyāyana says so: "Sumitra by name, Durmitra by quality". He praised in the manner just described. The doubled इत्थास्तौत् (*itthāstaut*) is for the completion of the praise; the repetition of the other words is for emphasis.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- असुर्य (*asurya*)—O Indra, possessed of power
- शतं वा (*śataṃ vā*)—wealth immeasurable, asking that it be given
- त्वा प्रति (*tvā prati*)—you
- यत् (*yat*)—when I prayed (and)
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- दस्युहत्ये (*dasyuhatye*)—at the time of slaying the enemies
- कुत्सपुत्रं (*kutsaputraṃ*)—Durmitra, son of the sage Kutsa
- आवः (*āvaḥ*)—you protected (likewise)
- कुत्सवत्सं (*kutsavatsaṃ*)—Kutsa's son
- प्रावः (*prāvaḥ*)—you protected, then
- सुमित्रः (*sumitraḥ*)—the sage named Sumitra
- इत्या (*ityā*)—in this manner, you
- अस्तौत् (*astaut*)—praised (and)
- दुर्मित्रः (*durmitraḥ*)—that same sage called Durmitra
- इत्या (*ityā*)—in this manner
- अस्तौत् (*astaut*)—praised

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O mighty Indra, when he prayed to you to give immeasurable wealth, when you protected Kutsa's son Durmitra or Sumitra at the slaughter of the enemy Dasyus, the sage named Sumitra praised you in this manner. That same sage Sumitra, bearing the name Durmitra, praised you in this manner.

### Printed p. 241

##### English translation
When you mighty (Indra, gave) hundredfold (riches), Sumitra praised you thus, Durmitra praised you thus, when at the slaughter of the Dasyus you protected Kutsa's son, when at the slaughter of the Dasyus you protected Kutsa's darling,

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

This sūkta was seen by the sage Sumitra or Durmitra, a son of Kutsa. Sumitra means "good friend"; Durmitra means "bad friend". One of these two names may be his real name, and the other may have become famous in accordance with his nature; that is, his own quality is the reason he got this name. In the first half of this last Ṛk the words इत्यास्तत् (*ityāstat*) are read twice. **NOTE**. In the second half the words "at the slaughter of the Dasyus, Kutsa's son (darling)" are likewise read as a repetition. In the first half the seer has stated both names, Sumitra and Durmitra, which were in use for him. The commentator explains that the Ṛk has the repetition for the sake of rhyme and for the completion of the sūkta.

वेदार्थस्य प्रकाशेन तमो हार्दं निवारयन् । पुमर्थांश्चतुरो देयाद्विद्यातीर्थमहेश्वरः ॥ (*vedārthasya prakāśena tamo hārdaṃ nivārayan | pumarthāṃścaturo deyādvidyātīrthamaheśvaraḥ ‖*)

इति श्रीमद्राजाधिराजपरमेश्वरनैदिकमार्गप्रवर्तकश्रिवीरबुक्कमहीपालसाम्राज्यधुरंधरेण सायणाचार्येण विरचिते माधवीये वेदार्थप्रकाशे ऋक्संहिताभाष्येष्टमाष्टके पंचमोध्यायः समाप्तः ॥ (*iti śrīmadrājādhirājaparameśvaranaidikamārgapravartakaśrivīrabukkamahīpālasāmrājyadhuraṃdhareṇa sāyaṇācāryeṇa viracite mādhavīye vedārthaprakāśe ṛksaṃhitābhāṣyeṣṭamāṣṭake paṃcamodhyāyaḥ samāptaḥ ‖*) ओं तत्त्वत् (*oṃ tattvat*)

Here ends the fifth chapter in the eighth aṣṭaka of the Ṛgveda Saṃhitā, with Śrī Sāyaṇa's commentary and a Kannada translation. May it be auspicious. Blessings.

Śrī. Salutation to Śrī Mahāgaṇādhipati.

वागीशाद्याः सुमनसः सर्वार्थानामुपक्रमे । यं नत्वा कृतकृत्याः स्युस्तं नमामि गजाननं ॥ (*vāgīśādyāḥ sumanasaḥ sarvārthānāmupakrame | yaṃ natvā kṛtakṛtyāḥ syustaṃ namāmi gajānanaṃ ‖*)

यस्य निश्वसितं वेदा यो वेदेभ्योऽखिलं जगत् । निर्ममे तमहं वंदे विद्यातीर्थमहेश्वरं ॥ (*yasya niśvasitaṃ vedā yo vedebhyo 'khilaṃ jagat | nirmame tamahaṃ vaṃde vidyātīrthamaheśvaraṃ ‖*)

वेदः शिवः शिवो वेदो वेदाध्यायी सदाशिवः । तस्मात्सर्वप्रयत्नेन वेदमेव सदा जपेत्॥ (*vedaḥ śivaḥ śivo vedo vedādhyāyī sadāśivaḥ | tasmātsarvaprayatnena vedameva sadā japet‖*)

अष्टमे पंचमाध्यायं व्याख्याय श्रुतिकोविदः । सायणार्यस्ततोध्यायं षष्ठं च व्याचिकीर्षति ॥ (*aṣṭame paṃcamādhyāyaṃ vyākhyāya śrutikovidaḥ | sāyaṇāryastatodhyāyaṃ ṣaṣṭhaṃ ca vyācikīrṣati ‖*)

Ṛgveda Saṃhitā with Sāyaṇa's commentary, Part 30. The sixth chapter in the eighth aṣṭaka. The sūktas of the tenth maṇḍala.

## Sūkta 106 (Maṇḍala 10)

Introduction. The seer of this sūkta is Bhūtāṃśa, of the Kaśyapa line. Because he had no children he praised the Aśvin deities. In the Bṛhaddevatā this is said about this seer:

भूतांशस्तु प्रजाकामः कर्माणि कृतवान्पुरा । न हि लेभे प्रजाः काश्चित्काश्यपो मुनिसत्तमः ॥ (*bhūtāṃśastu prajākāmaḥ karmāṇi kṛtavānpurā | na hi lebhe prajāḥ kāścitkāśyapo munisattamaḥ ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 8-18)

### Printed p. 244

In former times the seer Bhūtāṃśa of the Kaśyapa line, desiring to have children, performed many rites such as sacrifices. Yet this best of seers got no children.

उवाच भार्या भूतांशं सुतानिच्छसि यावतः । तावतो जनयिष्यामि देवता द्वंद्वशः स्तुहि ॥ (*uvāca bhāryā bhūtāṃśaṃ sutānicchasi yāvataḥ | tāvato janayiṣyāmi devatā dvaṃdvaśaḥ stuhi ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 8-19)

Then his wife said to the seer Bhūtāṃśa: "O best of seers, as many children as you wish, so many I shall bear. Therefore, to obtain children, praise the deities together, two by two."

तमभ्ययुस्तु सर्वाणि द्वंद्वानि स्तुतिकाम्यया । तान्यवेक्ष्याथ तच्चक्रे नासत्यौ सूक्तभागिन् ॥ (*tamabhyayustu sarvāṇi dvaṃdvāni stutikāmyayā | tānyavekṣyātha taccakre nāsatyau sūktabhāgin ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 8-20)

Knowing this, all the deities who exist in pairs came to him, desiring to be praised. The seer, looking at those paired deities, praised them in this sūkta (the 106th). But the chief deities of this sūkta are the Aśvins.

तदेतदंततो भावादाश्विनं सूक्तमुच्यते । न ह्यस्मिन्देवतालिंगं प्रागन्याद्दृश्यते पदात् ॥ (*tadetadaṃtato bhāvādāśvinaṃ sūktamucyate | na hyasmindevatāliṃgaṃ prāganyāddṛśyate padāt ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 8-21)

In this 106th sūkta it is not made clear until the end which deity is praised. The deity cannot be determined from characteristics such as names in the sūkta. Since the word अश्विनोः (*aśvinoḥ*) is used in the last pāda of the last Ṛk (11) of the sūkta, it has to be said that the Aśvins are chiefly praised in this sūkta. In this sūkta there are words that are especially out of use, difficult and of obscure meaning. In every sentence the subject is set forth by means of an upamāna (illustration).

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> तत्रोभा उ नूनुमित्येकादशर्चमाद्यं सूक्तं । अनुवाकापेक्षया सप्तमं
> सूक्तं । त्रैष्टुभमश्विदैवत्यं । कश्यपपुत्रो भूतांशो नामर्षिः । तथा चानुक्रम्यते । उभौ भूतांशः काश्यप आश्विनमिति ॥ अप्पोर्यामेऽच्छावाकातिरिक्कोक्ष्य
> एतत्तूक्तं । सूत्रितं च । उभा उ नूनं दैन्व्या होतारा प्रथमा पुरोहितेति
> परिधानीया । आ. ९-११ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Of these sūktas, the first, उभा उ नूनं (*ubhā u nūnaṃ*), has eleven Ṛks; counted by anuvāka it is the seventh sūkta. It is in the Triṣṭubh metre and its deity is the Aśvins. The seer is Bhūtāṃśa, a son of Kaśyapa. So the Anukramaṇī says: उभौ भूतांशः काश्यप आश्विनं (*ubhau bhūtāṃśaḥ kāśyapa āśvinaṃ*). In the Aptoryāma this sūkta is recited among the Atirikta-ukthya verses of the Acchāvāka. It is also given in the sūtra: उभा उ नूनं दैन्व्या होतारा प्रथमा पुरोहितेति परिधानीया (*ubhā u nūnaṃ dainvyā hotārā prathamā purohiteti paridhānīyā*) (Ā. 9-11). **NOTE**

### Printed p. 245

##### Anuvāda

Although the sūkta उभा उ नूनं (*ubhā u nūnaṃ*) is the first in this chapter, it is the seventh sūkta in the ninth anuvāka of the tenth maṇḍala. It has eleven Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Bhūtāṃśa, son of Kaśyapa; the deity is the Aśvins; the metre is Triṣṭubh. In the Anukramaṇī it is said: उभौ भूतांशः काश्यप आश्विनं (*ubhau bhūtāṃśaḥ kāśyapa āśvinaṃ*). It is explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, उभा उ नूनं दैव्या होतारा प्रथमा पुरोहितेति परिधानीया (*ubhā u nūnaṃ daivyā hotārā prathamā purohiteti paridhānīyā*) (Ā. 9-11), that this sūkta is to be recited by the priest called Acchāvāka among the Atirikta-ukthya mantras in the Aptoryāma.

Sūkta 106. Maṇḍala 10. Anuvāka 9. Sūkta 186. **NOTE**
Aṣṭaka 8. Adhyāya 6. Varga 1, 2.
Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 11.
Seer: Bhūtāṃśa Kāśyapa.
Deity: Aśvins.
Metre: Triṣṭubh.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒भा उ॑ नू॒नं तदिद॑र्थयेथे॒ वि त॑न्वाथे॒ धियो॒ वस्त्रा॒पसे॑व ।  
> स॒ध्री॒ची॒ना यात॑वे॒ प्रेम॑जीगः सु॒दिने॑व॒ पृक्ष॒ आ तं॑सयेथे ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उभौ । ऊं इति । नूनं । तत् । इत् । अर्थयेथे इति । वि । तन्नाथे इति । धियः ।  
> वस्त्रा । अपसा इव ।  
> सध्रीचीना । यातवे । प्र । ईं । अजीगरिति । सुदिनाइव । पृक्षः । आ । तंसयेथे  
> इति ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अश्विनावुभौ युवां नूनमिदानीं तदिदस्माभिर्दीयमानं हविः क्रियमाणं स्तोत्रमेवार्थयेथे । प्रार्थयथः ॥ अर्थ याज्ञायां चौरादिकः

> अनुदात्तेत् । उः पूरणः ॥ संप्रार्थ्य च धियः कर्माणि स्तुतीर्वा वि तन्वाथे !
> विस्तारयथः ॥ तनु विस्तारे तानादिकः ॥ तत्र दृष्टांतः । अपसेवापस्विनौ कुविंदौ
> यथा वस्त्रा वस्त्राणि विस्तारयतः ॥ अपःशब्दान्मत्वर्थियो विनिः । तस्य बहुलं
> छंदसीति लुक् । प्रातिपदिकस्वरेणांतोदात्तः । उभयत्र सुप आकारः !! ईमयं
> यजमानः सध्रीचीना सध्रीचीनौ सहांचनौ युवां ॥ सहपूर्वादंचतेः क्विनि सहस्य
> सध्रिरिति सध्रादेशः । विभाषांचेरदिक् स्त्रीयामिति खः । सुप आजादेशः ॥ तौ यातवे॥
> तुमर्थे तनेन्तृयः ॥ अभिमतप्राप्त्यर्थं प्राजीगः । प्रकर्षेण स्तौति । यद्वा ।
> युवां प्रति स्तुतीरुद्दिरति ॥ गृ शब्दे । गृ निगरणे वा । एताभ्यां लुजि बहुलं
> छंदसीति विकरणस्य श्लुः । बहुलं छंदसीत्यभ्यासस्यॆत्वं । दीर्घश्चांदसः । गुणे
> कृते हल् ज्यादिना तिपो लोपः ॥ किंच सुदिनेव सुदिनेष्टन्नानि : यथालंकुर्वंति ।
> यद्वा! सुदिना सुदिन् । अहोरात्रयोः प्रवर्तकत्वेन तद्वंतौ सूर्याचंद्रमसौ
> स्वरश्मिभिर्यथान्यलंकुरुतः । तद्विद्युवां पृक्षः । अन्ननामैतत् । भोक्तृभिः
> संपृच्यंत इति वृक्षोऽन्नानि । आ तंसयेथे । समंतादलंकुरुथः ॥ तसि भूष
> अलंकारे चौरादिकः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins, both of you, now you ask for this oblation being given by us, or this praise being made. अर्थयेथे (*arthayethe*) means "you pray"; the root अर्थ (*artha*) is in the sense of asking and belongs to the Curādi class.

### Printed p. 246

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Having prayed, you spread out the धियः (*dhiyaḥ*), the rites or the praises; तनु (*tanu*) is in the sense of spreading, of the Tanādi class. The illustration: as two weavers (*apasvinau kuviṃdau*) spread out cloths. This sacrificer, as you two go together (सध्रीचीना (*sadhrīcīnā*), "going along together"), praises you forth to obtain what he desires, that you may come; or else he raises praises towards you. Further: as on good days (सुदिनेव (*sudineva*)) people adorn their foods; or else, सुदिना (*sudinā*) are the sun and moon, who, as the makers of day and night, adorn with their own rays. In the same way you adorn पृक्षः (*pṛkṣaḥ*); this is a name for food, which is "mixed" by those who eat it, so the foods. आ तंसयेथे (*ā taṃsayethe*): you adorn all round; the root तसि (*tasi*) is in the sense of adorning, of the Curādi class.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (ऎलै अश्विनीदेवतॆगळे (*èlai aśvinīdevatègaḷe*))—O Aśvins
- उभौ (*ubhau*)—both of you
- नूनं (*nūnaṃ*)—now
- तदित् (*tadit*)—the praises or the oblation offered by us
- अर्थयेथे (*arthayethe*)—you desire
- वस्त्रा (*vastrā*)—cloths
- अपसेव (*apaseva*)—as two weavers spread out cloths
- धियः (*dhiyaḥ*)—our rites
- वि तन्नाथे (*vi tannāthe*)—you cause them to spread
- ईं (*īṃ*)—this sacrificer
- सध्रीचीना (*sadhrīcīnā*)—you who are gathered together
- यातने (*yātane*)—for the attainment of his desired object **NOTE**
- प्र अजीगः (*pra ajīgaḥ*)—prays and praises
- सुदिनेव (*sudineva*)—like the sun and moon, the excellent makers of the day
- पृक्षः (*pṛkṣaḥ*)—food
- आ तंसयेथे (*ā taṃsayethe*)—you adorn all round

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvins, both of you now desire the oblation or the praise offered by us. As two weavers spread out cloths, you cause our rites to be spread out. This sacrificer prays to you, who are gathered together, and praises you for the attainment of his desired object. Like the sun and moon, the excellent makers of the day, you adorn the food all round.

##### English translation
You both demand the oblation (Aswins), you spread out the ceremonials as two weavers (stretch) cloths; (the institutor of the rite) praised you

### Printed p. 247
associated together to attain (his desires); like two fortunate days you bestow food.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

वि तन्वाथे धियो वस्त्रापसेव (*vi tanvāthe dhiyo vastrāpaseva*)—As weavers weave a cloth, it is small at the beginning and grows larger as the weaving proceeds. In the same way you carry forward in the proper manner the sacrificial rites that we perform; that is, by your help and favour the sacrificial rites are completed in full. This is the meaning. The word धियः (*dhiyaḥ*) is the accusative plural of the word धीः (*dhīḥ*). The word धीः (*dhīḥ*) is read among the twenty-six names of action, beginning with अपः (*apaḥ*) and अप्पः (*appaḥ*) (Ni. 3-1).

सुदिनेव पृक्ष आ तंसयोथे (*sudineva pṛkṣa ā taṃsayothe*)—The word पृक्ष (*pṛkṣa*) is read among the twenty-eight names of food, beginning with अंधः (*aṃdhaḥ*) and वाजः (*vājaḥ*) (Ni. 3-9). पृक्षः (*pṛkṣaḥ*) means food, nourishment. The day on which we get abundant food is a good day (सुदिन (*sudina*)). Therefore you favour us so that we obtain food on a good day. Or else, as to सुदिन (*sudina*): सुदिना सुदिन् । अहोरात्रयोः प्रवर्तकत्वेन तद्वंतौ सूर्याचंद्रमसौ स्वरश्मिभिर्यथान्यलंकुरुतः । (*sudinā sudin | ahorātrayoḥ pravartakatvena tadvaṃtau sūryācaṃdramasau svaraśmibhiryathānyalaṃkurutaḥ |*). The Sun and Moon are the causes of day and night. By their rays and by acts such as rain they make food and other nourishment available to people. In the same way you (the Aśvin deities) favour us by providing food and the like. This is the meaning.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒ष्टारे॑व॒ फर्व॑रेषु श्रयेथे प्रायो॒गेव॒ श्वात्रा॒ शासु॒रेथः॑ ।  
> दू॒तेव॒ हि ष्ठो य॒शसा॒ जने॑षु॒ माप॑ स्थातं महि॒षेवा॑न॒पाना॑त् ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उष्टारा इव । फर्वरेषु । श्रयेथे इति । प्रायोगा इव । श्वात्रा । शासुः । आ ।  
> इथः ।  
> दूता इव । हि । स्थः । यशसा । जनेषु । मा । अप । स्थातं । महिषा इव । अव  
> पानात् ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 248

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उष्टारेव । उष्टः कामयेते गंतव्यं देशमित्युष्टारौ प्रासंगस्य वोढारावनड्वाहाविव ॥ वश कांतावित्यस्मात्तृचि च्छांदसं संप्रसारणमिडभावश्च ॥ तौ यथा
> संपूर्णघासेषु संचरतः तद्वत्पर्वरेषु स्तुतिभिर्हविर्भिश्च पूरयितृषु जनेषु
> श्रयेथे । तत्स्वीकरणार्थमाश्रयथः ॥ फर्वतिः पूर्णार्थः । अस्मादौणादिको
> रन्मत्तयः ॥ प्रायोगेव प्रयोक्तव्यावनड्वाहाविव ॥ प्रपूर्वाद्युजेः कर्मणि
> घइ्युपसर्गस्य दीर्घः ॥ थाथादिस्वरेणांतोदात्तः ॥ तौ यथा श्वात्रा श्वात्रौ ।
> शु क्षिप्रमतनं श्वात्रं । तत्र साधू भवतः तद्वत् । यद्वा । युद्धार्थ० प्रयोक्तव्यावश्वानिव श्वात्रौ । श्वात्रमिति धननाम । तत्र भवौ । धनस्य साधकावित्यर्थः । तौ
> युवां शासुः शंसितुः स्तोतुः स्तुतिं प्रत्येथः । आगच्छथः ॥ इण् गतौ आदादिकः ॥
> दूतेव दूताविन यथा राज्यः प्रियतमौ दूतौ जनपदेषु यशस्विनौ भवतः
> तद्वज्जनेषु स्तोतृषु यशसा यशस्विनौ स्थो हि । भवथः खलु ॥ अस्तेर्लटि रूपं ।
> हियोगादनिघातः ॥ महिषेव महिषाविन यथा श्रांतौ महिषाववपानात् । पीयतेऽस्मिन्निति पानं ह्रदादि ॥ अधिकरणे ल्युट् ॥ पानमेवावपानं । संनिकृष्टात्पानादपगच्छतः तद्विद्युवामस्मदीयाद्दविषः स्तोत्राच्च माप स्थातं । मापक्रम्य तिष्ठतं ॥
> तिष्ठतेर्लुजि गातिस्थेति सिचो लुक् । न माज्योग इत्यडभावः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"Like two camels": the two that desire (कामयेते (*kāmayete*)) to go to the place to be reached are उष्टारा (*uṣṭārā*), like two oxen that carry a cart. The root is वश कांत् (*vaśa kāṃt*). As those two roam among fully grown grass, so you resort to the people who fill you (फर्वरेषु (*pharvareṣu*)) with praises and oblations; you go to them in order to accept their offering. फर्वतिः (*pharvatiḥ*) means "to fill". प्रायोगा (*prāyogā*) are oxen to be yoked to a cart. श्वात्रा (*śvātrā*): शु (*śu*) is "quick"; श्वात्रं (*śvātraṃ*) is "swift going", and the two are good at that. Or else: like two horses to be employed for battle, श्वात्रौ (*śvātrau*); श्वात्रं (*śvātraṃ*) is a name for wealth, and those that are good for it, that is, that procure wealth. You two come (इथः (*ithaḥ*), from इण् गतौ (*iṇ gatau*)) to the praise of the praiser (शासुः (*śāsuḥ*)). "Like messengers": as the dearest messengers of a king become renowned in the countries, so you are indeed renowned among the people who praise you. "Like two buffaloes": as two buffaloes, wearied, do not leave the drinking-place (अवपानात् (*avapānāt*); पानं (*pānaṃ*) is the place where one drinks, a pool and the like), so you two do not leave off from our oblation and praise nearby; do not move away and stay apart.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उष्टारेव (*uṣṭāreva*)—like two bulls that go to a pasture to graze (you)
- फर्वरेषु (*pharvareṣu*)—among the people who satisfy you by offering praises and oblations
- श्रयेथे (*śrayethe*)—you take refuge, that is, you go to them
- प्रायोगेव (*prāyogeva*)—like oxen yoked to carts
- श्वात्र्यौ (*śvātryau*)—being of swift going
- शासुः (*śāsuḥ*)—to the sacrificer who praises you
- आ इथः (*ā ithaḥ*)—you come [or: श्वात्र्यौ (*śvātryau*)—like swift horses that take part in battle, or like horses that are the means of winning wealth in battle; शासुः (*śāsuḥ*)—towards the praise of the sacrificer who praises you; आ इथः (*ā ithaḥ*)—you come]
- दूतेव (*dūteva*)—as messengers appointed by the king, that is, royal servants or royal officers trusted by the king, win the confidence of the people in the kingdom (you)
- जनेषु (*janeṣu*)—among the people who praise you
- यशसा (*yaśasā*)—being renowned, that is, having won approval
- स्थः हि (*sthaḥ hi*)—you are indeed, are you not? (and moreover)
- महिषेव (*mahiṣeva*)—as buffaloes wearied by the sun
- अवपानात् (*avapānāt*)—do not rise up from the pool where there is water
- मा अप स्थातं (*mā apa sthātaṃ*)—(you) do not leave our oblation, or our praise, and go away

### Printed p. 249

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities! As bulls go of their own accord, with no one's urging, to a pasture to graze, so you go to the sacrificer who satisfies you with oblations and praises. As oxen yoked to a cart go quickly, or as horses employed in battle press forward and go swiftly, or as horses go swiftly to win and bring wealth in battle, so you come with exceeding speed towards the praise of the sacrificer, that is, quickly, to hear his praise. And as royal officers, trusted by the king's affection, win the trust and confidence of the people of the realm, so you win the regard and confidence of the people who praise you. Moreover, as buffaloes, wearied by the heat of the sun, lie in a pool of water and rid themselves of fatigue and do not rise from the water quickly, so you do not turn back without partaking of the sacrificer's oblation or praise.

##### English translation
Like two longing oxen (to the pastures), you have recourse to those_e who fill you (with oblations); you approach like two horses prepared for the fray, employed to bring treasure : you are like two (royal) messengers re-nowned among the people; turn not away (from our) oblation) like two buffaloes from the drinking-trough.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

उष्टारेव (*uṣṭāreva*)— उष्टः कामयेते गंतव्यं देशमित्युष्टार् प्रासंगस्य वोढारावनड्डाहाविव । (*uṣṭaḥ kāmayete gaṃtavyaṃ deśamityuṣṭār prāsaṃgasya voḍhārāvanaḍḍāhāviva |*) तौ यथा संपूर्णघासेषु संचरतः तद्वत् ॥ (*tau yathā saṃpūrṇaghāseṣu saṃcarataḥ tadvat ‖*) The word उष्टृ (*uṣṭṛ*) is derived from the root वश कांत् (*vaśa kāṃt*), "to desire". उष्टारा (*uṣṭārā*) means two creatures that desire. Desire what? In the present context, the meaning is: desire to go to a pasture full of grass in order to graze the grass there. Therefore, as bulls go searching for a place with grass in order to graze, you Aśvin deities go searching for the people who praise you and go to them.

फर्वरेषु (*pharvareṣu*) - फर्वतिः पूरणार्थः । (*pharvatiḥ pūraṇārthaḥ |*) स्तुतिभिः हर्विर्भिश्च पूरयितृषु जनेषु । (*stutibhiḥ harvirbhiśca pūrayitṛṣu janeṣu |*) The root फर्वति (*pharvati*) means "to fill, to satisfy". The word फर्वरेषु (*pharvareṣu*), formed from that root, here means among the people who satisfy you with praises and with oblations. श्रयथे (*śrayathe*)—you take refuge, that is, you go to them.

### Printed p. 250
प्रायोगेव श्वात्रा शासुः आ इथः (*prāyogeva śvātrā śāsuḥ ā ithaḥ*)— प्रायोगा (*prāyogā*) means प्रयोक्तव्यावनड्वाहौ (*prayoktavyāvanaḍvāhau*), oxen to be yoked to a cart. Or युद्धार्थवागि प्रयोक्तावश्वा (*yuddhārthavāgi prayoktāvaśvā*): horses employed for battle. श्वात्रा (*śvātrā*)—शु क्षिप्रमतनं (*śu kṣipramatanaṃ*): the meaning is "of swift going"; horses that press forward swiftly in battle. Or श्वात्र (*śvātra*) is also a name for wealth (Ni. 3-9); horses that are the means of winning and bringing wealth in battle are also called श्वात्रौ (*śvātrau*). Being of swift going like such horses or like oxen, you come towards the praise of the sacrificer who praises you, that is, to hear his praise.

दूतेव हि स्थः यशसा जनेषु (*dūteva hi sthaḥ yaśasā janeṣu*)— As royal officers who enjoy the king's trust also become objects of the regard and confidence of the people of the country, so you have won the approval of the people who praise you.

मा अप स्थातं महिषेव अवपानात् (*mā apa sthātaṃ mahiṣeva avapānāt*)—पीयतेऽस्मिन्निति पानं ह्रदादि । (*pīyate 'sminniti pānaṃ hradādi |*) पानमेव्ना वपानं । (*pānamevnā vapānaṃ |*) A pool containing drinking water is called पान (*pāna*). अवपान (*avapāna*) has the same meaning. Or अवपान (*avapāna*) may also be taken to mean a pool of dirty water not fit for drinking. It is known to everyone that buffaloes lie in such a pool to get rid of the heat of the sun. These buffaloes do not rise quickly from that water and go; they stay in it a long time, relieve their fatigue, and only then go. In the same way, you Aśvin deities do not quickly go away from the people who have offered you praise or oblation. You go only after accepting their praise. This is the meaning.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सा॒कं॒युजा॑ शकु॒नस्ये॑व प॒क्षा प॒श्वेव॑ चि॒त्रा यजु॒रा ग॑मिष्टं ।  
> अ॒ग्निरि॑व देव॒योर्दि॑दि॒वांसा॒ परि॑ज्ञानेव यजथः पुरु॒त्रा ॥३॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> साकंयुजा । शकुनस्य इव । पक्षा । पश्वा इव । चित्रा । यजुः । आ । गमिष्टं ।  
> अग्निः इव । देवयोः । दीदिवांसा । परिज्ञाना इव । यजथः । पुरुत्रा ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> शकुनस्येव पक्षिण इव यथा पक्षिणः पक्षा पक्ष संहत्य वर्तमान् तद्वत्साकंयुजा साकंयुजौ सहावियुज्य वर्तमान् भवधः ॥ युजेः सत्तूद्विषेत्यादिना

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"Like a bird": as the two wings of a bird exist joined together, so you two exist as साकंयुजा (*sākaṃyujā*), never separated from one another. "Like two animals": as two beasts to be gathered (चायनीयौ (*cāyanīyau*)), so चित्रा (*citrā*), worthy of being gathered or admired.

### Printed p. 251

> क्विप् ॥ पश्वेव पशू इव ॥ सुप आकारः ॥ तौ यथा चायनीयौ तद्वच्चित्रा चित्वा
> चायनीयौ युवां यजुर्यजनावस्मदीयावा गमिष्टं । आगच्छतं ॥ यजेरौणादिको भाव उसिन्प्रत्ययः । गमिष्टं । गमेर्लॆटि सिब्बहुलं लेटीति सिप् ॥ सिप
> आर्धधातुकत्वाद्यमेरिट् परस्मैपदेषु । सा. ७-२-५८ । इतीडागमः ॥ देवयोर्देवानिच्छतो यजमानस्य यज्ञेग्निरिवाग्निर्यथा हविर्भिः स्तुतिभिश्च दीप्यते
> तद्वद्दिदिवांसा दीदिवांसौ स्तुतिभिर्दिप्त भवथः ॥ दीव्यतेः क्वसौ वस्वेकाजादिति
> नियमादिडभावः । वलि लोपे तुजादित्वादभ्यासस्य दीर्घः ॥ परिज्ञानेव परिज्ञानाविव
> परितोऽजतो गच्छतः कर्मकरणार्थमिति परिज्ञानावध्वर्यू इव स्थितौ युवां
> पुरुत्रा बहुषु देशेषु यजथः । देवान्पूजयथः । अश्विनौ हि देवानामध्वर्यू
> इत्याम्मानात् । ऐ. ब्रा. १-१८ । यद्वा । परिज्ञानेव परितो गंतार् पुरो वा
> पश्चाद्वा तौ यथा बहुषु देशेषु यजथः संगच्छमान् भवथः ॥ यज देवपूजासंगतिकरणदानेषु । पुरुत्रा । देवमनुष्येत्यादिना त्राप्रत्ययः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

You two come to our sacrifice (यजुः (*yajuḥ*), "worship"; आ गमिष्टं (*ā gamiṣṭaṃ*), "come"). As Agni blazes with oblations and praises in the sacrifice of a sacrificer who desires the gods (देवयोः (*devayoḥ*)), so you two become radiant (दीदिवांसा (*dīdivāṃsā*)) through praises. परिज्ञाना (*parijñānā*): those who go (*ajataḥ*) all around for the performance of the rite, that is, you stand like two Adhvaryu priests; you worship (यजथः (*yajathaḥ*)) the gods in many places (पुरुत्रा (*purutrā*)), for it is handed down that the Aśvins are the Adhvaryus of the gods (Ai. Brā. 1-18). Or else: as two who go around, in front or behind, so you come together in many places. यज (*yaja*) is in the sense of worship of gods, association and giving.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Aśvin deities!) शकुनस्य (*śakunasya*)—of a bird
- पक्षा इव (*pakṣā iva*)—as two wings are joined together
- साकंयुजा (*sākaṃyujā*)—you who are joined together
- पश्वॆव (*paśvèva*)—like two animals
- चित्रा (*citrā*)—attractive
- यजुः (*yajuḥ*)—to the place of sacrifice
- आ गमिष्टं (*ā gamiṣṭaṃ*)—you come
- देवयोः (*devayoḥ*)—in the sacrifice of the sacrificer
- अग्निरिव (*agniriva*)—as Agni shines
- दीदिवांसा (*dīdivāṃsā*)—you who shine
- परिज्ञानेव (*parijñāneva*)—like Adhvaryus who go about for the rite
- पुरुत्रा (*purutrā*)—in many places
- यजथः (*yajathaḥ*)—you worship the gods

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities! You who are joined together like the two wings of a bird come attractively to the place of sacrifice like two animals. Shining as Agni shines in the sacrificer's sacrifice, you worship the gods in many places, like Adhvaryus who go about for the rite.

##### English translation
Connected together like the two wings of a bird, you come to the sacrifice like two choice animals; radiant as Agni (at the offering) of the devout (worshipper), like two perambulating priests yon celebrate worship in many places.

### Printed p. 252

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

शकुनस्येव पक्षा साकंयुजा (*śakunasyeva pakṣā sākaṃyujā*) - दक्षिणः पक्षाविव सहावियुज्य वर्तमानौ ॥ (*dakṣiṇaḥ pakṣāviva sahāviyujya vartamānau ‖*) As the left and right wings of a bird are attached to the bird in one and the same way and equally, so you Aśvin deities are of one and the same form and shape, and are always together, never separating from one another.

पश्वेव चित्रा (*paśveva citrā*)— As two well-grown, sturdy animals look beautiful to see, so you two appear attractive.

अग्निरिव देवयोनेः दीदिवांसा (*agniriva devayoneḥ dīdivāṃsā*)- देवानिच्छतो यजमानस्य यज्ञे (*devānicchato yajamānasya yajñe*) अग्निर्यथा हविर्भिः स्तुतिभिश्च दीप्यते तद्वत् स्तुतिभिर्दिप्ता भवतः ॥ (*agniryathā havirbhiḥ stutibhiśca dīpyate tadvat stutibhirdiptā bhavataḥ ‖*) As Agni, installed in the sacrificial house of the sacrificer who desires to worship the gods, blazes and shines with oblations such as ghee and with praises, so you, gratified by the praises of the sacrificers, shine on the sacrificial altar.

परिज्ञानेव यजथः पुरुत्रा (*parijñāneva yajathaḥ purutrā*)—परिज्ञाना ऎंदरॆ परितो अजतो गच्छतः । (*parijñānā èṃdarè parito ajato gacchataḥ |*) The meaning is: those who move about here and there on the sacrificial altar to carry out the work. Among the priests, the Hotṛ, Udgātṛ, Brahman and others sit in one fixed place of the altar and recite the mantras they have to recite according to the occasion. This is their duty. But the Adhvaryu's duty is not done sitting in one place. Because he must take part in all the work connected with the sacrifice and oversee it, he has to move about here and there. He has to go about on all sides to see whether the sacrificer and the other priests are performing their tasks properly and at the right times. Therefore the word परिज्मा (*parijmā*) applies to the Adhvaryu. Here the Aśvin deities too—अश्विन् हि देवानामध्वयू अस्तां (*aśvin hi devānāmadhvayū astāṃ*) (Ai. Brā. 1-18)—since they are the Adhvaryus of the gods, they too, like human Adhvaryus, move about all round and carry out their duty. **NOTE**. Therefore the idea suggested here is that in many countries they go to the sacrifices performed by many sacrificers and are helpers in carrying out those sacrifices.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ॒पी वो॑ अ॒स्मे पि॒तरे॑व पु॒त्रोग्रेव॑ रु॒चा नृ॒पती॑व तु॒र्य्यै ।  
> इर्ये॑व पु॒ष्ट्यै कि॒रणे॑व भु॒ज्यै श्रु॑ष्टी॒वाने॑व॒ हव॒मा  ॑
> गमिष्टं ॥ ४ ॥

### Printed p. 253

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आपी इति । वः । अस्मे इति । पितरा इव । पुत्रा । उग्रा इव । रुचा । नृपती इवेति  
> नृपती इव । तुर्य्यै ।  
> इर्याऽइव । पुष्टै । किरणाऽइव । भुज्यै । श्रुष्टीपानाऽइव । हवं । आ । गमिष्ठं ॥४॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अस्मे अस्माकं वो युवां । पूजायां बहुवचनं । आपी प्राप्तौ बंधुभूत् भवथः । पितरेव पितराविव यथात्र पितर् प्रति पुत्रा पुत्रौ बंधुभूतौ भवतः
> तद्वत् । यद्वा । पुत्रा ॥ षष्ठ्या आकारः ॥ पुत्राणां पितर् बंधू । तथोग्रेवोग्राविव यथोग् स्वतेजसोद्रोणावग्न्यादित् यथा रोचमान् भवतः तद्वद्रुचा
> दीप्त्या युक्ता ॥ रोचतेर्भावे क्विप् । सावेकाच इति विभक्तेरुदात्तत्वं ॥ यद्वा।
> रुचा रोचमान् भवथः ॥ रोचतेरिगुपधलक्षण, कः । सुप आकारः ॥ नृपतीव
> यथा नृणां पालयितार् राजान् संभ्रमयुक्ताय्कॆ सेनाय्कॆ रक्षकत्वेन भवतः
> तद्वत्तुर्यैकर्मार्थं त्वरमाणाय्कॆ संभ्रमवश्य जनताय्कॆ युवामभीष्टदातृत्वेन
> भवथः ॥ तुर त्वर्णे । इगुपधात्कित् । उ. ४-११९ । इतीन्प्रत्ययः । कृदिकारादक्तिनः ।
> पा. ४-१-४५ । इति जीष् । उदात्तयणो हल्पूर्वादिति विभक्तेरुदात्तत्वं ॥
> इर्येवेर्याविव । इरान्नं । तत्र भवौ ॥ भावार्थे यत् ॥ अन्नवंतानाढ्य
> स्वजनवरितोषणाय भवतः । तप्पद्युवां पुष्य धनादिदानेनास्माकं परितोषणाय
> भवथः । किरणेद किरणाविव यथाग्न्यादित्यकिरण सर्वॆषां प्रकाशनादिद्वारेण
> भोगाय भवतः तद्वद्बुज्यॆ स्तोत्कृणां परिभोगाय भवथः । श्रुष्टिवानेव
> श्रुष्टिवंताविव । श्रुष्टिति क्षिप्रनाम ॥ मत्त्वर्थीयो वनिप् ॥ तद्वंतावश् यथा
> लक्ष्यं देशं प्रति शीघ्रं गच्छतः तद्वत् । यद्वा । श्रुष्टिवानेव । श्रुष्टिति सुखनाम ।
> तद्वंतौ पुरुष यथा क्रीडार्थवागच्छतः तद्विद्युवां हवमस्मदीयमाह्वानं प्रत्या
> गमिष्टं । आगच्छतं ॥ हवं । भावेनुपसर्गस्येति हैयतेरप् संप्रसारणं च ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"You two" (वो (*vo*), plural used in reverence) are "kinsmen" (आपी (*āpī*)) to us, as two sons are kinsmen to a father, or as fathers are to sons. Alternatively, पुत्रा (*putrā*) carries the ā-ending of the genitive, "of the sons, the fathers (are) kinsmen". Like two fierce ones (उग्रेव (*ugreva*)), that is, Agni and Āditya, who shine by their own brilliance, so you shine with lustre (रुचा (*rucā*)), meaning splendour. Alternatively, रुचा (*rucā*) means "you shine". Like two kings who are protectors of an agitated army, so for a worshipper who hastens (तुर्यै (*turyai*)) towards the rite you are givers of what is wished for. Like इर्येव (*iryeva*), two who are born of इरा (*irā*) (food), you are for the nourishing (पुष्टै (*puṣṭai*)) of your wealthy kinsfolk. So you are for our satisfaction, by giving wealth and the like. Like two rays of fire and of the sun, which serve the enjoyment of all by lighting and the like, so you are for the enjoyment (भुज्यै (*bhujyai*)) of the worshippers. श्रुष्टिवानेव (*śruṣṭivāneva*): like two possessed of श्रुष्टि (*śruṣṭi*), a word for "swift", as two who go quickly to the goal. Alternatively, श्रुष्टि (*śruṣṭi*) means "happiness", and the two are like two happy men who come for sport. So come (आगमिष्टं (*āgamiṣṭaṃ*)) to our invocation (हवं (*havaṃ*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Aśvins)
- पुत्रा (*putrā*)—with regard to their sons
- पितरेव (*pitareva*)—as fathers are dear
- अस्मे (*asme*)—with regard to us
- वः (*vaḥ*)—you
- आपी (*āpī*)—you are kinsmen
- उग्रेव (*ugreva*)—like Agni and Āditya, of fierce radiance
- रुजा (*rujā*)—and radiant
- नृपशीव (*nṛpaśīva*)—

### Printed p. 254

- (continuing) like kings anxious to protect their army
- तुर्यै (*turyai*)—and anxious to be givers of desired objects to your worshippers
- इर्येव (*iryeva*)—as wealthy men are nourishers of their households
- पुष्टै (*puṣṭai*)—you are givers of nourishment to your worshippers
- किरणेव (*kiraṇeva*)—as the rays of fire or of the sun are desired by all
- भुज्यै (*bhujyai*)—you are fit to be enjoyed by all
- श्रुष्टिवानेव (*śruṣṭivāneva*)—you, like swift-going horses
- हवं (*havaṃ*)—heeding our invocation
- आगमिष्टं (*āgamiṣṭaṃ*)—be gracious and come

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvins, as fathers are dear to their sons, so you are kinsmen to us. Like Agni and Āditya of fierce radiance you are brilliant. Like kings anxious to protect their army, you are eager to grant their wishes to your worshippers. As wealthy men are nourishers of their households, you are givers of nourishment to your worshippers. As the rays of fire or of the sun are desired by all, you are fit to be enjoyed by all. You who are like swift horses, heed our invocation and come.

##### English translation
You are kinsmen to us, like parents (to) sons, like two fierce, shining
fires, like two princes hastening (to give protection), like two bestowers of
food are you for the nourisment (of men), like two luminaries for the enjoy-
ment (of your worshippers), like two quick (horses) you came to our
invocation.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वंस॑गेव पूष॒र्या॑ शिं॒बाता॑ मि॒त्रेव॑ ऋ॒ता श॒तरा॒ शात॑संता ।  
> वाजे॑वो॒च्चा वय॑सा घर्म्ये॒ष्णा मेषे॑वे॒षा स॑प॒र्या॒३॒॑३  ॑
> पुरीषा ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वंसगाइव । पूषर्या । शिंबाता । मित्राइव । ऋता । शतरा । शातपंता ।  
> वाजाइव । उच्चा । वयसा । घर्म्येस्था । मेषाइव । इषा । सपर्या । पुरीषा॥

### Printed p. 255

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वंसगेव वंसगाविन वननीयगमौ वृषभाविव तौ यथा पूषर्या पूषर्यौ
> पूषणे भवौ । पुष्पावयवानित्यर्थः । तद्विद्युवां स्त्रीतांगावयन् ॥ पुष पुष्टौ।
> अस्मादौणादिको भावेऽरन्मत्तयः । तदंताद्भावे छंदसीति यत् । तित्सरितं ॥ तथा
> शिंबाता शिंबात् । सुखनामैतत् । शिंबेन दुःखानां तनूकरणेन हेतुनातितं
> प्राप्तमिति ॥ शिञ् निशाने । अस्माच्चिंबमिति बाहुलकाद्दप्रत्ययो मुक्च निपात्यते ।
> अततेः कर्मणि घञ् । सुप आलादेशः॥ सुखकर् भवथः। मित्रेव मित्राविव। मित्रशब्देन
> वरुणोऽप्युपलक्ष्यते । सहावस्थितमित्रावरुणाविवर्तर्त् सत्यभूत् तद्विद्यथार्थदर्शिन् भवथः ॥ तथा शतरा शतं शतसंख्या कानि बहूनि रायो धनानि ययोः संति तौ
> तथोक्तौ ॥ सुपो डादेशः ॥ यद्वा । शतमनेकमिंद्रियप्रसादादि राति ददातीति
> शतरा सुखं ! तेन तद्वंतौ । शातपंता शातपंत् तीक्ष्णस्तुतिकौ ॥ शातं तीक्ष्णं ।
> पंतं । पंतेर्बाहुलकात्तन् । उ- ३-८६ ॥ यद्वा । शातपंता शातेन दुःखानां
> तनूकरणेन पन्यते सूयत इति शातपंतं सुखं ॥ शो तनूकरणे । निष्ठा । पनतेस्तन् ॥ तेन युवां सुखवंतौ भवथः ॥ यद्यपि शिंबाता शतरा शातपंतेति त्रीण्यपि
> सुखनामानि तथापि कालत्रयेऽप्यश्निनोः सुखकरत्वस्य विवक्षितत्वेन विषयभेदादपौनरुक्त्यं । वाजीव वाजाविन यथाश्च घासादिनोन्नतौ संपूर्णावयन् तद्वद्वयसा
> हवीरूपेणान्नेन :हेतुनोच्चोच्चैर्वर्तमान् । पुष्टावित्यर्थः ॥ उच्चा! उच्चैः
> शब्दात्तुपो डादेशः । टित्त्वाट्टिलोपः ॥ घर्म्यॆष्ठा घर्मस्थौ । घर्म० दीप्तमंतरिक्षं । तत्संबंधिनि देशे सूर्याचंद्रमसो रूपेणावस्थित् भवथः सूर्याचंद्रमसाविति यास्कृ । २. १२-१ । यद्वा । घर्मे प्रवर्गॆ भवं घर्म्यं हविः । तत्र
