> देवतात्वेनावस्थित् भवथः ॥ तिष्ठतेरन्येभ्योऽपि दृश्यंत इति विच् । तत्पुरुषे कृति
> बहुलमिति पूर्वपदस्य सप्तम्या अलुक् ॥ मेषेव मेषाविव । तौ यथान्नेन परिचरणीयौ तद्विद्युवामिषा हवीरूपेणान्नेन सपर्या सपर्यौ परिचरणीयौ भवथः॥
> सपरशब्दः कंड्वादिः । सपर्यतिः परिचरणकर्मा ॥ पुरीषा पुरीषौ धनादिदानेन
> स्तोतृणां पोषयितारौ यद्वा हविर्भिः पोषणीयौ भवथः । पुरीषं पुष्णातेः
> पोषयतेर्वॆति यास्कृ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

वंसगेव (*vaṃsageva*): like two bulls of lovely gait. As such, you are पूषर्या (*pūṣaryā*), belonging to nourishment, that is, you have well-nourished limbs. शिंबाता (*śiṃbātā*) (a word for happiness): you are brought (*atitaṃ*) to happiness by the thinning out of sorrows, and you make happiness. मित्रेव (*mitreva*): like Mitra, the word "Mitra" also standing for Varuṇa, you are like Mitra and Varuṇa abiding together, truthful (ऋता (*ṛtā*)) and seeing things as they are. शतरा (*śatarā*): those who have a hundred, that is many, riches (रायः (*rāyaḥ*)). Alternatively, शतरा (*śatarā*) is happiness, which gives (राति (*rāti*)) many things such as the clearness of the senses, and the two possess it. शातपंता (*śātapaṃtā*): those of sharp praise; शातं (*śātaṃ*) is "sharp", पंतं (*paṃtaṃ*) praise (Uṇ. 3-86). Alternatively, शातपंतं (*śātapaṃtaṃ*) is the happiness that is praised through the thinning out of sorrows, and by it you are happy. Though शिंबाता (*śiṃbātā*), शतरा (*śatarā*) and शातपंता (*śātapaṃtā*) are all names of happiness, there is no repetition, since the Aśvins' making of happiness in all three times is meant and the scope differs. वाजीव (*vājīva*): like two horses that stand tall, filled out by fodder and the like, you stand high (उच्चा (*uccā*)), that is nourished, through food (वयसा (*vayasā*)) in the form of oblation. घर्म्येष्ठा (*gharmyeṣṭhā*): abiding in घर्म (*gharma*), the bright mid-region; you abide in the region connected with it in the form of the sun and the moon, as Yāska says (printed reference यास्कृ । २. १२-१ (*yāskṛ | 2. 12-1*))**NOTE**. Alternatively, घर्म्यं (*gharmyaṃ*) is the oblation in the घर्म (*gharma*) (pravargya), and you abide there as deities. मेषेव (*meṣeva*): like two rams, which are to be tended with food, you are tended (सपर्या (*saparyā*)) with food (इषा (*iṣā*)) in the form of oblation. सपर्यतिः (*saparyatiḥ*) is a verb of service. पुरीषा (*purīṣā*): nourishers of the worshippers by gifts of wealth and the like, or you are to be nourished with oblations; Yāska is cited.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Aśvins)
- वंसगेव (*vaṃsageva*)—like bulls of attractive gait
- पूषर्या (*pūṣaryā*)—with well-nourished limbs
- शिंबाता (*śiṃbātā*)—you are makers of happiness
- मित्रेव (*mitreva*)—like Mitra and Varuṇa
- ऋता (*ṛtā*)—truthful
- शतरा (*śatarā*)—possessed of abundant wealth
- शातपंता (*śātapaṃtā*)—

### Printed p. 256

- (continuing) fit for abundant praise
- वाजीव (*vājīva*)—like well-fed horses
- वयसा (*vayasā*)—by the food in the form of oblation
- उच्चा (*uccā*)—being nourished
- घर्म्येष्ठा (*gharmyeṣṭhā*)—you abide in the mid-region
- मेषेव (*meṣeva*)—like rams
- इषा (*iṣā*)—with food
- सपर्या (*saparyā*)—you are those who are tended
- पुरीषा (*purīṣā*)—you nourish the worshippers by gifts of wealth and the like

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvins, like bulls of attractive gait you are makers of happiness, with well-nourished limbs. Like Mitra and Varuṇa you are truthful, possessed of abundant wealth and fit for abundant praise. Like well-fed horses you are nourished by the food in the form of oblation and abide in the mid-region. Like rams you are tended with food. You nourish the worshippers by gifts of wealth and the like.

##### English translation
You are like two pleasantly moving well-fed (hills) like Mitra and
Varuna, the rwo bestowers of felicity. veracious, possessors of infinate wealth,
happy, like two horses plump with fodder, abiding in the firmament, like
two rams (are you) to be nourished with sacrificial food, to be cherished
(with oblations).**NOTE**

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

शतरा -- शतं शतसंख्याकानि बहूनि रायो धनानि ययोः संति तौ । (*śatarā -- śataṃ śatasaṃkhyākāni bahūni rāyo dhanāni yayoḥ saṃti tau |*) "शत (*śata*)" means many; "रायः (*rāyaḥ*)" means wealth. The meaning is: those who are endowed with manifold wealth. Or: शमनेकनिंद्रियप्रसादादि राति ददातीति शतरा सुखं तेन तद्वंतौ (*śamanekaniṃdriyaprasādādi rāti dadātīti śatarā sukhaṃ tena tadvaṃtau*). Since it causes delight to many senses, inner and outer, "शतर (*śatara*)" is happiness; the meaning is: those who have such happiness, or who give happiness.

शातपन्ता (*śātapantā*) -- शातपंता तीक्ष्ण स्तुतिकौ । (*śātapaṃtā tīkṣṇa stutikau |*) "शत (*śata*)" means sharp, "panta" praise: those who are fit for abundant praise. Or शातेन दुःखानां तनूकरणेन पन्यते स्तूयत इति शातपन्तं सुखं । (*śātena duḥkhānāṃ tanūkaraṇena panyate stūyata iti śātapantaṃ sukhaṃ |*) तेन युवां सुखवन् भवथः । (*tena yuvāṃ sukhavan bhavathaḥ |*) Because you lessen the sorrows of your worshippers, you are praised; that is, you create happiness for them. Therefore the meaning is that you are givers of happiness. Although the three words शिंबाता (*śiṃbātā*), शतरा (*śatarā*) and शातपन्ता (*śātapantā*) convey, more or less, the one meaning "bringing happiness", it should be understood that, since the Aśvins create happiness for their worshippers in all three times, this epithet is used here three times and there is no repetition.

### Printed p. 257

वाजीव उच्चा वयसा (*vājīva uccā vayasā*)—As horses are attractive through bodies filled out by eating grass and the like, so you shine, well nourished by the food in the form of oblation offered to you in sacrifices and the like.

घर्म्येस्था (*gharmyesthā*)—घर्म (*gharma*) means the shining mid-region. In that region you shine like the sun and moon. Or the oblation offered in the pravargya sacrifice called घर्म (*gharma*): it may also be understood that you come to that sacrifice like deities and accept that oblation.

मेषेव इषा सपर्या पुरीषा (*meṣeva iṣā saparyā purīṣā*)—सपर्यतिः परिचरणकर्मा । (*saparyatiḥ paricaraṇakarmā |*) "सपर्या (*saparyā*)" means tending and worshipping. As to पुरीषा (*purīṣā*): धनादिदानेन स्तोतृणां पोषयितारौ अथवा हविर्भिः पोषणीयौ भवथः । (*dhanādidānena stotṛṇāṃ poṣayitārau athavā havirbhiḥ poṣaṇīyau bhavathaḥ |*) Those who give wealth and the like to the worshippers and nourish them, or those who are nourished by the food in the form of oblation. As people feed and rear two rams, giving them food and the like, so people offer food in the form of oblation to these Aśvins and make them well nourished with love and respect. Or the chief intention of this sentence is that these Aśvins nourish the worshippers who praise them by giving wealth and the like.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सृ॒ण्ये॑व ज॒र्भरी॑ तु॒र्फरी॑तू नैतो॒शेव॑ तु॒र्फरी॑ पर्फ॒रीका॑ ।  
> उ॒द॒न्य॒जीव॒ जेम॑ना मदे॒रू ता मे॑ ज॒राय्व॒जरं॑ म॒रायु॑ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सृण्या इव । जर्भरी इति । तुर्फरीतू इति । नैतोशा इव । तुर्फरी इति । पर्फरीका ॥  
> उदन्यजा इव । जेमना । मदेरू इति । ता । मे । जरायु । अजरं । मरायु ॥६॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सृण्येव सृण्याविव । सृणिरंकुशः ॥ तत्र साधुरिति यत् ॥ अंकुशार्हौ मत्तगजाविन जर्भरी गात्रविनामं कुर्वंत् ॥ जभ जृभि गात्र विनामे । अस्मादौणादिको
> रिप्रत्ययः ॥ यद्वा । सृण्येव । सृणिर्द्विविधा । मत्तगजस्यैकत्रावस्थापयित्र्येका ।
> अपरा बाधयित्रि ॥ सर्तॆः किच्च । उ. ४-४९-१०४ । इत्यौणादिको निप्रत्ययः ।
> 17

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

सृण्येव (*sṛṇyeva*): like two rutting elephants fit for the goad (सृणि (*sṛṇi*)), you are जर्भरी (*jarbharī*), making the body bend (Uṇ. 4-49-104 is cited for the affix). Alternatively, the goad is of two kinds: one that makes a rutting elephant stand in one place, the other that checks it. You two are like those, holders who keep (the worshipper) in one place. And you are तुर्फरीतू (*turpharītū*), smiters of enemies. Nirukta (printed reference नि. १३-५ (*ni. 13-5*)): द्विविधा सृणिर्भवति भर्ता च हंता च तथाश्विन् चापि भर्तारौ जर्भरी भर्तारावित्यर्थस्तुर्फरीतू हंतारावित्यादि । (*dvividhā sṛṇirbhavati bhartā ca haṃtā ca tathāśvin cāpi bhartārau jarbharī bhartārāvityarthasturpharītū haṃtārāvityādi |*) नैतोशेव (*naitośeva*): नितोशति (*nitośati*) is a verb of killing; नितोश (*nitośa*) is one who kills; his offspring is नैतोश (*naitośa*). Like those two you are तुर्फरी (*turpharī*), smiters of enemies. पर्फरीका (*parpharīkā*): splitters of enemies; or fillers of the worshippers with wealth and the like.

### Printed p. 258

> कित्वादगुणः । कृदिकारादक्तिन इति जीष् । सुप आकारः । उदात्तस्वरितयोर्यण
> इति स्वरितः ॥ तादृश्यौ सृण्यानिव जर्भरी भर्तार वेकत्रैवावस्थापकौ । तथा तुर्फरीतू
> तर्फितारौ शत्रोणां हंतारौ ॥ जर्भरी । भरतेयज्लुगंतादौणादिक इप्रत्ययः ।
> अभ्यासस्य जकारश्चांदसः । तृफ तृंफ हिंसायां । अस्मात्तृजंतस्य तुर्फरीतारावित्यस्य
> पृषोदरादित्वाद्वर्णविकारः । यद्वा । अस्माद्बाहुलकादौणादिकोऽरीतुप्रत्यय
> उत्वं च । अत्र निरुक्तं । द्विविधा सृणिर्भवति भर्ता च हंता च तथाश्विन् चापि भर्तारौ
> जर्भरी भर्तारावित्यर्थस्तुर्फरीतू हंतारावित्यादि । नि. १३-५ ॥ नैतोशेव । नितोशतिर्वधकर्मा । नितोशयतीति नितोशः । तस्यापत्यं नैतोशः । ताविव तुर्फरी
> शत्रोणां हंतारौ ॥ तृफतिर्हिंसार्थः । अस्मादौणादिको रिप्रत्यय उत्वं च ॥
> पर्फरीका पर्फरीकौ शत्रोणां विदारयितार् ॥ इफला विशरणे । अस्मात्पर्फरीकादयश्च । उ. ४-२० । इतीकप्रत्ययांतो निपात्यते ॥ यद्वा । स्तोतृजनानां धनादिदानेन पूरयितार् ॥ प्य पालनपूरणयोः । अस्मादीकनि पूर्ववत्सर्वं निपात्यते ।
> फर्वतेः पूरणार्थाद्वा ॥ उपन्यजीव उपन्यजे इव । उवके भवमुदन्यं ॥
> पद्दन्नोमास् । पा. ६-१-६३ : इत्यादिनारस्मृतिष्टप्युदन्नादेशं ॥ तत्र जाते रत्ने
> इव निर्मल् कांतियुक्तावित्यर्थः । जेमना जेमन् जयशील् ॥ जयतेरन्येभ्योऽपि दृश्यंत इति मनिन् । दीर्घाभावश्चांवसं ॥ मदेरू बलातिशयेन मत्तौ
> स्तुत्या वा । ता तौ पूर्वोक्तगुणावश्विन् युवां मे मदीयं जरायु जरायुजं
> आत एव मरायु मरणशीलं शरीरमजरु जरारहितं मरणधर्मरहितं कुरुतं ॥
> नञ्चो जरमरुक्रमृता इति नञ उत्तरस्य जरशब्दस्याद्युदात्तत्वं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

(continuing) पर्फरीका (*parpharīkā*) is explained from the root फर्व (*pharva*), "to fill", as well. उदन्यजीव (*udanyajīva*): like two jewels born in water (उदन्य (*udanya*)), that is, pure and radiant. जेमना (*jemanā*): victorious by nature. मदेरू (*maderū*): exceedingly intoxicated with strength, or fit for praise. ता (*tā*): you two, of the qualities stated, make my body (जरायु (*jarāyu*), born of the membrane, hence मरायु (*marāyu*), subject to death) ageless (अजरं (*ajaraṃ*)), free from old age and from the nature of death.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Aśvins)
- सृण्येव (*sṛṇyeva*)—like rutting elephants
- जर्भरी (*jarbharī*)—of a bent body
- तुर्फरीतू (*turpharītū*)—and destroyers of enemies
- नैतोशेव (*naitośeva*)—like the sons of Nitośa
- तुर्फरी (*turpharī*)—and destroyers of enemies
- पर्फरीका (*parpharīkā*)—splitters of enemy folk, or givers of fruit to the worshippers
- उदन्यजीव (*udanyajīva*)—shining like jewels born in water
- जेमना (*jemanā*)—victorious
- मदेरू (*maderū*)—full of joy, or fit for praise
- ता (*tā*)—you
- जरायु (*jarāyu*)—which is attended by old age
- मरायु (*marāyu*)—and for that very reason inclined to death
- मे (*me*)—my body
- अजरं (*ajaraṃ*)—make free from old age

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvins, you who are of bent body like rutting elephants or like goads, and destroyers of enemies, who are, like the sons of Sitośa**NOTE**, destroyers of enemies, and givers of fruit to your worshippers, and lovers of praise, and

### Printed p. 259
You make my body, which is old and therefore facing death, free from decay.

##### English translation
You are like two mad elephants bending their forequarters and smiting the foe, like the two sons of Nitosha destroying (foes), and cheristing (friends); you are as bright as two water-born (jewels), do you, who are victorious, (render) my decaying mortal body free from decay.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Sṛṇyeva jarbharī—sṛṇi means a goad (aṅkuśa). Here it is explained as "intoxicated elephants on which the goad is used". The bhāṣyakāra explains it also as those who go with the body bent a little, as elephants bend when they walk. Or else sṛṇi is the weapon called aṅkuśa, used to keep an elephant under control; it is bent crookedly at one part. The word sṛṇi has yet another meaning, a bent implement, namely the sickle for reaping grain and the like. Both these implements are bent crookedly. It is not stated here why the bodies of the Aśvins are bent. We have given the derivation and explanation of the word sṛṇa in this same volume, in the Special Topics of Ṛk 3 of Sūkta 101 (on p. 154). The word jarbharī is formed from the root जभ जृभि (*jabha jṛbhi*) in the sense "body" (gātravināme).

Turpharītū, naitośeva turpharī, parpharīkā—the word turpharītū or turpharī is formed from the root तृप हिंसायं (*tṛpa hiṃsāyaṃ*), meaning "to injure"; the meaning is those who injure (enemies). Naitośeva—नितोशतिर्वधकर्मा ! नितॊशयतीति नितोशः । तस्यापत्यं नैतोशः । ताविव शत्रूणां हंतारौ । (*nitośatirvadhakarmā ! nitòśayatīti nitośaḥ | tasyāpatyaṃ naitośaḥ | tāviva śatrūṇāṃ haṃtārau |*) The root nitośati means "to kill". The killer is called nitośa; his sons are called naitośas. Like them, the meaning is slayers of enemies. Parpharīkā—the word is formed from the root पैपालनपूरणयोः (*paipālanapūraṇayoḥ*) meaning "protect, fill". The meaning is those who protect the people who praise them, or fulfil their wishes.

Udanyajeva—उदके भवमुदन्यं । तत्र जाते रत्ने इव निर्मलौ ॥ रत्नाकरो जलनिधिः (*udake bhavamudanyaṃ | tatra jāte ratne iva nirmalau ‖ ratnākaro jalanidhiḥ*) As this lexicon statement says, the sea (the water) is the place of origin of jewels and the like. The sense is: pure like jewels produced in water.

### Printed p. 260
Jemanā maderū—जेमन् जयशीलौ । मदेरू बलातिशयेन मत्तौ स्तुत्यौ वा । (*jeman jayaśīlau | maderū balātiśayena mattau stutyau vā |*) Jemanā means those victorious in battles and contests. Maderū means those intoxicated with their immeasurable strength, i.e. with their power and capacity, or those worthy of praise.

Tā me jarāyu marāyu ajaraṃ (kurutaṃ)—you, endowed with such qualities (the Aśvins), are here prayed to make my body, which is worn out by old age and subject to death, free from old age and free from death.

Yāska, taking one Ṛk each as an example from the Ṛks containing hyperbolic praise (atistuti), has given the Nirukta on it. He has explained Ṛks praising the deities Agni, Indra, Āditya, the Aśvins, yajña, vāk and akṣara. Among them, for the Ṛk praising the Aśvins he has taken the present Ṛk as the example and explained it.

सृण्येव जर्भरी तुर्फरी तू नैतोशेव तुर्फरी पर्फरीका । उदन्यजीव जेमना मदेरू ता मे जराय्वजरं मरायु ॥ (*sṛṇyeva jarbharī turpharī tū naitośeva turpharī parpharīkā | udanyajīva jemanā maderū tā me jarāyvajaraṃ marāyu ‖*)
सृण्येवेति द्विविधा सृणिर्भवति । भर्ता च हंता च । तथाश्विन् चापि भर्तारौ जर्भरी भर्तारावित्यर्थस्तुर्फरीतू हंतारौ । नैतोशेव तुर्फरी पर्फरीका । नितोशस्यापत्यं नैतोशं नैतोशेव तुर्फरी क्षिप्रहंतारौ । उदन्यजीव जेमना मदेरू । उदन्यजीवेत्युदकजे इव रत्ने सामुद्रे चांद्रमसेति वा जेमने जयमने । जेमना मदेरू । ता मे जरा य्वजरं मरायु । एतज्जरायुजं शरीरं शरदमजीर्णं ॥ (*sṛṇyeveti dvividhā sṛṇirbhavati | bhartā ca haṃtā ca | tathāśvin cāpi bhartārau jarbharī bhartārāvityarthasturpharītū haṃtārau | naitośeva turpharī parpharīkā | nitośasyāpatyaṃ naitośaṃ naitośeva turpharī kṣiprahaṃtārau | udanyajīva jemanā maderū | udanyajīvetyudakaje iva ratne sāmudre cāṃdramaseti vā jemane jayamane | jemanā maderū | tā me jarā yvajaraṃ marāyu | etajjarāyujaṃ śarīraṃ śaradamajīrṇaṃ ‖*) (Ni. 13-5)

Sṛṇyeveti dvividhā … turpharītū haṃtār—in this Ṛk beginning sṛṇyeva the Aśvins are praised as having two distinct kinds of qualities. The word sṛṇi has two meanings: it is the name of a bent implement. These are the sickle and the goad. With the sickle they reap the ears of the crop; that grain bears (bharati), i.e. nourishes, people. The goad injures the elephant. So the sṛṇi does both: it nourishes people and it injures. In the same way the Aśvins fulfil the wishes of those who praise them and nourish them, and they injure enemies. Hence the Aśvins, like the sṛṇi, do the two kinds of work, nourishing and injuring; this is the intention of the sentence.

### Printed p. 261
To indicate these two distinct meanings the two words jarbharī and turpharītū are used. The word jarbharī, formed from the root भृञ् भरणे (*bhṛñ bharaṇe*) (nourish), means "those who nourish"; the word turpharītū, formed from the root तृफ तृंफ हिंसायां (*tṛpha tṛṃpha hiṃsāyāṃ*) (injure), means "those who injure enemies".

Naitośeva turpharī parpharīkā … kṣiprahaṃtār—nitośa means a slayer, a killer, or a man named Nitośa, who may have been famous for killing people (enemies). His son is called naitośa. Here the dual naitośā is used; since the Aśvins are two, it is natural that the adjectives describing them are also in the dual. Like these naitośas, i.e. like the sons of Nitośa, turpharī means those who quickly kill enemies.

Udanyajeva jemanā maderū—udanya means water. Udanyajā means two jewels born in water or in the sea (in sea-water); the meaning is: those shining with surpassing radiance, or delightful like the moon. Jemanā is jayamanā, victorious in battle. Maderū means intoxicated with excess of joy, or worthy of praise.

Tā me jarāyv ajaraṃ marāyu—एतज्जरायुजं शरीरं शरदमजीर्णं ॥ (*etajjarāyujaṃ śarīraṃ śaradamajīrṇaṃ ‖*) This body is jarāyu, i.e. subject to old age, so that the limbs of the body are weak. Marāyu means subject to death, in the state of dying, facing death. The seer prays to the Aśvins that they make this my body, devoid of vigour and facing death, ajara, free from old age, and full of strength.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प॒ज्रेव॒ चर्च॑रं॒ जारं॑ म॒रायु॒ क्षद्मे॒वार्थे॑षु तर्तरीथ  
> उग्रा ।  
> ऋ॒भू नाप॑त्परम॒ज्रा ख॒रज्रु॑र्वा॒युर्न प॑र्फरत्कयद्रया॒णां ॥ ७ ॥

### Printed p. 262

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> पज्रा इव । चर्चरं । जारं । मरायु । क्षद्मइव । अर्थेषु । तर्तरीथः । उग्रा ।  
> ऋभू इति । न । आपत् । खरमज्रा । खरज्जुः । वायुः । न । पर्फरत् । क्षयत् ।  
> रयाणां ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे उग्रोग्रावुद्गूर्णतेजस्कावश्विन् पज्रेव पज्राविन प्रार्जितबलौ वीराविव
> स्थितौ । यद्वा । पज्रेव ॥ जि ज्रि अभिभवे ॥ पादाभ्यामभिभवंतौ समर्थाविन स्थितौ ।
> युवां चर्चरं चरणशीलं जारं जरायुक्तं अत एव मरायु मरणशीलं
> मदीयं शरीरमर्थेषु गंतव्येषु तरीतव्यॆष्वापदादिषु क्षदेव । उदकनामैतत् ।
> उदकमिव तर्तरीथः ॥ अत्यंतं तारयथः ॥ त्य प्लवनतरणयोः । यज्लुगंतालटि
> च्छंदस्युभयथेति थस अर्धधातुकत्वेनेडागमः । व्यतो ना । पा. ७ २-३८ । इति
> दीर्घः ॥ ऋभू नर्भू इव । दार्ष्टांतिकसाम्यादत्र द्विवचनं । ऋभू यथा
> स्वनिर्मितो रथ आपत् तद्वत् खरमज्रा खरं तीक्ष्णं मज्जयितारौ शोधयितारौ ।
> अत्यंतं शुद्धबलावित्यर्थः ॥ टुमस्टो शुद्धौ ! आस्मादौणादिको रप्रत्ययः ॥ तादृशौ
> युवां खरजुस्तीक्ष्णगतिरतिशयेन वेगवानृभुभिर्दत्तो रथो युवामापत् ।
> आप्नोत् ॥ अप्ल व्याप्तौ । लुजि लृदित्वाच्चेरज् ॥ स रथो वायुरिव पर्फरत् ।
> सर्वत्रापूरयत् । सर्वत्र व्याप्तानित्यर्थः ॥ फर्व पूरणे । आस्माल्लेट बहुलं
> छंदसीति शपः श्लुः । अभ्यासस्य रुगागमो वलोपश्च । लेटोऽडाटावित्यडागमः ॥
> ततः स रथो रयाणां धनानां धनानि क्षयत् । शत्रुभ्य आगमयत् ॥ क्षि निवासगत्योः । आस्मादंतर्णीतण्यर्थाल्लेट्यडागमः ॥ यद्वा । क्षयतिरैश्यर्यकर्मा ।
> धनानामिष्टे ॥ छांदसमनुडात्तत्वं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O fierce Aśvins, of surging splendour, you are like pajras, i.e. heroes of great strength. Or else, the word पज्रेव (*pajreva*) is from the root जि ज्रि अभिभवे (*ji jri abhibhave*): those who overcome with their feet, like capable men. You, my body—moving (carcara), aged (jāra), therefore prone to die (marāyu)—in the objects to be reached and the dangers to be crossed, like water (kṣadma is a name of water), you carry across completely. Like the Ribhus (the dual is used because of the parity with the Aśvins), as the chariot made by themselves came to them, so you are kharamajrā, sharp purifiers, of utterly pure strength. You, of sharp motion, very swift, the chariot given by the Ribhus reached (āpat); it filled everything like the wind (parpharat), i.e. pervaded all. Then that chariot brought (kṣayat) the riches of enemies to you; or else, kṣayati is a verb of lordship: it rules over riches.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उग्रा (*ugrā*)—O Aśvins of exceeding splendour
- पज्रेव (*pajreva*)—like heroes of great strength
- चर्चरं (*carcaraṃ*)—moving
- जारं (*jāraṃ*)—aged
- मरायु (*marāyu*)—facing death, (this) body
- अर्थेषु (*artheṣu*)—to a safe place
- क्षद्मेव (*kṣadmeva*)—as water is crossed by boats
- तर्तरीथः (*tartarīthaḥ*)—(carrying across from the place of danger) take and deposit it
- ऋभू न आपत् (*ṛbhū na āpat*)—like the Ribhus
- खरमज्रा (*kharamajrā*)—you are of excellent strength
- खरज्रुः (*kharajruḥ*)—your powerful chariot given by them
- वायुर्न (*vāyurna*)—swiftly like the wind
- आपत् (*āpat*)—

### Printed p. 263
 has come to the desired place
- पफः रत् (*paphaḥ rat*)—has pervaded everywhere
- रयीणां (*rayīṇāṃ*)—the wealth of enemies
- क्षयत् (*kṣayat*)—has brought

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvins of exceeding splendour, like heroes of great strength, take this moving, aged body, facing death, from the place of danger and deposit it in a safe place, as water is crossed by boats. You are of excellent strength like the Ribhus. Your powerful chariot given by them has come swift as the wind to the desired place, has pervaded everywhere, and has brought the wealth of enemies.

##### English translation
Fierce (Ashwins): like two powerful (heroes), you enable this moving perishable mortal (frame) to cross over to the objects (of its destination) as over water; extremely strong, like the Ribhus, your chariot attained its destination swift as the wind, it pervaded (everywhere), it dispensed riches.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Pajreva—पज्राविन प्रार्जितबल् वीराविन स्थितौ । (*pajrāvina prārjitabal vīrāvina sthitau |*) Those who are like heroes capable of fighting by their own strength; that is, like warriors who, without wanting another's help, have firm confidence in their own power and capacity.

Ṛbhū na—although the Ribhus are three, Ṛbhu, Vibhu and Vāja, here the dual ṛbhū is used as the simile for the two Aśvins.

Vāyuḥ na parpharat—the word parpharat is formed from the root पर्व पूरणे (*parva pūraṇe*) and means "filling, pervading". As the wind pervades and fills all places, so the chariot of the Aśvins pervades everywhere, i.e. moves in all places.

Kṣayat rayāṇāṃ—this chariot wins the wealth of enemies from them and brings it; or, as the statement क्षयतिरैश्वर्यकर्मा (*kṣayatiraiśvaryakarmā*) says, this chariot is the chief abode or support of riches.

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> घ॒र्मेव॒ मधु॑ ज॒ठरे॑ स॒नेरू॒ भगे॑विता तु॒र्फरी॒ फारि॒वारं॑ ।  
> प॒त॒रेव॑ चच॒रा चं॒द्रनि॑र्णि॒ज् मन॑ऋंगा मन॒न्या॒३॒॑ न जग्मी॑ ॥ ८ ॥

> पतराऽइव । चचरा । चंद्रनिर्निक् । मनःयंगा । मनन्या ' न । जग्मी इति ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> घर्मेव घर्माविव महावीराविव जठरे स्वोदरे मधु मधुनः ॥ षष्ठा
> लुक् ॥ धृतस्य सनेरू संभक्तारौ ॥ वन षण संभक्तौ । अस्मादौणादिक एरुप्रत्ययः ॥
> भगेविता । भगो धनं । तद्विषयरक्षणयुक्तौ ॥ अवतेर्भावेकः । तत्पुरुषे कृति
> बहुलमिति बहुलवचनाद्दहुप्रीहावप्यलुक् । यद्वा । अवतेस्त्वजंतस्य सुपो
> डादेशः । स्वरस्तु च्छांदसः ॥ अरमलं तुर्फरी शत्रूणां हिंसितारौ अत एव फारिवा ।
> फारिरायुधं । तद्वंतौ भवतः ॥ स्पुरतिर्वधकर्मा । अस्माण्णिचि चिस्सुरोण् ।
> पा. ६-१-५४ । इत्यात्वं । तदंतादिप्रत्ययः । सकारलोपश्चांदसः । छंदसीवनिपाविति
> मत्त्वर्थियो वनिप् । सुपो डादेशः ॥ पतरेव पतराविन पतनशीलौ पक्षिणाविन चचरा
> संचरंत् चंद्रनिर्णिक् । निर्णिगिति रूपनाम ॥ सुपो लुक् ॥ चंद्रनिर्णिजौ चंद्रसदृशरूपयुक्तौ । यद्वा । चंद्रमाह्लादिकं रूपं ययोस्तौ वर्तेते । मनऋंगा
> मनसा व्रसाधनं ययोस्त् ॥ ऋंजतिः प्रसाधनकर्मा । अस्माद्भावे घञ् ।
> बहुव्रीहिस्वरः ॥ मनन्या न मनन्यौ यथा ! मनने साधू ! स्तुत्यावित्यर्थः ॥
> मननशब्दात्तत्र साधुरिति यत् । तित्स्वरितं ॥ तादृशानिव जग्मी यज्ञं प्रत्यागमनशीलौ
> भवतः ॥ गमेरादृगमहन इति किन्द्रत्ययः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Like gharmas, i.e. like the great vessels (mahāvīra), you are the sharers (sanerū) of honey, i.e. of ghee (the genitive suffix being dropped), in your own belly. Bhage-vitā: bhaga is wealth; you are endowed with protection concerning it. Aram: enough; turpharī: destroyers of enemies, and hence phārivā, having the weapon phāri. Like pataras, i.e. falling birds, ranging about (cacarā). Candranirṇik: nirṇik is a name for form; of moon-like form; or else, those whose form is delightful like the moon. Manaṛṅgā: those whose adornment is by mind (ṛñjati means adorning). Mananyā na: like those good at reflection, praiseworthy. Jagmī: those who are inclined to come to the sacrifice.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Aśvins) घर्मेव (*gharmeva*)—as the camasa vessels called Mahāvīra are filled with oblation
- जठरे (*jaṭhare*)—in your bellies
- मधु (*madhu*)—the sweet (Soma)
- सनेरू (*sanerū*)—

### Printed p. 265

*(The Pratipadārtha continues from the previous page.)*

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- …—those who are filled (with it)
- भगेविता (*bhagevitā*)—as protectors of wealth
- अरं (*araṃ*)—fully
- तुर्फरी (*turpharī*)—as destroyers of enemies
- फारिवा (*phārivā*)—as those who carry weapons
- पतरेव (*patareva*)—like birds
- चचरा (*cacarā*)—those who move about
- चंद्रनिर्निक् (*caṃdranirnik*)—those having an attractive form
- मनयंगा (*manayaṃgā*)—those who are adorned
- मनन्या न (*mananyā na*)—as if worthy of praise
- जग्मी (*jagmī*)—those who come to the sacrificial hall

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvins, as in the sacrifice called Mahāvīra the Camasa vessels are full of oblation, so you keep your bellies filled with honey. You are protectors of wealth and wielders of weapons that fully destroy enemies. You move about like birds; you have attractive forms, are adorned, and are worthy of praise. Such as you are, you come to our sacrificial hall.

##### English translation
With your bellies full of the Soma, like two saucepans, preservers of wealth, destroyers of enemies, (you are) armed with hatchets, moving like two flying (birds) with forms like the moon, attaining success through the mind, like two laudable beings, ( you are) approaching (the sacrifice).

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> बृ॒हंते॑व गं॒भरे॑षु प्रति॒ष्ठां पादे॑व गा॒धं तर॑ते विदाथः ।  
> कर्णे॑व॒ शासु॒रनु॒ हि स्मरा॒थोंशे॑व नो भजतं चि॒त्रमप्नः॑ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> बृहंता इव । गंभरेषु । प्रतिस्थां । पादा इव । गाधं । तरते । विदाथः ।  
> कर्णा इव । शासुः । अनु । हि । स्मराथः । अंशा इव । नः । भजतं । चित्रं । अप्पः ॥९॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> बृहंतेव बृहंताविव प्रांशू पुरुषौ यथा गंभीरेषु गहनेषु जलेषु
> प्रतिष्ठां विंदतः तद्वद्गह्वरेषु गंभीरेषु दुष्टवेशेषु स्थानेषु प्रतिष्ठामवस्थितिमा-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

बृहंतेव (*bṛhaṃteva*) means "like two tall men". As tall men find a footing in deep, hard-to-cross waters, so you find a footing, a secure standing place, in deep, hard-to-pass places. विदाथः (*vidāthaḥ*) means "you know". ()

### Printed p. 266

> सदं युवां विदाथः । जानीथः । पादेव पादाविन यथा तरते । षष्ठ्यर्थे चतुर्थि।
> तरतः पुरुषस्य पादौ यथा गाधं जलं वित्तः तद्वद्युवां तार्येषु जलेषु गाधं
> विदाथः ॥ जानीथः ॥ विद ज्ञाने । लेट्यडागमः ॥ कर्णेव यथा कर्णावुक्तं शब्दं
> विदतुः तद्वद्युवां शासुः शासितारं स्तुतिं कुर्वाणं जनमनु हि स्मराथः । अनुकूलं
> जानीथः खलु ॥ स्मरतेर्लेट्यडागमः । हियोगादनिघातः । अधीगर्थदयेशां
> कर्मणि । पा. २-३-५२ । इति शासुरित्यस्य षष्ठि । अंशेवांशाविव यथावयन् तद्वंतं
> यज्ञं भजेते तद्वद्युवां नोऽस्मदीयं चित्रं चायनीयमप्पः कर्म भजतं ।
> आश्रयतं ॥ अप्पः । अप्ल व्याप्तौ । अस्मादापः कर्माख्यायां ह्रस्वो नुट्ट वा ।
> उ. ४-२०७ । इत्यसुन् तत्संनियोगेन ह्रस्वो नुडागमश्च । नित्स्वरेणाद्युदात्तः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

पादेव (*pādeva*) means "like two feet". As the feet of a man crossing find a ford in water, so you find fords in waters that can be crossed. कर्णेव (*karṇeva*): as two ears grasp a spoken sound, so you attentively note the शासुः (*śāsuḥ*), the praiser, the man who offers praise; "you know him favourably". Sāyaṇa cites Pā. 2-3-52 for the genitive sense of शासुः (*śāsuḥ*). अंशेव (*aṃśeva*): as two shares partake of the one who owns them, so you take up अप्पः (*appaḥ*), our wonderful, desirable rite; भजतं (*bhajataṃ*) means "resort to it". He then explains the formation of अप्पः (*appaḥ*) (Uṇ. Sū. 4-207 is cited).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- बृहंतेव (*bṛhaṃteva*)—like tall men who, though they stand in deep water, make their condition known
- गंभरेषु (*gaṃbhareṣu*)—in deep water
- प्रतिष्ठां (*pratiṣṭhāṃ*)—your being (there)
- विदाथः (*vidāthaḥ*)—you know
- तरतः (*tarataḥ*)—of a man crossing a river
- पादेव (*pādeva*)—like the feet
- गाधं (*gādhaṃ*)—you know also the deep places
- कर्णेव (*karṇeva*)—like ears
- शासुः (*śāsuḥ*)—the praise of the praiser who praises you
- अनु हि स्मराथः (*anu hi smarāthaḥ*)—you understand readily
- अंशेव (*aṃśeva*)—you who are like two shoulders that support the sacrifice
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- चित्रं (*citraṃ*)—attractive
- अप्नः (*apnaḥ*)—the (sacrificial) rite
- भजतं (*bhajataṃ*)—attend to it

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvins, as tall men standing in deep water make their condition known, so you know your being (there). Like the feet of a man crossing a river, you know the deep places. Like ears, you readily understand the praise of the praiser who praises you. You who are like two shoulders supporting the sacrifice, attend to our attractive rite.

##### English translation
Like two big (men) in deep waters you find a resting-place, like two fcet crossing a ford you find (the fording-place), like two ears you recognize (the voice) of the reciter of your praises, like two shoulders (supporting the sacrifice, come to our desirable rite.

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ॒रं॒ग॒रेव॒ मध्येर॑येथे सार॒घेव॒ गवि॑ नी॒चीन॑बारे ।  
> की॒नारे॑व॒ स्वेद॑मासिष्टिदा॒ना क्षामे॑वो॒र्जा सू॑यव॒सात्स॑चेथे ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आरंगराइव । मधु । आ । ईरयेथे इति । सारघाइव । गवि । नीचीनबारे ।  
> कीनाराइव । स्वेदं । आसिस्विदाना । क्षामइव । ऊर्जा । सुयवसअत् । सचेथे  
> इति ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आरंगरेव ॥ :ग्य शब्दे । अस्माद्भाव ऋदोरप् । अरमलं पर्याप्तं गरः
> शब्दनं । तस्य संबंधिनौ । तस्येदमित्यण् ॥ शब्दवंतौ मेघाविव यथा मधु जलं
> प्रेरयतः तद्वद्युवामेरयेथे । प्रेरयथः ॥ ईर प्रेरणे चौरादिकः ॥ सारघेव
> सारघाविव । सरघा मधुमक्षिका । सरष्टैव सारघा ॥ प्रज्ञादित्वादण् । पा. ५-४-३८ ॥
> ते यथा नीचीनबारे नीचीनद्वारे मधुपुष्टे मधु प्रेरयतः तद्वद्युवां नीचीनबारे न्यग्भूतद्वारे गवि गोरूधसि क्षीरं प्रक्षिपथः । कीनारेव कीनाराविव कुत्सितमनुष्यौ यथा स्विद्यमान् भवतः तद्वत्वेदं स्विद्यमानं जलमासिस्विदाना समंतात्
> स्वेदयंत् प्रक्षारयंत् भवथः ॥ इष्षिदा गात्रप्रक्षणे ! अनुदात्तेत् । तस्माच्छांदसे लिट कानजादेशः । क्षामेव । पै जै पै क्षये । अस्मादातो मनिन् ।
> ह्रस्वश्चांदसः ॥ क्षामा क्षीणा गौः सुयवसात् ॥ अदेः क्विप् ॥ शोभनं यवसं
> घासादिकं भक्षयंती क्षीररूपेणान्नेन समवेता यथा भवति तद्वद्युवामूर्जा
> हवीरूपेणान्नेन सचेथे ! संगच्छेथे ॥ षच समवाये ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

आरंगरेव (*āraṃgareva*): अरं (*araṃ*) means "enough, sufficient", and गरः (*garaḥ*) means "sounding"; the compound refers to things connected with sound, that is, resounding clouds. As clouds send forth मधु (*madhu*), water, so you two send it. सारघेव (*sāragheva*): सरघा (*saraghā*) is the honey-bee, and सारघा (*sāraghā*) is the same (Pā. 5-4-38 is cited). As they put honey into a hive with a downward opening, so you pour milk into the cow's udder, which has its opening turned downward. कीनारेव (*kīnāreva*): as two wretched men come to sweat, so you two are sprinkling and washing about the sweating water. क्षामेव (*kṣāmeva*): क्षामा (*kṣāmā*) is a wasted cow. As a cow eating good fodder, grass and the like, is joined with food in the form of milk, so you are joined with ऊर्जा (*ūrjā*), food in the form of oblation; सचेथे (*sacethe*) means "you come together".

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- आरंगरेव (*āraṃgareva*)—like clouds that make sound
- मधु (*madhu*)—tasty water
- ईरयेथे (*īrayethe*)—you send
- सारघेव (*sāragheva*)—as honey-bees gather honey

### Printed p. 268

- नीचीनबारे (*nīcīnabāre*)—having an opening that faces downward
- गवि (*gavi*)—you fill milk in the udder of the cow
- कीनारेव (*kīnāreva*)—like people who work hard
- स्वेदं (*svedaṃ*)—as they pour out drops of sweat
- आसिस्विदाना (*āsisvidānā*)—you sprinkle water all around us
- क्षामा (*kṣāmā*)—a lean cow
- सुयवसादिव (*suyavasādiva*)—as it eats excellent grass
- ऊर्जा (*ūrjā*)—food in the form of oblation
- सचेथे (*sacethe*)—you partake of

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvins, like clouds that give out sound, you send down tasty water. As honey-bees gather honey, you fill the udder of the cow, which has turned its opening downward, with milk. As people who work hard pour out sweat, you sprinkle drops of water all around us. As a lean cow eats good grass, you partake of the food in the form of oblation.

##### English translation
Like two loud-sounding (clonds). you send forth the sweet (rain); like two bees (you deposit milk) in (the udder of) the cow which has its opening turned downwards; like two labourers you are dripping with perspi- ration, like a tired cow eating sweet herbage, you attend (the sacrifice).

#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ऋ॒ध्याम॒ स्तोमं॑ सनु॒याम॒ वाज॒ना नो॒ मंत्रं॑ स॒रथे॒होप॑ यातं ।  
> यशो॒ न प॒क्वं मधु॒ गोष्टं॒तरा भू॒तांशो॑ अ॒श्विनोः॒  ॑
> काममप्राः ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ऋध्याम । स्तोमं । सनुयाम । वाजं । आ । नः । मंत्रं । सरथा । इह । उप ।  
> यातं ।  
> यशः । न । पक्वं । मधु । गोषु । अंतः । आ । भूतांशः । अश्विनोः । कामं ।  
> अप्राः ॥ ११ ॥

### Printed p. 269

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अश्विन् वयं स्तोमं त्रिवृत्पंचदशादिकमृध्याम । ऋध्यास्म । वर्धयेम ॥ यधु वृद्धौ । आस्मादाशीर्लिजि किदाशिषीति यासुटः कित्त्वं ॥ वाजं
> हनिर्लक्षणमन्तं युवाभ्यां सनुयाम । प्रयच्छेम ॥ षणु दाने तानादिकः ।
> विधिलिज् । वाक्यभेदादनिघातः ॥ तस्माद्युवां सरथा सरथ् समानरथावेकमेव
> रथमारूढौ संताविहास्मिन्कर्मणि नोऽस्मदीयं मंत्रं मननीयं स्तोत्रमुप
> यातं । उपागच्छतं । गोष्टंतर्गॊरूधस्यवस्थितं पक्वं परिणतं आत एव मधु
> मधुरं यशो न । यश इत्यन्ननाम । नश्चार्थे । क्षीराज्यादिलक्षणं महावीरेवनीयमानमन्नं चापेक्ष्यागच्छतमिति संबंधः । एवमुक्तप्रकारेण भूतांश एतन्नामर्षिरश्विनोः काममभिलाषमात्मीयाभिः स्तुतिभिरप्राः । आपूरयत् । संपूर्णमकार्षिदित्यर्थः ॥ प्रा पूरणे आदादिकः । व्यत्ययेन मध्यमः ॥ अत्राश्विने सूक्ते
> सर्वाण्यपि पदानि दुर्व्युत्पादान्यस्माभिर्दिङ्मात्रं प्रदर्शितानि । एवं निरुक्तव्याकरणादिभिरर्थविशेषे बुद्धिमद्भिरुन्नेयानि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins, may we increase (ऋध्याम (*ṛdhyāma*), "may we make grow") the स्तोमं (*stomaṃ*), the Stoma such as the Trivṛt and Pañcadaśa. May we give (सनुयाम (*sanuyāma*)) to you two the वाजं (*vājaṃ*), food in the form of oblation. Therefore you two, being सरथा (*sarathā*), mounted on one and the same chariot, come here to this rite, to our मंत्रं (*maṃtraṃ*), our hymn to be pondered. Come also to the पक्वं (*pakvaṃ*), ripened, and thus मधु (*madhu*), sweet, thing that lies within the cow's udder. यशः (*yaśaḥ*) is a name for food, and न (*na*) has the sense of "and". The connection is: come having regard to milk, ghee and the like, and to the food being brought in the Mahāvīra vessel. In this way the seer named Bhūtāṃśa अप्राः (*aprāḥ*), filled (that is, fully satisfied) the desire of the Aśvins with his own praises. Sāyaṇa adds that in this Aśvin hymn all the words are hard to derive and have been shown by him only in outline, and that the intelligent should work out the particular meanings by means of the Nirukta, grammar and the like.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- स्तोमं (*stomaṃ*)—the praise offered with you in view
- यध्याम (*yadhyāma*)—may we increase it
- वाजं (*vājaṃ*)—food in the form of oblation
- सनुयाम (*sanuyāma*)—may we offer it to you
- सरथा (*sarathā*)—seated in one chariot
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- मंत्रं (*maṃtraṃ*)—to accept the praise
- इह (*iha*)—here
- उप यातं (*upa yātaṃ*)—come
- गोष्टंतः (*goṣṭaṃtaḥ*)—that which is in the udder
- पक्वं (*pakvaṃ*)—and ripened
- मधु (*madhu*)—and sweet
- यशो न (*yaśo na*)—also for the food (turn towards it, having in view milk and the like); come
- भूतांशः (*bhūtāṃśaḥ*)—the seer Bhūtāṃśa
- अश्विनोः (*aśvinoḥ*)—of the Aśvins
- कामं (*kāmaṃ*)—the desired object
- अप्राः (*aprāḥ*)—has filled with his praises **NOTE**

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvins, may we increase (may we recite more) the Trivṛt and other praises offered with you in view. May we offer you food in the form of oblation. Seated in one chariot, come here to accept our praise. Come here also having in view the milk and the like produced from the cow's udder. The seer named Bhūtāṃśa has filled the desire of the Aśvins with his praises.

### Printed p. 270

##### English translation
May we increase our praise, (O Ashwins), may we offer you food, come hither in the same chariot to our worships; and accept the sweet food prepared from within the cows. Bhuthamsha has fulfilled the desires of the learned.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

On the use of this hymn in ritual, the Ṛgvidhāna says as follows: भूतांशस्य ऋषेः सूक्तं प्रजाकामः शुचिर्जपन् । अनुरूपां प्रजामाशु लभते नात्र संशयः ॥ स्थालीपाकेन नासत्यावश्विन् तु यजेद्विजः । अनेनैवोपचारेण हुत्वाश्वाननुमंत्रयेत् ॥ पायसं कृशरामांसनोदनं वधिसक्तुकान् । कुल्माषांश्च करंभांश्च फलानि विविधानि च ॥ चित्रं माल्यं शुभान् गंधान् अन्नपानानि यानि च । भक्ष्यं भोज्यं च पेयं च समाहृत्योदिते रवौ । भूतांशमभ्यसेत्तावद्यावदस्तमितो रविः ॥ अर्धरात्रे त्वतिक्रांते ततोऽश्विभ्यां निवेदयेत् ॥ दीर्घायुषं सरूपं च लभेत्पुत्रं सुवर्चसं । रूपवांश्च भवेन्नित्यं भूतांशं योऽभ्यसेत्सदा । सर्वकामिकमित्येतम्मृषिं विद्याद्विचक्षणः ॥ (*bhūtāṃśasya ṛṣeḥ sūktaṃ prajākāmaḥ śucirjapan | anurūpāṃ prajāmāśu labhate nātra saṃśayaḥ ‖ sthālīpākena nāsatyāvaśvin tu yajedvijaḥ | anenaivopacāreṇa hutvāśvānanumaṃtrayet ‖ pāyasaṃ kṛśarāmāṃsanodanaṃ vadhisaktukān | kulmāṣāṃśca karaṃbhāṃśca phalāni vividhāni ca ‖ citraṃ mālyaṃ śubhān gaṃdhān annapānāni yāni ca | bhakṣyaṃ bhojyaṃ ca peyaṃ ca samāhṛtyodite ravau | bhūtāṃśamabhyasettāvadyāvadastamito raviḥ ‖ ardharātre tvatikrāṃte tato 'śvibhyāṃ nivedayet ‖ dīrghāyuṣaṃ sarūpaṃ ca labhetputraṃ suvarcasaṃ | rūpavāṃśca bhavennityaṃ bhūtāṃśaṃ yo 'bhyasetsadā | sarvakāmikamityetammṛṣiṃ vidyādvicakṣaṇaḥ ‖*) (Ṛgvidhāna, Adhyāya 4, Ślokas 10-15).

If a man who desires offspring recites this hymn, उभा उ नूनं (*ubhā u nūnaṃ*), seen by the seer Bhūtāṃśa, while he is ritually pure, he obtains a worthy, excellent son. There is no doubt in this matter. The Aśvins are to be invoked according to rule; then, by the method of the sthālīpāka, offerings are made in the fire of milk-rice, meat, cooked rice, flour mixed with curds, various fruits and the like. Afterwards they are worshipped with various perfumes, garlands and the like, and various foods to eat are made ready. From sunrise until sunset (the hymn of) this Bhūtāṃśa-seen (seer) ….

### Printed p. 271
…(the sūktas) recited by a person who has become pure and keeps this Bhūtāṃśa sūkta in mind. If, after midnight has passed, he makes an offering to the Aśvin deities, he obtains a son endowed with long life, good form and radiance. It is said that if this Bhūtāṃśa sūkta is recited daily, that man becomes handsome and obtains all his desired objects.

## Sūkta 107 (Maṇḍala 10)

**Introduction.**
This sūkta praises the dakṣiṇā given to the ṛtviks (officiating priests) in sacrifices, or else the sacrificers who give that dakṣiṇā. About this sūkta the Bṛhaddevatā says as follows:
सूक्तेन तु परेणात्र स्वयमाविरभूदिति । आत्मानमेव तुष्टाव प्राजापत्याथ दक्षिणा ॥ दात्यनत्र स्तुतानेके दक्षिणानां वदंति तु । दातृत्वाद्दक्षिणानां च भोज्याश्च तसृभिः स्तुताः ॥ (*sūktena tu pareṇātra svayamāvirabhūditi | ātmānameva tuṣṭāva prājāpatyātha dakṣiṇā ‖ dātyanatra stutāneke dakṣiṇānāṃ vadaṃti tu | dātṛtvāddakṣiṇānāṃ ca bhojyāśca tasṛbhiḥ stutāḥ ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 8-22, 23)

In this sūkta 107, beginning आविरभूत् (*āvirabhūt*), the deity praised is Dakṣiṇā Prājāpatyā. Although Divya of the Aṅgirasa line is the seer of this sūkta, Dakṣiṇā Prājāpatyā, the daughter of Prajāpati, is also said to be a seer of it along with him. It may be noted that this deity Dakṣiṇā Prājāpatyā has praised herself in this sūkta. Some say that this sūkta praises the donors (sacrificers) who are to give the dakṣiṇā in sacrifices and similar rites; others say that the dakṣiṇā itself is praised. Since the generous givers called भोजाः (*bhojāḥ*) are also included in these acts of giving, these generous donors, the भोजाः (*bhojāḥ*), are praised in the last four ṛks (8–11).

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आविरित्येकादशर्चमष्टमं सूक्तं । दिव्यो नामांगिरस ऋषिः । प्रजापतेः
> सुता दक्षिणा सा वर्षिका । शतधारं वायुमिति चतुर्थी जगती शिष्टा दश त्रिष्टुभः ।
> अनेन सूक्तेनर्त्विग्यो दीयमाना दक्षिणा तद्दातारो यजमाना वा स्तूयंते ।
> अतः सैव देवता । तथा चानुक्रांतं । आविर्दिव्यो दक्षिणा वा प्राजापत्या दक्षिणां
> तद्दात्वान्स्चतुर्थी जगतीति ॥ गतो विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This is the eighth sūkta, of eleven ṛks. The seer is Divya of the Aṅgirasa line; Dakṣiṇā, the daughter of Prajāpati, is also a seer. The fourth ṛk, *śatadhāraṃ vāyuṃ*, is in Jagatī; the remaining ten are in Triṣṭubh. By this sūkta the dakṣiṇā given to the ṛtviks, or the sacrificers who give it, are praised; hence that same dakṣiṇā is the deity. He quotes the Anukramaṇī to this effect: आविर्दिव्यो दक्षिणा वा प्राजापत्या दक्षिणां तद्दात्वान्स्चतुर्थी जगतीति ॥ (*āvirdivyo dakṣiṇā vā prājāpatyā dakṣiṇāṃ taddātvānscaturthī jagatīti ‖*) The viniyoga (ritual application) has been stated.

### Printed p. 272
Anuvāda: This sūkta beginning आविः (*āviḥ*) is the eighth sūkta in the ninth anuvāka. It has eleven ṛks. Its seer is Divya of the Aṅgirasa line; or else the woman Dakṣiṇā, the daughter of Prajāpati, may also be the seer. In this sūkta the fourth ṛk, शतधारं (*śatadhāraṃ*), is in the Jagatī metre; the remaining ten ṛks are all in Triṣṭubh. In this sūkta the dakṣiṇā that is to be given to the ṛtviks, and the sacrificers who give it, are praised. Therefore the deity of this sūkta is the dakṣiṇā or the sacrificers; in the Anukramaṇī it is said: आविर्दिव्यो दक्षिणा वा प्राजापत्या दक्षिणां तद्दात्नास्तौच्चतुर्थि जगती (*āvirdivyo dakṣiṇā vā prājāpatyā dakṣiṇāṃ taddātnāstauccaturthi jagatī*). The viniyoga was stated earlier.

Sūkta 107
Maṇḍala 10 ‖ Anuvāka 9 ‖ Sūkta 107 ‖
Aṣṭaka 8 ‖ Adhyāya 6 ‖ Varga 3, 4 ‖
Number of ṛks in the sūkta: 11 ‖
Ṛṣi: Dimma (Divya?) **NOTE** or Dakṣiṇā Prājāpatyā ‖
Deity: Dakṣiṇā or her givers ‖
Metre: 1–3, 5–11 Triṣṭubh ‖ 4 Jagatī ‖

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ॒विर॑भू॒न्महि॒ माघो॑नमेषां॒ विश्वं॑ जी॒वं तम॑सो॒ निर॑मोचि ।  
> महि॒ ज्योतिः॑ पि॒तृभि॑र्द॒त्तमागा॑दु॒रुः पंथा॒ दक्षि॑णाया अदर्शि॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आविः । अभूत् । महि । माघोनं । एषां । विश्वं । जीवं । तमसः । निः । अमोचि ।  
> महि । ज्योतिः । पितृभिः । दत्तं । आ । अगात् । उरुः । पंथाः । दक्षिणायाः । अदर्शि ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इदं सूक्तं दक्षिणाया ना तद्दात्यणां ना स्तावकं । दक्षिणा तु यागांगं
> यागस्तु सायंकाले न क्रियते । न सायमस्ति देवया अजुष्टं । ऋ. ५-७७-२ ।
> इत्यादिश्रवणात् । तस्मादह्नेव कर्तव्यः । अहश्च सूर्योदयात्पश्चाद्भवतीति

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This sūkta praises dakṣiṇā or those who give it. Dakṣiṇā is a part of the sacrifice, and the sacrifice is not performed in the evening, since it is heard (in the Veda): न सायमस्ति देवया अजुष्टं (*na sāyamasti devayā ajuṣṭaṃ*) (Ṛ. 5-77-2) and the like. Therefore it must be done by day, and day comes after sunrise; hence sunrise is spoken of at the beginning of the sūkta.

### Printed p. 273

> सूक्तादौ सूर्योदयोऽभिधीयते । माघोनं । मघवेंद्रः । इंद्रश्च सूर्यचैत्रमासे तयोरिंद्र इति स्मरणात् । तस्य संबंधि ॥ तस्येदमित्यण् । आतद्धिते
> प्रत्यये संप्रसारणमभिहितं । पा. ६-४-१३३ । सर्वविधीनां छंदसि विकल्पितत्वादत्र
> तद्धितेऽपि संप्रसारणं ॥ सूर्यात्मकस्येंद्रस्य स्वभूतं महि महत्तेज एषां
> यजमानानां यागसिद्ध्यर्थमाविरभूत् । प्रकाशितमुद्धतमभूत् । तत्र विश्वं सर्वं
> जीवं स्थावरजंगमात्मकं जगत्तमसः सकाशान्निरमोचि । निर्मुक्तमभूत् ॥
> मुंचतेः कर्मलुजि चिणि रूपं ॥ अथानंतरं पितृभिर्यत्पितृभिर्देवैर्दत्तं नो
> हविषामागमनाय तन्महि महज्योतिः सूर्याख्यमागात् । आगच्छति । पश्चाद्दक्षिणाया यागांगभूताया उरुर्महान् पंथा मार्गोऽदर्शि । सर्वैर्यजमानैर्दृष्टोऽभूत् । सर्वे यागं कृत्वर्त्विग्भो दक्षिणां दत्तवंत इत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

माघोनं (*māghonaṃ*): Maghavan is Indra, and Indra is Sūrya, since it is recalled that in the month of Caitra "of the two (Sūrya and Indra) it is Indra" **NOTE**. The word means "belonging to him". The great splendour belonging to Indra in the form of Sūrya has become manifest, risen up, for the success of the sacrifice of these sacrificers. Thereupon the whole living world, moving and unmoving, has been freed from darkness. Next, the great light called Sūrya, bestowed by the Pitṛs (the divine fathers) for the coming of our oblations, has arrived. Afterwards a broad path of the dakṣiṇā, which is part of the sacrifice, has been seen by all the sacrificers; the meaning is that all, having performed the sacrifice, gave dakṣiṇā to the ṛtviks.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- माघोनं (*māghonaṃ*)—belonging to Indra
- महि (*mahi*)—wonderful splendour
- एषां (*eṣāṃ*)—of these sacrificers, for the success of the sacrifice
- आविरभूत् (*āvirabhūt*)—has become manifest
- विश्वं जीवं (*viśvaṃ jīvaṃ*)—the whole world, moving and unmoving
- तमसः (*tamasaḥ*)—from darkness
- निरमोचि (*niramoci*)—has been freed
- पितृभिः (*pitṛbhiḥ*)—by the Pitṛ deities
- दत्तं (*dattaṃ*)—offered
- महि (*mahi*)—great
- ज्योतिः (*jyotiḥ*)—this light, called Sūrya
- आगात् (*āgāt*)—is approaching
- दक्षिणायाः (*dakṣiṇāyāḥ*)—of the sacrificial ground
- उरुः पंथाः (*uruḥ paṃthāḥ*)—the broad path
- अदर्शि (*adarśi*)—is becoming visible

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The wonderful splendour of Indra (Sūrya) has become manifest for the success of the sacrificer's sacrifice. The whole world, moving and unmoving, is freed from darkness. This light called Sūrya, offered by the Pitṛ deities and great, is approaching. The broad path of the sacrificial ground is becoming visible; that is, the sacrificial rite has begun.

##### English translation
The great (splendour) of Maghavan has become manifest (for the rites) of these (worshippers); all life has been extricated from darkness; the vast light bestowed by the progenirors has arrived, a spacious path for the dakshina has been displayed.

### Printed p. 274
#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒च्चा दि॒वि दक्षि॑णावंतो अस्थु॒र्ये अ॑श्व॒दाः स॒ह ते  ॑
> सूर्येण ।  
> हि॒र॒ण्य॒दा अ॑मृत॒त्वं भ॑जंते वासो॒दाः सो॑म॒ प्र ति॑रंत॒  ॑
> आयुः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उच्चा । दिवि । दक्षिणावंतः । अस्तुः । ये । अश्वदाः । सह । ते । सूर्येण ।  
> हिरण्यदाः । अमृतत्वं । भजंते । वासदाः । सोम । प्र । तिरंते । आयुः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> दक्षिणावंतो दक्षिणाया देयत्वेन तद्वंतो दक्षिणां दत्तवंतो यजमाना
> उच्चोच्चैः स्थानैः स्थिते दिवि द्युलोकेऽस्कुः । तिष्ठंति । सामान्येनोक्त्वा दातृविशेषाणां फलविशेषमाह अश्वदा अश्वानां दातारो ये यजमानाः ते सूर्येण
> सर्वस्य स्वस्वकर्मणि प्रेरकेणादित्येन सह तिष्ठंति । ये हिरण्यदा हिरण्यदात्रारः
> तेऽमृतत्वममरणधर्मत्वं देवत्वं भजंते । अमृतं वै हिरण्यमित्याम्नानात् ।
> तथा सौम्यं वै वास इति श्रवणात्सोम एव संबोध्यते । हे सोम वासोदास्त्वद्देवत्यवस्त्राणां दातारो ये संति ते त्वया सह तिष्ठंतीति शेषः । एते सर्व
> आयुर्जीवनं प्रतिरंते । प्रवर्धयंति । प्रपूर्वस्तिरतिर्वृद्ध्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The दक्षिणावंतः (*dakṣiṇāvaṃtaḥ*) are the sacrificers who have given dakṣiṇā, as something to be given; they stand in heaven, in the high places. Having said this generally, he states the particular fruit for particular givers: those sacrificers who are givers of horses stay together with Sūrya, the impeller of all to their own works, that is Āditya. Those who give gold obtain immortality, the state of not dying, godhood, since it is handed down that "gold is immortality". Likewise, since it is heard that "raiment belongs to Soma", Soma himself is addressed: O Soma, those who are givers of garments dedicated to you stay with you (this is to be supplied). All these prolong, increase, their life; the verb तिरति (*tirati*) with the prefix प्र (*pra*) has the sense of growth.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- दक्षिणावंतः (*dakṣiṇāvaṃtaḥ*)—sacrificers who gave dakṣiṇā
- उच्चा (*uccā*)—high
- दिवि (*divi*)—in the heavenly world
- अस्थुः (*asthuḥ*)—are established
- ये (*ye*)—which sacrificers
- अश्वदाः (*aśvadāḥ*)—gave a horse
- ते (*te*)—they
- सूर्येण सह (*sūryeṇa saha*)—are united with Āditya
- हिरण्यदाः (*hiraṇyadāḥ*)—those who gave gold
- अमृतत्वं (*amṛtatvaṃ*)—freedom from death (godhood)
- भजंते (*bhajaṃte*)—have attained
- सोम (*soma*)—O Soma
- वासोदाः (*vāsodāḥ*)—those who gave garments
- आयुः (*āyuḥ*)—their life
- प्रति तिरंते (*prati tiraṃte*)—increase

### Printed p. 275

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The sacrificers who distributed dakṣiṇā are established in heaven, the high region. The sacrificers who gave horses are united with Āditya. Those who gave gold have attained immortality (godhood). O Soma, those who gave garments increase their life.

##### English translation
The donors of the dakshina have mounted high in heaven; those who are givers of horses dwell with the sun ; the givers of gold obtain immortality the givers of raiment, O Soma, prolong their life.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> दैवी॑ पू॒र्तिर्दक्षि॑णा देवय॒ज्या न क॑वा॒रिभ्यो॑ न॒हि ते पृ॒णंति॑ ।  
> अथा॒ नरः॒ प्रय॑तदक्षिणासोऽवद्यभि॒या ब॒हवः॑ पृणंति ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> दैवी । पूर्तिः । दक्षिणा । देवयज्या । न । कव अरिभ्यः । नहि । ते । पृणंति ।  
> अथ । नरः । प्रयतदक्षिणासः । अवद्यभिया । बहवः । पृणंति ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> दैवी देवसंबंधिनी ॥ देवाद्यञञौ । पा. ४-१-८५-३ । इत्यञ् ॥ तादृशी
> पूर्तिः पालिनी तत्साध्या देवयज्या ॥ छंदसि निष्कर्क्यति निपातितः ॥ देवयागः ।
> तदंगभूता दक्षिणा न कवारिभ्यः ॥ ऋ गतानित्यस्याच इः । उ ४-१३८ । इतीप्रत्ययः ॥
> कुत्सितगंतृभ्योऽयष्टृभ्यः । एतेभ्यो न भवति । यद्वा! देवानां स्वभूता पूर्तिः
> पूरयित्रि स्तुतिभिर्हविर्भिश्च तादृशी देवयज्या दक्षिणा च तेभ्यो न भवतः । तत्र
> हेतुरुच्यते । हि यस्मात्ते कुत्सितगंतारो नहि पृणंति देवान्सुतिभिर्हविर्भिवा न
> प्रीणयंति ॥ पृण प्रीणने तौदादिकः ॥ अथेति प्रश्ने । यष्टृणां देवयागादि
> कथं भवति । उच्यते । नरः कर्मणां नेतारः अत एव प्रयतदक्षिणासो दत्तदक्षिणा

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

दैवी (*daivī*) means "belonging to the gods"; such a पूर्तिः (*pūrtiḥ*) is a protector (pālinī); the देवयज्या (*devayajyā*) accomplished by it is the sacrifice to the gods, and the dakṣiṇā that is part of it is not for the कवारिभ्यः (*kavāribhyaḥ*), those who go badly, who are non-sacrificers. Alternatively: the gods' own पूर्तिः (*pūrtiḥ*) is that which fills (them) with praises and oblations; such sacrifice to the gods and dakṣiṇā do not belong to those people. The reason is given: because those who go badly do not please the gods with praises or oblations. Then, as a question: how do the sacrificers come to have the sacrifice to the gods and the rest? The answer: the नरः (*naraḥ*) are the leaders of rites, and therefore those who have given dakṣiṇā, many sacrificers…

### Printed p. 276

> बहवो यजमाना अवद्यभिया ॥ अवद्यपण्यवर्या गर्हैति । पा. ३-१-१०१ ।
> यत्पत्ययांतत्वेन निपातितः ॥ पापभिया । विहिताननुष्ठाने दुरितानि भवंति खलु ।
> तस्मात्तद्भीत्या पृणंति । देवान्प्रीणयंति । तस्माद्यष्टृणां देवपालनयागदक्षिणा
> भवतीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

…many sacrificers, अवद्यभिया (*avadyabhiyā*), through fear of sin, please the gods. For failure to perform what is ordained brings sins; therefore, fearing that, they gratify, they please the gods. The meaning is that the sacrificers' dakṣiṇā of the sacrifice, the protection of the gods, comes about.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पूर्तिः (*pūrtiḥ*)—that which protects the sacrificer
- देवयज्या (*devayajyā*)—that which is part of the sacrifice to the gods
- दैवी (*daivī*)—belonging to the deities
- दक्षिणा (*dakṣiṇā*)—the dakṣiṇā
- कवारिभ्यः (*kavāribhyaḥ*)—for the wicked (those who are not performers of sacrifice)
- न (*na*)—is not to go
- ते (*te*)—they
- नहि पृणंति (*nahi pṛṇaṃti*)—do not worship the deities with sacrifices etc. and satisfy them
- अथ (*atha*)—but
- अवद्यभिया (*avadyabhiyā*)—through fear of sin
- बहवः नरः (*bahavaḥ naraḥ*)—many sacrificers
- प्रयतदक्षिणासः (*prayatadakṣiṇāsaḥ*)—having distributed dakṣiṇā in the sacrifice
- पृणंति (*pṛṇaṃti*)—satisfy the deities

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The dakṣiṇā, which protects the sacrificer, is part of the sacrifice to the gods and belongs to the deities, is not for the wicked who are not performers of sacrifice. They do not worship and satisfy the deities by sacrifices etc. But many sacrificers, through fear of sin, give dakṣiṇā in the sacrifice and satisfy the deities.

##### English translation
The divine nourishing dakshina, which is part of the sacrifice to the gods, is not for those who go wrong, for they do not gratify (the gods with worship), but many men through fear of incurring sin liberally extending the dakshina gratify the gods.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> श॒तधा॑रं वा॒युम॒र्कं स्व॒र्विदं॑ नृ॒चक्ष॑स॒स्ते अ॒भि च॑क्षते ह॒विः ।  
> ये पृ॒णंति॒ प्र च॒ यच्छं॑ति संग॒मे ते दक्षि॑णां दुहते  ॒॑
> सप्तमातरं ॥ ४ ॥

### Printed p. 277

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> शतधारं । वायुं । अर्कं । स्वःसिविदं । नृचक्षसः । ते । अभि । चक्षते । हविः ।  
> ये । पृणंति । प्र । च । यच्छंति । सॆंगमे । ते । दक्षिणां । दुहते । सप्तमातरं॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> शतधारं बहुधारेणोपेतं वायुमेतन्नामकं स्वर्विदं स्वर्गस्य लंभकं
> सर्वस्य वेत्तारं वार्कमर्चनीयमादित्यं च तौ नृचक्षसो नृणां द्रष्टानन्यानिंद्रादीन्देवांश्चाभिलक्ष्य ते यजमाना हनिश्चक्षते । तेभ्यो दातुं पश्यंति जानंति ॥
> चष्टेर्लटि रूपं !! किंच संगमे । कर्मकरणार्थमृत्विजोऽत्र संगंच्छंत इति
> संगमः ॥ ग्रहवृद्यनिश्चिगमश्चेत्यधिकरणेऽप् । थाथादिस्वरः ॥ तस्मिन्यज्ञे ये यष्टारः
> पृणंति स्तुतिभिः प्रीणयंति । ये च तेभ्यो देवेभ्यो हवींषि प्रयच्छंति ॥
> उभयत्र यद्योगादनिघातः ॥ ते सप्तमातरं । सप्तसंख्याका मातृभूता अग्निष्टोमादिसंस्था यस्यां भवंति सा । यद्वा । सप्तसंख्याका मातरः कर्मणां
> निर्मातारः कर्तारो होत्रादयः संति । तादृशीं दक्षिणां दुहते । ऋत्विग्यो
> दुहंति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sacrificers look to the wind शतधारं (*śatadhāraṃ*) "of a hundred streams", i.e. endowed with many streams, which is "heaven-finding" (स्वर्विदं (*svarvidaṃ*)), that is, it attains heaven or knows all; and to the adorable sun, the अर्कं (*arkaṃ*). These two, and the other gods such as Indra, are "men-beholders" (नृचक्षसः (*nṛcakṣasaḥ*), seers of men). Having them in view, those sacrificers see, i.e. know, that they are to give the oblation to them. चक्षते (*cakṣate*) is the present form of the root *caṣṭi*. Further, संगमे (*saṃgame*): the priests come together here to perform the rite, hence it is a "meeting" (the sacrifice). At that sacrifice those sacrificers who gratify the gods with praises, and who give them oblations, are meant; the accent is not lowered in both clauses because of the connection with the relative. ते सप्तमातरं (*te saptamātaraṃ*): the dakshina in which the seven sacrificial forms such as the Agniṣṭoma are "mothers"; or else, in which seven mothers, namely makers of the rites, the Hotṛ and the others, are present. Such a dakshina दुहते (*duhate*): the priests milk it out, that is, receive it.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- शतधारं (*śatadhāraṃ*)—having streams of many kinds
- वायुं (*vāyuṃ*)—the wind
- स्वर्विदं (*svarvidaṃ*)—the all-knowing
- अर्कं (*arkaṃ*)—the sun
- नृचक्षसः (*nṛcakṣasaḥ*)—having in view the deities Indra and others who can see human beings
- ते (*te*)—the sacrificers
- हविः (*haviḥ*)—that the oblation must be offered
- चक्षते (*cakṣate*)—they know
- संगमे (*saṃgame*)—in the sacrifice
- ये (*ye*)—which sacrificers
- प्र यच्छंति (*pra yacchaṃti*)—offer oblations to the gods
- च (*ca*)—and
- पृणंति (*pṛṇaṃti*)—gratify the gods
- ते (*te*)—they
- सप्तमातरं (*saptamātaraṃ*)—in the sacrificial institutions that have seven kinds of sacrifice-makers, the Hotṛ and the others
- दक्षिणां (*dakṣiṇāṃ*)—the dakshina
- दुहते (*duhate*)—they give liberally

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The sacrificers know that the oblation is to be offered with reference to the wind of many kinds of streams, the all-knowing sun, and the gods such as Indra who can see human beings. The sacrificers who at the sacrifice offer oblations to the gods and gratify the gods give liberally the dakshina, which has the seven kinds of sacrifice-makers such as the Hotṛ, in all seven kinds of sacrifice.

### Printed p. 278

##### English translation
They see (to the offering of) the oblation of Yayu of the hundred streams, to the all-conscious sun, to the men-beholding (deities); they who please (the gods) and offer (oblations) at the festival, let flow the dakshina the mother of seven.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> दक्षि॑णावान्प्रथ॒मो हू॒त ए॑ति॒ दक्षि॑णावान्नाम॒णीरग्र॑मेति ।  
> तमे॒व म॑न्ये नृ॒पतिं॒ जना॑नां॒ यः प्र॑थ॒मो दक्षि॑णामावि॒वाय॑ ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> दक्षिणावान् । प्रथमः । हूतः । एति । दक्षिणावान् । ग्रामनीः । अग्रं । एति ।  
> तं । एव । मन्ये । नृपतिं । जनानां । यः । प्रथमः । दक्षिणां । आविवाय ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हूत ऋत्विभिराहूतो दक्षिणावान्य जमानः प्रथमः सर्वेषां मुख्यः सन्नेति ।
> सर्वत्र गच्छति । तथा ग्रामणीर्ग्रामाणां नेता धनवत्त्वेन तेषां कर्ता दक्षिणावान्
> सोऽग्रमेति । सर्वेषां प्रथममेव गच्छति । तमेव नृपतिं नराणां पालयितारमिति
> मन्य ऋषिरहमवबुध्ये प्रथमो यो जनानां दक्षिणामाविवाय आगमयति ॥ वी
> गत्यादिषु । तस्य लिटि रूपं ॥ तं नृपतिमिति मन्ये । यद्वा। जनानामिति पूर्ववाक्यशेषः । जनानां मध्ये तमेव मनुष्याणां स्वामिनमिति मन्ये ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sacrificer who is called by the priests and gives the dakshina, *hūtaḥ dakṣiṇāvān*, goes first, being foremost of all, to every place. Likewise the ग्रामणीः (*grāmaṇīḥ*), leader of villages, who makes them by his wealth, and is a giver of dakshina, goes at the front of all. The seer says: "I regard him as नृपतिं (*nṛpatiṃ*), protector of men, who first brings the dakshina among the people." आविवाय (*āvivāya*) is from the root वी (*vī*) in the sense of going etc., and the form is perfect. "I think of him as the lord of men." Or else जनानां (*janānāṃ*) belongs to the preceding sentence: among the people I regard him as the master of men.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- हूतः (*hūtaḥ*)—called by the priests
- दक्षिणावान् (*dakṣiṇāvān*)—the sacrificer who wishes to give dakshina
- प्रथमः (*prathamaḥ*)—before all
- एति (*eti*)—goes (gains esteem)
- ग्रामणीः (*grāmaṇīḥ*)—one who by his wealth is the maker of a village
- दक्षिणावान् (*dakṣiṇāvān*)—the sacrificer who is endowed with the means to distribute dakshina

### Printed p. 279
- अग्रं एति (*agraṃ eti*)—goes ahead of everyone
- प्रथमः (*prathamaḥ*)—as the first of all
- यः (*yaḥ*)—who
- जनानां (*janānāṃ*)—to the people
- दक्षिणां (*dakṣiṇāṃ*)—having made known the necessity of dakshina
- आविपाय (*āvipāya*)—began **NOTE**
- तमेव (*tameva*)—him alone
- नृपतिं (*nṛpatiṃ*)—as king
- मन्ये (*manye*)—I regard

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The sacrificer who at the priests' desire wishes to give dakshina to the priests goes first of all (gains esteem). The sacrificer who by his own wealth is the maker of a village and has the means of distributing dakshina gains esteem above all. Him who first began the dakshina I regard as king among men.

##### English translation
Summoned (by the priests) first advances the dakshina bearing (sacrificer); the head of the village bearing the dakshina goes in the front. I regard him as a king amongst men who first introdnced the dakshina.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तमे॒व ऋषिं॒ तमु॑ ब्र॒ह्माण॑माहुर्यज्ञ॒न्यं॑ साम॒गामु॑क्थ॒शासं॑ ।  
> स शु॒क्रस्य॑ त॒न्नो॑ वेद ति॒स्रो यः प्र॑थ॒मो दक्षि॑णया र॒राध॑ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तं । एव । ऋषिं । तं । ऊं इति । ब्रह्माणं । आहुः । यज्ञन्यं । सामगां ।  
> उक्तशसं ।  
> सः । शुक्रस्य । तन्वः । वेद । तिस्रः । यः । प्रथमः । दक्षिणया । रराध ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> तमेव दक्षिणाया दातारमृषिमतींद्रियार्थदर्शिनं यद्वा सत्कर्मकरणेनर्षिमित्याहुः । तथा तमु तमेव ब्रह्माणमाहुः । किंच तमेव यज्ञन्यं ॥ णीञ्
> प्रापणे । क्विप् । उदात्तस्वरितयोर्यण इत्यमः स्वरितत्वं ॥ यज्ञस्य नेतारमध्व-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Him, the giver of dakshina, they call ऋषिं (*ṛṣiṃ*), seer of what lies beyond the senses, or else a seer because of his performing good rites. Likewise they call him ब्रह्माणं (*brahmāṇaṃ*). And him they call यज्ञन्यं (*yajñanyaṃ*), from the root णीञ् (*ṇīñ*) in the sense of leading, the leader of the sacrifice, the Adhvaryu.

> र्युमित्याहुः । तमेव सामगां साम्मां गातारमिति । तथोक्तशासं ॥ शन्नु स्तुता ।
> क्विप् । संहितायां दीर्घश्चांदसः ॥ उक्धशसं शस्त्राणां शंसितारं होतारमिति
> ब्रुवंति । दक्षिणादानेन तत्कर्मणः परिग्रहात् । स दाता शुक्रस्य दीप्यमानस्य
> ज्योतिषस्ति प्रसन्नोऽग्निवैद्युतादित्यात्मकानि श्रेणि शरीराणि वेद । जानाति । यद्वा।
> शुक्रस्य दीप्तस्याग्नेराहवनीयाद्यात्मकानि त्रीणि शरीराणि जानाति । प्रथमो मुख्यो
> यो जनो दक्षिणया रराध ऋत्विगादीनाराधयति ॥ राध संसिद्धौ । लिटि रूपं ।
> लिट्स्वरः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Him also they call सामगां (*sāmagāṃ*), the singer of Sāmans, and उक्तशसं (*uktaśasaṃ*), the reciter of the recitations, the Hotṛ, because by giving dakshina he takes over that priest's work. That giver knows the three bodies of the shining light शुक्रस्य (*śukrasya*), in the form of Agni, lightning and the sun; or else he knows the three bodies of the shining Agni, the Āhavanīya and the others. The first, the chief, who gratifies the priests and others with the dakshina रराध (*rarādha*), from the root राध (*rādha*) in the sense of accomplishing, perfect.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- तं एव (*taṃ eva*)—him who gives the dakshina
- ऋषिं (*ṛṣiṃ*)—as a seer
- तमु (*tamu*)—him
- ब्रह्माणं (*brahmāṇaṃ*)—as a Brāhmaṇa
- आहुः (*āhuḥ*)—the learned say (of him)
- सामगां (*sāmagāṃ*)—as the singer of Sāmans
- उक्त शासं (*ukta śāsaṃ*)—they say he is the reciter of the śastras etc. **NOTE**
- यः (*yaḥ*)—the sacrificer who
- प्रथमः (*prathamaḥ*)—before all
- दक्षिणया (*dakṣiṇayā*)—by offering dakshina
- रराध (*rarādha*)—honours the priests and others
- सः (*saḥ*)—he alone
- शुक्रस्य (*śukrasya*)—of the shining divine light
- तिस्रः तन्नः (*tisraḥ tannaḥ*)—the three forms called Agni, Vāyu and Āditya
- वेद (*veda*)—knows

### Printed p. 280

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The sacrificer who gives dakshina is called a seer, a Brāhmaṇa, a Sāman singer and a reciter of the śastra mantras. The sacrificer who, before all, honours the priests and others by offering dakshina, he knows the three forms, Agni, Vāyu and Āditya, of the shining divine light.

##### English translation
They call him the Rishi, the Brahmana, the leader of the sacrifice the chanter of the hymn, the reciter of the prayer, he knows the three forms of light who first worshipped with the dakshina.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> दक्षि॒णाश्वं॒ दक्षि॑णा॒ गां द॑दाति॒ दक्षि॑णा चं॒द्रमु॒त यद्धिर॑ण्यं ।  
> दक्षि॒णान्नं॑ वनुते॒ यो न॑ आ॒त्मा दक्षि॑णां॒ वर्म॑ कृणुते  ॒
> विजानन् ॥ ७ ॥

### Printed p. 281

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> दक्षिणा । अश्वं । दक्षिणा । गां । ददाति । दक्षिणा । चंद्रं । उत । यत् । हिरण्यं ।  
> दक्षिणा । अन्नं । वनुते । यः । नः । आत्मा । दक्षिणां । वर्म । कृणुते । विजानन् ॥७॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> दक्षिणा तद्दातृभ्योऽश्वं ददाति । तथा गां च ददाति तथा चंद्रं सुवर्णं
> ददाति । उतापि च यद्धिरण्यं रजतमस्ति । रजतं हिरण्यमित्याम्नानात्। तच्च ददाति ।
> किंच सान्नं वनुते । ददाति । वनोतिरत्र दानार्थः । तस्मान्नोऽस्मदीयो य
> आत्मास्ति स वर्म विजानन् कवचं यथायुधानां निवारकं तद्वद्दुरितानि वारयतीति
> कवचमिति विजानन् दक्षिणामश्वादिदानशीलां कृणुते । ऋत्विग्भ्यः करोति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The dakshina gives horses to those who give it; likewise it gives a cow, and चंद्रं (*caṃdraṃ*), gold. Also the हिरण्यं (*hiraṇyaṃ*), silver, which is so called by tradition; that too it gives. It also gives food: वनुते (*vanute*) here means giving. Therefore that self of ours, knowing the dakshina to be वर्म (*varma*), armour, which wards off sins as a coat of mail wards off weapons, makes the dakshina one that gives horses and so on: it gives it to the priests.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- दक्षिणा (*dakṣiṇā*)—the dakshina
- अश्वं (*aśvaṃ*)—a horse
- ददाति (*dadāti*)—gives to the giver
- गां (*gāṃ*)—a cow
- चंद्रं (*caṃdraṃ*)—gold
- उत (*uta*)—and
- हिरण्यं (*hiraṇyaṃ*)—silver
- अन्नं (*annaṃ*)—food
- वनुते (*vanute*)—gives
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- यः आत्मा (*yaḥ ātmā*)—the self that is
- वर्म (*varma*)—as protective armour
- विजानन् (*vijānan*)—knowing
- दक्षिणां (*dakṣiṇāṃ*)—the dakshina
- कृणुते (*kṛṇute*)—gives to the priests

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The dakshina gives a horse to the giver. It gives a cow, gold, silver and food. Our self, knowing dakshina to be protective armour, gives dakshina to the priests.

##### English translation
Dakshina gives horses, Dakshina gives cows, Dakshina gives gold and also silver. Dakhina bestows food. Our spirit discriminating (all things) puts on Dakshina for armour.

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> न भो॒जा म॑मु॒र्न न्य॒र्थमा॑यु॒र्न रि॑ष्यंति॒ न व्य॑थंते ह  ॒
> भोजाः ।

> इदं यद्विश्वं भुवनं स्वश्चैतत्सर्वं दक्षिणैभ्यो ददाति ॥ ८ ॥

> न । भोजाः । मम्रुः । न । निऽर्थं । ईयुः । न । रिष्यंति । न । व्यथंते । ह ।  
> भोजाः ।  
> इदं । यत् । विश्वं । भुवनं । स्वरिति स्वः । च । एतत् । सर्वं । दक्षिणा । एभ्यः ।  
> ददाति ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> भोजा भोजयितारो धादिदानेन दातारो न मम्रुः । न म्रियंते ।
> देवत्वं भजंत इत्यर्थः । अत एव न्यर्थ० ॥ ऋ गत् । उषिकुषिगार्तिभ्यस्थन् । उ. २-४।
> समासे थाथादिस्वरः ॥ निकृष्टां गतिं नेयुः । न प्राप्नु वंति ॥ इण् गत् । लिटि दीर्घ
> इणः कितीति दीर्घः ॥ तथा च न रिष्यंति । कैश्चिदप्यहिंसिता भवंति ॥ कर्मणि व्यत्ययेन
> परस्मैपदं ॥ आत एव भोजा दातारो न व्यथंते । परस्परं न भीता न बाधिता वा
> भवंति । ह प्रसिद्धौ । किंचेदं परिदृश्यमानं विश्वं सर्वं भुवनं यद्भूतजातमस्ति
> स्वश्च स्वर्गलोकश्च एतत्सर्वं दक्षिणा स्वयमेभ्यस्तद्दातृभ्यो ददाति । प्रयच्छति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The भोजाः (*bhojāḥ*), those who feed others, givers by giving wealth and the like, न मम्रुः (*na mamruḥ*) do not die; that is, they attain godhood. For that very reason न्यर्थं (*nyarthaṃ*): they do not attain a low state; the word is from ऋ (*ṛ*) in the sense of going, with the affix थन् (*than*) (Uṇ. Sū. 2-4). They do not obtain it. ईयुः (*īyuḥ*) is from the root इण् (*iṇ*), perfect. Likewise न रिष्यंति (*na riṣyaṃti*): they are harmed by no one. Therefore the givers are not afraid of one another nor oppressed: ह (*ha*) is in the sense of "well known". Moreover this whole visible world, whatever beings exist, and the heaven-world: all this the dakshina herself gives to these givers.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- भोजाः (*bhojāḥ*)—those who give pleasure by gifts of wealth
- न मम्रुः (*na mamruḥ*)—do not die
- न्यर्थं (*nyarthaṃ*)—a low state
- न ईयुः (*na īyuḥ*)—do not attain
- न रिष्यंति (*na riṣyaṃti*)—are not harmed
- भोजाः (*bhojāḥ*)—those who give pleasure by gifts of wealth
- न व्यथंते (*na vyathaṃte*)—do not meet destruction
- यदिदं विश्वं भुवनं (*yadidaṃ viśvaṃ bhuvanaṃ*)—all this multitude of beings that exists
- यत् स्वश्च (*yat svaśca*)—the heaven-world that exists
- एतत् सर्वं (*etat sarvaṃ*)—all that
- दक्षिणां (*dakṣiṇāṃ*)—the dakshina
- एभ्यः (*ebhyaḥ*)—to the givers
- ददाति (*dadāti*)—provides

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Those who give pleasure by the gift of dakshina do not die. They do not attain a low state. They are not harmed. For them there is no destruction. All this multitude of beings that exists, the heaven that exists, all that the dakshina provides for the givers.

### Printed p. 283

##### English translation
The givers of enjoyment do not die. they do not go to destruction, they suffer no injury, the givers of enjoyment snffer no pain. Dakshina gives them all this world and the entire heaven.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> भो॒जा जि॑ग्युः सुर॒भिं योनि॒मग्रे॑ भो॒जा जि॑ग्युर्व॒ध्वं१॒॑  ॒॑
> या सु॒वासाः॑ ।  
> भो॒जा जि॑ग्युरंतः॒पेयं॒ सुरा॑या भो॒जा जि॑ग्यु॒र्ये अहू॑ताः प्र॒यंति॑ ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> भोजाः । जिग्युः । सुरभिं । योनिं । आग्रे । भोजाः । जिग्युः । वध्वं । या । सुनासाः ।  
> भोजाः । जिग्युः । अंतःपेयं । सुरायाः । भोजाः । जिग्युः । ये । अहूताः ।  
> प्रयंति ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> भोजा दातारोऽग्रे प्रथममेव योनिं क्षीरादेरुत्पत्तिस्थानं सुरभिं
> धेनुं जिग्युः । शत्रुभ्यो जितवंतः ॥ जि जये । लिटि सन्‌लिटोर्जॆरित्यभ्यासादुत्तरस्य कवर्गादेशः ॥ तथा भोजा दातारो वध्वं वधूं नवोढां स्त्रीं जिग्युः । या
> स्त्री सुवासाः शोभनवासा अस्ति तां जिग्युः । किंच भोजाः शत्रूणां सुराया
> अंतःपेयमंतःपानं जिग्युः ॥ पा पाने । भावे यत् । ईद्यतीतीत्वं । अर्धधातुकलक्षणो गुणः । कृदुत्तरपदप्रकृतिस्वरत्वं ॥ ये शत्रवोऽहूता बलाधिक्येनाहूयमानाः प्रयंति प्रमुखमागच्छंति तान्भोजा दक्षिणाया दातारो जना
> जिग्युः । जयंति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The bhojas, the givers, at the very first won the cow, the source (योनिं (*yoniṃ*)) of milk and the like, from their enemies. Likewise the bhojas won the bride (वध्वं (*vadhvaṃ*)), a newly wedded woman, the woman who is well-clad (सुवासाः (*suvāsāḥ*)). Further, the bhojas won from their enemies the inner drink (अंतःपेयं (*aṃtaḥpeyaṃ*)) of surā. And those enemies who come forward unsummoned (अहूताः (*ahūtāḥ*)), that is, called forth on account of their superior strength, advancing to the front, them the bhojas, the givers of dakshina, conquer.

### Printed p. 284

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- भोजाः (*bhojāḥ*)—those who give dakshina
- अग्रे (*agre*)—at the very first
- योनिं (*yoniṃ*)—the source of milk and the like
- सुरभिं (*surabhiṃ*)—the cow
- जिग्युः (*jigyuḥ*)—win from their enemies
- या (*yā*)—who
- सुवासाः (*suvāsāḥ*)—adorned with excellent garments and the like
- वध्वं (*vadhvaṃ*)—a beautiful maiden
- जिग्युः (*jigyuḥ*)—obtain
- भोजाः (*bhojāḥ*)—the givers of dakshina
- सुरायाः (*surāyāḥ*)—of the enemies' surā
- अंतःपेयं (*aṃtaḥpeyaṃ*)—the excellent drink
- जिग्युः (*jigyuḥ*)—obtain
- ये (*ye*)—which enemies
- अहूताः (*ahūtāḥ*)—without confronting
- प्रयंति (*prayaṃti*)—come under control; those enemies
- भोजाः (*bhojāḥ*)—the givers of dakshina
- जिग्युः (*jigyuḥ*)—conquer

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Those who give dakshina win from their enemies the cow, the source of milk and the like. They obtain a beautiful maiden adorned with excellent garments and the like. They drink the enemies' surā. Without toil they bring their enemies under control.

##### English translation
The givers of enjoyment first won the cow the source (of food), the givers of enjoyment have won a bride who was beautifully attaired, the givers of enjoyment have won deep potations of wine, the givers of enjoyment have conquered those who without being challenged advance to meet them.

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> भो॒जायाश्वं॒ सं मृ॑जंत्या॒शुं भो॒जया॑स्ते क॒न्या॒३॒॑शु शुंभ॑माना ।  
> भो॒जस्ये॒दं पु॑ष्क॒रिणी॑व॒ वेश्म॒ परि॑ष्कृतं देवमा॒नेव॑ चि॒त्रं ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> भोजाय ! अश्वं । सं । मृजंति । आशुं । भोजाय । आस्ते । कन्या । शुंभमाना ।  
> भोजस्य । इदं । पुष्करिणीइव । वेश्म । परिष्कृतं । देवमानाइव । चित्रं ॥ १० ॥

### Printed p. 285

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> भोजाय दात्र आशुं शीघ्रगामिनमश्वं सं मृजंति । परिचारकाः सम्यगलंकुर्वंति ॥ मृजू शौचालंकारयोः ॥ तथा कन्या । कन्यात्वेनात्र संनिकर्षादभिनवं
> यौवनं लक्ष्यते । अभिनवयौवना शुंभमाना शरीरावयवशोभया वस्त्राद्यलंकारशोभया च शोभमाना स्त्री तस्मै भोजाय दात्र आस्ते । तथा भोजस्य
> दातुरेव हृदयाह्लादकरं वेश्म गृहं भवति । कीदृशं । पुष्करिणीव । पुष्कराणि
> पद्मानि यस्या संतीति पुष्करिणी सरसी । सा यथा पद्महंसादिभिरलंकृता भवति तद्वत्
> परिष्कृतं वितानादिभिरलंकृतं ॥ परिपूर्वस्य करोतेर्भूषणार्थे संहितायां
> सुडागमः । परिनिविभ्य इति सुटः षत्वं । गतिरनंतर इति गतेः प्रकृतिस्वरत्वं ॥ तथा
> देवमानेव ॥ सुपां सुलुगिति प्रथमैकवचनस्याकारः ॥ देवमानमिव चित्रं
> मनोहरं वेश्म तस्य भवति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

For the bhoja, the giver, attendants deck out the swift-going horse (आशुं (*āśuṃ*)), adorning it well. Likewise the kanyā: by the word kanyā, because of closeness to that state, fresh youth is meant here. A woman of fresh youth, shining (शुंभमाना (*śuṃbhamānā*)) with the beauty of her bodily limbs and the beauty of adornment by garments and the like, is for that bhoja, the giver. Likewise the dwelling (वेश्म (*veśma*)) of the giver alone becomes a delight to the heart. Of what kind? Like a puṣkariṇī: a lake which has lotuses (puṣkara). As that is adorned with lotuses, swans and the like, so the house is adorned (परिष्कृतं (*pariṣkṛtaṃ*)) with canopies and the like. Likewise, like a devamāna, a charming, wonderful dwelling belongs to him.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- भोजाय (*bhojāya*)—for him who gives dakshina
- आशुं (*āśuṃ*)—swift-going
- अश्वं (*aśvaṃ*)—the horse
- सं मृजंति (*saṃ mṛjaṃti*)—attendants adorn
- कन्या (*kanyā*)—the beautiful maiden
- शुंभमाना (*śuṃbhamānā*)—adorned
- भोजाय (*bhojāya*)—to him who gives dakshina
- आस्ते (*āste*)—is presented
- पुष्करणीव (*puṣkaraṇīva*)—as a lake is adorned with lotus flowers
- परिष्कृतं (*pariṣkṛtaṃ*)—well adorned
- वेश्म (*veśma*)—the house
- भोजस्य (*bhojasya*)—belongs to the giver of dakshina
- देवमानेव (*devamāneva*)—like the dwelling place of the gods
- चित्रं (*citraṃ*)—is charming (the same house)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

For the master of the sacrifice who gives dakshina, attendants adorn the swift-going horse. A beautiful maiden, adorned, is presented to him. As a lake is adorned with lotus flowers, a house adorned with banners and the like falls to the giver of dakshina. That same house is charming like the dwelling place of the gods.

##### English translation
For the giver of enjoyment they deck out a fleet horse, to the giver enjoyment is presented a maiden beautifully adorned, to the giver of enjoyment belongs this dwelling, adorned like a lake full of lotus flowers, delightful as a dwelling of the gods.

### Printed p. 286

#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> भो॒जमश्वाः॑ सुष्टु॒वाहो॑ वहंति सु॒वृद्रथो॑ वर्तते॒  ॑
> दक्षि॑णायाः ।  
> भो॒जं दे॑वासोऽवता॒ भरे॑षु भो॒जः शत्रू॑न्त्समनी॒केषु॒  ॑
> जेता ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> भोजं । अश्वाः । सुष्टुवाहः । वहंति । सुवृत् । रथः । वर्तते । दक्षिणायाः ।  
> भोजं । देवासः । अवत । भरेषु । भोजः । शत्रून् । संअनीकेषु । जेता ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सुष्टुवाहः ॥ वह प्रापणे । वहश्चति णिप्रत्ययः ॥ शोभनवहनसमर्था
> अश्वा भोजं दातारं वहंति । लक्ष्यं देशं प्रापयंति । ततो दक्षिणाया दातुर्यजमानस्य सुवृत् सुष्टु चक्रादिवर्तनं यस्य सोऽश्वादिसहितो रथो वर्तते ।
> अथ प्रत्यक्षः । हे देवासो हे देवा इंद्रादयः भरेषु । भ्रियत आहवनीयादिरूपेणाग्निरत्रेति भरा यज्ञाः । यद्वा ॥ भृ भर्तने ॥ भृणंति भर्त्सयंति
> योद्धारोऽत्रेति भराः संग्रामाः । तेषु भोजं दातारमवत । रक्षत । ततो
> युष्माभिः पालितो भोजो धनादिदानेन जनानां भोजयिता सॆन् समनीकेषु
> संग्रामेषु शत्रू इज्जॆता जयशीलो भवति ॥ जि जये । ताच्चिलिकस्तृन् । अत एव न
> लोकाव्ययेति शत्रुशब्दस्य षष्ठिप्रतिषेधः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Horses well able to carry (सुष्टुवाहः (*suṣṭuvāhaḥ*)) bear the bhoja, the giver; they bring him to the goal he aims at. Then for the giver of dakshina, the sacrificer, there is at hand the chariot with the horses and the rest, whose wheels and the like turn well (सुवृत् (*suvṛt*)). Now he addresses directly: O gods, O Indra and the others, protect the giver, the bhoja, in the bharas. Bharas are sacrifices, since Agni is borne (भ्रियत (*bhriyata*)) in them in the forms of the āhavanīya and the rest; or else, from the root भृ भर्तने (*bhṛ bhartane*), bharas are battles, where the warriors threaten one another. Protect the giver in them. Then, protected by you, the bhoja, who feeds people by giving wealth and the like, becomes victorious over his enemies in the battles (समनीकेषु (*samanīkeṣu*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सुष्टुवाहः (*suṣṭuvāhaḥ*)—able to carry heavy loads
- अश्वाः (*aśvāḥ*)—horses
- भोजं (*bhojaṃ*)—him who gives dakshina
- हंति (*haṃti*)—carry
- दक्षिणायाः (*dakṣiṇāyāḥ*)—for the master of the sacrifice who gives dakshina
- सुवृत् (*suvṛt*)—furnished with an excellent wheel
- रथः (*rathaḥ*)—the chariot
- वर्तते (*vartate*)—is ready
- देवासः (*devāsaḥ*)—O gods
- भरेषु (*bhareṣu*)—in battles
- भोजं (*bhojaṃ*)—the giver of dakshina
- अवत (*avata*)—protect
- भोजः (*bhojaḥ*)—he who gives dakshina
- समनीकेषु (*samanīkeṣu*)—in battles
- शत्रून् (*śatrūn*)—the enemies
- जेता (*jetā*)—becomes able to conquer

### Printed p. 287

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Horses able to carry heavy loads carry him who gives dakshina. For the master of the sacrifice who gives dakshina, a chariot furnished with an excellent wheel is ready. O gods, protect in battles him who gives dakshina. He who gives dakshina conquers his enemies in battles.

##### English translation
Horses capable of bearing hevvy burdens bear the giver of enjoyment, a well-constructed chariot rolls along (for the giver) of the dakshina; O gods, protect the giver of enjoyment in combats; may the giver of enjoyment be the victor over his foes in battles.

## Sūkta 108 (Maṇḍala 10)

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Introduction. In this sūkta the conversation between the asuras called Paṇis and Saramā, the divine bitch (female dog) who was with Indra, is told. The matter of Saramā and her two pups, the two dogs called Sārameyau, is stated in the Ṛks
इंद्रस्यांगिरसां जेष्टौ विदत्सरमा तनयाय धासिं । बृहस्पतिर्भिनदद्रिं विदद्गाः समुस्रियाभिर्नावशंत नरः ॥ (*iṃdrasyāṃgirasāṃ jeṣṭau vidatsaramā tanayāya dhāsiṃ | bṛhaspatirbhinadadriṃ vidadgāḥ samusriyābhirnāvaśaṃta naraḥ ‖*)
अनूनोदत्र हस्तयतो अद्रिरार्चन्येन दश मासो नवग्वाः । ऋतं यती सरमा गा अविंदद्विश्वानि सत्यांगिराश्चकार ॥ (*anūnodatra hastayato adrirārcanyena daśa māso navagvāḥ | ṛtaṃ yatī saramā gā aviṃdadviśvāni satyāṃgirāścakāra ‖*)
विश्वे अस्या वृषि माहिनायाः सं यद्योभिरंगिरसो नवंत । उत्स आसां परमे सदस्य ऋतस्य पथा सरमा विदद्दाः ॥ (*viśve asyā vṛṣi māhināyāḥ saṃ yadyobhiraṃgiraso navaṃta | utsa āsāṃ parame sadasya ṛtasya pathā saramā vidaddāḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-85-7, 8)
(the first of these is given with the reference Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-62-3). In the matter of her searching out the cows of the gods hidden by the Paṇis, and bringing the news and informing the gods, Sāyaṇa, in his commentary on Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-62-3, tells a story:
अत्रेदमाख्यानं । सरमा नाम देवशुनी । पणिभिर्गोष्टपहृतासु तद्गवेषणाय तां सरमामिंद्रः प्राहैषीत् । यथा लोके व्याधो वनांतर्गत- मृगान्वेषणाय श्वानं विसृजति तद्वत् । सा च सरमैवमवोचत् । हे इंद्र अस्मदीयाय शिशवे तद्दोसंबंधि क्षीरान्नं यदि प्रयच्छसि तर्हि गमिष्या माति । स तथेत्यब्रवीत् । तथा च शाट्कायनकं । अन्नादिनीं ते सरमे प्रजां करोमि या नो गा अन्वविंद इति । ततो गत्वा गवां स्थानमज्ञासीत् । ज्ञात्वा चास्कै न्यवेदयत् । तथा निवेदितासु गोषु तमसुरं हत्वा ता गाः स इंद्रोऽलभतेति । अयमर्थोऽस्यां प्रतिपाद्यते ॥ (*atredamākhyānaṃ | saramā nāma devaśunī | paṇibhirgoṣṭapahṛtāsu tadgaveṣaṇāya tāṃ saramāmiṃdraḥ prāhaiṣīt | yathā loke vyādho vanāṃtargata- mṛgānveṣaṇāya śvānaṃ visṛjati tadvat | sā ca saramaivamavocat | he iṃdra asmadīyāya śiśave taddosaṃbaṃdhi kṣīrānnaṃ yadi prayacchasi tarhi gamiṣyā māti | sa tathetyabravīt | tathā ca śāṭkāyanakaṃ | annādinīṃ te sarame prajāṃ karomi yā no gā anvaviṃda iti | tato gatvā gavāṃ sthānamajñāsīt | jñātvā cāskai nyavedayat | tathā niveditāsu goṣu tamasuraṃ hatvā tā gāḥ sa iṃdro 'labhateti | ayamartho 'syāṃ pratipādyate ‖*) (Sā. Bhā.)

Saramā is the bitch that is with the gods. When the demons named Paṇis had stolen the cows of the gods and hidden them in a cave, Indra sent this divine bitch Saramā to search for those cows. As in the world a hunter sends the hunting dog he keeps by him to look for animals, so Indra too sent this divine bitch to seek the cows. Then she spoke to Indra thus: "O Indra, if you give milk-food for my pup, I will go and bring the cows after searching for them."
