(As the people of the Śāṭyāyana school relate: अन्नादिनीं ते सरमे प्रजां करोमि या नो गा अन्वविंद इति (*annādinīṃ te sarame prajāṃ karomi yā no gā anvaviṃda iti*)—"O Saramā, I will provide food for your pup; go and find the cows and come back.") Indra said "So be it". The bitch set out, searched, found the region where the Paṇis had hidden the cows, and told Indra. Indra, learning it, destroyed those asuras, freed the cows of the gods and brought them back. This is the sense that is set forth in this Ṛk.

Saramā is the bitch of the gods, that is, of Indra. It is known that she had two pups, that they were with Yama, the god of death, and that they were called Sārameyau after their mother's name:
आति द्रव सारमेय् श्वान् चतुरक्षौ शबल् साधुना पथा । (*āti drava sāramey śvān caturakṣau śabal sādhunā pathā |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-14-10)
यौ शे श्वानौ यमरक्षितार् चतुरक्षौ पथिरक्षी नृचक्षस् ॥ (*yau śe śvānau yamarakṣitār caturakṣau pathirakṣī nṛcakṣas ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-14-11)
**NOTE**
In these Ṛks the matter of the two dogs called Sārameyau, the pups of the divine bitch Saramā, and the matter of their having four eyes, is learnt.

### Printed p. 288

The matter of the divine bitch Saramā mentioned above going to the Paṇis and negotiating with them is described in the 108th sūkta of the 10th Maṇḍala of the Ṛgveda (the present sūkta) in this way—

### Printed p. 289
Formerly the cows of Bṛhaspati were stolen by the Paṇis, the followers of Vala, and hidden in a secret place. At Bṛhaspati's request Indra then sent Saramā, the divine bitch, to search for the cows. Searching for them, Saramā travelled a very long way. She crossed a great river called Rasā that lay across her path and came near the Paṇis. The Paṇis learned why she had come and, wishing to make friends with her, held this conversation:

Paṇis: O Saramā, you seem to have come here from far away with much hardship. What is your purpose in coming here? How did you come here? How did you cross the river Rasā?

Saramā: Listen, O Paṇis. I have come here commissioned by Indra, as his messenger. Indra desires the wealth that you have hidden. The river Rasā took fright and helped me to cross. Therefore I crossed that river and came.

Paṇis: Very well, Saramā. You say that Indra has sent you as his messenger. What does that Indra look like? (The Paṇis say among themselves:) Let Saramā come if she will. Let us make friends with her, and let her be the mistress of our cows.

Saramā: It is not possible to conquer that Indra, as you imagine. The Indra who sent me conquers his enemies; others cannot conquer him. Even deep rivers cannot stop him. Struck down by him, you will have to lie on the bed of death.

Paṇis: O Saramā, is it not for these cows that you have come this long way by the path of the sky and desire them? But who will hand those cows over to you for nothing, without a fight? Our weapons are very sharp.

Saramā: What use are your words? Is there an army's strength in those words? Do your sin-filled bodies have the power of bows and arrows? The path you follow is full of hardship. Bṛhaspati (the owner of the cows) will show you no mercy and will set no value on your words.

Paṇis: O Saramā, this wealth of cattle, that is, cows, horses, goods and so on, we have gathered and kept in the mountains, and we, the Paṇis, guard it with great care. It is in vain that you have come alone to such an inaccessible place.

### Printed p. 290
Saramā: Listen, O Paṇis. The Ṛṣis and the Aṅgirases, having gained strength and power by drinking Soma, will come here under the leadership of Ayāsya, seize these cows by force and divide them among themselves. Then you will know that your talk was in vain.

Paṇis: O Saramā, you seem to have the support of the gods. Therefore we shall regard you as our sister. Do not go back to Indra. Stay here. We shall give you a share of these cows too.

Saramā: I do not want the friendship and sisterhood you offer. Indra and the fearsome Aṅgirases know my nature. When I came here searching for the cows, they came to know your dwelling by a hidden path. Therefore, O Paṇis, run far away and save your lives.

Do not delay; go to a safe region far off. Let the cows kept in the cave come out in order. Bṛhaspati, Soma and the wise Ṛṣis, having discovered this secret place, will break open the door of the cave where the cows are and rush in.

In this conversation the Paṇis speak one Ṛk and Saramā speaks another as the reply. The theft of cows by these Paṇis is hinted at in many places in the Ṛgveda.

Although in the Ṛgveda the word paṇi has the meaning of a rich man who does trade and the like, it also means a man who performs no sacrifices and gives no dakṣiṇā or gifts; so the Ṛṣis seem to have looked on him with contempt and thought him unworthy of respect and a base man. In some places paṇi appears as a single person, but since the plural use of this word is especially frequent, it should be understood that the word पणयः (*paṇayaḥ*) denotes a class of people. According to some, these Paṇis were earlier inhabitants of this country and engaged in trade and the like; but as they lacked honesty, people said that they belonged to the group of the Asuras. Because they performed no sacrifices, had no interest in any good rite, and regarded the mere acquisition of wealth, by just or unjust means, as the chief thing and acted accordingly, the Ṛṣis took them for robbers and men of base occupation and expressed that opinion in the mantras and elsewhere. There are many Ṛks in the Ṛgveda about the Paṇis.

### Printed p. 291
Regarding this sūkta, the 8th chapter of the Bṛhaddevatā says:
असुराः पणयो नाम रसापारनिवासिनः । गास्तेऽपजहुरिंद्रस्य न्यगूहंश्च प्रयत्नतः ॥ २४ ॥ (*asurāḥ paṇayo nāma rasāpāranivāsinaḥ | gāste 'pajahuriṃdrasya nyagūhaṃśca prayatnataḥ ‖ 24 ‖*)
24. In former times there were Asuras called Paṇis. They lived in the regions on the far bank of the river Rasā. Once they carried off Indra's cows and hid them in a secret place.
बृहस्पतिस्तथापश्यद्दृष्ट्वेंद्राय शशंस च । प्राहिणोत्तत्र दूत्येऽथ सरमां पाकशासनः ॥ २५ ॥ (*bṛhaspatistathāpaśyaddṛṣṭveṃdrāya śaśaṃsa ca | prāhiṇottatra dūtye 'tha saramāṃ pākaśāsanaḥ ‖ 25 ‖*)
25. Bṛhaspati learned that the Paṇis had carried off the cows and told Indra. Indra then sent Saramā, the divine bitch (the bitch of the world of the gods) who was with him, to the Paṇis as a messenger.
किमित्यत्रायुजाभिस्तां पप्रच्छुः पणयोऽसुराः । कुतः कस्यासि कल्याणि किं ना कार्यमिहास्ति ते ॥२६॥ (*kimityatrāyujābhistāṃ papracchuḥ paṇayo 'surāḥ | kutaḥ kasyāsi kalyāṇi kiṃ nā kāryamihāsti te ‖26‖*)
26. After Saramā went to the Paṇis, they questioned the bitch with the first Ṛk of the 108th sūkta, किमिच्छंती (*kimicchaṃtī*), and the odd-numbered Ṛks of this sūkta, 1, 3 and so on, contain the words the Paṇis addressed to Saramā. The Paṇis asked her: O fair one, where have you come from? With whom are you, that is, who is your master? For what business have you come here?
अथाब्रवीत्तान्सरमा दूत्यैंद्री विचराम्यहं । युष्मान्वजं चान्विष्यंती गाश्चवेंद्रस्य सृच्छतः ॥ २७ ॥ (*athābravīttānsaramā dūtyaiṃdrī vicarāmyahaṃ | yuṣmānvajaṃ cānviṣyaṃtī gāścaveṃdrasya sṛcchataḥ ‖ 27 ‖*)
27. Saramā spoke to them thus: I am Indra's messenger. I am travelling on his business. Since Indra's cows have been stolen, I have come here by Indra's command, searching for where your cow-pen is, in order to find them.
विदित्वेंद्रस्य दूतीं तामसुराः पापचेतसः । ऊचुर्मा सरमे गास्तमिहास्माकं स्वसा भव ॥ २८ ॥ (*viditveṃdrasya dūtīṃ tāmasurāḥ pāpacetasaḥ | ūcurmā sarame gāstamihāsmākaṃ svasā bhava ‖ 28 ‖*)
28. Learning that Saramā had come to them as Indra's messenger, those wicked Paṇis said to her: O Saramā, do not go back. Stay here as our sister.

### Printed p. 292
विभजामो गवां भागं माहिता ह ततः पुनः । सूक्तस्यास्यान्तया चर्चायुग्माभिस्तेव सर्वशः ॥ २९ ॥ (*vibhajāmo gavāṃ bhāgaṃ māhitā ha tataḥ punaḥ | sūktasyāsyāntayā carcāyugmābhisteva sarvaśaḥ ‖ 29 ‖*)
29. (They said:) We shall give you too a share of the cows we have brought. Henceforth do not be like an enemy to us. In this way the dialogue went on between them. By the last Ṛk of this sūkta, and by the other even-numbered Ṛks of this sūkta,
**NOTE**
साब्रवीन्नाहमिच्छामि स्वसृत्वं वा धनानि वा ! पिबेयं तु पयस्तासां गवां यास्ता निगूहथ ॥ ३० ॥ (*sābravīnnāhamicchāmi svasṛtvaṃ vā dhanāni vā ! pibeyaṃ tu payastāsāṃ gavāṃ yāstā nigūhatha ‖ 30 ‖*)
30. Saramā, stating her intention plainly, said: O Paṇis, I want neither your sisterhood nor your wealth, but I wish to drink the milk of the cows you have hidden.
असुरास्तां तफेत्युक्ता तदाजहुः पयस्ततः । सा स्वधावाच्च लौल्याच्च पीत्वा तत्पय आसुरं ॥ ३१ ॥ (*asurāstāṃ taphetyuktā tadājahuḥ payastataḥ | sā svadhāvācca laulyācca pītvā tatpaya āsuraṃ ‖ 31 ‖*)
31. The Asuras said "So be it" and brought the milk of those cows to Saramā. Saramā, in keeping with her nature and her greed, drank the milk the Asuras had brought,
परं संवननं हृद्यं बलपुष्टिकरं ततः । शतॆयोजनविस्तारामतरत्तां रसां पुनः ॥ ३२ ॥ (*paraṃ saṃvananaṃ hṛdyaṃ balapuṣṭikaraṃ tataḥ | śatèyojanavistārāmatarattāṃ rasāṃ punaḥ ‖ 32 ‖*)
32. and, praising that milk ("Ah! how good this milk is, how tasty, delightful and strength-giving!"), she crossed again the river Rasā, a hundred yojanas wide, reached her own place and went to Indra. For,
यस्याः पारे परे तेषां पुरमासीत्सुदुर्जयं । पप्रच्छेंद्रश्च सरमां कच्चिद्दा दृष्टवत्यसि ॥ ३३ ॥ (*yasyāḥ pāre pare teṣāṃ puramāsītsudurjayaṃ | papraccheṃdraśca saramāṃ kacciddā dṛṣṭavatyasi ‖ 33 ‖*)
33. on the far bank of the river Rasā stood the impregnable city of those Paṇis. Therefore, when she had crossed Rasā again and come to Indra, Indra asked Saramā: O Saramā, what came of the business you went on? Did you see the cows?
सा नेति प्रत्युवाचेंद्र प्रभावादासुरस्य तु । कां जघान पदा क्रुद्धः उद्दिरंती पयस्ततः ॥ ३४ ॥ (*sā neti pratyuvāceṃdra prabhāvādāsurasya tu | kāṃ jaghāna padā kruddhaḥ uddiraṃtī payastataḥ ‖ 34 ‖*)

### Printed p. 293
34. Saramā, intoxicated by the Asuras' milk that she had drunk, was frenzied and answered Indra that she had not seen the cows (told a lie). Indra grew angry and kicked Saramā with his foot. Thereupon (from the blow of Indra's foot) Saramā, vomiting the milk she had drunk,
जगाम सा भयोद्विग्ना पुनरेव पणीन्मृति । पदानुसारिपद्भत्या रथेन हरिवाहनः ॥ ३५ ॥ (*jagāma sā bhayodvignā punareva paṇīnmṛti | padānusāripadbhatyā rathena harivāhanaḥ ‖ 35 ‖*)
35. trembling with fear, went again to the Paṇis. Then Indra, following the very footprints of Saramā, seated in his chariot,
गत्वा जघ न च पणीन् गाश्च ताः पुनराहरत् । (*gatvā jagha na ca paṇīn gāśca tāḥ punarāharat |*)
36. went to where the Paṇis were, killed them and brought the cows back. So it is said.

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> किमिच्छंतीत्येकादशर्चं नवमं सूक्तं त्रैष्टुभं । ऐंद्रपुरोहितस्य बृहस्पतेर्गोषु वलनान्नोऽसुरस्य भटैः पणिनामकैरसुरैरपहृत्य गुहायां निहितासु
> सतीषु बृहस्पतिप्रेरितेनेंद्रॆण गवामन्वेषणाय सरमा नाम देवशुनी प्रेषिता ।
> सा च महतीं नदीम्ुत्तीर्य वलपुरं प्राप्य गुप्तस्थाने नीतास्ता गा ददर्श । अथ
> तस्मिन्नंतरे पणय इदं वृत्तांतमवगच्छंत एनां मित्रिकर्तुं संवादमकुर्वन् ।
> तत्र प्रथमातृतीयाद्या अयुजोऽ०त्यावर्जिताः पणीनां वाक्यानि । अत्र त ऋषयः
> सरमा देवता । द्वितीयाचतुर्थ्याद्या युज एकादशी च षट् सरमाया वाक्यानि ।
> अतस्तासु सर्षिः पणयो देवता । तथा चानुक्रांतं । किमिच्छंती पणिभिरसुरैर्निगूळ्हा गा अन्वेष्टुं सरमां देवशुनीमिंद्रेण प्रहितामयुग्भिः पणयो मित्रियंतः प्रोचुः सा तान्युग्मांत्याभिरनिच्छंती प्रत्याचष्ट इति ॥ गतो विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The ninth sūkta, किमिच्छंती (*kimicchaṃtī*), of eleven Ṛks, in the Triṣṭubh metre. The cows of Bṛhaspati, Indra's priest, had been stolen by the Asuras called Paṇis, the henchmen of the Asura Vala, and placed in a cave. Indra, urged by Bṛhaspati, sent a divine bitch named Saramā to seek the cows. She crossed a great river, reached Vala's city and saw the cows taken to a hidden place. Meanwhile the Paṇis, learning of this, held a dialogue with her to make her their friend. In it the first, third and the other odd Ṛks except the last are the words of the Paṇis; for these the seers are those Paṇis and the deity is Saramā. The second, the fourth and the other even Ṛks, and the eleventh, six in all, are the words of Saramā; for these the seer is Saramā and the deity the Paṇis. He cites the Anukramaṇī: किमिच्छंती पणिभिरसुरैर्निगूळ्हा गा अन्वेष्टुं सरमां देवशुनीमिंद्रेण प्रहितामयुग्भिः पणयो मित्रियंतः प्रोचुः सा तान्युग्मांत्याभिरनिच्छंती प्रत्याचष्ट इति ॥ (*kimicchaṃtī paṇibhirasurairnigūḷhā gā anveṣṭuṃ saramāṃ devaśunīmiṃdreṇa prahitāmayugbhiḥ paṇayo mitriyaṃtaḥ procuḥ sā tānyugmāṃtyābhiranicchaṃtī pratyācaṣṭa iti ‖*) The viniyoga has been given before.

Anuvāda: this sūkta किमिच्छंती (*kimicchaṃtī*) is the ninth sūkta in the ninth anuvāka. It has eleven Ṛks. In it the cows of Bṛhaspati, Indra's priest, were stolen by the Asuras called Paṇis, followers of the Asura Vala, and hidden in a mountain cave. At Bṛhaspati's request Indra sent his divine bitch Saramā to find the place where the cows were. The dog travelled a long way, crossed a great river in her path, went to the city of the Asura Vala and there saw the cows the Asuras had hidden in a secret place. When the Asuras learned that Indra's dog had come and seen the cows they had put in a secret place, they at once

### Printed p. 294
came to the dog and, wishing to win her over to their side and gain her friendship, held a conversation with her. This conversation is what the sūkta describes. Therefore in the odd-numbered Ṛks of this sūkta, one, three and so on (except the last Ṛk), there are words of the Paṇis. So for those Ṛks the Paṇis are the seers and Saramā is the deity. In the even-numbered Ṛks, two, four and so on, and in the eleventh Ṛk, six Ṛks in all, there are words of Saramā. So for those Ṛks Saramā is the seer and the Paṇis are the deities. In the Anukramaṇī it is said: किमिच्छंती पणिभिरसुरैर्निगूळ्ता गा अन्वेष्टुं सरमां देवशुनीमिंद्रॆण प्रहितामयुग्भिः पणयो मित्रीयंतः प्रोचुः सा तान्युग्यांत्याभिरनिच्छंती प्रत्याचष्टे (*kimicchaṃtī paṇibhirasurairnigūḷtā gā anveṣṭuṃ saramāṃ devaśunīmiṃdrèṇa prahitāmayugbhiḥ paṇayo mitrīyaṃtaḥ procuḥ sā tānyugyāṃtyābhiranicchaṃtī pratyācaṣṭe*). The viniyoga has been stated earlier.

Sūkta—108
Maṇḍala 10; Anuvāka 9; Sūkta 108.
Aṣṭaka 8; Adhyāya 6; Varga 5, 6.
Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 11.
Seer: 1, 3, 5, 7, 9: the Paṇis, Asuras. 2, 4, 6, 8, 10, 11: Saramā, the divine bitch.
Deity: 1, 3, 5, 7, 9: Saramā. 2, 4, 6, 8, 10, 11: the Paṇis.
Metre: Triṣṭubh.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> किमि॒च्छंती॑ स॒रमा॒ प्रेदमा॑नड् दू॒रे ह्यध्वा॒ जगु॑रिः परा॒चैः ।  
> कास्मेहि॑तिः॒ का परि॑तक्क्यासीत्क॒थं र॒साया॑ अतरः॒ पयां॑सि ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> किं । इच्छंती । सरमा । प्र । इदं । आनट् । दूरे । हि । अध्वा । जगुरिः । पराचैः ।  
> का । अस्मेऽहितिः । का । परितत्क्र्या । आसीत् । कथं । रसायाः । अतरः । पयांसि ॥१॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अनयागच्छंतीं सरमां दृष्ट्वा पणयो वदंति । सरमा सरणशीलैतन्नामिका
> देवशुनी किमिच्छंती किं प्रार्थयमाना सतीदमस्मदीयं स्थानं प्रानट् ।
> प्राप्नोत् ॥...आज्‌पूर्वो नशिर्व्याप्तिकर्मा । तस्य लुजि मंत्रे घसेत्यादिना

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Seeing Saramā approach with this Ṛk, the Paṇis speak. Saramā, the divine bitch of that name, from *saraṇaśīlā* (given to going): "What do you seek, what are you asking for, that you have reached this place of ours?" प्रानट् (*prānaṭ*) means प्राप्नोत् (*prāpnot*), "reached"; the root नशि (*naśi*) with आज् (*āj*) prefixed has the sense of pervading. The remainder, on its aorist form, is grammar and is cut off here.

### Printed p. 295

> चेर्लुक् । छंदस्यपि दृश्यत इत्याडागमः ॥ पराचैः परांचि पराङ्मुखान्यावृत्तिवर्जितानि यानि गमनानि तैर्जगुरिरुद्धूर्णः । महता प्रयत्नेनापि गंतुं न शक्यत
> इत्यर्थः ॥ गृ निगरणे । आदृगमहनेत्यादिना किन्द्रत्ययः । बहुलं छंदसि । पा. ७-१-
> १०३ । इत्युत्वं ॥ तादृशोऽयमध्वा दूरे हि । विप्रकृष्टः खलु । यद्वा । पराचैः
> परांचनैर्जगुरिरत्यर्थं गंत्रि पौष्टिभागमनालोकमाना सतीदं स्थानं प्राप्नोति ।
> दूरेऽयमध्वा यदृच्छया गंतुं न शक्यते । अतो वयमेतां पृच्छामः । हे
> सरमे का कीदृश्यस्नेहितिः । कोऽस्मास्वर्थहितिः । कोऽस्मासु त्वदपेक्षितार्थो निहितः।
> यद्वा । अस्मासु कोऽर्थो गतः ॥ दधातेर्हिनोतीर्वा क्तिनि रूपं ॥ आगच्छंत्यास्तव
> का कीदृशी परितक्क्या रात्रिरासीत्। यद्वा । तकतिर्गत्यर्थः । परितकनं परितो गमनं
> भ्रमणं वा कीदृशमासीत् । कथं च रसायाः शब्दायमानाया अंतरिक्षनद्या
> योजनशतविस्तीर्णायाः पयांस्युदकान्यतरः । तीर्णवत्यसि । एतद्वद । अत्र किमिच्छंती सरमेदं प्रानडित्यादिकं निरुक्तं द्रष्टव्यं ॥ नि. ११-२५ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa takes पराचैः (*parācaiḥ*) to mean goings that are turned away and free of return. The path cannot be travelled even with great effort. Such a path is indeed far away. **NOTE**

Alternatively, he says, Sarama, seeing the path, reaches this place. The way is far and cannot be travelled at will, so the Paṇis question her: "O Sarama, what is the अस्मेहितिः (*asmehitiḥ*)?" He explains it as what purpose of yours is lodged in us, or alternatively what object has gone to us. The form comes from the root दधातेर्हिनोतीर्वा (*dadhāterhinotīrvā*) with the affix ktin. Then "what sort of परितक्क्या (*paritakkyā*), a night, was yours as you came?" Alternatively, the root तकति (*takati*) means going, so the question is what sort of going about or wandering it was. And how did you cross the waters of the sounding aerial river Rasā, a hundred yojanas broad? Tell us. On "what do you seek" and *prānaḍ* and so on, the Nirukta is to be seen (Ni. 11-25). **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सरमा (*saramā*)—Sarama
- किं इच्छंती (*kiṃ icchaṃtī*)—desiring what
- प्र आनट् (*pra ānaṭ*)—has she come here?
- अध्वा (*adhvā*)—the road coming here
- दूरे हि (*dūre hi*)—is very long
- पराचैः (*parācaiḥ*)—even by those who walk on continuously **NOTE**
- जगुरिः (*jaguriḥ*)—it cannot be crossed; therefore, O Sarama
- अस्मेहितिः (*asmehitiḥ*)—the purpose for which you have come to us
- का (*kā*)—what?
- परितक्क्या (*paritakkyā*)—the manner of coming here
- का आसीत् (*kā āsīt*)—what was it like
- रसायाः (*rasāyāḥ*)—of the river Rasā
- पयांसि (*payāṃsi*)—the waters
- कथं (*kathaṃ*)—how
- अतरः (*ataraḥ*)—did you cross?

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

With what desire has Sarama come here? The road coming here is very long. Even those who walk on continuously cannot come along this road. Therefore, O Sarama, what is the purpose for which you have come to us? By what kind of going have you come here? How have you crossed the waters of the river Rasā?

##### English translation
(The Panis :) With what intention has Sarama come to this place ? Verily the way is long and difficult to be traversed by the persevering. What is the motive of your comming to us? What sort of waudering was yours ? How have you crossed the waters of the Rasa ?

### Printed p. 296

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Yāska has given an etymology both for this Ṛk and for the word सरमा (*saramā*) in it: सरमा सरणात् (*saramā saraṇāt*) (Ni. 11-24). Of her there is this verse: किमिच्छंती सरमेदं प्रानट् (*kimicchaṃtī saramedaṃ prānaṭ*), दूरे ह्यध्वा जगुरिर्जंगम्यतेः परांचनैरचितः (*dūre hyadhvā jagurirjaṃgamyateḥ parāṃcanairacitaḥ*), का तवास्मास्वर्थहितिरासीत् (*kā tavāsmāsvarthahitirāsīt*) and so on (Ni. 11-25). The Nirukta passage continues with परितक्म्या रात्रिः परित एनां शक्क (*paritakmyā rātriḥ parita enāṃ śakka*), with the explanation of the word रसा (*rasā*), and with the story. **NOTE**

सरमा सरणात् (*saramā saraṇāt*)—The word सरमा (*saramā*) is derived from the word सरण (*saraṇa*), which is formed from the root सृ (*sṛ*) ("to move"). The meaning is "one who moves" or "who wanders". The idea is a divine bitch whose nature is to wander.

किमिच्छंती सरमेदं प्रानट् (*kimicchaṃtī saramedaṃ prānaṭ*)—The Asuras first say among themselves, "Desiring what from us may Sarama have come here?" Then they ask Sarama directly.

दूरे ह्यध्वा जगुरिर्जंगम्यतेः परांचनैरचितः (*dūre hyadhvā jagurirjaṃgamyateḥ parāṃcanairacitaḥ*)—The road by which she has come is very far. The word जगुरि (*jaguri*) is formed from the root गम्ल गत् (*gamla gat*), "go". जगुरि (*jaguri*) means going, wandering. परांचन (*parāṃcana*) means going from one place to another, leaving one region behind and moving on to the region ahead (the printed text also has पर अंचनैः पराज्मुखैर्गमनैः विप्रकृष्टो देवनिवासात् (*para aṃcanaiḥ parājmukhairgamanaiḥ viprakṛṣṭo devanivāsāt*)). The idea is that Sarama, the divine bitch, has set out from Indra's dwelling and come to the city of the Paṇis.

का तवास्मास्वर्थहितिरासीत् (*kā tavāsmāsvarthahitirāsīt*)—What business have you with us? For what purpose have you come to us?

परितक्क्या रात्रिः परित एनां तक्क (*paritakkyā rātriḥ parita enāṃ takka*); तक्केत्युष्णनाम तकत इति सतः (*takketyuṣṇanāma takata iti sataḥ*)—The word तक्क (*takka*) is formed from the root तकति (*takati*), which means "go". तक्क (*takka*) means heat, sunshine. There is heat on both sides of the night, before the night and at its end. Before the night means before sunset, when there is sunshine (heat). At the end of the night means after the night has passed, in the morning after sunrise, when there is sunshine (heat). So the time that is enveloped by heat at both ends, namely the night, is परितक्क्या (*paritakkyā*) — it is a name. **NOTE**

कथं रसाया आतरः पयांसीति (*kathaṃ rasāyā ātaraḥ payāṃsīti*); रसा नदी रसतेः शब्दकर्मणः (*rasā nadī rasateḥ śabdakarmaṇaḥ*); कथं रसानि तान्युदकानीति वा (*kathaṃ rasāni tānyudakānīti vā*)—O Sarama, how did you cross the fathomless water of the river Rasā?

### Printed p. 297
रसा (*rasā*) is the name of a river. The word is formed from the root रसति (*rasati*), meaning "make a sound". रसा (*rasā*) means a river that flows roaring as its water runs. Or else रस (*rasa*) is also a name for water; the meaning is then a river full of fathomless masses of water.

देवशुनींद्रेण प्रहिता पणिभिरसुरैः समूद इत्याख्यानं (*devaśunīṃdreṇa prahitā paṇibhirasuraiḥ samūda ityākhyānaṃ*)—There is a story that the divine bitch named Sarama, sent by Indra, conversed with the Asuras called Paṇis. It is told in this sūkta.

In this Ṛk, as soon as the Asuras called Paṇis saw Sarama, they questioned her thus: "O Sarama, why have you come here? What business have you with us? You have come from a very distant region. What business have you with us? How did you cross this very deep Rasā river, full of fathomless water?" The answer is given by Sarama in the next (2nd) Ṛk. **NOTE**

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्र॑स्य दू॒तीरि॑षि॒ता च॑रामि म॒ह इ॒च्छंती॑ पणयो नि॒धीन्वः॑ ।  
> अ॒ति॒ष्कदो॑ भि॒यसा॒ तन्न॑ आव॒त्तथा॑ र॒साया॑ अतरं॒ पयां॑सि ॥२॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इंद्रस्य । दूतीः । इषिता । चरामि । महः । इच्छंती । पणयः । निधीन् । वः ।  
> अतिस्कदः । भियसा । तत् । नः । आवत् । तथा । रसायाः । अतरं । पयांसि ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अनया तान्सरमा प्रत्युवाच । हे पणय एतन्नामका असुराः इंद्रस्य
> दूतीः ॥ सुपां सुलुगिति प्रथमैकवचनस्य सुश्चांदसः ॥ अहमिषिता तेनैव प्रेषिता
> सती चरामि । युष्मदीयं स्थानमागच्छामि । किमर्थं । वो युष्मदीयान्युष्मदीये पर्वतेऽधिष्ठापितान्महो महतो निधीन्बृहस्पतेर्गोनिधीनिच्छंती कामयमाना सती चरामि । किंचातिष्कदः ॥ स्कंदिर् गतिशोषणयोः । भावे क्विप् ॥ अति-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

With this Ṛk Sarama replied to them. "O Paṇis, Asuras of that name, I, the messenger (दूतीः (*dūtīḥ*), the case ending सु (*su*) being Vedic by the rule *supāṃ suluk*) of Indra, sent (इषिता (*iṣitā*)) by him, go about; I come to your place." For what purpose? "Desiring *mahaḥ nidhīn*, your great treasures, namely Bṛhaspati's treasures of cattle, set up on your mountain, I go about." Further, अतिष्कदः (*atiṣkadaḥ*) is formed from the root स्कंदि (*skaṃdi*), meaning going and drying up, with kvip in the abstract sense. By fear (भियसा (*bhiyasā*)) arising from the transgression, from the leaping over, that river-water (तत् (*tat*)) protected me. The plural नः (*naḥ*) is used for honour (the speaker means herself). In that way I crossed the waters of the river Rasā.

### Printed p. 298

> ष्कंदनादतिक्रमणाज्ञातेन भियसा भयेन तन्नदीजलं नः । पूजायां बहुवचनं ।
> मामावत् । अरक्षत् । तथा तेन प्रकारेण रसाया नद्याः पयांस्युदकान्यतरं ।
> तीर्णवत्यस्मि ॥

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पणयः (*paṇayaḥ*)—O Paṇis
- इंद्रस्य (*iṃdrasya*)—of Indra
- दूती (*dūtī*)—I, the messenger
- वः (*vaḥ*)—in your possession
- महः (*mahaḥ*)—great
- निधीन् (*nidhīn*)—the treasures in the form of cattle
- इच्छंती (*icchaṃtī*)—desiring
- इषिता (*iṣitā*)—sent by Indra
- चरामि (*carāmi*)—I am wandering
- अतिष्कदः (*atiṣkadaḥ*)—of transgressing entry
- भियसा (*bhiyasā*)—through fear
- तत् (*tat*)—the river-water
- नः (*naḥ*)—me
- आवत् (*āvat*)—protected
- तथा (*tathā*)—in the same way
- रसायाः (*rasāyāḥ*)—of the river Rasā
- पयांसि (*payāṃsi*)—the waters
- अतरं (*ataraṃ*)—I have crossed

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(Sarama's words.) O Paṇis, I, the messenger of Indra, desiring the great treasures in the form of cattle that are with you, am wandering here, sent by Indra. Through fear of transgressing entry, the river-water protected me. In the same way I have crossed the waters of the river Rasā.

##### English translation
(Sarama :) I come, the appointed messenger of Indra, desiring, Panis your great hidden treasures; through fear of being crossed the (water) helped us, thus I passed over the waters of the Rasa.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> की॒दृज्जंद्रः॑ सरमे॒ का दृ॑शी॒का यस्ये॒दं दू॒तीरस॑रः परा॒कात् ।  
> आ च॒ गच्छा॑न्मि॒त्रमे॑ना दधा॒माथा॒ गवां॒ गोप॑तिर्नो भवाति॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कीदृज् । इंद्रः । सरमे । का । दृशीका । यस्य । इदं । दूतीः । असरः । पराकात् ।  
> आ । च । गच्छात् । मित्रं । एन । दधाम । अथ । गवां । गोऽपतिः । नः । भवाति ॥ ३ ॥

### Printed p. 299

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> तेषां वाक्यं । हे सरमे तव स्वामींद्रः कीदृक् । कियत्पराक्रमवान् । का
> दृशीका । तस्य कीदृशी दृष्टिः । दृष्टिरूपा सेना कियती । यस्य दूतीर्दूती त्वमिदमस्मदीयं स्थानं पराकादतिदूरादसरः आगमः । इति तामुक्त्येदानीं ते परस्परमाहुः । एषा सरमा गच्छाच्च । आगच्छतु च ॥ गमर्लॆट्याडागमः ॥ स्वामी भवाति ।
> भवतु । न ह्येकस्या गोः किंतु बहूनां गवां स्वामी भवतु । वृत्त्यवृत्तिभ्यां
> स्वामित्वं बाहुल्यं च विवक्ष्यते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The words of the Paṇis. "O Sarama, what is your master Indra like (कीदृक् (*kīdṛk*))? How great is his prowess? What is his दृशीका (*dṛśīkā*): what is his sight like? How large is his army, which has the form of sight? Whose messenger you are, having come (असरः (*asaraḥ*), an aorist) from afar, from a very distant place, to this place of ours." Having said this to her, they now say to one another: "Let this Sarama come (गच्छात् (*gacchāt*), from गम् (*gam*) with the augment *āṭ* in the subjunctive)." And let her become mistress (भवाति (*bhavāti*), "let her be"), not of one cow only but of many cows. Ownership and plurality are intended by the use and non-use of the affix. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सरमे (*sarame*)—O Sarama
- यस्य (*yasya*)—of which Indra
- दूतीः (*dūtīḥ*)—you, the messenger
- इदं (*idaṃ*)—to this place of ours
- पराकात् (*parākāt*)—from a very distant region
- असरः (*asaraḥ*)—you have come, that
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- कीदृक् (*kīdṛk*)—what sort of one?
- का दृशीका (*kā dṛśīkā*)—of what form?
- आ च गच्छात् (*ā ca gacchāt*)—let her come here, becoming worthy of our regard
- एना (*enā*)—this woman
- मित्रं (*mitraṃ*)—as my (our) friend
- दधाम (*dadhāma*)—let us take
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- गवां (*gavāṃ*)—of the cows
- गोपतिः (*gopatiḥ*)—as mistress
- भवाति (*bhavāti*)—let her be

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(The Paṇis:) O Sarama, what sort of person is that Indra whose messenger you are, you who have come to this place of ours from a very distant region? What form has he? (Among themselves:) Let this Sarama come here, becoming worthy of our regard. Let us take her as our friend. Let her be mistress of our cows.

##### English translation
(The Panis:) What is Indra like, O Sarama ? What is the appearance of him as whose messenger you have come to this place from afar? (They then say to one another:) Let her approach : let us make friends with her and let her be the lord of our cattle.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> नाहं तं वे॑द॒ दभ्यं॒ दभ॒त्स यस्ये॒दं दू॒तीरस॑रं परा॒कात् ।  
> न तं गू॑हंति स्र॒वतो॑ गभी॒रा ह॒ता इंद्रे॑ण पणयः शयध्यॆ ॥

### Printed p. 300

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> न । अहं । तं । वेद । दभ्यं । दभत् । सः । यस्य । इदं । दूतीः । असरं । पराकात् ।  
> न । तं । गूहंति । स्रवतः । गभीराः । हताः । इंद्रेण । पणयः । शयद्वे ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सरमा वदति । हे पणयः तमिंद्रं दभ्यं हंतव्यमिति न वेद । न जानामि ॥
> दभेरचो यत् ॥ कथं । स इंद्रो दभत् । सर्वाञ्जनान्दभति । हिनस्त्वेव ॥
> दभेर्लेटि रूपं । वाक्यभेदादनिघातः ॥ यस्य दूतीर्दूत्यहमिदं युष्मदीयं
> स्थानं पराकादतिदूराद्देशादसरं प्राप्ताभूवं । इंद्रो हिंसितवो न भवतीत्यत्र
> युक्तिमाह । स्रवतः । स्रवणं स्रवः । तमाचरंति ॥ आचारार्थे क्विप् । तुगागमः ।
> जसि रूपं ॥ स्रवणशीला गभीरा गंभीरा नद्यस्तमिंद्रं न गूहंति । न संवृण्वंति ।
> नाच्छादयंति । किंत्वाविष्टुर्वंति । वयं यस्य महिम्ना समुद्रं प्रतिसरामः ।
> तस्मादहिंस्य इत्येनं प्रकटीकुर्वंति ॥ गुहू संवरणे भौवादिकः ॥ तस्माद्दे पणयः
> यूयमिंद्रॆण तादृशपराक्रमेण हताः संतः शयद्वे ॥ शीञ् स्वष्टे । बहुलं
> छंदसीति शपो लुगभावः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sarama says: "O Paṇis, I do not know (न वेद (*na veda*)) that Indra to be दभ्यं (*dabhyaṃ*), one to be slain (दभेरचो यत् (*dabheraco yat*))." How so? That Indra (दभत् (*dabhat*)) slays all people; the form is from दभ (*dabha*) in the *leṭ*. Because the sentence is divided, there is no निघात (*nighāta*). "Whose messenger I am, I have come (असरं (*asaraṃ*)) to this place of yours from a very distant region." She gives the reason why Indra cannot be slain. स्रवतः (*sravataḥ*): स्रवणं स्रवः (*sravaṇaṃ sravaḥ*), and they behave like it; the affix is kvip in the sense of conduct, with the augment *tuk*, and the form is in *jas*. The flowing, deep rivers do not hide Indra: they do not cover or conceal him but make him manifest. "By whose greatness we run to the sea, he cannot be harmed", thus they reveal him. The root is गुहू (*guhū*) in the sense of covering, of the भू (*bhū*) class. Therefore, O Paṇis, you, slain by Indra of such prowess, will lie down (शयद्वे (*śayadve*), from *śīṅ*, to sleep; *śap* is not elided by "bahulaṃ chandasi").

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Paṇis) तं (*taṃ*)—that Indra
- दभ्यं (*dabhyaṃ*)—as one to be slain by you
- न वेद (*na veda*)—I do not know
- यस्य (*yasya*)—to which Indra
- दूती (*dūtī*)—I, the messenger
- इदं (*idaṃ*)—to this region of yours
- पराकात् (*parākāt*)—from a very distant region
- अतरं (*ataraṃ*)—I have come across
- सः (*saḥ*)—that Indra
- दभत् (*dabhat*)—destroys everyone
- स्रवतः (*sravataḥ*)—flowing
- गभीराः (*gabhīrāḥ*)—deep rivers
- तं (*taṃ*)—Indra
- न गूहंति (*na gūhaṃti*)—cannot hide
- पणयः (*paṇayaḥ*)—O Paṇis
- इंद्रेण (*iṃdreṇa*)—by Indra
- हताः (*hatāḥ*)—slain
- शयद्वे (*śayadve*)—you will lie down

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(Sarama:) O Paṇis, I have certainly no fear that you will slay Indra. That Indra, whose messenger I am and for whom I have come here from a very distant region, destroys everyone. Even the flowing, deep rivers cannot hide Indra (cannot stop him). O Paṇis, you will lie down (on the battlefield), slain by Indra.

### Printed p. 301

##### English translation
(Sarama:) I do not believe that he can be subdued; he as whose messenger I have come to this place from afar subdues (his enemies). The deep rivers do not conceal him; you, Panis, slain by Indra, will sleep (in death).

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इ॒मा गावः॑ सरमे॒ या ऐच्छः॒ परि॑ दि॒वो अंता॑न्तुभगे॒  ॑
> पतंती ।  
> कस्त॑ एना॒ अव॑ सृजा॒दयु॑ध्वृ॒तास्माक॒मायु॑धा संति ति॒ग्मा ॥५॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इमाः । गावः । सरमे । याः । ऐच्छः । परि । दिवः । अंतान् । सुभगे । पतंती ।  
> कः । ते । एनाः । अव । सृजात् । आयुध्वी । उत । अस्माकं । आयुधा । संति । तिग्मा ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> क्रुद्धाः पणयः प्रत्युचुः । हे सुभगे शोभनसौभाग्यवति हे सरमे दिवो
> द्युलोकस्यांतान्पर्यंतान्परि पतंती कुत्र गावस्तिष्ठंतीति परितो गच्छंती त्वमिमाः
> परिदृश्यमाना या गावः ॥ सुब्बत्ययः ॥ गा ऐच्छः कामयसे ता एना गास्ते
> त्वदीयः कोऽयुध्वयुद्ध्वाव सृजात् । अस्मात्पर्वतादवसृजेत् । विनर्गमयेत् ॥
> सृजेर्लॆटि रूपं । आयुध्वी युधेः क्ताप्रत्यये स्नात्वादयश्चेति निपातितः ।
> नञ्समासत्वाल्ल्यबादेशाभावः । नञ्षः प्रकृतिस्वरत्वं ॥ उतापि चास्माकं तिग्मा तीक्ष्णान्यायुधायुधानि संति । तस्मादस्माभिर्युद्धमकृत्वा को नाम गा आहरति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The angry Panis replied. "O fortunate Sarama, you who go about to the very ends of the sky, asking where the cows are: these cows that are seen, which you desire, who of your side would release them without a fight? (अयुध्वी (*ayudhvī*) is a form of *yudhvā*, 'without fighting', derived from the root युध (*yudha*); being a compound with the negative, it keeps the accent of the original word.) अव सृजात् (*ava sṛjāt*) means 'would release from this mountain', 'would send forth'; it is a leṭ form of सृज (*sṛja*). And our weapons are sharp. So who would carry off the cows without making war on us?"

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सुभगे (*subhage*)—O fortunate one
- सरमे (*sarame*)—O Sarama
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—of the heaven
- अंतान् परि (*aṃtān pari*)—to the very end regions
- पतंती (*pataṃtī*)—you who have come
- याः इमाः गावः (*yāḥ imāḥ gāvaḥ*)—these cows which
- ऐच्छः (*aicchaḥ*)—you desire
- एनाः (*enāḥ*)—these very cows
- ते (*te*)—to you
- कः (*kaḥ*)—who indeed
- अयुध्वी (*ayudhvī*)—without battle
- अव सृजात् (*ava sṛjāt*)—would give up?
- अस्माकं (*asmākaṃ*)—our
- आयुधा (*āyudhā*)—weapons
- तिग्मा (*tigmā*)—sharp
- संति (*saṃti*)—are

### Printed p. 302

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(The Panis:) O fortunate Sarama, you who have come to the very end regions of the sky: these cows that you desire, who indeed would give them up to you without fighting? Our weapons are sharp.

##### English translation
(The Panis:) These are the cows which you, auspicious Sarama, coming to the extremities of the sky, demand. Who will give them up to you without a combat? and our weapons are sharp.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒से॒न्या वः॑ पणयो॒ वचां॑स्यनिष॒व्यास्त॒न्वः॑ संतु पा॒पीः ।  
> अधृ॑ष्टो व॒ एत॒वा अ॑स्तु॒ पंथा॒ बृह॒स्पति॑र्व उभ॒या न  ॑
> मृळात् ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> असेन्या । वः । पणयः । वचांसि । अनिषव्याः । तन्नः । संतु । पापीः ।  
> अधृष्टः । वः । एतवै । अस्तु । पंथाः । बृहस्पतिः । वः । उभया । न । मृळात् ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सा तान्निराह । हे पणयः वो युष्माकं वचांसि पूर्वोक्तानि वचनान्यसेन्यासेन्यानि । सेनार्हाणि न भवंति ॥ सेनाशब्दात्तदर्हतीत्यर्थे छंदसि चेति
> यत्प्रत्ययः । नञ्जा समासः । ययतोश्चातदर्थे । पा. ६-२-१५६ । इत्युत्तरपदां.
> तोदात्तत्वं ॥ तथा तन्नो युष्मदीयानि शरीराण्यनिषव्या इष्वर्हाणि न संतु
> पराक्रमराहित्येन ॥ पूर्ववत्पत्ययः । ओर्गुणः । पा. ६-४-१४६ । इति गुणः । स्वरश्च
> तादृक् ॥ यतः पापीः पापयुक्तानि खलु ॥ छंदसीवनिपावितीप्रत्ययः । जसः शः ॥
> किंच वो युष्मदीयः पंथा मार्ग एतवै गंतुमधृष्टोऽसमर्थोऽस्तु ॥ इण्

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sarama answers them. "O Panis, your words, those spoken before, are असेन्या (*asenyā*): not fit for an army, not the words of warriors. (The word is formed from सेना (*senā*) with the affix यत् (*yat*) in the sense 'worthy of that', compounded with the negative; the final syllable is acute.) Likewise let your bodies be अनिषव्याः (*aniṣavyāḥ*), unfit for arrows, since they lack valour. For they are पापीः (*pāpīḥ*), full of sin. Moreover let your path be अधृष्टः (*adhṛṣṭaḥ*), unable, for travelling (एतवै (*etavai*))." The explanation of एतवै (*etavai*) continues in the next block.

### Printed p. 303

> गतावित्यस्य तुमर्थे तवैप्रत्ययः । तन्नै चांतश्च युगपत् । पा. ६-२-५१ । इति
> धातोः प्रत्ययस्य च युगपदुदात्तत्वं । तत्र हेतुमाह । वो युष्मदीयानुभयोभयविधान्पूर्वोक्तांस्तन्वो देहान्बृहस्पतिरिंद्रप्रेरितो न मृळात् । न
> सुखियतु । किंतु बाधेत ॥ मृड सुखने । लेट्यडागमः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

(Continuing) एतवै (*etavai*) is the infinitive of the root इण् (*iṇ*) 'to go', with the affix तवै (*tavai*), the root and the affix being acute together. The reason is given: Bṛhaspati, impelled by Indra, will not मृळात् (*mṛḷāt*), will not comfort, your bodies in either of the two ways (उभया (*ubhayā*): the words and the bodies mentioned before), but will afflict them. The root मृड (*mṛḍa*) is in the sense of giving comfort; in the leṭ the augment aṭ is added.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पणयः (*paṇayaḥ*)—O Panis
- वः (*vaḥ*)—your
- वचांसि (*vacāṃsi*)—words
- असेन्या (*asenyā*)—are not fit for an army (soldiers)
- तन्नः (*tannaḥ*)—your bodies
- अनिषव्याः (*aniṣavyāḥ*)—not fit for arrows
- संतु (*saṃtu*)—let them be (they are)
- पापीः (*pāpīḥ*)—your bodies are full of sin
- वः (*vaḥ*)—your
- पंथाः (*paṃthāḥ*)—path
- एतवै (*etavai*)—for travelling
- अधृष्टः (*adhṛṣṭaḥ*)—unable
- अस्तु (*astu*)—let it be (it is)
- वः (*vaḥ*)—your
- उभया (*ubhayā*)—either the speech or the body
- बृहस्पतिः (*bṛhaspatiḥ*)—Bṛhaspati
- न मृळात् (*na mṛḷāt*)—let him not comfort (he will not heed)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(Sarama:) O Panis, your words are not the words that soldiers speak; your bodies are not impervious to arrows. Your bodies are full of sin. The way you follow is of no use. Bṛhaspati will take no heed of your speech or of your bodies.

##### English translation
(Sarama :) Your words, Panis, are not in the place of armies; your sinful bodies will not be equal to arrows. Let your path be difficult to follow, let Brihaspathi show no favour to either (your words or your persons).

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒यं नि॒धिः स॑रमे॒ अद्रि॑बुध्नो॒ गोभि॒रश्वे॑भि॒र्वसु॑भि॒र्नृष्य॑ष्टः ।  
> रक्षं॑ति॒ तं प॒णयो॒ ये सु॑गो॒पा रेकु॑ प॒दमल॑क॒मा  ॑
> जगंथ ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आयं । निधिः । सरमे । अद्रिबुध्नः । गोभिः । अश्वेभिः । वसुभिः । निऋष्टः ।  
> रक्षंति । तं । पणयः । ये. । सुगोपाः । रेकु । पदं । अलकं । आ । जगंथ ॥ ७ ॥

### Printed p. 304

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ते पुनराहुः । हे सरमे अयं निधिरस्मदीयः कोशोऽद्रिबुध्नः ॥ बंध
> बंधने । बंधेर्व्रधिबुधी च । उ. ३-५ । इति नप्रत्ययो बुध इत्यादेशः ॥ अद्रिर्बंधको यस्य तादृशः । तथाहृतैर्गॊभिरश्वेभिरश्वैश्च वसुभिरात्मीयैर्धनैश
> न्यृष्टो नितरां प्राप्तो भवति ॥ ऋषी गत् । क्तप्रत्यये श्विदितो निष्ठायामितीट् प्रतिषेधः । गतिरनंतर इति गतेः प्रकृतिस्वरत्वं ॥ सुगोपाः ॥ गुपू रक्षणे । आयप्र
> त्ययांतात्क्विप्यातोलोपयलोपौ ॥ सुष्टु गोपायितारो ये पणयस्तेऽसुरास्तं निधिं रक्षंति । पालयंति । रेकु ॥ रेकृ शंकायां । औणादिक उप्रत्ययः ॥
> शंकितं गोभिः शब्दायमानं पदमस्माभिः पालितं स्थानमलकं व्यर्थमेवा जगंथ ।
> आगतवत्यसि ॥ गमर्लिटि रूपं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The Panis answer again. "O Sarama, this treasure of ours, our store, is अद्रिबुध्नः (*adribudhnaḥ*): one whose base or binding is a mountain. (बुध्न (*budhna*) is formed from the root बंध (*baṃdha*) 'to bind' with the affix na, the substitute बुध (*budha*) coming in.) It is न्यृष्टो (*nyṛṣṭo*), fully furnished, with cows brought here, with horses, and with its own riches. The Panis, the Asuras, who are good guardians (सुगोपाः (*sugopāḥ*), from the root गुपू (*gupū*) 'to protect') guard it. रेकु (*reku*) (from the root रेकृ (*rekṛ*) 'to doubt', with an Uṇādi affix u) means causing doubt, resounding with cows: this place, guarded by us, you have come to अलकं (*alakaṃ*), in vain." आ जगंथ (*ā jagaṃtha*) is a liṭ form of गम (*gama*), 'you have come'.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सरमे (*sarame*)—O Sarama
- अद्रिबुध्नः (*adribudhnaḥ*)—situated in the hidden region of the mountain
- अयं निधिः (*ayaṃ nidhiḥ*)—this treasure
- गोभिः (*gobhiḥ*)—with cows
- अश्वेभिः (*aśvebhiḥ*)—with horses
- वसुभिः (*vasubhiḥ*)—with other riches
- नृष्टः (*nṛṣṭaḥ*)—is filled
- ये पणयः (*ye paṇayaḥ*)—the Panis who
- तं (*taṃ*)—that treasure
- रक्षंति (*rakṣaṃti*)—protect, they
- सु गोपाः (*su gopāḥ*)—are excellent guardians
- रेकु (*reku*)—hidden (causing suspicion)
- पदं (*padaṃ*)—to our place
- अलकं (*alakaṃ*)—in vain
- आ जगंथ (*ā jagaṃtha*)—you have come

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(The Panis:) O Sarama, this treasure in the hidden region of the mountain is filled with cows, horses and other riches. The Panis who guard that treasure are its excellent guardians. Your coming to our suspicious place is in vain.

##### English translation
(The Panis :) This treasure, Sarama, secured in the mountain is composed of cows horses and riches: the Panis protect it who are good watchers; you have come this lonely spot in vain.

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> एह ग॑म॒न्नृष॑यः॒ सोम॑शिता अ॒यास्को॒ अंगि॑रसो॒ नव॑ग्वाः ।  
> त ए॒तमू॒र्वं वि भ॑जंत॒ गोना॒मथै॒तद्वचः॑ प॒णयो॒ वम॒न्नित् ॥ ८ ॥

### Printed p. 305

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । इह । गमन् । ऋषयः । सोमशिताः । अयास्यः । अंगिरसः । नवग्वाः ।  
> ते । एतं । ऊर्वं । वि । भजंत । गोनां । अथ । एतत् । वचः । पणयः । वमन् ।  
> इत् ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सरमा पुनः प्रत्युवाच । हे पणयः सोमशिताः सोमेन तीक्ष्णीकृताः
> सोमपानेन मत्ताः ॥ शिञ् निशाने । कर्मणि क्तप्रत्ययः । तृतीया कर्मणीति
> पूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरत्वं ॥ तादृशा नवग्वा नवगतॆयः । यद्वा । अंगिरसां सत्रमासीनानां मध्यॆ केचन नवसु माःस्वध्यतिष्ठन् ते नवग्वाः । अनेन दशग्वा अप्युपलक्ष्यंते । उभयविधास्ते०गिरस ऋषयः तेषां प्रथमोऽयास्य एतन्नामा च त एत
> इह युष्मदीये स्थान आ गमन् । आगच्छेयुः ॥ गमेशृंदसि लुज्लज्लिट इति
> सार्वकालिको लुज् । लृदित्वाच्चेरज् ॥ आगत्य च ते गोनां ॥ गोः पादांत इति
> च्छंदसि नुडागमः ॥ गवामूर्व० तं समूहं वि भजंत । विभागं कुर्युः ॥ अत्रापि
> पूर्ववत्सार्वकालिको लुज् ॥ अथानंतरं पणयो यूयमेतद्वचः पूर्व०
> यद्व्यर्थमागतासीति यद्वाक्यमवोचत तद्वाक्यं तदा वमन्निद्वमंतः परित्यजंत
> एव भवथ ॥ वमु उद्गिरणे । शतरि सुपां सुलुगिति जसो लुक् । नुमागमः ।
> संयोगांतस्य लोपः । यद्वा । लजि रूपं । स्वरश्चांदसः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sarama replies again. "O Panis, the seers सोमशिताः (*somaśitāḥ*), sharpened by Soma, that is, exhilarated by drinking Soma (from the root शिञ् (*śiñ*) 'to sharpen'; the affix क्त (*kta*) is used in the passive, and the first member keeps its own accent), such नवग्वाः (*navagvāḥ*), 'those of nine goings'. Or else: among the Aṅgirases sitting at a sattra, some performed it through nine months; these are the नवग्वाः (*navagvāḥ*), and by this word the दशग्वा (*daśagvā*) are also indicated. These Aṅgirasa seers are of both kinds; the first of them is अयास्यः (*ayāsyaḥ*), so named. They will come here to your place (आ गमन् (*ā gaman*) stands for 'would come'; the lun form is used in the Veda for all times). Having come, they will divide that herd (ऊर्वं (*ūrvaṃ*)) of cows (गोनां (*gonāṃ*), with the augment nuṭ). Here too, as before, lun is used for all times. Then you Panis will be वमन्नित् (*vamannit*), spewing out, simply abandoning, this speech that you uttered before, 'you have come in vain'." (वम (*vama*) from the root वमु (*vamu*) 'to vomit'.) Or else, it is a laṅ form; the accent is Vedic.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Panis) सोमशिताः (*somaśitāḥ*)—stimulated by drinking Soma
- नवग्वाः (*navagvāḥ*)—those with the sacrificial rite of nine months
- अंगिरसः (*aṃgirasaḥ*)—called Aṅgirases
- ऋषयः (*ṛṣayaḥ*)—seers
- अयास्यः (*ayāsyaḥ*)—together with Ayasya
- इह (*iha*)—to this place of yours
- आ गमन् (*ā gaman*)—come
- ते (*te*)—they
- गोनां (*gonāṃ*)—of the cows
- ऊर्वं (*ūrvaṃ*)—the herd
- वि भजंत (*vi bhajaṃta*)—divide
- अथ (*atha*)—afterwards
- पणयः (*paṇayaḥ*)—you Panis
- एतद्वचः (*etadvacaḥ*)—the word with which you taunted me
- वमन्नित् (*vamannit*)—you will regard as vain

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(Sarama:) O Panis, the seers called Aṅgirases, stimulated by drinking Soma and engaged in the nine-month sacrificial rite, will come to this place of yours together with Ayasya. They will divide the herd of cows among themselves. Afterwards you will regard as vain the word with which you taunted me.

### Printed p. 306

##### English translation
(Sarma :) Excited by the Soma, the Rishis, the Angirasas of the nine month's rite, headed by Ayasya, will come hither : they will partition this herd of cattle, then the Panis will retract their words.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ए॒वा च॒ त्वं स॑रम आज॒गंथ॒ प्रबा॑धिता॒ सह॑सा॒ दैव्ये॑न ।  
> स्वसा॑रं त्वा कृणवै॒ मा पुन॑र्गा॒ अप॑ ते॒ गवां॑ सुभगे  
> भजाम ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> एव । च । त्वं । सरमे । आजगंध । प्रबाधिता । सहसा । दैव्येन ।  
> स्वसारं । त्वा । कृणवै । मा । पुनः । गाः । अप । ते । गवां । सुभगे । भजाम ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> तय्येवमुक्ते सति पणयः प्रणयवाक्यमाहुः । हे सरमे त्वं दैव्येन देवसंबंधिना सहसा बलेन प्रबाधिता यथा तथा वलपुरं प्राप्य तत्र स्थिता गा दृष्ट्वा
> पुनरागच्छेति तेन प्रपीडिता त्वमेवं चेदाजगंथ आगतवत्यसि ॥ चशब्दश्चेदर्थे ।
> निपातैर्यद्यदिहंतेति तिजो निघाताभावः । गमर्लिटि थलि रूपं । सह सुपेत्यत्र
> सहेति योगविभागात्समासः । तिजि चोदात्तवतीति गतेर्निघातः । लित्स्वरः ॥ तर्हि
> त्वा त्वां स्वसारं भगिनीं कृणवै । करवै । समूहापेक्षैकवचनं । त्वं तु पुनर्मा गाः ।
> इंद्रादीन्मा गच्छ । अपि तर्हि हे सुभगे सरमे ते त्वदीयानां गवां समूहं
> पर्वतादपगमय्य भजाम । त्वं च वयं च विभजाम । विभागं करवामेत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

When she had spoken thus, the Panis uttered words of conciliation. "O Sarama, if you have come (आजगंथ (*ājagaṃtha*); the word च (*ca*) is in the sense 'if') pressed, प्रबाधिता (*prabādhitā*), by the divine सहसा (*sahasā*), the force belonging to the gods, having reached the city of Vala and seen the cows standing there, and being told by him 'come back', then we shall make you our sister (स्वसारं (*svasāraṃ*), भगिनीं (*bhaginīṃ*); the singular is used with regard to the whole group). But do not go back again to Indra and the others. And so, O fortunate Sarama, having brought away your herd of cows from the mountain, we shall share it: you and we shall divide it, that is, make a division."

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सरमे (*sarame*)—O Sarama
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- दैव्येन सहसा (*daivyena sahasā*)—by power belonging to the gods
- प्रबाधिता (*prabādhitā*)—compelled
- आ जगंथ (*ā jagaṃtha*)—have come here
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- स्वसारं (*svasāraṃ*)—as our sister
- कृणवै (*kṛṇavai*)—we shall make
- पुनः (*punaḥ*)—back again
- मा गाः (*mā gāḥ*)—do not go
- सुभगे (*subhage*)—O beautiful Sarama
- ते (*te*)—with you
- गवां (*gavāṃ*)—the cows
- भजाम (*bhajāma*)—let us share

### Printed p. 307

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(The Paṇis:) O Saramā, you have come here by divine power, that is, to constrain us on behalf of the gods. We regard you as our sister. Do not go back. O fair-limbed Saramā, let us share the cows with you. (We shall give you also a share of our cows.)

##### English translation
(The Panis :) You have indeed come hither, Sarama, constrained by divine power; we will make you our sister, do not return, we will share the cattle with you, auspicious one.

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> नाहं वे॑द भ्रातृ॒त्वं नो स्व॑सृ॒त्वमिंद्रो॑ विदु॒रंगि॑रसश्च  ॒
> घोराः ।  
> गोका॑मा मे अच्छदय॒न्यदाय॒मपात॑ इत पणयो॒  ॑
> वरीयः ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> न । अहं । वेद । भ्रातृत्वं । नो इति । स्वसृत्वं । इंद्रः । विदुः । अंगिरसः । च ।  
> घोराः ।  
> गोऽकामाः । मे । अच्छदयन् । यत् । आयं । अप । अतः । इत । पणयः ।  
> ।  
> वरीयः ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सा तान्प्रत्याचष्टे । हे पणयः अहं भ्रातृत्वं न वेद । न जानामि । तथा
> स्वसृत्वं च नो वेद । नैव जानामि । के जानंति । तानाह । इंद्रो घोराः
> शत्रुणां भयंकरा अंगिरसॆश्च विदुः । जानंति । किंचास्मात्स्थानादहं यद्यदायं
> इंद्रादीन्प्राप्नवं ॥ अय पय गतौ । लजि रूपं ॥ तदा मे मदीया गोकामा

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Saramā answers them. "O Paṇis, I do not know brotherhood, and I do not know sisterhood either." Who does know? He names them: Indra and the Aṅgirases, terrible to enemies, know it. Further, he explains that from this place I came to Indra and the others (इंद्रादीन्प्राप्नवं (*iṃdrādīnprāpnavaṃ*)). Then my people, Indra and the others, who desire the cows, cover over (the place)…

### Printed p. 308

> युष्माभिरपहृता गाः कामयमाना इंद्रादय अच्चदय. । युष्मदीयं स्थानमाच्छादयंति ॥ छद अपवारणे ॥ अतः कारणाद्वे पणयः वरीय उरुतरं गवां
> वृंदं परित्यज्यापेत । अन्यत्स्थानं प्रति गच्छत । यद्वा । वरीयः प्रभूतमतिदूरं
> देशं गच्छत ॥ इत । इण् गतौ । लोटि रूपं वरीयः । उरिशब्दादीयसुनि प्रियस्थिरेत्यादिना वरादेशः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

…who desire the cows carried off by you, shroud your place (the root is छद (*chada*), "to cover"). For this reason, O Paṇis, abandon the herd of cows and go away to another place, to a wider, vaster one. Or else: go to a great and very distant region. इत (*ita*) is from the root इण् (*iṇ*) "to go", in the imperative; वरीयः (*varīyaḥ*) means "wider", "vaster".

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (ऎलै पणिगळे (*èlai paṇigaḷe*)) अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I
- भ्रातृत्वं (*bhrātṛtvaṃ*)—your relation of brotherhood with me
- न वेद (*na veda*)—I do not know
- स्वसृत्वं (*svasṛtvaṃ*)—my relation of sisterhood with you
- नो (*no*)—I do not know
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- घोराः (*ghorāḥ*)—terrible to enemies
- अंगिरसश्च (*aṃgirasaśca*)—and the Aṅgirases
- विदुः (*viduḥ*)—know this matter
- पणयः (*paṇayaḥ*)—O Paṇis
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- आयं (*āyaṃ*)—when I came here, then
- गोकामाः (*gokāmāḥ*)—desiring cows
- मे (*me*)—my kinsmen, Indra and the others
- अच्छदयन् (*acchadayan*)—have besieged this region
- अतः (*ataḥ*)—therefore, leaving this region
- वरीयः (*varīyaḥ*)—to a distant region
- अपेत (*apeta*)—go away

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(Saramā:) O Paṇis, I do not know what the relation of brotherhood or of sisterhood is that you wish with me. Indra and the Aṅgirases, terrible to enemies, know this matter. O Paṇis, from the moment I came here, Indra and the others, who desire the cows, have taken possession of this region. Leave this region and go far away.

##### English translation
(Sarama :) I recognize not fraternity nor sisterhood ; Indra and the terrible Angirasas know (my kindred); my (masters) desiring the cattle over-shadowed (your habitation) when I came ; depart hence, Panis, to a distant (spot).

#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> दू॒रमि॑त पणयो॒ वरी॑य॒ उद्गावो॑ यंतु मिन॒तीर्यु॒तेन॑ ।  
> बृह॒स्पति॒र्या अविं॑द॒न्नि गू॑ळ्हाः॒ सोमो॒ ग्रावा॑ण॒ ऋष॑यश्च॒  ॑
> विप्राः ॥ ११ ॥

### Printed p. 309

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> दूरं । इत । पणयः । वरीयः । उत् । गावः । यंतु । मिनतीः । ऋतेन ।  
> बृहस्पतिः । याः । अविंदत् । निगूळ्हाः । सोमः । ग्रावाणः । ऋषयः । च । विप्राः॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे पणयः यूयं वरीय उरुतरं दूरं दूरदेशमित । गच्छत ।
> युष्माभिरपहृता गाव ऋतेन सत्येन मिनतीर्मिनत्यो द्वारस्य पिधायकं पर्वतं
> हिंसत्यो विदारयंत्य उद्यंतु । तस्मादुद्दच्छंतु । यद्वा । मिनतीः ॥ व्यत्ययेन
> कर्मणि शतृ ॥ मीयमाना युष्माभिर्बाध्यमानास्ता गावः ॥ सुब्व्यत्ययः ॥ गा
> ऋतेन स्तुतिभिर्गंतव्येनेंद्रेण सहायेन बृहस्पत्यादय उद्यंतु । पर्वतादुद्धमयंतु । निगूढा नितरां स्थापिता या गा बृहस्पतिरविंदत् लप्स्यते तथा सोमस्तदभिषवकारिणो ग्रावाणश्च विप्रा मेधाविन ऋषयोऽंगिरसश्च लप्स्यंते ॥ विद्ल
> लाभे तौदादिकः । तस्माच्चंदसि लुज्लुज्लिट् इति भविष्यदर्थॆ लज् । शे मुचादीनामिति नुमागमः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Paṇis, go far away to a wider, more distant region. Let the cows carried off by you rise up, मिनतीः (*minatīḥ*) by ऋतेन (*ṛtena*), by truth, injuring and splitting the mountain that closes the door; so let them go up and out." Or else: मिनतीः (*minatīḥ*) has the participle ending used in the passive by a transposition, and means "being measured", that is, "being oppressed by you", those cows. By a transposition of case-endings (सुब्व्यत्ययः (*subvyatyayaḥ*)) ऋतेन (*ṛtena*) would mean: with Indra as helper, he who is to be approached by praises; Bṛhaspati and the others shall bring them up out of the mountain. The cows that have been hidden away, those Bṛhaspati will find; and likewise Soma, the pressing stones that press him, the wise seers, and the Aṅgirases will find them. The root is विद्ल (*vidla*) "to obtain" of the tudādi class.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पणयः (*paṇayaḥ*)—O Paṇis
- वरीयः दूरं (*varīyaḥ dūraṃ*)—to a very distant region
- इत (*ita*)—go away
- निगूढाः (*nigūḍhāḥ*)—hidden
- याः (*yāḥ*)—which cows
- बृहस्पतिः (*bṛhaspatiḥ*)—Bṛhaspati
- अविंदत् (*aviṃdat*)—will obtain, and so too
- सोमः (*somaḥ*)—Soma
- ग्रावाणः (*grāvāṇaḥ*)—the pressing stones
- विप्राः (*viprāḥ*)—the wise
- ऋषयः (*ṛṣayaḥ*)—the Aṅgirasa seers will obtain, such
- गावः (*gāvaḥ*)—cows
- ऋतेन (*ṛtena*)—in the form of truth
- मिनतीः (*minatīḥ*)—opening the door that confines them
- उद्यंतु (*udyaṃtu*)—let them come out

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(Saramā:) O Paṇis, go away to a very distant region. Let these cows open the door that confines them and come out in due order. After that let Bṛhaspati, Soma, the pressing stones, and the wise Aṅgirases obtain them.

##### English translation
Go hence, Panis, to a far-off distant (spot), let the cattle come forth in due order, bursting through (the door) the concealed cattle which Brihaspathi, the Soma, the grinding stones and the wise Rishis have found.

### Printed p. 310

## Sūkta 109 (Maṇḍala 10)

The commentator has told a story in connection with this sūkta: अत्रेतिहासमाचक्षते । जुहूरिति वाङ्नाम । सा ब्रह्मणो जाया च । बृहस्पतेर्वाचस्पतित्वादृहस्पतेर्जुहूर्नाम भार्या बभूव । कदाचिदस्य किल्बिषमस्या दौर्भाग्यरूपेणासांचक्रे । अत एव स एनां पर्यत्याक्षीत् । अनंतरमादित्यादयो देवा मिथो विचार्यॆनामकिल्बिषां कृत्वा पुनर्बृहस्पतये प्रादुरिति । तदत्र वर्ण्यते ॥ (*atretihāsamācakṣate | juhūriti vāṅnāma | sā brahmaṇo jāyā ca | bṛhaspatervācaspatitvādṛhaspaterjuhūrnāma bhāryā babhūva | kadācidasya kilbiṣamasyā daurbhāgyarūpeṇāsāṃcakre | ata eva sa enāṃ paryatyākṣīt | anaṃtaramādityādayo devā mitho vicāryènāmakilbiṣāṃ kṛtvā punarbṛhaspataye prāduriti | tadatra varṇyate ‖*) Juhū is a name for Speech (vāk). This goddess presiding over speech is the wife of Brahmā. Since Bṛhaspati too is said to be vācaspati, that is, lord of Speech (the presiding goddess of speech), this goddess of speech became Bṛhaspati's wife as well. At one time Bṛhaspati's sin turned into ill-fortune for this goddess of speech. Therefore Bṛhaspati abandoned his wife, this goddess of speech. Learning of it, the Ādityas and the other gods deliberated among themselves, removed the goddess's sin, and restored her to her husband Bṛhaspati. This very matter is described in this sūkta.

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> तेवदन्निति सप्तर्चं दशमं सूक्तं । जुहुर्नाम ब्रह्मवादिन्यृषिः । ब्रह्मपुत्र
> ऊर्ध्वनाभा नाम वा । षष्ठीसप्तम्यावनुष्टुभौ । शिष्टाः पंच त्रिष्टुभः । विश्वे
> देवा देवता । तथा चानुक्रांतं । तेवदन्नप्त जुहूर्ब्रह्मजाया ब्राह्मो वोर्ध्वनाभा वैश्वदेवं द्व्यनुष्टुबंतमिति ॥ गतो विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning तेवदन् (*tevadan*) has seven Ṛks and is the tenth sūkta. The seer is the Brahmavādinī named Juhū, or else Ūrdhvanābhā, a son of Brahmā. The sixth and seventh Ṛks are in Anuṣṭubh, the remaining five in Triṣṭubh. The deity is the Viśve Devāḥ. He cites the Anukramaṇī: तेवदन्नप्त जुहूर्ब्रह्मजाया ब्राह्मो वोर्ध्वनाभा वैश्वदेवं द्व्यनुष्टुबंतमिति (*tevadannapta juhūrbrahmajāyā brāhmo vordhvanābhā vaiśvadevaṃ dvyanuṣṭubaṃtamiti*). The application (viniyoga) has been stated already. **NOTE**

##### Anuvāda (Kannada)

This sūkta beginning तेवदन् (*tevadan*) is the tenth sūkta in the ninth anuvāka. It has seven Ṛks. For this sūkta the seer is the Brahmavādinī (seeress) named Juhū, the daughter of Brahmā; or else the seer is the son of Brahmā named Ūrdhvanābhā. The sixth and seventh Ṛks of this sūkta are in the Anuṣṭubh metre. The remaining five Ṛks are in the Triṣṭubh metre. The deity is the Viśve Devāḥ. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: तेवदन्सप्त जुहूर्ब्रह्मजाया ब्राह्मो वोर्ध्वनाभा वैश्वदेवं ह्यनुष्टुबंतं (*tevadansapta juhūrbrahmajāyā brāhmo vordhvanābhā vaiśvadevaṃ hyanuṣṭubaṃtaṃ*). The application has been stated before.

### Printed p. 311

Sūkta 109. Maṇḍala 10. Anuvāka 9. Sūkta 109. Aṣṭaka 8. Adhyāya 9. Varga 7. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 7.
Seer: जुहूर्ब्रह्मजायोर्ध्वनाभा ना ब्राह्मः (*juhūrbrahmajāyordhvanābhā nā brāhmaḥ*).
Deity: विश्वेदेवाः (*viśvedevāḥ*).
Metre: 1–5 त्रिष्टुप् (*triṣṭup*); 6, 7 अनुष्टुप् (*anuṣṭup*).

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ते॑ऽवदन्प्रथ॒मा ब्र॑ह्मकिल्बि॒षेऽकू॑पारः सलि॒लो मा॑त॒रिश्वा॑ ।  
> वी॒ळुह॑रा॒स्तप॑ उ॒ग्रो म॑यो॒भूरापो॑ दे॒वीः प्र॑थम॒जा  ॒॑
> ऋतेन ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ते । अवदन् । प्रथमाः । ब्रह्मकिल्बिषे । अकूपारः । सलिलः । मातरिश्वा ।  
> वीळुहराः । तपः । उग्रः । मयःभूः । आपः । देवीः । प्रथमजाः । ऋतेन ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अत्रेतिहासमाचक्षते । जुहूरिति वाग्नाम । सा ब्रह्मणो जाया च । बृहस्पतेर्वाचस्पतित्वादृहस्पतेर्जुहूर्नाम भार्या बभूव । कदाचिदस्य किल्पिषमस्या
> दौर्भाग्यरूपेणासांचक्रे । अत एव स एनां पर्यत्याक्षीत् । अनंतरमादित्यादयो
> देवा मिथो विचार्यैनामकिल्पिषां कृत्वा पुनर्बृहस्पतये प्रादुरिति । तदत्र
> वर्ण्यते । प्रथमा मुख्यास्ते देवा ब्रह्मकिल्पिषे ॥ षष्टिसमासः । समासस्वरः ॥
> ब्रह्मणो बृहस्पतेः किल्पिषे पापे जुहूर्दौभाग्यरूपे विषयेऽवदन् । निष्कृत्युपायमवोचन् ॥ वकारपरत्वादत्र प्रकृतिभावाभावः ॥ के ते । अकूपारः । आत्र
> यास्कः । आदित्योऽप्यकूपार उच्यतेऽकूपारो भवति दूरपारः । नि.४-१८ । इति ।
> अकुत्सितपारो महागतिरादित्यः सलिलोऽब्बेवता वरुणो मातरिश्वा वायुर्वीळुहराः ॥ हरतेरसुनि रूपं हर इति ॥ हरति विनाशयति तमांसीति हरस्तेजः । प्रभू-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

He recounts the story: Juhū is a name for Speech; she is Brahmā's wife, and since Bṛhaspati is lord of Speech, Juhū was also Bṛhaspati's wife. Once his sin turned into her ill-fortune, and for that reason he abandoned her. Afterwards the Ādityas and the other gods deliberated together, freed her from the sin, and gave her back to Bṛhaspati. This is what is described here. प्रथमाः (*prathamāḥ*) means those gods, the foremost. ब्रह्मकिल्बिषे (*brahmakilbiṣe*) is a genitive compound: in the sin of Brahman (Bṛhaspati), which has taken the form of Juhū's ill-fortune, they अवदन् (*avadan*), that is, they declared the remedy. Who are they? अकूपारः (*akūpāraḥ*): here Yāska says आदित्योऽप्यकूपार उच्यतेऽकूपारो भवति दूरपारः (*ādityo 'pyakūpāra ucyate 'kūpāro bhavati dūrapāraḥ*) (Ni. 4-18). The Āditya, whose crossing is not mean, of great motion, is अकूपारः (*akūpāraḥ*). सलिलः (*salilaḥ*) is Varuṇa, the deity of the waters. मातरिश्वा (*mātariśvā*) is Vāyu. वीळुहराः (*vīḷuharāḥ*) is derived from the root हृ (*hṛ*) with the suffix *asun*, giving हर (*hara*). It means what carries off, destroys the darknesses: brightness, that is, one of abundant brightness.

### Printed p. 312

> ततेजस्कः । तपः ॥ तृतीयायाः सुः ॥ तपसा तापनेनोग्र उद्योणोग्निर्मयोभूः सुखस्य भावयिता सोमो देवीर्देव्य आपः । कीदृश्यः । ऋतेन सत्यभूतेन
> ब्रह्मणा प्रथमजा आदित एवोत्पादिताः । एत उपायमुक्ता प्रायश्चित्तमप्यकारयन्निति भावः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

तपः (*tapaḥ*) (with सु (*su*) for the instrumental) is the fire, fierce and energetic through its burning heat. मयोभूः (*mayobhūḥ*) is Soma, producer of happiness. देवीः (*devīḥ*) are the divine waters. What kind? प्रथमजाः (*prathamajāḥ*), born first, produced in the beginning by Brahman, who is ऋतेन (*ṛtena*), truth. The meaning is that these gods, having stated the remedy, also had the expiation performed.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अकूपारः (*akūpāraḥ*)—the Āditya of endless motion
- सलिलः (*salilaḥ*)—the water-deity (Varuṇa)
- मातरिश्वा (*mātariśvā*)—Vāyu
- वीळुहराः (*vīḷuharāḥ*)—the very radiant one
- तपः (*tapaḥ*)—of burning nature
- उग्रः (*ugraḥ*)—possessed of great power, namely Agni
- मयोभूः (*mayobhūḥ*)—Soma, the giver of happiness
- ऋतेन (*ṛtena*)—by Brahman, who is of the nature of truth
- प्रथमजाः (*prathamajāḥ*)—produced first
- देवीः आपः (*devīḥ āpaḥ*)—the waters of divine form, and these and others
- प्रथमाः ते (*prathamāḥ te*)—all those foremost gods
- ब्रह्मकिल्बिषे (*brahmakilbiṣe*)—concerning Bṛhaspati's sin
- अवदन् (*avadan*)—explained

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The Āditya of endless motion, Varuṇa the water-deity, Vāyu, Agni the very radiant, of burning nature and great power, Soma the giver of happiness, the waters of divine form first produced by Brahman, who is of the nature of truth, and the other foremost gods, all explained the matter of Bṛhaspati's sin. **NOTE**

##### English translation
These spoke first about Brahma's sin, the boundless (sun), the water-god (Varuna), the wind god (Vayu), the fierce, wide-consuming fire, the source of happiness, (Soma), the divine waters, the first-born sons of the truthful (Brahma).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

बृहस्पतेः किल्बिषे पापे जुहूर्दौर्भाग्यरूपे विषये ते देवाः अवदन् ॥ (*bṛhaspateḥ kilbiṣe pāpe juhūrdaurbhāgyarūpe viṣaye te devāḥ avadan ‖*). Bṛhaspati's sin turned into ill-fortune for his wife, the goddess Juhū, and in the matter of her having been abandoned by her husband the gods spoke in this way. Here ते (*te*) is said to mean the gods. Who are those gods? प्रथमाः (*prathamāḥ*): the foremost ones. Who are they? अकूपारः (*akūpāraḥ*): आदित्योऽकूपार उच्यतेऽकूपारो भवति दूरपारः (*ādityo 'kūpāra ucyate 'kūpāro bhavati dūrapāraḥ*) (Ni. 4-18). The Āditya is called Akūpāra. Because this Āditya travels in the sky for a very great distance (from east to west), the Āditya is called Akūpāra.

### Printed p. 313

सलिलः (*salilaḥ*)—salila means water; here, Varuṇa, the deity presiding over water.

मातरिश्वा (*mātariśvā*)—the wind. Yāska says: मातरिश्वा वायुर्मातर्यंतरिक्षे श्वसिति मातर्याश्वनितीति (*mātariśvā vāyurmātaryaṃtarikṣe śvasiti mātaryāśvanitīti*) (Ni. 7-26). Because it pervades the atmosphere, or because it moves swiftly, the wind is called Mātariśvā.

वीळुहराः तप उग्रः (*vīḷuharāḥ tapa ugraḥ*)—हरति विनाशयति तमांसीति हरस्तेजः प्रभूततेजस्कः (*harati vināśayati tamāṃsīti harastejaḥ prabhūtatejaskaḥ*). Because he destroys darkness with his light, and because he is very fierce by nature through his heat, the word Vīḷuharāḥ here denotes Agni. तपः तपसा उग्र उद्धूर्णोऽग्निः (*tapaḥ tapasā ugra uddhūrṇo 'gniḥ*).

मयोभूः (*mayobhūḥ*)—maya means happiness; the one who produces that happiness, that is, who gives happiness or joy to those who drink him, is Soma.

आपः देवीः प्रथमजा ऋतेन (*āpaḥ devīḥ prathamajā ṛtena*)—the divine ones born first, produced in the beginning by Brahman, who is truth: the deities presiding over water. The Śruti and Smṛti say that when Brahmā created in the beginning, he created water before everything else. The sentences आपो वा इदमासन् सलिलमेव (*āpo vā idamāsan salilameva*), आपो वा इदमग्रे सलिलमासीत् (*āpo vā idamagre salilamāsīt*) and अप एव ससर्जादौ (*apa eva sasarjādau*) support this.

Hence the deities indicated by the word "te" at the beginning of this Ṛk are: Āditya, Varuṇa, Vāyu, Agni, Soma, the deity of the waters, and all the other deities. The main purport is that all of them consulted together, devised a fitting expiation to remove the ill fortune of Bṛhaspati's wife, which was a form of sin, freed her from the fault, and handed her back to Bṛhaspati.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सोमो॒ राजा॑ प्रथ॒मो ब्र॑ह्मजा॒यां पुनः॒ प्राय॑च्छ॒दहृ॑णीयमानः ।  
> अ॒न्व॒र्ति॒ता वरु॑णो मि॒त्र आ॑सीद॒ग्निर्होता॑ हस्त॒गृह्या नि॑नायि॥

### Printed p. 314

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सोमः । राजा । प्रथमः । ब्रह्मजायां । पुनरिति । प्र । अयच्छत् । अहृणीयमानः ।  
> अनुऽर्तिता । वरुणः । मित्रः । आसीत् । अग्निः । होता । हस्तगृह्य । आ । निनाय॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> प्रथमो मुख्यः सोमो राजाहृणीयमाणः । पापापगमनेनालज्जमानः ।
> संस्तामेनामकिल्पिषां ब्रह्मजायां पुनर्बृहस्पतये प्रायच्छत् । ततो वरुणोऽन्वर्तिता ॥ ऋतिः सौत्रो धातुर्घृणायां वर्तते । तस्य तृचि रूपं ॥ सोमनुनुमोदयितासीत् । सर्वथा त्वं परिग्रहाणेति दयामकार्षित् । तथा मित्रश्च ।
> अनंतरं होता देवानामाह्वाता मनुष्याणां होमनिष्पादको वाग्निर्हस्तगृह्य
> तां हस्ते गृहीत्वा निनाय । आनैषीत् । प्रादादित्यर्थः ॥ हस्तगृह्य । नित्यं हस्ते
> पाणावुपयमने । पा-१ ४-७७ । इति हस्तशब्दस्य गतिसंज्ञायां ग्रहिणा समासे
> ल्यप् । एकारलोपश्चांदसः । यद्वा । हस्तशब्दात्परस्य गृहेश्छांदसौ समासल्यपौ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Soma, the foremost and the king, not ashamed (अहृणीयमानः (*ahṛṇīyamānaḥ*) = not feeling shame at approaching the fault), gave back to Bṛhaspati this Brahman's wife, now free of sin. Then Varuṇa was the अन्वर्तिता (*anvartitā*): the root ऋतिः (*ṛtiḥ*) (sautra) is used in the sense of compassion; its form with *tṛc* is meant. He was the one who encouraged Soma, saying "Accept her in every way", and showed compassion; so too Mitra. Afterwards Agni, the Hotṛ, the summoner of the gods and accomplisher of the human homa, took her by the hand (हस्तगृह्य (*hastagṛhya*)) and led her; that is, he brought her, he gave her back. The rest of the block gives the formation of हस्तगृह्य (*hastagṛhya*) (grammar).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- प्रथमः (*prathamaḥ*)—the chief
- राजा (*rājā*)—the lord
- सोमः (*somaḥ*)—Soma
- अहृणीयमानः (*ahṛṇīyamānaḥ*)—without any hesitation, as the result of the removal of the fault
- ब्रह्मजायां (*brahmajāyāṃ*)—Brahman's wife, the goddess Juhū
- पुनः (*punaḥ*)—again
- प्रायच्छत् (*prāyacchat*)—gave to her husband
- वरुणः (*varuṇaḥ*)—Varuṇa
- अन्वर्तिता (*anvartitā*)—as the one who gave assent to this act
- आसीत् (*āsīt*)—became
- मित्रः (*mitraḥ*)—Mitra also approved
- होता (*hotā*)—the accomplisher of the homa
- अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—Agni
- हस्तगृह्य (*hastagṛhya*)—taking her hand
- आ निनाय (*ā nināya*)—brought her

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Soma, the chief lord, without any hesitation as the result of the removal of the fault, gave Juhū, the wife of Brahman, back to her husband. Varuṇa became the one who gave assent to this act. Mitra became the one who summons the gods. Agni took her by the hand and brought her.

##### English translation
First, the royal Soma, without being ashamed, rstored Brahma's wife (to Brihaspati), Varuna was the inviter, and Mitra, Agni as the ministrant priest taking her by the hand, led her (to her husband).
